# Changelog

All notable changes to hypergumbo are documented in this file. The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

- Released **tool** is at: v7.0.0
- Released **schema** is at: v0.19.0

This changelog tracks the **tool version** (package releases). The **schema version** is tracked separately in `schema.py` as `SCHEMA_VERSION`. The schema version changes when `docs/schema.json` has significant updates: breaking changes to the behavior map output format (minor bump) or additions like new type definitions for YAML validation (patch bump).

> **Looking for the reader-friendly summary?** See **[docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md)** for the audience-organized release notes (CLI users, JSON consumers, per language/framework). This file is the **implementer log**: structured, mechanism-level entries per release.

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed
- **`verify-claims` said `confirmed` about calls it had never examined (INV-buzab P0, INV-zubuh P1).** Reproduced on the shipped CLI with no overlay, no flags and no third-party dependency, alongside controls that behaved correctly in the same runs:

  | fixture (~10 lines of Python) | claim | before | after |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `telnetlib.Telnet(host,23)`; `conn.write(secret)` | no `net_send` | **confirmed, rc 0** | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | `ssl.create_default_context()`; `sock.sendall(secret)` | no `net_send` | **confirmed, rc 0** | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | `ctypes.CDLL("libc.so.6").system(b"curl …")` | no `net_send` | **confirmed, rc 0** | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | `os.sendfile(sock_fd, file_fd, 0, 4096)` | no `net_send` | **confirmed, rc 0** | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | `os.open(p, O_WRONLY)`; `os.write(fd, data)` | no `fs_write` | **confirmed, rc 0** | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | *control* — `requests.post(url, data=secret)` | no `net_send` | inconclusive, rc 2 | inconclusive, rc 2 |
  | *control* — `os.makedirs(p)`; `os.remove(p)` | no `fs_write` | violated, rc 1 | violated, rc 1 |
  | *control* — `with open(...) as fh: json.dump(obj, fh)` | no `net_send` | confirmed, rc 0 | **confirmed, rc 0** |

  The controls are what make the first five a defect rather than an analyzer that cannot see: the run works, classifies `os.makedirs`, and refuses to adjudicate `requests`. It then reports silence as safety for a program that opens a telnet session and writes a secret into it. **The gate refused on the library ADR-0016 excludes from the catalogue and waved through the stdlib it includes.** The last row is the guard in the other direction — a program whose external calls are all classified still confirms.

- **The unit of examination is the CALL SITE, not the module.** The gate permitted a module on either `is_stdlib_module` — membership in a 300-entry list generated from `sys.stdlib_module_names`, where the gate's own docstring called the branch *"an examined negative"* though 283 of those 300 carry no catalogue row — or on **row presence**, which is boundary-blind and surface-blind: `os` has 40 rows, none of them `os.open` / `os.write` / `os.sendfile`, and a module catalogued only for `fs_read` (`csv`, `pickle`, `shelve`, `dbm`, `fileinput`) vouched for a **network** claim. Both are gone. A call the catalogue **classified** was examined — that is what examination is — and the far smaller remainder, the calls it could *not* classify, is settled by an explicit dated per-module audit (`stdlib_module_completeness`, via the new `IoBoundaryCatalog.module_io_is_enumerated`). An intermediate draft answered this per-module and was wrong in both directions: it reported `builtins` and `json` as having "no I/O catalog coverage" one line above `2 fs_write chain(s) found` — chains found through those very modules.

- **The classification question now has one home, `io_boundary.classify_call`, consumed by the tagging pass and by the gate.** They were about to hold separate answers to "did the catalogue classify this call", which is how the gate came to call a site unexamined that the tagger had just tagged. `is_stdlib_module_complete` is folded into `module_io_is_enumerated` for the same reason — one closed-world predicate, read by the coverage gate and by the F3 Filter 2 `external_potential` skip.

- **Matching is EXACT — not prefix, not suffix, not component.** An earlier draft let a declaration descend a separator, and that is wrong in three shipped languages at once: `urllib` is a namespace package whose submodules have unrelated I/O surfaces (`urllib.request` opens URLs, `urllib.parse` does string work); Go's `/` is not containment, so a declaration for `os` would have vouched for `os/exec`, the subprocess surface; and Rust's `::` was not in the separator list at all. Nor is it a suffix: the boundary **tagger**'s `_module_matches` is component-suffix-aware so `unix` finds `golang.org/x/sys/unix`, and the two rules stay separate on purpose — the tagger is permissive because a missed tag loses a finding, the gate is strict because a wrong permit manufactures a false all-clear.

- **The strict direction is affordable because it cannot suppress a detection.** `verify_claim` returns `violated` outside the coverage branch, so coverage gates only the all-clear. **Measured on six real repos across six languages — httpx, full-stack-fastapi-template, poetry, hypergumbo itself, pretix, django — every one was ALREADY `inconclusive`, with 27 to 329 unadjudicable modules apiece, so no verdict changed on any of the six.** That is 0-of-6 and is not a claim about the population: a wider corpus was not run, and one adversarial reviewer reports 2 would-confirm→inconclusive flips on a 60-repo sweep, which this cohort is too small to have seen. The instrument is in-repo and its positive control is production's own `_uncatalogued_external_modules` output — a control that fired, correctly, the moment the fix landed and left the instrument modelling a rule the tree no longer ran.

- **The blast radius includes TAINT claims, which the boundary framing does not imply.** `_taint_blind_reason` (cli.py) deliberately reuses `compute_boundary_coverage` rather than growing a second "did this language produce call edges" implementation, so tightening the boundary gate tightens every `taint_flow` verdict too. Stated here because nothing in the boundary-claim story would lead a reader to expect it, and because the reuse means a future change to this gate silently moves the taint arm as well.

- **The defect was language-shaped by accident, and that is now a parity test over the live catalogue registry.** `stdlib_modules` is populated for Python (300 entries) and empty for the other 13, because it was added for supply-chain ecosystem stamping, not claim verification — so the same predicate ran permissive on Python and strict everywhere else. Measured at both ends: a Python program exfiltrating through `telnetlib` confirmed, while a Go program with **no filesystem I/O at all** returned inconclusive naming `fmt`, `sort` and `strings`. Post-fix every language that HAS an I/O catalogue is held to one rule. **A language with an analyzer and no catalogue is still skipped entirely, and that is a live false confirm this PR does not close:** a 12-line Ruby fixture doing `Net::HTTP.new(...).post(path, "key=\#{ENV['API_KEY']}")` returns `confirmed` rc 0 for both the network and filesystem claims, with no disclosure — and the analyzer is not blind, it emits the call edge. The gate's own SCOPE paragraph asserted this case was "already decided upstream"; it is not, and the paragraph is corrected rather than the residual quietly inherited. Filed.

- **A six-line overlay could re-open the exact defect this PR closes.** `stdlib_module_completeness` is now the single grant of confirmability, and `load_overlay_catalog` passed it through untouched while refusing the strictly WEAKER `status: complete` claim three lines earlier. Measured on the shipped CLI: an overlay with zero primitive rows and one completeness entry for `telnetlib` turned the exfiltration fixture from `inconclusive` rc 2 back to **`confirmed` rc 0**, disclosed by nothing but a stderr line naming the overlay path. An overlay declaring it is now a loud `IoPrimitiveOverlayError` rather than a silent drop — a silent drop would leave the author believing they had vouched for a module. Rows remain available to overlays.

- **CORRECTION to the entry above, which shipped with a false safety claim.** It said rows stay "because rows ADD detection, which is the safe direction for a user-authored file." **That is false under the rule this same PR introduced.** A row no longer only adds detection — a classified call is now what *examined* MEANS, so a row also grants examined-ness, and a row carrying the wrong boundary converts an unexamined call into an examined one that produces no chain for the boundary actually claimed. Measured three ways on one fixture (`requests.post` of `os.environ["API_KEY"]`, claim "never sends data over the network"), the middle run being the control that proves the row matched and the machinery works: **no overlay → `inconclusive` rc 2; `requests.post` declared `net_send` → `violated` rc 1; the same row declared `fs_read` → `confirmed` rc 0.** One wrong word buys an all-clear. Rows stay because the overlay channel exists for a use ADR-0016 §27 requires — cataloguing third-party egress so it becomes visible — and refusing them would close it; what separates a row from a completeness entry is SCOPE, not safety, since a row vouches for one named call surface while a completeness entry vouches for every call the catalogue could not classify. **Not a regression:** before this PR, row PRESENCE alone permitted the module on strictly weaker evidence. Tracked as INV-zosun, where the first move is DISCLOSURE — a verdict recording which catalogue it trusted — not restriction.

- **An overlay could also relabel a PyPI package as stdlib.** ADR-0016 forbids it and `load_overlay_catalog` pops `stdlib_modules` to enforce it — but `_from_dict` auto-promoted every `stdlib_module_completeness` entry into the same set, so an overlay that never mentioned `stdlib_modules` still made `is_stdlib_module("requests")` answer `True` on the merged catalogue. Measured, with the popped spelling as the control. ADR-0041 §3's normative single-source paragraph is amended to match: stdlib recognition still has exactly one source, and it no longer has a second writer smuggling audit results into it.

- **The reason string was reworded because the old one became false.** It said "module(s) with no I/O catalog coverage", accurate while the gate blamed modules the catalogue had never heard of; the gate now blames a module for the calls it could not classify, and those modules often have extensive coverage. It now reads "makes calls into N module(s) that the I/O catalog could not classify", which is both true and actionable — it says what to catalogue. **Disclosed limit:** the list is capped at 5 names plus a count, and the names are sorted alphabetically, so a stdlib module can evict the third-party library a reader most needs to see.

- **Known and disclosed: `confirmed` over a module the catalogue cannot classify requires an audit that has been written for exactly one module.** `stdlib_module_completeness` holds one entry across all 14 shipped catalogues (`math`). Programs whose external calls are all catalogued still confirm — that is most small programs, and the `json.dump` control above is one — but a program touching any unclassified call needs that module audited. The reason string names the gap rather than hiding it. Closing it is recall work with a measured backlog: across the six-repo cohort, 172 distinct Python stdlib module strings (105 top-level names) were reaching the gate on name recognition alone.

- **Three tests in `TestConfirmationIsPreservedWhereItIsEarned` asserted the removed doctrine in their own docstrings** — *"The catalogue still KNOWS it, so a call into it is an examined negative"* — and their fixture declared no completeness data, so they could never have exercised the correct rule. They were rewritten, and the two rejected rules are pinned to a refusal in `TestWeakerEvidenceDoesNotEarnIt`. Three *other* tests that the fix initially broke — including the project's own named guard against the blanket-downgrade failure mode — turned out to be right and the fix wrong; they pass unmodified under the call-site rule.


### Changed
- **Go's web-framework I/O rows move out of the shipped catalogue and into a project-local overlay — and they resolve for the first time (INV-safig).** `go.yaml` shipped 20 method rows for gin, echo, fiber and gRPC. Two independent reasons to remove them, either sufficient on its own:
  - **They are third-party, and ADR-0016 scopes the built-in catalogue to the stdlib** — *"a curated list of stdlib functions, not an unbounded set of library APIs"* (§27) — with §35 resolving exactly this gap as *"a project-local overlay, not more built-in rows"* and §55 placing worked examples under `docs/` because *"the user owns those rows."*
  - **They matched nothing.** They declared a package-**identifier** module (`gin.Context`) while the Go analyzer emits the module **path** it read from the import (`github.com/gin-gonic/gin`). Those never compare equal, so all twenty were unreachable from any real Go program: `lookup_with_module("JSON", "github.com/gin-gonic/gin", cc="method")` → `None`, while `lookup_with_module("JSON", "gin", …)` → `gin.Context.JSON`. The second is what a catalogue-reach probe produces when its fixture writes `import ("gin")` — an import no Go program contains — which is how rows that could never fire kept scoring reachable.
- **So this is a move plus a correction, not a deletion.** [`docs/io-primitives-overlays/go-web-frameworks.yaml`](docs/io-primitives-overlays/go-web-frameworks.yaml) re-declares all twenty against the real import path, including the `/vN` suffix that Go's semantic import versioning makes part of the path (`github.com/labstack/echo/v4.Context`). Paired with INV-javid, which fixed the analyzer half in the same release — before it, a `/vN` import bound the alias `vN` and echo and fiber would have stayed unreachable however the catalogue spelled them.

  **Measured on an idiomatic Go fixture, both arms, with a stdlib control firing in each so a zero is distinguishable from a broken fixture:**

  | | chains |
  |---|---:|
  | shipped catalogue alone (post-cull) | **0** framework chains |
  | + `go-web-frameworks.yaml` | **6** — gin `Context.JSON` / `Engine.Run`, echo `Context.JSON` / `Echo.Start`, fiber `App.Listen`, grpc `Server.Serve` |

  All six attribute to the calling function. Before this change the same fixture produced zero in *both* arms.
- **NON-DESTRUCTION ASSERTED, NOT ASSUMED**, because when "no change" is the correct answer a broken run looks better than a working one. A/B on real Go repos that actually import these frameworks, re-adding the pre-cull spellings through the overlay channel so the second arm reproduces the old built-in behaviour exactly: **keda (173 framework-importing files) — 105 io edges and 145 chains in both arms, 0 lost, 0 gained.**
- **The contract is pinned from both ends, in both packages, because the defect WAS the two ends drifting apart.** `test_go_web_framework_overlay` (core) asserts each row resolves from the literal import path — and, as a separate negative half, that the old short hint (`gin`, `echo`, `fiber`, `grpc`) now resolves to `None`, so the two spellings cannot quietly coexist and keep the fabricated-import probe reporting these rows as reachable. `TestFrameworkImportPathsReachTheModuleSlot` (mainstream) asserts the analyzer emits those same literal strings into the module slot. CI tests packages in isolation, so neither can check the other's half; asserting the same literals from opposite ends is what makes a one-sided edit fail.
- **Still shipping in `go.yaml` and NOT addressed here: 33 further third-party rows** (`golang.org/x/sys/unix` 27, `google.golang.org/grpc` functions 3, `golang.org/x/sys/execabs` 3). Unlike the 20, these are **reachable** — `_module_matches` compares trailing components and `unix` *is* the trailing component of `golang.org/x/sys/unix` — so culling them would delete working detections rather than dead rows. That is a genuine trade-off between ADR-0016 scope compliance and default detection, and it is filed for a decision rather than taken unilaterally.

### Added
- **Go semantic import versioning no longer erases a package's identity (INV-javid).** `_process_import_spec` derived a package's identifier as the last element of its import path, so a module at Go major version 2 or higher bound the literal string `vN` and its real identifier was never registered — `github.com/labstack/echo/v4` bound `v4`, never `echo`. Every call on such a package then missed `import_aliases` and its dst fell to the `external` placeholder, losing the module hint. Since Go modules, `/vN` is the standard for any library at v2 or above, so this was not an edge case. Now derived by `_go_package_identifier`, which also handles gopkg.in's dotted spelling (`gopkg.in/yaml.v2` → `yaml`).
  - **PAIRED A/B ON 13 REAL GO REPOS, 377,503 call edges, both directions reported.** `scripts/measure-go-import-versioning.py` lands in-repo and runs each arm in a **separate interpreter** — patching the derivation in-process and analysing twice would share every module-level cache the analyzer holds, which is how a run reports the first arm's answer twice.

    | | legacy | fixed |
    |---|---:|---:|
    | calls edges | 377,503 | **377,503 — identical in every repo** |
    | dsts with an `external` module slot | 83,267 | 81,974 |

    **1,293 `external` slots removed (1.55%). Zero regressions.** Of 1,446 gained dsts, 1,289 recovered an import path and 157 resolved to an in-repo symbol. Non-destruction is asserted rather than reported, because when "no change" is the correct answer for a metric a broken run looks better than a working one.
  - **The hijack bucket is reported separately and never folded into the headline**, because the prior Go typing attempt moved 692 edges and put 118 of them (17.1%) onto the wrong in-repo symbol. Here the 157 in-repo landings are `type` 84, `function` 62, `method` 9, `struct` 1, `field` 1. **The 84 `type` landings are Go type CONVERSIONS** (`caddy.Duration(x)`), which are calls only syntactically — the edge existed and was already a `calls` edge before this change, pointing at `external` instead; the change makes its target accurate and thereby makes a pre-existing modelling question visible. That question is WI-jalal's family (a `calls` edge whose dst is not callable), not this one's.
  - **2 of 13 repos changed by exactly zero** (cosign, gocryptfs), and that null is only reportable because the positive control fires: a synthetic `/v2` fixture moves 1 site in the same instrument.
  - **Likely root cause of WI-tivij**, open since July with "alias imports?" as an untested guess. Caddy's module path is `github.com/caddyserver/caddy/v2`; `caddy.RegisterModule(...)` previously emitted `go:external:0-0:RegisterModule` and now carries its import path, which is the precondition for the `init → RegisterModule → Module.CaddyModule` chain that item reports as broken. **Not claimed to close it** — that item reports 16 of 136 markers unreachable, and if every registration went through the root package all 136 would fail.
  - **The instrument had two defects of its own, both found by its own controls and both fixed before any number here was believed.** It first keyed each call site on `(src, line, dst NAME slot)` — a discriminator derived from the thing under test, since resolving a dst in-repo can re-spell its name slot receiver-qualified — and duly reported a non-destruction failure on caddy that was its own. It then paired the two arms' dsts positionally within a site, which silently matched *unrelated* calls on one line whenever sort order shifted and manufactured **5 regressions that do not exist** (`BlobStatter.Stat → external:Digest` is two different calls). Both are now a multiset difference over a caller-side-only key, which needs no pairing at all.

- **Catalogue reach now runs on Go, JavaScript and Java — and the probe was excluding its own fixture.** `scripts/measure-catalogue-reach.py` gains per-language call-site emitters with per-language FORM vocabularies (Go has one idiomatic form, Java's pair is instance/static rather than dotted/bare), plus a `go.mod` and one file per import path. Numbers and caveats in [`docs/measurements/0002-catalogue-reach-multilanguage.md`](docs/measurements/0002-catalogue-reach-multilanguage.md).
  - **THE FIRST GO RUN REPORTED 0 OF 210 AND THE INSTRUMENT REFUSED TO PUBLISH IT.** The probe's own banner read `// generated by scripts/measure-catalogue-reach.py`, which matches `supply_chain.is_likely_minified` heuristic 3 — `/(Generated by|@generated|DO NOT EDIT)/i` anywhere in the first five lines — and classifies the file `Tier.DERIVED`. Controlled, identical body: no header → 2 boundaries, `// leading comment` → 2, `// generated by …` → **0**; bisected to the exact phrase (`// generated` scores 2). The positive control (*"NOTHING RESOLVED … refusing to report a 100% miss"*) is the only reason a fabricated 100% catalogue gap did not ship.
  - **Filed as INV-pogob:** `supply_chain.py` carries TWO generated-file predicates with different patterns — `_GENERATED_HEADER_PATTERN` (strict, comment-anchored, needs `Code generated by … DO NOT EDIT`) and the loose heuristic above — and they disagree about this very file. Worse, the same header on a `.py` file changes nothing (3 boundaries either way), so `Tier.DERIVED` suppresses Go analysis and not Python's.
  - **Go: 178 of 210 (84.8%) production-faithful. The earlier "162 of 210, ALL 45 method-kind unattributed" result was a fixture artefact and is WITHDRAWN, along with its conclusion.** The probe emitted every Go method call as a parenthesized composite literal, `(&http.Client{}).Get(u)`; go.py types a receiver only when the operand is a bare identifier, so that one spelling carries no receiver evidence and mints `go:external:0-0:Get`, while `var c http.Client; c.Get(u)`, `c := &http.Client{}; c.Get(u)` and a typed parameter all mint `go:net/http:0-0:Get`. **The withdrawn conclusion — that Go was "the next target" for Python's receiver-typing fix — was the expensive half: Go receiver typing already exists and works, so acting on that sentence would have built a mechanism the analyzer has.** The instrument now probes both spellings and keeps the parenthesized one, so the analyzer hole stays measured instead of hidden.
    - **The corrected 198/210 is itself inflated by 20, so this arm reports two numbers.** The probe's import path is the catalogue's own module slot, which for a third-party entry is a package *identifier* rather than a module *path* — so the fixture writes `import ("gin")`, a line no Go program contains, and the entry scores reachable against a hint production can never emit (`lookup("JSON", "github.com/gin-gonic/gin")` → `None`; `lookup("JSON", "gin")` → `gin.Context.JSON`). The probe fabricates an import for **53** entries, but the fabrication is load-bearing for only **20** of them — all method-kind. A single-component module name survives the mismatch by accident of `_module_matches`'s suffix arm (`unix` is the trailing component of `golang.org/x/sys/unix`), whereas a method row's module carries a type qualifier (`gin.Context`) that is the trailing component of no real import path. So Go's third-party *function* surface is reachable on real code and its third-party *method* surface, all 20 rows, is not. The instrument now discloses its synthesised import paths rather than classifying them — separating a real single-segment stdlib path (`os`, `net`, `testing`) from a fabricated one (`gin`, `echo`) needs Go's stdlib list, which go.yaml does not carry: it marks the framework rows with a YAML comment and nothing machine-readable.
    - **Two different 53s, and the coincidence is disclosed rather than left to be misread.** The plan separately records go.yaml carrying *"53 of 210 entries violating the strict-stdlib rule"*. The sets overlap in 50 and differ by `filepath` (stdlib, written unimportably) versus `golang.org/x/sys/execabs` (third-party, but path-shaped so faithfully imported).
    - **Go's semantic import versioning erases the import path outright.** The package alias is the last path segment, so `github.com/labstack/echo/v4` registers `v4` and `echo` is never bound — `echo.Context.JSON` and `fiber.App.Listen` both fall to `go:external`. This affects any call on a value from a `/vN` package, not only catalogued ones, so its blast radius is the Go call graph rather than the io-boundary map. Unmeasured on any corpus, and structurally invisible to this instrument, whose fixture writes `import ("fiber")`.
    - **The 9 residuals are mis-credits, not misses**, and they invert sign between instrument and product: `net/http.Client.{Get,Post,Head}`, `log/slog.Logger.{Debug,Info,Warn,Error,Log}` and `testing.B.TempDir` each produce a chain under a *same-package function* row. Confirmed live through the CLI — a `*testing.B` receiver is credited `testing.T.TempDir`, and `tr := &http.Transport{}; tr.Do(..)` is credited `net/http.Client.Do`, a false positive since `Transport` has no `Do`. **`call_construct` cannot be used to fix this:** Go stamps it `"method"` on *every* selector-expression call, package-level free functions included, so `http.Get(u)` and `c.Get(u)` produce byte-identical `(name, module_hint, call_construct)` triples.
  - **JavaScript: the fixture shape moved the answer 35×.** With a function-local `require`, 4 of 187 (2.1%) attribute; with a module-scope `const m = require('fs')` — the dominant idiom — **182 of 187 (97.3%)**. The final numbers use module scope, but the lazy-`require`-inside-a-function shape is real code and is 97.9% blind, which is a recall gap with a clear shape and is **not** what INV-gijis describes.
  - **Java: 129 of 142 (90.8%) attribute via the STATIC form and ZERO via the instance form.** Reported as an observation, not a conclusion — the fixture demonstrably resolves, since the static form scores 90.8% on the same file. **This does not reproduce INV-suril:** the `java.nio.file.Files` entries attribute normally here because the import supplies a module hint, so the no-module-hint gate that item names is never reached. Its own repro needs re-running before either result is trusted.
  - **Python re-measured and it has improved:** `194 both / 16 dotted / 0 bare / 5 neither` against the recorded `153 / 16 / 41 / 5` — 41 primitives moved from bare-only to both, with the denominator and residual unchanged.

- **Taint-flow precision is measured on real repositories for the first time — ~41% population-weighted, and the obvious remedy is refuted.** `scripts/measure-taint-precision.py` and `docs/example-claims/generic-taint-claims.yaml` land in-repo, with the full verdict table in [`docs/measurements/0001-taint-flow-precision.md`](docs/measurements/0001-taint-flow-precision.md). The only prior datapoint was WI-ruhol's **50% (3 of 6)** on a *synthetic* reference implementation, closed 2026-03-25 before any of ADR-0017's nine phases shipped.
  - **Two populations, both adjudicated by reading source.** A **census** of every flow on caddy / mitmproxy / poetry / express / apollo-server — 60 flows, **26.2%** (11 TP of 42 adjudicated, 18 unadjudicable) — and a **stratified sample** of pretix, 44 of 205, **39.5%** (17 TP of 43). Combined 28 of 85 = 32.9% raw, **≈41% population-weighted**. Per WI-sivuz's pre-registered bands, named before any interval was computed, this is the **`<50%`** band: *"precision is the headline problem and recall work should stop until it is understood."*
  - **The pre-registered population had ceased to exist, and that was verified rather than assumed.** WI-sivuz fixed its frame as "the 1,890 flows on hypergumbo's two violated self-claims". Both claims were repaired (`runtime-cli-no-host-fs` by wrapping eight writes, `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` by declaring the `repo_inspection` zone), and a repaired claim reports **zero** violating flows because `verify_claims` never downgrades a `violated` verdict — only a would-be `confirmed` one. A live run returns 18 verdicts, all `inconclusive`. Four revisions are recorded on the item with their reasons; the one that makes the method *stronger* is that pretix's primary pass was handed to a second independent blind agent, removing the bias the pre-registration named and could not control ("I wrote much of the code being measured").
  - **A CENSUS, and the evidence cap is why that needed saying.** `_MAX_EVIDENCE_ROWS = 100` head-truncates the displayed evidence list, and head-truncation is not sampling — rows arrive in propagation order. The instrument raises that ceiling for its own in-process run (production unchanged) and prints the effective limit. No repo approached it; raw flow counts equalled distinct counts throughout.
  - **`analysis_method` REVERSES between populations, so it is not a filter.** `ddg` is 100% on both (4/4 and 3/3), but `ddg_mixed` is **11.8%** on the census and **57.9%** on pretix — five times more trustworthy on one substrate than the other. **And filtering to `ddg` keeps 7 of 85 flows while losing 21 of the 28 true positives (75%)**, including caddy's `for _, v := range os.Environ() { fmt.Println(v) }` — a genuine whole-environment dump, one of the three real leaks the earlier removal-authority trial found — and eleven Django ORM read→invoice/mail write chains in pretix. **Do not filter on `confidence`.**
  - **The security questions score very differently.** `untrusted-input-no-database` **60.9%** (Django read→write chains are long, explicit and genuinely data-carrying) down to `untrusted-input-no-subprocess` **14.3%** — and command injection fails *systematically*: all six pretix false positives are `subprocess.run([settings.PDFTK, *tempdir_paths, ...])`, where the tainted rows reach the PDF pdftk *reads* and never its argv. The tool is right to model the argument rather than the effect; a user asking "can input reach a shell?" still gets six answers, none of them yes.
  - **Claim-level correctness is roughly twice flow-level: 9 of 17 violated verdicts (52.9%) rest on at least one true flow.** A verdict is a disjunction, so the headline a user reads first is more trustworthy than any individual evidence row they drill into. Caddy's `printEnvironment` is the clean case — correctly `violated`, with the flow row naming the wrong `os.Getenv` read.
  - **19 flows cannot be adjudicated at all, and it is one defect.** Their source symbol is `javascript:<external>:0-0:file:external_symbol` — no file, no span. On the census the 18 such rows are the cartesian product of six catalogued browser globals against three `console` sinks: one collapsed bucket generates *sources × sinks* distinct "violations", scaling with the catalogue rather than the code. The split is exact — every unadjudicable flow is unanchored, every anchored flow was adjudicated.
  - **False positives are named and counted rather than left as a rate:** `co-location-function` 21, `content-not-argument` 8, `reachability-only` 8 (the first hop takes no arguments at all — `caddy.TrapSignals()`, `_get_preview_position()`), `co-location-file` 7, `control-dependence-only` 7, `misattributed-source-read` 2, `unrelated-files` 2 (a vendored `cropper.js` and a Vue component that never references it), `short-name-collision` 1 (`Checkin.objects.create` resolved to `QuestionSerializer.create`), `opened-path-not-content` 1.
  - **Independent verification: 2 disagreements in 104 (1.9%).** Both were the same question — does taint flow through a mutating call? — and resolving them required a principle the rubric had not stated: *taint flows through in-program computation, not through an external resource selected by the tainted value.* An argv-derived filename that is opened is FP; an ORM row rendered onto a canvas whose buffer is then written is TP. Stated in the record: a low disagreement rate shows the rubric is applied consistently, **not** that it is right — every pass shares it, and its control-dependence convention moves the headline 8.3pp on its own.
  - **What it does not support, carried in the record rather than implied:** this is precision, not accuracy — it says nothing about recall; six repositories are not a random sample of software, and pretix alone is 77% of the population and 14 of the 28 true positives, mostly one Django shape; the pretix interval is ±14pp and does not by itself exclude 50%.

- **Closing EVERY §3a escape site changes ZERO findings — the escape-closing family is priced at its ceiling, and the answer is that it cannot buy recall.** `scripts/measure-escape-closing-ceiling.py` lands in-repo. Several open items propose to close escape sites (INV-busis's `4a` catalogue gap, INV-mumov's missing emission, INV-linub's untyped receivers), each filed with a site count that invites the inference that closing them buys proportionate findings. The instrument refuses to measure an increment and forces `_use_site_terminates` to return True unconditionally, so every site closes at once — an upper bound no catalogue, emission or typing fix can exceed. **An increment measured at zero is indistinguishable from an instrument that cannot see; a ceiling measured at zero is a proof about the family.**
  - **Instrument control passed first** (16 walks left `none`, so the patch is demonstrably on the walk's path). hypergumbo: §3a walks went 22 True / 0 False / 20 None → **22 True / 16 False / 4 None**, and findings were `2 violated / 1453 sanitized / 2279 evidence` in **both** arms — delta zero on all three.
  - **Why it cannot be otherwise, read in code and confirmed at unit level.** `summaries` has exactly one consumer chain (`_qualified_callee` → `_catalogue_key_for_edge` → `callees_at` → `_use_site_terminates`) and only ever decides `continue` vs `escaped = True`; it never extends the frontier. The walk returns True at `if uses & targets: return True` *before* any escape is considered. So closing an escape can only convert `None` → `False` — which the §3a arm collapses (`is True`) and the barrier arm consumes as `is False`, where a `False` earns `sanitized` and **drops a flow from the violation set**. The only direction available to this family is the finding-DELETING one.
  - **Honest limits, carried not buried:** barrier walks were 0 in *both* arms here, so the suppressive direction is unexercised on this repo rather than disproven; and this is one repo, Python. The §3a inertness is structural and corpus-independent; the barrier-arm magnitude is not.
  - **The `4a` bucket is also misnamed.** Its 8 sites carry 7 distinct keys and only 4 are catalogue absences. `sys.sys.stderr` and `os.os.path` have a **doubled module segment** — the analyzer puts a module-qualified name in the NAME slot and the key builder concatenates `module + "." + name` anyway — and `file.write` carries a receiver variable in the module slot. No catalogue entry can match a malformed key. Filed separately.
- **38.6% of catalogued Python I/O primitives produced no boundary attributable to the function that called them, and the probe that says so generates its own fixture from the catalogue.** `scripts/measure-catalogue-reach.py` emits one call site per entry in `load_catalog(lang).primitives`, in **both** import forms (`import os; os.makedirs(p)` and `from os import makedirs; makedirs(p)`) because they exercise different resolution paths and a single-form probe scores a primitive half of real code cannot reach. Before: `BOTH 105 (48.8%) / DOTTED-only 27 / BARE-only 0 / **NEITHER 83 (38.6%)**`, with **78 of the 83 method-kind**. After the fix below: `BOTH 153 / DOTTED 16 / BARE 41 / **NEITHER 5**`, and the 5 residual are all function-kind (`subprocess.Popen`, `fileinput.FileInput`, the three `tempfile` factories). Reproduced back-to-back on separate workdirs.
  - **"ATTRIBUTED", NOT "REACHABLE" — the first cut of this entry said "unreachable" and that overstates it.** The verdict asks whether production named the CALLING FUNCTION in the chain's `io_edge_src`. All five residual primitives *do* produce a chain; it is anchored to `python:<external>:0-0:file:external_symbol` — a non-caller name — so the defect is "sees the boundary, cannot say who called it", not "cannot see the boundary". Different defect, different owner. The probe now prints the `io_edge_src` KIND-slot tally and the anchored-to-a-non-caller count alongside the verdicts so the distinction cannot be lost again.
  - **A slot-reading error of my own is recorded here because it cost real time.** An attributed chain reads `python:probe.py:0-0:<probe_fn>:external_symbol`: the caller sits in the NAME slot while the KIND slot says `external_symbol` for *every* chain on this fixture. Counting the kind slot and calling it "the anchor" made two correct readings look contradictory and briefly produced a false alarm that the instrument was non-reproducible. It is reproducible; the reading was wrong. `entry_points` is likewise empty on all 476 chains here, so it cannot discriminate either and is no longer used as the signal.
  - **Stated as a lower bound, because the number invites over-reading.** It measures the two idiomatic forms only: a primitive scoring attributed can still be missed through an alias, a re-export or a wrapped receiver, and 7 entries (`builtins.open`, the synthetic `file.*` rows) are inexpressible as generated call sites and are reported separately rather than counted as failures. It is an attribution probe, not a recall estimate — the real-code delta for the change that motivated it was **zero edges on two repos** while this probe moved 78 primitives.
- **The Go receiver-typing gap is sized, and the answer is DON'T BUILD IT — 6 sites across 9 repos.** `scripts/measure-go-receiver-typing-gap.py` lands in-repo (the plan records instruments living in session scratchpads as a durability hazard). Go's inline `exec.Command(...).Run()` types correctly while the assigned `cmd := exec.Command(...); cmd.Run()` does not — the mirror of the Python defect fixed in #254, and therefore a distinct fix rather than a port of it.
  - **The denominator distinction is what decides it.** Across caddy, prometheus, alertmanager, kserve, cosign, act, grype, dnscrypt-proxy and gocryptfs: **143** candidate sites, **107** blocked today by `ambiguous_names`, but only **27** where the repo reports no boundary *anywhere* — because **116 of the 143 have a constructor that is itself a catalogued row** (`exec.Command`, `net.Dial`, `smtp.Dial`), so a boundary already fires at that line and the method call is a second signal at the sink, not a missed crossing. Quoting 143 or 107 as "the gap" would oversell it 24× / 18×.
  - **Of the 27, only 6 are reachable by the assignment fix.** The other **21 are rooted at a COMPOSITE LITERAL** (`&http.Client{}`), which offers no constructor call to type from. Counting them as this fix's payoff would have overstated it 4.5×.
  - **The dominant row is not an assignment gap at all.** `net/http.Client.Do` is 17 of the 27, and with a positive control that an earlier probe lacked (`http.Get` tags, so `net/http` is reachable), **both** forms return zero — inline and assigned. That is a composite-literal typing gap. The asked-about gap is confirmed cleanly and separately on `slog`, where the constructor is a call: `l := slog.Default(); l.Info(m)` → 0 against `slog.Default().Info(m)` → 1.
  - **What the data says to build instead:** composite-literal receiver typing (**21** sites, and the only security-relevant one — `client := &http.Client{}; client.Do(req)` with a tainted URL is SSRF and invisible in every syntactic form today). The typed-PARAM shape is larger (653 candidates, 145 blocked) and technically easier, but **~136 of the 145 blocked are `slog.Logger.{Info,Error,Warn,Debug}`** — the `logging` boundary — and the headline 653 is dominated by `testing.T.TempDir`, which already tags by short name.
  - **The instrument's own positive control caught a scanner bug before any corpus number was believed:** tree-sitter-go spells a type-position `pkg.Name` as `qualified_type`, not `selector_expression`, so every composite-literal constructor was being dropped and the four-site fixture reported three.
  - **Caveats that bound the number:** the constructor→type map is hand-built (the catalogue records which *methods* are I/O, not which functions *return* the receiving type), nine repos is a corpus statement rather than a language one, and these are static-scan **sites** — not production boundary **chains**, which the Python work established are a different population.

- **The proposed Python factory→receiver map is measured and REFUTED — 7 sites outside this repo, 1 on a production path (WI-jidij, closed `wont_do`).** The item was filed on the hypothesis that real code obtains catalogued receivers from factory FUNCTIONS (`conn = sqlite3.connect(p); conn.execute(q)`) rather than constructors, and it required a corpus measurement before anyone wrote the declared data such a map needs. `scripts/measure-factory-receiver-shape.py` lands in-repo and answers it over 11,674 Python files across hypergumbo, pretix, superset, mlflow, litellm, fastapi and nova.
  - **Ground truth is `ast`, deliberately not the analyzer** — the question is "what does real code contain", and asking hypergumbo would answer "what can hypergumbo see", which is the thing under test.
  - `CTOR` 192 sites (132 production, already handled) · `FACTORY` **29** (21 production) · `MANAGER` 4,690. **But 22 of the 29 factory sites are hypergumbo's OWN tracker cache**, so excluding self-bias the shape is **7 sites across six repos with exactly one on a production path**. The three types the item named fare worse: `sqlite3.connect` 4 bindings cohort-wide, `asyncio.open_connection` **zero**, `multiprocessing.Pipe` **zero**. A hand-maintained map serving one external production site is not worth the surface.
  - **The positive control failed on its first run, and that is why the null is believable.** The scanner tracked only local `Name` bindings, so it could not see `self._conn = sqlite3.connect(...)` — an instance attribute, which is how a long-lived connection is actually written and the exact shape of the control file. Without the control the corpus would have returned near-zero and the item would have been closed on a scanner bug rather than on evidence.
  - **A wrong claim made and corrected the same day:** the run's first write-up called the Django `Model.objects.<method>()` shape "not modelled at all". It is modelled — WI-sozoj, gated on `.objects.` plus a closed method set — and pretix yields **846** django-attributed chains, with `django.db.models.filter` (447) the largest single primitive in the repo. The scanner had reused WI-sozoj's own `.objects.` discriminator, so it reproduced a shipped feature's rule and reported it as a gap. Also corrected: 4,690 is a SITE count and 846 is a CHAIN count (deduplicated per boundary/primitive/src), so no coverage ratio between them is claimed — sites, chains and walks are different populations.

### Added
- **A `repo_inspection` safety zone: runtime subprocess use is now DECLARED rather than denied (WI-fasuv).** The shipped `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` claim read *"Runtime CLI subcommands do not shell out. All subprocess invocations (curl, git, pip, rustup, gitleaks) happen only via extras / build-time subcommands."* **That was false as written.** `strace -f -e trace=execve` on a default `sketch` of a two-file repo executes three binaries: `git` ×25 (`rev-parse HEAD`, `rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`, `config --get remote.origin.url`), **`gitleaks` running a real secret scan over the user's content**, and `rust-analyzer --version`. gitleaks is named in the claim as build-time-only and is not.
  - **The owner chose to declare the capability, not reword the claim.** Three options were put; amending the prose to carve out "read-only repo inspection" was the cheap one and is the one that **launders** — it weakens a published security claim with nothing enforcing the carve-out's bounds, so the sentence becomes true by meaning less. Routing through declared wrappers keeps the claim strong *and* true, and makes the boundary machine-checked.
  - **Three wrappers, not one**, despite near-identical bodies — mirroring the existing `cache_write` / `user_out_write` / `tmp_artifact_write` split. The taint machinery keys on the function, so `repo_inspect_git` / `repo_inspect_scan` / `repo_inspect_probe` let a future claim prohibit one activity while permitting another. One wrapper would collapse "reads your git metadata" and "reads every byte of your source" into a single indistinguishable permission.
  - **`rust-analyzer` is probed, never asked to index — and that distinction is pinned by a test.** It executes a project's `build.rs` and proc macros when it indexes; that is the most dangerous capability reachable from this tree, and the whole difference is `--version` versus an index request. `TestTheProbeDoesNotIndex` fails if `analysis-stats` / `scip` / `lsif` / `diagnostics` ever appear at that site.
  - **Behaviour is byte-identical, which is the point.** The strace canary re-run after the change shows the same three binaries at the same counts — git ×25, gitleaks ×1, rust-analyzer `--version` ×1. Nothing was added, removed, or gated; the capability was made visible to the analysis.
  - **MEASURED**: `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` `violated` → `inconclusive`, 1110 raw / 33 distinct unsanitized → **0 unsanitized, with 390 flows still reaching the `subprocess` zone and passing a sanitizer on every route**. The self-claim tally moves from `{violated 2, inconclusive 16}` at the start of this work to **`{inconclusive 18}`**.
  - **Zero violated is NOT "proven safe", and `confirmed` is still 0.** Every verdict is `inconclusive` because this repo contains bash/csharp/solidity with no taint catalogue, so hypergumbo declines to confirm a negative it cannot check. `SECURITY.md` regenerates to say exactly that, and now publishes the enumerated repo-inspection surface plus a note that the previous wording was false.
  - **Deferred with an explicit re-evaluation trigger**, per the rule that a KEEP verdict needs one: the `--version` probe shares the zone with git and gitleaks, which is the weakest part of the grouping — what unites the three is what they *cannot* do (no host write, no network action, no execution of repository code), not what they read. A separate `tool_probe` zone was considered and deferred because no claim today needs to prohibit the probe while permitting git. **If anything is ever added to this zone that reads repository CONTENT and acts on it rather than reporting on it, split the zone first.**

### Fixed
- **Runtime CLI subcommands wrote to the filesystem outside the wrapper discipline, and the `user_out` zone — the one whose path comes from the USER — was the only zone with no `mkdir` wrapper to use (INV-zudak, reopened).** The invariant was closed `satisfied` in May after migrating `build_grammars.py` and `gitleaks.py`; those are the *install* entry points, and the *runtime* ones carry the identical defect and were never in scope. Its own statement covers them: *every entry point with an asserted "no direct host_fs writes" claim must route every fs-write primitive through a `safety_zones.py` wrapper*, and `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` is such a claim.
  - **Eight unwrapped call sites**: `cli.py` ×6 (five `Path.mkdir`, one `shutil.rmtree` in `cmd_sketch`) and `sketch_embeddings.py` ×2 (`Path.mkdir` on the cache dir). **Four of the five `cli.py` mkdirs sit on the line DIRECTLY ABOVE a `user_out_write` / `user_out_open_json_dump` call** — the write was already wrapped and the `mkdir` creating its directory could not be, because `user_out` had no mkdir wrapper. `cache_mkdir` and `tmp_artifact_rmtree` already existed and were simply not used.
  - **New `user_out_mkdir`, deliberately NOT zone-bounded** unlike `cache_rmtree` / `tmp_artifact_rmtree`. There is no zone root to check: the user names the path with `--out`. What the wrapper buys is **attribution, not restriction** — the zone-barrier sanitizer stops the walk, so the write records as a `user_out` crossing instead of an unattributed `host_fs` flow.
  - **MEASURED, and the flows did not vanish — they became attributed.** `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` went `violated` → `inconclusive`, with **0 unsanitized flows** where there were 370, and **2190 flows still reaching `host_fs` and passing a sanitizer on every route**. `inconclusive` rather than `confirmed` is the honest-verdict machinery working as designed: this repo contains bash/csharp/solidity, which have no taint catalogue, so the analysis declines to assert a negative it could not check — the same reason 16 of the 18 claims are inconclusive. `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` is unchanged at 33 distinct (WI-fasuv owns it). One snapshot line ratified, in the improving direction.
  - **Behavioural evidence, per the closure-evidence discipline**: the item's own filed repro re-run — `survey --out <arbitrary>/deep/nested/tree/map.json` still creates all four directory levels and writes its output, and `cmd_sketch` still drains the legacy `/tmp/hypergumbo_sketch_compare/` backlog, now through a wrapper that *enforces* the path lies under the system temp dir. The bare `rmtree` carried `ignore_errors=True`, which the wrapper does not accept; the fail-open intent is preserved explicitly rather than silently dropped, so a stale directory owned by another user cannot abort `sketch`.
  - **Enforced as the rule, not the eight instances.** A new invariant test walks the runtime modules' ASTs for direct calls to catalogued `fs_write` primitives. It reuses the catalogue's own curated `ambiguous_names` to exclude bare-name collisions — without that filter it flagged 21 false positives against 8 real ones (`sym.id.replace(prefix, "")`, `sys.stderr.write(...)`, `yaml.dump(...)`) — and carries a positive control plus a stated limit: an unwrapped `os.remove` is *not* caught, because `remove` is ambiguous, so a null here means "no unambiguous direct write", not "no direct write".
- **`builtins.open` was dual-classified with nothing implementing the rule, and it failed in OPPOSITE directions in the two consumers (WI-rusof).** `io_primitives/python.yaml` has always stated the rule in prose — *"Dual-classified: fs_read when mode is 'r'/'rb' (default), fs_write when 'w'/'a'/'x'"* — but `notes:` is free text nothing consumes, so the rule was **documented and unimplemented**. Every `open()` therefore matched both boundaries, and each consumer resolved the tie its own way:
  - **`io-boundaries` called every `open()` a READ**, because the qualified-name index kept one row per name ("first one wins — shouldn't have duplicates") and `fs_read` is declared first. `open(p, "w")` and `open(p, mode="a")` were reported `fs_read`. **A false negative on real writes**, and the write row was not merely out-ranked — it was absent from the index entirely, so no downstream fix could have recovered it.
  - **The taint sink derivation called every `open()` a WRITE**, because it turns each `fs_write` row into a `host_fs` sink regardless of the call. On hypergumbo's own tree this was **24 of the 35 distinct `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` violations** — the dominant false-positive population on a shipped security claim.
  - **Fixing one side alone moves the error rather than removing it**, so both route through one predicate (`select_by_mode` / `resolve_mode_boundary`) and the analyzer records the mode literal once, at `_process_call`'s single invocation, keyed on the exact `ast.Call` node.
  - **The fix WI-rusof had recorded was measured and refuted before being built.** Its filed proposal was to read `meta["access_mode"]`, "which carries `'write'` for `open(p, "w")`". Run against the live analyzer, `access_mode` is **`'read'`** for `open(p, "w")` — it derives from the reference's AST *role* per ADR-0038, not from the call's arguments — so that fix would have classified every `open()` as a read and converted the false positive into a false *negative*, the expensive direction for a security gate. A regression test (`TestAccessModeIsNotTheSameFact`) keeps that distinction executable rather than remembered.
  - **Scope was decided by sorting the 17 dual-classified rows across 6 languages by what the fix would be**, not by sweeping them together: only `builtins.open` and `stdio.fopen` are settled by a mode literal. `gen_udp.open` (net_recv+net_send) and `NSURLConnection.send*` are calls that genuinely cross two boundaries; `unistd.read`/`write` are undecidable at the call site because the fd's kind is unknown; `System.IO.hPrint`×4 (fs_write+logging) is a taxonomy overlap; `scala…Process.apply`/`run` (fs_write+subprocess) is WI-bibuk's zone question. `mode_discriminated_names` therefore admits **only** the fs_read/fs_write pair, and derives membership from the catalogue so a new dual-classified row is covered without a code change — with a parity test asserting the analyzer knows where each flagged name's mode argument sits.
  - **Absence is treated as the language default, not as licence to guess.** `open(p)` and the computed `open(p, m)` both record no mode and resolve to `fs_read`; inventing `fs_write` on suspicion would rebuild the very population this removes.
  - **MEASURED end-to-end on hypergumbo's own tree, both directions, before and after.** `runtime-cli-no-host-fs`: raw flows **1189 → 370**, distinct violations **35 → 11**, with `open` **24 → 0** and `mkdir` **10 → 10** / `rmtree` **1 → 1** unchanged — every false positive removed, every real finding kept. `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` is the untouched control at **33 → 33 distinct**, so the change did not bleed into an adjacent claim. **All 18 verdicts are unchanged**, which is the point: `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` stays correctly `violated` on its 11 genuine unwrapped writes (INV-zudak, reopened), so this raises precision without laundering a real capability, and the `check-self-claims` snapshot needs no rewrite.
  - **A near-miss recorded because the corpus could not have caught it.** The first cut wired the gate into the matcher but left all four production call sites passing no `io_mode`, so it was always `None`, always resolved to `fs_read`, and `builtins.open` matched *nothing* rather than matching writes only — blanket suppression wearing discrimination's clothes. It produced the **identical** 24 → 0 corpus number, because hypergumbo's own `open()` calls are all read-mode, and 22,350 tests passed over the dead path since the unit tests supplied `io_mode` themselves. Two independent signals caught it: reading the diff, and `check-package-coverage`, whose core-isolation run dropped to 88% because suppressed matches made the downstream propagator bodies unreachable. Both a structural probe (every call site threads the argument; positive-controlled against the pre-fix source) and a behavioural pair through the real propagator (a write-mode `open` carrying tainted data still yields a `host_fs` finding, a read-mode one yields none) now pin it — the latter being the **non-vacuity floor for the whole change**, since suppression and discrimination are indistinguishable on read-only data. Re-measured after wiring: **identical at 370/11**, which is itself the evidence that none of the 24 was a genuine write.
  - **The denominator is complete, not sampled.** `_MAX_EVIDENCE_ROWS` is 100 and head-truncates a *deduplicated* list, so an evidence array of 35 (and 33) is the entire distinct population rather than the 3.1% sample an earlier reading of this data assumed. On the io-boundary side the prior corpus measurement stands as the size statement: 273 of 273 `builtins.open` edges across 8 repos were reported `fs_read`, 89 of them carrying a write mode.
- **Third-party I/O was undetectable and the catalogue header said it wasn't — `requests.post(url, secret)` produced ZERO `net_send` chains (INV-fotav).** `io_primitives/python.yaml` explained this away: third-party libraries "are detected transitively — they ultimately call these primitives." **That is false at hypergumbo's analysis scope.** hypergumbo analyzes the target repository, not `site-packages`, so no edge ever reaches requests' internals and its own `socket` call is never seen. Reproduced with controls firing in the same run: a file whose body is `requests.post(url, data={"s": secret})` yields **0** `net_send` chains while `open()` / `file.read` / `os.environ` in the same file yield theirs.
  - **The fix is NOT more built-in rows, and the first draft of this change got that wrong.** Adding requests/httpx/urllib3 to the shipped catalogue contradicts ADR-0016 §27 — "a curated list of stdlib functions, **not an unbounded set of library APIs**" — with §300 pricing the curation cost. Owning every third-party HTTP API's surface is the maintenance burden that ADR deliberately declined. The stdlib scope stays; only the false justification printed beside it is corrected.
  - **What was actually missing: the boundary arm had no user-supplied channel at all.** ADR-0017 §370 granted project-local catalogues to the **taint** arm ("any project can define its own taint sources, sinks, and sanitizers... with project-local entries taking precedence"), shipped as `--taint-sources` and friends. That pattern was never extended to **boundaries**: `load_catalog(language)` read only the packaged directory and `extra_catalogs:` accepted only the three taint keys. ADR-0016 predates ADR-0017 and made its scoping decision before the project-local pattern existed.
  - **New: `--io-primitives PATH` (repeatable) on `io-boundaries` and `verify-claims`, plus `extra_catalogs: io_primitives:` in a claims file.** Precedence mirrors the taint arm rather than inventing a second rule — **built-in < claims-file extras < CLI flags**, later path wins, all keyed on qualified name via the `IoBoundaryCatalog.merge` primitive language inheritance (scala → java) already uses. Measured end-to-end on the reproducer: `net_send` **0 → 2** (`requests.get`, `requests.post`) with the `env_read`/`fs_read` controls unchanged, and the overlay set disclosed on stderr so an `io-boundaries` diff stays attributable.
  - **ONE DECLARATION FEEDS BOTH ARMS.** ADR-0017 §453 already made `io_primitives` the single source of truth for built-in taint sinks — every write-side primitive auto-derives via `AUTO_SINK_ZONE_MAP` and no `taint_sinks/` directory ships — explicitly "without a second source of truth that could drift out of sync." An overlay feeding only the boundary arm would have re-created that drift one layer up, with the **user** paying for it by declaring `requests.post` twice in two schemas. Overlays are therefore threaded into the same derivation, grouped by declared language so a Go overlay never seeds Python sinks: Python taint sinks **113 → 172**, `requests.post` arriving as `zone=network, trust_level=untrusted`. Direction is additive-only (more sinks can add findings, never delete one) and non-destruction of the built-in sink set is asserted, not assumed.
  - **The formats stay separate, because only SINKS overlap.** A taint *source* carries a label and a *sanitizer* clears taint — neither is an I/O crossing, neither has an `io_primitives` counterpart — and a sink additionally carries zone + trust level, which `boundary` deliberately does not model (it names *what crossing happened*, not *how trusted the destination is*). Merging the schemas would re-conflate the kind of axis ADR-0023/0027/0028/0031/0032 exist to keep apart.
  - **Three guards keep an overlay from laundering itself into the curated catalogue's standing:** `status: overlay` is required and `status: complete` is **refused** (that status asserts a provenance-backed stdlib enumeration); `stdlib_modules` / `stdlib_prefixes` are dropped so `is_stdlib_module` keeps answering about the actual interpreter (a `requests` overlay must not relabel a PyPI package as stdlib — that feeds the dependency classifier and the F3 filter); and a missing or malformed path is an **error at exit 2**, never a silent skip, because degrading to "no extra primitives" reads exactly like a clean repo.
  - A worked example ships at `docs/io-primitives-overlays/python-http-clients.yaml` — deliberately **not** beside the shipped catalogues, because the user owns those rows. ADR-0016 carries the amendment.
- **Eight pre-flight credential guards across five forge entry scripts read `FORGEJO_TOKEN` directly, so a machine holding only `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` hard-fails every one of them while the API layer underneath would have worked (INV-zofog).** `scripts/lib/forgejo-api.sh::resolve_forge_token` has been the single source of truth for the credential since the GitHub migration (comment tag C9: github backend → `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` wins, else `FORGEJO_TOKEN`), and `api_call()` already invokes it per call — **only the guards were stale.** The same "N places that should share one rule, drifted" shape, with the shared rule already built and simply not consumed.
  - **A TIME BOMB, NOT A LIVE OUTAGE, AND THAT IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** This repo's `.env` still carries populated legacy `FORGEJO_*` values, and those values are the only thing satisfying the stale guards. The bug is invisible here and reproduces immediately in a sibling repo that reuses this tooling with a clean `.env`. Fixes are proven live there across four subsequent PRs that ran the full push → CI → merge loop.
  - **BEHAVIOURAL EVIDENCE, both directions, from this repo's own repro** (legacy values masked in `.env`, restored by a trap, values never printed). Before: `ci-debug status` and `list-my-prs` both **exit 1** at the guard. After: both **exit 0**, with `ci-debug` returning live commit status.
  - **`ci-debug status` succeeding is NOT proof the token resolved** — this repo is public, so that call succeeds anonymously, and reading it as evidence of auth is the exact error that hid a two-hour CI outage the day before. A separate probe pins the credential path itself: with `FORGEJO_TOKEN` masked empty, `FORGE_BACKEND=github`, `FORGE_TOKEN` non-empty and **equal to `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN`**.
  - **Call-order verified rather than assumed.** `resolve_forge_token` reads `FORGE_BACKEND`, so it is only correct after `detect_forge_backend`. `detect_api_base` calls that internally, and ci-debug, merge-pr and list-my-prs all run `detect_api_base` before their guards — so a bare `resolve_forge_token` is right at those three sites. auto-pr's two guards run *before* its `detect_api_base`, so they call `detect_forge_backend` explicitly. `contribute`'s credential belongs to the **upstream** forge, so its resolve moves after `detect_forge_backend "$UPSTREAM_URL"`; its `FORGEJO_USER` guard stays, because the fork-push credential helper genuinely uses it.
  - `FORGEJO_USER` is now required **only on the forgejo backend** — github pushes ride SSH and the API authenticates by token alone. A dead `local token=` assignment in auto-pr's `gov_cleanup` is deleted (verified unread across the whole function body).
  - **The test stub had drifted from the library it mirrors, and it failed loudly in the worst way.** `test_merge_pr_close.py` replaces the *entire* library with a hand-written stub; that stub had no `resolve_forge_token`, so bash exited **127** and five tests failed with assertions about PATCH bodies and return codes that said nothing about the cause. The stub now mirrors C9, and a new `TestStubMirrorsTheRealLibrary` asserts the stub defines every library function `merge-pr` actually calls — which immediately surfaced two more it lacked (`do_merge_guarded`, `ci_verdict_permits_merge`, both merge-path). The next migration fails there, with a readable message.
- **A Python constructor under a DOTTED module could never be typed, so `import http.client; conn = http.client.HTTPConnection(h); conn.request(...)` was invisible as a network boundary (WI-lifol, INV-linub).** `_external_constructor_type`'s attribute branch required the constructor's base to be a bare `ast.Name`; for `http.client.HTTPConnection(h)` the base is itself an `ast.Attribute`, so the branch was structurally unreachable no matter what the table held. The branch now unwinds the whole chain through the existing `_unwind_attribute_chain`, which **subsumes** the single-segment case (`socket.socket` unwinds to a one-element chain and takes the same path) rather than sitting beside it — one rule, not two that drift. Depth is not special-cased anywhere, so a five-segment type added to the YAML tomorrow resolves without a code change.
  - **The parity test that should have caught this passed the whole time, and that is the transferable lesson.** `test_every_catalogue_type_has_a_dotted_key` asserts the TABLE holds a key; this was a RESOLUTION defect. **Table coverage and reachability were never the same claim.** The new assertion runs the live catalogue through production's own import extraction and the real resolver.
  - **THE ITEM'S OWN HEADLINE FIGURE WAS INFLATED 3.4× AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** WI-lifol was filed at "41 primitives reachable in only one call form", from the generated reach probe. **29 of the 41 are `django.db.models`** — which is a *module*, not a constructible type, so `models(...)` constructs nothing and no constructor fix can reach them. They already reach production by an entirely different route (the `.objects.` dispatch, WI-sozoj: 447 `filter` + 121 `get` chains on pretix). The probe scored a call form that cannot exist in real code. Honest scope: **12 primitives across 4 types** (`http.client.HTTPConnection` / `HTTPSConnection`, `http.server.HTTPServer`, `xmlrpc.server.SimpleXMLRPCServer`).
  - **Real-code floor measured BEFORE building, because a generated probe is a ceiling and this log has now recorded that confusion four times.** Over the 103 corpus files naming these types, counting only the JOINED shape that can mint a boundary (constructor bound to a name, that name later the receiver of a *catalogued* method): **dotted 10 production / 21 test · bare 4 production / 13 test**. The dotted form is the *more* common of the two in real code for these types — not a long tail. The ten production sites are genuine `net_send`: mitmproxy's two release scripts, envoy's `socket_passing` tool, ceph's barbican task, ClickHouse CI.
  - **Direction A/B at the analyzer, 8 repos** (`scripts/measure-dotted-ctor-resolution-ab.py`, in-repo): **60 call edges retyped, 0 lost, 0 net change in edge count** — every "gain" is paired with a "loss" because these are the *same* edges moving from `external.<method>` to `<Type>.<method>`, not new edges. mitmproxy 10 · ClickHouse 38 · ceph 8 · envoy 3 · sherpa-onnx 1. **Three neutral controls (django, pretix, kserve) moved 0/0**, django being the one that matters: if the derived `django.db.models` row could mint anything spurious, django is where it would.
  - **End-to-end through the real CLI, because retyping an edge is not the same claim as minting a boundary.** envoy: **631 → 632 chains, +1 `net_send / http.client.HTTPConnection.request`, 0 lost.** Each arm ran under its own fresh `XDG_CACHE_HOME` so neither could be served the other's map. **Verified on one repo only**; across the cohort **30 of the 60 retyped edges land on a catalogued method** (the boundary-capable subset — `getresponse`, `close` and `server_close` are not catalogued), which is the ceiling for boundary gain before the production/test split cuts it further.
  - **RESIDUAL, STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED CLOSED.** `module_imports` is built by an `ast.walk` over the whole file, so it is file-scoped and cannot see a local binding that shadows an imported module name. A parameter named `socket` **already** mistyped `socket.socket(h)` as the stdlib type before this change; unwinding widens that exposure to depth ≥ 2 rather than introducing it. Pinned by an `xfail(strict=True)` so a scope-aware fix goes RED instead of passing unnoticed.
  - **Two instrument controls failed first and each caught a real bug.** The floor scanner used one flat variable map across the file, so two functions that both named a connection `conn` collided and the last binding won — mis-attributing the very FORM the instrument exists to measure; it is now scope-aware. And the end-to-end arm discriminator matched *both* arms twice (`_unwind_attribute_chain(func)` already exists at HEAD in the WI-zigah chain code; `isinstance(func.value, ast.Name)` appears at four other sites in `py.py`), which produced one refused run and one false "restore failed". **A discriminator that is not unique to the change under test is not a control.**
- **The Python external-constructor table covered 4 of the 17 receiver types the catalogue already declares, so fifteen types could never be typed and every method on them was structurally unreachable (INV-linub).** `io_primitives/python.yaml` names a receiver type in the `module` slot of every `kind: method` primitive; `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` was a hand-curated four-row restatement of the same fact. Two homes for one fact, drifted. `smtplib.SMTP(a).sendmail(...)` and `sqlite3.Cursor(a).execute(...)` emitted **no method-call edge at all** while `pathlib.Path(a).write_text(b)` emitted a correctly typed one. The table is now **derived** from the catalogue, so a type added to the YAML is mintable the same day, and a parity test enumerates the live catalogue rather than three examples.
  - **REAL-CODE PAYOFF IS ~ZERO AND THAT IS THE HEADLINE, not the 83→5.** A/B at the analyzer (`scripts/measure-ctor-table-derivation-ab.py`, in-repo): hypergumbo **99,470 → 99,470** call edges, 0 gained / 0 lost; pretix **62,272 → 62,273**, 1 gained / 0 lost. Same ceiling-vs-floor error this log has recorded twice before (143 Go sites → 6; 108 gained edges → 6 production). **Why:** real code obtains these objects from FACTORY functions, not class constructors — `sqlite3.connect(path)` then `conn.cursor()`, never `sqlite3.Cursor(...)`; hypergumbo's own tracker cache is exactly that shape. Filed as its own item, with the note that unlike the constructor table it *cannot* be catalogue-derived (io_primitives records which methods are I/O, never which functions return the receiving type).
  - **The A/B is measured at the analyzer, not through `io-boundaries`, precisely because the latter caches** under `~/.cache/hypergumbo` keyed on repo state and analyzer identity — an in-process monkeypatch changes neither, so a second arm would be served the first arm's map and report a flawless zero. Caches fake nulls.
  - **The one gained edge was a REGRESSION this change introduced, caught by the direction check rather than by review.** `_process_call` carries an `elif callee_name in EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` arm that emits `python:builtins:0-0:<name>:unresolved` — an assertion that the name IS a builtin — under a comment stating "only the bare key `open` can match". That was an unstated dependency on the table being curated down to builtins, and seventeen new bare type names falsified it immediately: pretix's `StreamWriter = codecs.getwriter('utf-8'); StreamWriter(byte_data)`, a **local rebinding**, minted `builtins.StreamWriter`. The sibling `_external_constructor_type` refused the very same name a few lines earlier via its INV-kipor binding check — two consumers of one table disagreeing about what membership licenses. The arm is now gated on `BUILTIN_CONSTRUCTOR_NAMES`, which already means exactly "bare rows that are REAL builtins", so this consolidates onto an existing rule rather than minting a third; it also closes the pre-existing case where an unimported bare `Path(...)` minted `builtins.Path`.
  - **Direction measured before the change, not argued:** the fifteen newly-mintable types carry **43 of 83** Python taint sources and **35 of 113** sinks, and **0 of 4 sanitizers** — so the change cannot arm the WI-fasub barrier arm, where a `False` earns `sanitized`. That last figure is pinned executably, and its first draft was an *uncontrolled zero*: sanitizer rows carry only `qualified_name`, no `kind`, so a `kind == "method"` filter matched nothing and intersected everything to zero.

- **A language whose catalogued I/O is method-shaped and which emits no method calls now reports `inconclusive` instead of `confirmed`.** A real Kotlin repo that reads a socket and writes the bytes to disk returned `rc=0 confirmed` with 93 catalogued sinks, `dataflow_capable: False` and zero findings — the tool claiming support, looking at nothing, and reporting silence as safety. The gate asked *"did this language produce ANY call edge"*, and one intra-repo call was enough to look covered. Now `verify_claims.method_starved_modules` asks whether any **method-construct** call edge landed in a method-keyed catalogue module the repo actually calls; the boundary gate and the taint gate share the one predicate. Live: `rc=0 confirmed` → `rc=2 inconclusive`, and the pinned `xfail` closes.
  - **Four candidate predicates were measured over six fixtures before any code changed** (`scripts/measure-blind-language-signal.py`, in-repo). Two of the three obvious ones are wrong. *"Count only non-first-party dsts"* does not discriminate at all — Kotlin's intra-repo dst is `kotlin:App.kt:9-12:helper:function`, whose path slot is RELATIVE, and `is_first_party_callable_dst` requires an ABSOLUTE one; that is the disclosed relative-path gap surfacing in a second consumer. *"Did the language emit any method-construct edge"* catches Kotlin but also downgrades a pure-computation Python repo that simply calls nothing external — conflating "cannot see this language" with "this repo has no external calls", which is the blanket-downgrade failure mode that made `confirmed` unreachable in this gate's first version. Anchoring on **modules the repo actually calls** is what separates them.
  - **The first version of the shipped predicate was ALSO wrong, and only real repos showed it.** It reported `fs`, `console` and `process` starved on BOTH JavaScript repos measured — i.e. it would have downgraded every JS repo in existence. Cause: **JS/TS populate `call_construct` on ZERO of 4,872 call edges** while Go populates 7,741 (6,012 of them `method`). For those analyzers the field carries no information, so "no method call landed here" is absence of evidence, not evidence of blindness. The predicate now **abstains** per-language for any analyzer that never stamps the field — `None` (could not check) is not `empty` (checked, found none) — and the abstention is per-language, so one un-stamped language cannot silently disable the gate for the rest of a polyglot repo. Filed as its own invariant rather than papered over.
  - **Direction measured on a real 14-language repo (sherpa-onnx): 12 of 14 languages pass.** The two catches name specific modules rather than condemning a language: `kotlin` on `java.io.File` / `java.io.FileOutputStream` (2,414 call edges, zero method constructs — WI-nasuf reproduced on real code at scale), and `java` on `java.nio.file.Files` / `java.net.http.HttpClient`. Across the fixture set exactly one verdict moved, in the intended direction; a repo with a genuine `asyncio.start_server` → `os.mkdir` flow still reports `violated`.
  - **The Java catch was new and is a real recall hole.** `java.io.File.exists` stamps `call_construct: method` and matches; `java.nio.file.Files.copy` and `createTempDirectory` stamp nothing, because those methods are **static** while `io_primitives/java.yaml` declares every `Files` entry `kind: method`. F3 therefore refuses the entire `Files` **sink** surface (`write`, `copy`, `createDirectories`, …), which auto-derives taint sinks under ADR-0017 §2b. `kotlin.yaml` had already solved the mirror of this for `println` — catalogued as `functions:` precisely so F3 would not suppress it — and the reasoning was never applied to static methods. Filed separately with the direction warning that re-keying ADDS sinks and the names involved (`write`, `copy`, `list`) are highly collidable.
  - **Only call edges count, not imports.** `import java.io.File` performs no I/O, so an unused import must not be read as blindness. The Kotlin case is caught regardless, because its evidence is a CALL edge — the **constructor** `File(...)`, which is emitted, while `writeText` produces no edge at all.
- **Go's chained-method return-type registry was consulted by a branch that could never execute, and the three tests that would have said so had never run (WI-jolif).** `result := e.Query(); result.Rows()` emitted `go:external:0-0:Rows:unresolved` instead of resolving to `Result.Rows`. The WI-kuroj lookup guarded on `rhs.type == "call_expression"`, but a `short_var_declaration`'s last child is an **`expression_list` wrapper** — a condition that node can never satisfy. So the registry was built, threaded through three call layers, and read by dead code from the day it shipped. `_type_from_rhs` unwrapped the wrapper inline and worked; the registry lookup did not, and the two consumers now share one `_unwrap_rhs_expression` helper so they cannot disagree about what "the right-hand side" means.
  - **The hole was invisible because the fixture could not fail.** `go_available` probed `_analyzer.is_grammar_available()` — a method that does not exist; it is a module function in `analyze.base` — inside a bare `except Exception: pytest.skip`. Every call raised `AttributeError`, the handler swallowed it, and all three integration tests reported "grammar unavailable" on a machine where the grammar is installed and the 15 unit tests beside them pass. The unit half of the feature was covered; the end-to-end half was a green tick over a hole. They were marked `xfail(strict=True)` rather than left skipped, and `strict` is what turned this fix into a red suite demanding the marker's removal.
  - **A stale `# pragma: no cover - requires Go tree-sitter grammar` sat on the dead branch**, excusing it from coverage on the same false premise that kept the tests skipped. Removed with the fix.
  - **MEASURED BOTH DIRECTIONS ON A REAL REPO, WITH BOTH DENOMINATORS.** alertmanager: 108 edges gained, 109 lost, near 1:1 — these are *upgrades*, not inventions. External placeholders 1,576 → 1,467; `resolution_quality: typed_receiver` 577 → 685. Sampled pairs confirm the replacement (`go:external:0-0:isEOF` → `matcher/parse/token.go:token.isEOF:method`). **But 102 of the 108 gained have a `_test.go` source, so the production-path payoff is 6, not 108** — quoting 108 alone would overstate it 18×.
  - **The receiver-typing collision landmine fired exactly once and is disclosed rather than buried.** 107 of 108 gained edges resolve to a `:method` dst; one resolves to a `:field` (`Alert.Fingerprint:field`), which is the known shape where typing a receiver walks an external qualified type into a lookup against the repo's own symbols. `calls`-to-`field` edges pre-exist at 266 on this repo and this change takes it to 267, so the pattern is not introduced here — but the 266 are worth their own look.
- **Four Go emit sites minted a "resolved" edge onto a dst whose own kind slot reads `unresolved` (INV-fazim).** `go:external:0-0:<callee>:unresolved` names a call the analyzer could not attribute to any package, and `Edge.create` defaults `is_resolved=True`, so every site that stayed silent about the flag asserted resolution it did not have.
