Under ADR-0037 ruling 4 that flag is authoritative and a consumer may not re-derive the verdict from the dst string, so nothing downstream could defend itself: `taint._match_propagation_entry` branches on it, finds no path evidence for the `external` placeholder, and takes the legacy escape `if not path_module: return hits[0]` — an ungated bare-name match that never reaches the F3 gate.
  - **Each site is reachable only by a distinct receiver shape, which is why they survived.** The two guards that already stated the flag both consume a *tracked local identifier* receiver and shadow the others for the obvious test input — the first draft of the fixtures had all three collapse onto the already-correct WI-jopar guard and would have passed while testing nothing. Reproduced live before the fix: `items[0].Handle()` (ambiguous, 2+ in-repo candidates), `items[0].doWork()` (unexported), `r.Body.Close()` (stdlib-interface name on an unknown receiver). A non-vacuity floor now asserts the fixtures hit three *different* guards.
  - **The fourth site takes the flag from its BRANCH, not a constant.** The route-mount emit resolves to an in-repo symbol when it can and falls back to the placeholder when it cannot, so blanket-setting `False` would have been as wrong as inheriting `True`. Both branches are asserted from one analysis of one file.
  - **BLAST RADIUS ON A SHIPPED MAP IS ZERO, AND IT IS STATED RATHER THAN SOLD.** A/B on alertmanager: 8,322 call edges in both arms, 1,576 external in both, **0 carrying `is_resolved=True` in either**, identical confidence distribution, 0 edges gained or lost. `finalize`'s edge-resolution sub-step already flips the flag and (since PR #248) re-derives confidence from its own verdict, so the producer bug was being papered over downstream. This is a latent-correctness fix for consumers that read edges before finalize — tests, `dst_ref` derivation — and no recall or precision win is claimed from it. The item's own thread warns that both obvious measurements of this channel are tautologies; the non-tautological evidence is the analyzer-stage repro, which is what the new tests pin.
- **The def/use extractor registry could not recover from a clear, so whether JavaScript reported data-flow capable depended on which tests shared an xdist worker.** `ensure_def_use_extractors_registered` re-registers by importing the extractor modules for their decorator side effect — and a bare import is a no-op once the module is in `sys.modules`, so after `clear_def_use_extractors()` it restored nothing while still returning `True`. Every language then read `dataflow_capable: False`. Surfaced as two `test_taint_recall_corpus` failures in a full-suite run that passed at every narrower scope and passed again on re-run: the worst shape of flake, because it makes a green suite uninformative. The registrar now reloads the modules when the registry no longer holds what it held after the first successful registration.
  - **The hazard was already known, worked around once, locally.** `test_ddg_build.py::test_python_still_produces_edges` carries a hand-rolled `importlib.reload` guard whose docstring names the mechanism exactly — "``test_cfg.py`` calls ``clear_def_use_extractors()``; a bare ``import`` … is a no-op once it is already in ``sys.modules``". Every other consumer was unguarded. The fix went to the one place that owns registration rather than into each test that trips over it.
  - **The first version of the fix was WRONG in a way its own tests passed**, and is recorded because the shape recurs: it keyed on "is the registry empty", which never fires after a PARTIAL restore. `test_py_def_use.py` clears the registry and reloads only its own module, leaving it populated but missing `javascript` — health by the emptiness test, and exactly the state that produced the failures. The guard now compares against the full expected set. That set is **learned** from the first successful registration rather than hardcoded, so it does not become a second home for a fact the decorators already hold.
- **Two spec paragraphs asserted the opposite of the code, both in the taint section (S10 coherence sweep).** Found by cross-reading a measurement against the document, not by reading the document.
  - The verdict table said **"Inconclusive (no DDG) — *Specified, never emitted*"** while `inconclusive` is the MAJORITY outcome: hypergumbo's own pinned verdicts are **16 inconclusive / 2 violated / 0 confirmed**. Both statements were true of DIFFERENT producers — `verify_taint_claim` still does not emit the ADR-0017 §3c sense, while the capability gate (`_taint_blind_reason`, INV-javam / WI-kajil) emits it constantly and is what refuses to certify a claim the analysis could not check. The table now names both, quotes the split, and carries the known residual: a language emitting ONE token call edge still looks covered.
  - **Function summaries** said "*no production consumer* … no taint code path calls `infer_summary` or `load_function_summaries` today." Half false, and the load-bearing half — `propagate_taint_ddg` calls `load_function_summaries()` with 38 live terminating summaries. `taint.py` already carried the correction in a docstring; it was never propagated to the spec. §4a (`infer_summary`) genuinely has zero callers. The distinction is not pedantic: **8 of 26 call-node escape sites (30.8%) are "summary key present, no summary entry"** — a live mechanism, not an unwired feature. *(Amended after the ceiling measurement above: calling those 8 sites "a catalogue gap" was wrong twice over. Only 4 of their 7 distinct keys are catalogue absences — two are malformed by a doubled module segment and one carries a receiver variable in the module slot — and closing **every** escape site was then measured to move zero findings, so the mechanism is live but inert.)*
  - **Field-sensitivity lite was RE-VERIFIED rather than inherited** (`is_field_tainted` still has zero callers) and now carries the re-verification date, since its sibling claim went stale by exactly the route of being copied forward unchecked.
  - **ADR-0016 §4 Phase 4 was deliberately NOT amended.** The plan lists it as sweep work, but the obligation ("a CI step that runs claim verification on every PR") was satisfied by BUILDING the gate — reality moved to match the doc, so softening the ADR would now make it wrong.
- **Every one of Rust's nine colon-bearing sink modules was unreachable through the legacy id parse, and fixing the half the item named moved nothing (INV-fokik).** ADR-0036 Ruling 1 makes `path` the one colon-TOLERANT slot in `{lang}:{path}:{span}:{name}:{kind}`. `io_boundary._extract_module_hint` took `parts[1]`, so `rust:std::env:0-0:var:external_symbol` yielded `std`; `_module_matches("std::env", "std")` is False; and `_lookup_named_entry` treats a present-but-MISMATCHED module as a REJECTION rather than a degrade, so the finding was dropped in silence.
  - **The prescribed one-line fix would have traded one corruption for another, and only a corpus count showed it.** The item said "route the caller through `symbol_path_slot`". That chokepoint was RIGHT-anchored (`parts[1:-3]`) on the grammar's promise that `name` is colon-free — a promise the data breaks. Scanned over three real maps with `scripts/measure-symbol-id-colon-conformance.py` (just, rage, hypergumbo itself): **352,254 conformant · 740 colon-bearing path, where the LEFT-anchored caller is wrong · 483 colon-bearing name, where the RIGHT-anchored chokepoint is wrong · 584 with no span token at all.** Migrating as filed would have fixed the 740 and regressed the 483.
  - **Anchoring on the span is the only parse that is right on both**, and it makes `symbol_path_slot` agree with `taint._extract_callee_name`, which had anchored that way all along — the item names that disagreement between two parsers of one string as "the tell".
  - **FAILS SAFE BY CHOICE.** A malformed id has no true path slot, so the only question is which wrong answer to give: `external` is a placeholder and degrades to short-name matching plus the F3 gate, while `external:0-0:std::env` is not, and rejects everything silently.
  - **THE PATH FIX ALONE MOVED ZERO, AND THE POSITIVE CONTROL IS WHAT CAUGHT IT.** With the module hint corrected to `std::fs`, tagging a `rust:std::fs:0-0:write:external_symbol` edge still produced no boundary. `io_boundary._extract_callee_name` carried the MIRROR defect in the same file — "everything after the first three fields" assumes a colon-free PATH — and returned `fs:0-0:write`, so the lookup missed before the module hint could matter. Both slots now route through `ir.symbol_path_slot` / the new `ir.symbol_name_slot`, and `taint`'s copy routes there too, so the two consumers cannot drift apart again.
  - **BLAST RADIUS ON A REAL PIPELINE IS ZERO, AND THAT ZERO IS EXPLAINED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Both call sites prefer the structured `dst_ref` when present (WI-tihup), and `finalize` populates it on every `is_resolved=False` edge (ADR-0037 ruling 2). Measured: **all 171 of rage's `rust:std::` dsts carry `dst_ref`**, so they bypass the legacy parse entirely — 0 gained / 0 lost across 10,656 legacy-parse edges on rage + just, with a positive control proving the detector fires on the exact shapes (`('env:0-0:var','std')` → no match becomes `('var','std::env')` → `std::env.var`). So this is a LATENT hole closed for the paths `dst_ref` does not cover, not a live recall win, and no boundary-count improvement is claimed.
  - **A new emission defect fell out of the scan and is filed rather than folded in (INV-papih).** Rust emits DOUBLE-SPAN ids — `rust:external:0-0:std::env:0-0:var:external_symbol` — alongside the conformant `rust:std::env:0-0:var:external_symbol`, both in the same map (rage splits 94 conformant / 76 double-span). That shape is what the 483 above actually are; they are not the objc selectors the item predicted. Consumers now survive it by failing safe, but producers should not emit it.
- **`c := &http.Client{}; c.Do(req)` — an outbound HTTP request with a possibly-tainted URL — was invisible as an I/O boundary in EVERY syntactic form.** Not "typed in one spelling and not another", as with the Python constructor-root defect: there was no way to write this in Go that produced a boundary. `Do` and `Get` sit in `go.yaml`'s `ambiguous_names` **by design** (a bare `.Do()` collides with every other `Do` in a corpus), so receiver typing is the only thing that can reach those rows, and the receiver never carried a type.
  - **A DRIFTED COPY, not a missing feature — the rule already had a home.** `_type_identifier_from_node` handles `type_identifier`, `qualified_type` and `pointer_type`, and its docstring states outright that returning the full `http.Client` is "critical for IO boundary detection". `_type_from_rhs` re-implemented a narrower version inline that scanned for a **direct** `type_identifier` child. The observable symptom was that two spellings of one declaration disagreed: `var client http.Client` bound `http.Client` while `client := http.Client{}` bound the empty string. The fix routes the composite-literal branch through the existing predicate; the parity test pins AGREEMENT across all three spellings rather than pinning the new path to a new expectation, so a fourth spelling has to join the agreement or fail.
  - **The tree-sitter-go distinction that caused it, stated because it is a repeat offender:** a package-qualified name is `selector_expression` in EXPRESSION position and `qualified_type` in TYPE position. Code handling only one silently drops the other. The same distinction produced a scanner bug in `measure-go-receiver-typing-gap.py` caught by its own positive control, and `# pragma: no cover - defensive for unrecognized RHS` on `_type_from_rhs`'s fall-through was factually wrong: ordinary `&http.Client{}` reached it.
  - **POPULATION, with both denominators.** Of the 27 sites across 9 Go repos where the repo reports no boundary anywhere, **21 are composite-literal rooted** — 17 `net/http.Client.Do` and 4 `.Get`, all `http.Client`. **9 of the 21 are in `_test.go` files** (prometheus 8, kserve 1), so the production-path population is **~12**. Quoting 21 alone would overstate it 1.75x. The remaining 6 are constructor-call rooted (`slog.New`, `gin.Default`) and are NOT addressed here — that is a separate defect in `_type_from_constructor_call`, which recognises only the `NewXxx` convention.
  - **21 is a LOWER BOUND on a different question than the fix answers, and the two must not be quoted as one number.** The sizing instrument counts sites against a hand-built `COMPOSITE_TYPES` map holding **three** entries (`http.Client`, `http.Transport`, `tls.Config`), because the catalogue records which *methods* are I/O, not which types a literal constructs. The production fix is not scoped to that map — it types **every** package-qualified composite literal, so its reach exceeds what the census could see. The A/B below is the unbounded measure; the census is what decided the fix was worth writing.
  - **MEASURED THROUGH PRODUCTION, both directions, on 6 Go repos: call-edge count IDENTICAL in both arms (76,689), +25 I/O boundaries tagged, and net +76 intra-repo dsts.** `scripts/measure-go-composite-literal-ab.py` lands in-repo. It measures the WHOLE diff in two processes rather than reverting one knob in-process, because this change has two observable parts and a single-knob arm would report a smaller change than the one that ships.
  - **The positive control was itself wrong once, and caught it.** Counting the fixture's TOTAL tags is not a control: `http.NewRequest` is a catalogued `net/http` function that tags in BOTH arms, so a `tagged > 0` test read as "change visible" in the arm where the change is absent. The control now names the edge under test: PRE `go:external:0-0:Do:unresolved` tags 0, POST `go:net/http:0-0:Do:unresolved` tags 1.
  - **Read edge-by-edge on caddy, because an aggregate delta cannot tell a removed FALSE edge from a removed TRUE one.** 222 distinct edges lost: **79** upgraded off the `external` placeholder to a real module, **~95** re-spelled from the full module path to the repo-relative one (the documented convention — the resolver's suffix matching operates on repo-relative paths), and **48 intra-repo edges removed, 17 of them pointing from production code at a symbol defined in a `_test.go` file**. Sampled removals are unambiguously false: `admin.go:handleConfig` to `responsewriter_test.go:baseRespWriter.Header` (receiver is an external `http.ResponseWriter`), `exitProcess` to `LogBufferCore.With` (receiver is a `*zap.Logger`).
  - **The fix needed a second half, and the guard test is what proved it.** Binding the qualified type makes previously-unknown receivers known, which walks them into the branch that strips `url.Values` to a bare `Values` and looks it up among the repo's OWN symbols. go.py already records that `q.Set("k","v")` on a `url.Values` once "absorbed 13 spurious in-edges into one struct, poisoning the centrality ranking" on alertmanager — and the first version of this fix brought that straight back, emitting a resolved edge to a local `Values.Set` stamped `resolution_quality: typed_receiver`. `_external_package_for_type` is the added discriminator: a type whose package lies outside this go.mod module cannot be defined by this repo, so the local lookup is skipped entirely. It ABSTAINS when no module path is known — in-repo and external are genuinely the same shape without go.mod — which is a **disclosed** gap rather than a guess.
  - **A downstream guard was keyed on a proxy that this change inverts.** The WI-jopar guard fired on `import_path_hint is None`, which worked only while a qualified composite literal left its receiver untyped: no type meant no hint, so hint-absence stood in for "the receiver is a tracked local". Once such receivers type correctly the hint is set, the proxy inverts, and the guard stopped firing exactly where it was most needed. It now keys on the receiver directly and terminates with the package it knows instead of the `external` placeholder.
  - **INV-fazim's producer half is closed at the filed line as a side effect.** That guard called `Edge.create` with no `is_resolved=`, taking the `True` default onto a dst whose kind slot reads `unresolved` — the exact incoherence INV-fazim is filed for, latent only because the guard seldom fired. Making the guard fire more often forced the issue; it now states `is_resolved=False`. Producer-stage `is_resolved=True` falls by 10,319 across the 6 repos. **This is a producer-stage coherence fix and is NOT claimed to change the serialized map** — finalize's edge-resolution sub-step overwrites the producer verdict from the dst node's kind. Three sibling emission sites carry the same omission and are NOT touched here.
- **Three Go integration tests had never executed, and all three fail when un-skipped (WI-jolif).** `test_go_return_type_registry.py`'s `go_available` fixture probed `_analyzer.is_grammar_available()` inside a bare `except Exception: pytest.skip`. No such method exists — `is_grammar_available` is a module function in `analyze.base` — so every call raised `AttributeError`, the handler swallowed it, and the end-to-end half of WI-kuroj / INV-dihos reported "Go analyzer not available" on a machine where the grammar is installed and the 15 unit tests in the same file pass. Repairing the fixture shows `result := e.Query(); result.Rows()` emitting `go:external:0-0:Rows:unresolved` instead of resolving to `Result.Rows`. **A/B against unmodified dev reproduces all three failures identically**, so they are pre-existing rather than introduced. They are now marked `xfail(strict=True)` rather than left skipped, so they RUN and fail honestly and the suite turns red the moment the feature starts working. Both repaired fixtures have **no exception handler at all** — a skip reachable by a typo is a green tick over a hole, which is what this was. A file defining `func MkdirAll(...)` — with no import of `os` anywhere in it — had an `fs_write` boundary attributed to it naming `os.MkdirAll`. A local `Command(...)` was reported as `os/exec.Command` under **`subprocess`**, the one category `HIGH_RISK_PRIMITIVES` flags `*** HIGH RISK ***` on the stated invariant that launching an external program is arbitrary code execution. It launched nothing. Because a write-side primitive auto-derives a taint sink (ADR-0017 §2b), the false primitive propagates into claim verdicts rather than staying cosmetic.
  - **Confirmed in python, go, javascript and typescript**, each with a passing positive control (the real stdlib call must still tag) and a neutral control (an unrelated first-party name must never tag).
  - **FOUND ONLY BY TESTING A SECOND LANGUAGE, which is the point.** The plan's two-languages-minimum rule exists because Python-only work produces Python-shaped abstractions that merely look general. Here it paid directly: the *first* Python probe came back clean, and that was an artifact of the NAME tested — `remove` happens to sit in python.yaml's `ambiguous_names` (34 entries), as does `write` in rust's. **That list is a curated per-name BLOCKLIST, not a structural rule**; pick a name outside it (`makedirs`) and Python fails exactly like Go. A Go probe is what exposed the shape.
  - **Mechanism.** `_extract_module_hint` returns `None` only for a module slot beginning `/` or `\`. Its docstring states the right intent — a file path is not a useful module hint — but implements only the ABSOLUTE case. With no hint, `lookup_with_module` skips the module filter and falls through to `gate_named_entry`, whose no-module branch permits a function-kind hit. This is the production path, not a fixture artifact: `cli.py` resolves the repo root, so every real run carries absolute first-party paths.
  - **The fix withholds ONLY the ungated short-name fallback**, not the edge. The first attempt refused first-party dsts at the top of the tagging loop and **broke 50 existing tests, correctly** — they model a *resolved external* primitive as `python:/stdlib/os.py:100-102:os.listdir:function`, a genuine stdlib call with a file-path module slot and a `function` kind. Kind alone cannot separate that from a first-party definition; the module-qualified NAME it still carries can, via the exact-qualified match. So exact and module-filtered lookups are untouched and only the bare, hint-less short name is gated.
  - **A FALSE NEGATIVE was introduced and caught by the suite, not by the preceding measurement.** Keying the predicate on the kind slot alone silently un-tagged Haskell's `readFile`/`writeFile` — Haskell emits external placeholders as `haskell:external:0-0:readFile:function`, module slot `external`, kind `function` (the kind slot carrying a different vocabulary across emission paths is INV-kurup's territory). The corpus scan that preceded the fix showed no tagged boundary carrying a `function` kind, which was true of the four repos scanned and false of Haskell — a language absent from the scan because it is not dataflow-capable. The predicate now requires BOTH a callable kind AND a filesystem module slot, and the Haskell shape is pinned as a regression test.
  - **Scoped by measurement, not by caution.** Refusing *every* first-party dst would have deleted true positives: on express, 11 tagged boundaries resolve to first-party dsts of kind `variable`, and `lib/view.js` reaches them through `var dirname = path.dirname` — an alias to the real function, where the tag is CORRECT. Alias bindings are deliberately left alone as a separate, unresolved question, pinned by a test so a future widening has to argue with an assertion.
  - **Blast radius measured BEFORE the gate moved:** across hypergumbo, poetry, caddy and express, every currently-tagged boundary carries a dst kind of `unresolved` (12,377), `attribute` (691), `variable` (11) or `symbol` (3) — **not one carries `function` or `method`**. So this removes zero boundaries that exist today. That check was not optional; LIVE.md records that gating the hinted path once destroyed 61.5–87.2% of real boundaries for zero gain.
  - **Honest scope: this is a LATENT hole, not a live epidemic.** Zero occurrences on hypergumbo, poetry and caddy. The fixtures prove the mechanism; the corpora say it rarely fires today.
  - **A PARITY TEST enumerating the live DDG registry ships with it** — the plan's operational requirement that per-language work "ships with a parity test that enumerates every dataflow-capable language, so the next language fails the test rather than silently skipping the fix." It had no implementation before this. It forces registration through production's own helper rather than trusting ambient import order, because that order is itself a live defect (WI-kogop) and a parity test that silently enumerates one language is worse than none.
  - **One gap DISCLOSED rather than hidden:** a first-party dst carrying a RELATIVE path is not recognised by the predicate. Rust emits that shape and is currently protected by a different mechanism — the module filter, where `_module_matches("std::fs", "app.rs")` fails. Two mechanisms covering one case is fragile, so it ships pinned with a re-evaluation trigger: if an analyzer ever emits a relative first-party path AND a module slot that could pass `_module_matches`, the gap becomes reachable and the predicate must widen to a real path test.
- **`Path(raw).write_text(x)` tagged no I/O boundary while `p = Path(raw); p.write_text(x)` tagged one — the same filesystem write, invisible in the inline form.** PR #253 taught the receiver resolver that a constructor call carries a type, but wired it into the two ASSIGNMENT call sites only. So the type existed and the *emission* site never asked for it: the `.write_text` call produced **no edge at all**, in any branch, and only the `instantiates` edge for `Path(raw)` itself survived. Measured on the shipping tree before the change — assigned form 1 boundary, inline form 0, on byte-identical I/O.
  - **Two gaps, which is why the obvious one-line change could not close it.** The emission site called `_derived_receiver_module` without the constructor resolver, so `(Path(raw) / "o").write_text(x)` could not type its root; *and* `_derived_receiver_module` answers "what does this DERIVATION preserve", while a bare `Path(raw)` is not a derivation but the root — no branch matched it. Fixing only the first leaves the commonest shape broken.
  - **Closed by extracting the question, not by adding a branch.** `_receiver_type` — "what external type does this receiver expression have?" — was already being answered privately inside `_preserved_receiver_type`, so the emission site could only ask the narrower derivation question. Both callers now route through the one predicate, and the propagation rule (`TYPE_PRESERVING_MEMBERS`) stays a separate decision, so widening which receivers carry a type does not widen which members preserve it. `_external_constructor_type` moves to module level for the same reason: it had one consumer and took its import maps from an enclosing scope; it now has two, in different scopes, and they share the INV-kipor binding check instead of growing a second copy. **Four separate answers to "may I trust this callee?" have already drifted in this area**, one with a docstring asserting a parity that did not hold, so a second copy here was the failure mode rather than the convenience.
  - **SIZE, with the denominator named, because this closes a slice and not a family.** Across pretix, sqlalchemy, mitmproxy, poetry, pyright, meson and kserve there are **27,354** call sites whose receiver is itself a call or a `/` expression. Only **269** are typeable (257 rooted directly at a recognized constructor, 12 at a derivation of one) and **128** land on a name `python.yaml` admits for that module — so this is **~0.5%** of the CALL_RESULT_ROOT family. The remaining ~27,085 have receivers of genuinely unknown type, and emitting an untyped edge for those is exactly what PR #231 measured as moving **zero** findings.
  - **MEASURED THROUGH PRODUCTION: +125 boundary chains, +260 typed call edges, +260 total edges — every new edge NET-NEW, zero re-keyed.** Per repo: meson +89, mitmproxy +14, poetry +7, kserve +6, sqlalchemy +5, pretix +4, pyright +0. **TWO POPULATIONS, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME NUMBER:** the static census counts **128 reaching SITES** and the A/B counts **125 boundary CHAINS**, because `tag_io_boundaries` collapses same-caller-same-primitive sites — mitmproxy's `test_init_dir` calls `Path(mydir).exists()` twice in one function, and that file exists twice in the tree, which is the whole of the 3-count difference. Neither number is wrong; quoting one as the other would be.
  - **CONCENTRATION, stated because it limits the claim: meson alone supplies 89 of the 125 chains (71.2%).** This is not an evenly distributed win, and no general "Python gains ~125 boundaries" claim is supported by it.
  - **`open(p, "w").write(x)` comes with it.** `python.yaml`'s synthetic `file` module (`read`/`readline`/`readlines`, `write`/`writelines`) was reachable only through an assignment, so the inline idiom was invisible to the boundary detector.
  - **DIRECTION, both signs, because since PR #214 a `False` earns `sanitized` and DELETES a flow.** Typed edges ADD boundaries and taint sinks; they also populate `callees_at`, which lets `_use_site_terminates` decide a use site it previously had to treat as an escape — producing more `False` verdicts. `scripts/measure-ctor-root-typing-ab.py` lands in-repo and reports both, with a positive control printed first (0 → 1 boundary on a known-positive fixture): if the arms agree there, every corpus zero it prints is an uncontrolled null rather than evidence. Its one knob drops the constructor resolver **only** for `_process_call`, so #253's assignment-site typing is untouched and the measurement is of this change rather than of both. **The suppression direction measured zero on hypergumbo's own claims** — `check-self-claims` is byte-identical across the change at 18 claims / 16 inconclusive / 2 violated, and that snapshot was recorded pre-fix, so it is a genuine A/B rather than a re-run. **That zero is weak in a specific, nameable way and is not evidence of safety:** hypergumbo's own tree is one repo, and the barrier arm — the only consumer of a `False` — has been measured at zero walks on every repo tried. A corpus where the arm runs has not been found, so this change has not been observed under the conditions where it could suppress.
  - **The binding check is inherited, not bypassed.** A locally defined `class Path` and a `from decoy_lib import Path` are refused at a chain root exactly as they are at an assignment — pinned by tests, since widening the root is precisely where a back door around INV-kipor would appear. 254 corpus sites share a constructor name with an in-repo class.
  - **One asymmetry deliberately NOT closed, disclosed rather than discovered.** For a receiver of unknown type the assigned form still emits an untyped `external` placeholder edge and the inline form emits nothing. Both mean the same thing to every consumer (`_module_from_symbol_path` returns `""` for the placeholder by documented design) and both tag zero boundaries — verified, not assumed. Closing it would mean minting ~27,085 placeholder edges to no measured effect. It ships with a written re-evaluation trigger: if a consumer is ever added that distinguishes "no edge" from "an edge to an untyped receiver" — a call-graph completeness metric, or a gate counting unresolved call sites — this starts to matter.

### Added
- **JavaScript reaches the DDG machinery, via the TypeScript grammar it already shared (WI-nonad).** `cfg_nodes/typescript.yaml` has asserted in its own header since it landed that it "also covers JavaScript (same node types in tree-sitter-javascript)" — but the mapping, the def/use extractor and the `LanguageDdgSpec` were all keyed to `typescript` and the spec's glob was `*.ts`, so a `.js` file reached **none** of it. JavaScript is the half of that pair whose edges can move a taint verdict: 50 sources and **83 sinks** against TypeScript's 6 and **zero**.
  - **Three keys, no new logic.** A second `@register_def_use_extractor("javascript")` on the existing extractor class, a second `LanguageDdgSpec(language="javascript", file_glob="*.js")`, and one entry in a new `cfg._CFG_MAPPING_ALIASES`. The alternative — copying `typescript.yaml` to `javascript.yaml` — duplicates the *consequences* of the shared-grammar assertion into a second artifact free to drift from the first; the alias encodes the assertion once, so a node type added for TypeScript is automatically available to JavaScript, which is what "same grammar" means. If the grammars genuinely diverge, delete the entry and add the real file.
  - **Measured effect on a fixture, which is more than wiring:** the `http.createServer` → `fs.writeFileSync` flow in the recall corpus moves from `approximate`/`structural` to **`precise`/`ddg`** — the walk now runs on JavaScript and confirms the data dependence. **This is a fixture, not a cohort.** No claim is made here about express or apollo-server; that A/B is tagged `awaits_bakeoff_validation` and read per-flow against source, deliberately not folded in so its delta stays legible.
  - **`register_def_use_extractor` now stamps `language` on each registration.** Stacking two decorators constructs two instances, and without the stamp the second would carry the first's name — a Protocol attribute true only by class-author convention, now true by construction. No-op for every existing single registration.
  - **A gate was testing its own copy of the thing it gates.** `test_cfg_mapping_declares_atomic_statement` re-derived `_CFG_NODES_DIR / f"{language}.yaml"` itself instead of asking production's loader — a second, independent implementation of "where does a language's mapping live", which went wrong the moment a mapping legitimately lived under another key. It now calls `load_cfg_mapping` and reads the parsed field the CFG builder actually consumes.
  - **Wiring a language INVALIDATED three tests that used it as their incapable exemplar, and the fix was to replace the exemplar rather than flip the constants.** `test_taint_recall_corpus.py`'s scope tests distinguish data-flow-capable languages from incapable ones; with JavaScript capable, asserting `True` and moving on would have left a test that distinguishes nothing. Java takes the role — it has a cfg mapping but no `atomic_statement` and no extractor, so `blockers` is a strict subset and the partial case is exercised for the first time. Its fixture contributes **zero** evidence rows, verified rather than assumed, so it cannot perturb any verdict.
  - **One test lost coverage that could not be replaced, and says so in place.** `test_adjudication_label_does_not_depend_on_another_language` drew its power from the flow's own language being incapable; JavaScript was the only language that both produced a fixture flow and lacked an extractor — java, c, php and ruby were each tried and every one yields zero evidence rows. The invariant remains pinned at unit level by `test_taint.py::test_function_the_ddg_never_saw_is_structural_not_mixed`, which builds the shape synthetically (`ddg_symbols={"other_fn"}`) and cannot be invalidated by wiring a language — a better home for the property than a fixture whose validity depended on a language staying unwired. What is gone is the end-to-end confirmation, recorded in the test's docstring rather than absorbed silently.
  - **Two filing claims corrected rather than propagated.** "JavaScript is the largest unwired taint surface in the tree" is **false** — by sink count it is fifth (elixir 301, erlang 157, haskell 131, kotlin 93, javascript 83); the real argument is cost, not size: it was the only unwired language whose mapping and extractor already existed under another key. And an in-file comment cited apollo-server at 588 DDG edges / 93 symbols, measured on an older tree; it is now marked for re-measurement rather than copied forward. The `*.js` glob deliberately does **not** claim `.mjs` / `.cjs` / `.jsx` — each is a separate question about what discovery classifies as `javascript`, and widening a glob on an assumption is how a spec starts lying about its own coverage.
- **`dead-code-maybe` candidates carry the reachability cohort the summary already counted (WI-jozah).** `test_only_reachable` was computed and emitted as a **summary count only**, while every candidate record carried an identical field set — so the highest-value cohort in the report was unselectable. "Reachable only from tests" means the function has tests but no production caller, so its tests are load-bearing for nothing and a 100% coverage gate is actively concealing the deadness; on hypergumbo's own tree that is **1747 of 2107** candidates, a list a consumer could count but not filter. The set was already being computed and collapsed to a `len()` on the way out.
  - Each candidate now carries `reachability`: `test_only`, `unreachable`, or **`null`** under a non-default seed mode where no test-seeded walk ran. Null rather than a default, because "the walk did not run" and "it ran and found nobody" are different facts, and defaulting would assert one the tool never computed — the shape of the defect this field exists to fix. Text mode annotates the line, since a disclosure that exists only under `--json` is half shipped.
  - **The filing proposed three values and one is meaningless.** It suggested `test_only` / `entrypoint_only` / `unreachable`. But a dead candidate is by definition unreachable from the *production* seeds, and those are a superset of the entrypoint seeds — so every dead candidate is entrypoint-only-dead by construction, and that label would partition nothing. `entrypoint_only_dead` stays a summary count of a different, larger cohort (**4314** against 2107). The per-candidate discriminator is binary.
  - **A second denominator ships with it, because the first test asserted a false equality and passed.** That test compared the summed per-item labels against `test_only_reachable`. Those are two different populations: the summary counts the cohort over *all production symbols*, while the emitted candidate list has the FP demoters applied (generated files, cross-language name hits, virtual-dispatch overrides, `--exclude-*`). The fixture is small enough that no demoter fires, so the two happened to be equal and the assertion held — on the self-tree they are **1859 against 1747**, a 112-symbol gap that would have shipped as a false equality nobody could reproduce from the fixture. Caught by measuring on a real repository, not by the test. Both numbers are now published (`test_only_reachable_candidates`), and the test asserts the relationship that is actually invariant: the candidate-scoped count is exact and can only be ≤ the production-scoped one.
  - **This view promoted out of the shared read-view schema placeholder** to its own `DEAD_CODE_MAYBE_SCHEMA_VERSION` (`0.2.0`), which is the escape hatch `schema.py` documents for a view that evolves its wire shape. Bumping the shared `READ_VIEW_SCHEMA_VERSION` was tried first and is wrong on two counts: it asserts a falsehood (a reader of the `routes` or `cache-status` envelope would see a new version for a view that did not change), and **four separate test sites pin that value**, so every bump is a four-file edit in unrelated views. That cost is the signal the placeholder had stopped fitting. Three of those four sites pinned a bare `"0.1.0"` literal and now reference the constant; the fourth pinned the constant's *value*, and now asserts the inequality against the behavior-map version that it was actually guarding.

### Added
- **Each escape site now names WHICH of the walk's four branches lost the value — and measuring that way found that the arm which consumes refutation never runs.** `escape_sites` records an `EscapeSite(symbol_id, line, reason)` against a closed `ESCAPE_REASONS` vocabulary instead of a bare pair. Still purely observational; the verdict is identical whether or not it is passed.
  - **Why the reason is not cosmetic.** Two of the four branches (`source_undefined`, `definition_unrecorded`) mean the DDG never held the fact — an EXTRACTION gap, INV-lupav's territory. Two (`call_beside_heir`, `no_heir`) mean the walk saw the use and could not account for it, and only `no_heir` is the bucket ADR-0017 §7b's alias exclusion is invoked for. A histogram keyed on `(symbol_id, line)` folds an extractor gap into a scope exclusion, which is the specific mechanism that priced the "expression read" family at 78.6% of INV-busis's blockers.
  - **THE BLOCKER POPULATION IS NOT THE ESCAPE POPULATION, and this was measured wrong twice.** The walk returns `True` the moment a tainted value is used at a sink line, but escapes recorded *before* that stay in the out-param — so a walk that CONFIRMED a flow still reports escape sites, and those blocked nothing. On pretix that is **52 production sites over all walks against 15 over blocked walks**, and restricting to blocked walks *inverts* which family is largest (calls → field writes). INV-busis's own earlier run had already documented and fixed this ("sums over UNKNOWN non-barrier walks only"); the first version of the new instrument repeated it, and a control added for an unrelated purpose is what caught it.
  - **The finding that decides the plan: ZERO barrier-arm walks, on all four repos that produce walks at all** (hypergumbo 36, pretix 35, prometheus 7, alertmanager 3 — every invocation on the §3a arm). The §3a arm tests `is True`, so `False` and `None` collapse there; only the barrier arm reads `False`, where it earns `sanitized` and drops the flow. **So any change to the escape accounting — expression-read reclassification, §7a field sensitivity — is structurally inert on this corpus**: it cannot delete a finding and cannot add one. The barrier arm runs only when §3a already returned `True` *and* the source function calls a catalogued sanitizer. **CORRECTED, same day, before this entry was released:** the sentence that followed here attributed the zero to the sanitizer vocabulary being crypto-only, and concluded that "a sanitizer catalogue is what arms" the suppression hazard. That mechanism was never established — the zero was believed without a positive control. Two purpose-built fixtures both produced **zero walks**: one where `f = Fernet(key); f.decrypt(...); f.encrypt(...)` emits `python:external:0-0:decrypt:external_symbol` because the receiver type is never inferred, and one written module-qualified where the source and sanitizer *do* resolve but the sink `pathlib.Path(...).write_text(x)` emits **no call edge at all** (an attribute chain rooted at a constructor result — #231 fixed only chains rooted at a local). **Every sanitizer in the shipped catalogue is an instance method** — `Fernet.encrypt`, `AESGCM.encrypt`, `AEAD.Seal`, `Cipher.doFinal` — which is true and load-bearing, but the conclusion originally drawn from it here (that registering a barrier therefore requires the broken receiver typing, making the arm a third INV-linub symptom family) is **excised: it was wrong.** **SUPERSEDED THE FOLLOWING DAY:** the conclusion drawn here — that *receiver typing* is what arms the hazard — was the second wrong mechanism proposed for this zero, and it was also never positive-controlled. Receiver typing is **necessary but not sufficient**. A probe with receiver typing *working* (parameter annotation pinned `cryptography.fernet.Fernet` into the module slot, source matched, walk adjudicated `precise`) still produced zero barrier walks, because the sanitizer was refused independently by `_register_sanitizer_callers` reading the name slot for receiver evidence that lives in the module slot. See the **sanitizer gate** entry under Fixed above for the established mechanism and the positive control that finally fired the arm. WI-joluk's gate landing first survives all three accounts. Correspondingly, INV-karud clause (b)'s zero is **not** independent corroboration: a sanitizer call site can only register if its callee resolves, so both zeros may share one upstream defect.
  - **Measured reach of the reclassification proposal, for the record:** 32 of 81 production blocker sites, but **3 of 49 blocked walks (6.1%)** — because a walk stays `None` if *any* of its sites escapes, so a per-site share systematically overstates what a per-site fix buys.
- **Two measurement instruments land in-repo** — `scripts/measure-escape-shapes.py` (escape shapes by reason × node type, with arm and blocked-walk denominators) and `scripts/measure-call-escape-cause.py` (why a call-node escape escaped, through the four stages it can drop out at). The two previous instruments in this area lived in session scratchpads and drifted out of sync with the function they measured, which INV-busis records as its own durability hazard.
  - **What the cause probe found, and it is not what either prior account said.** A call-node escape can fail at four places, and the two repos disagree about which. On **pretix**, **zero** sites lack an edge and **13 of 17 (76.5%)** carry the literal `external` module placeholder — the receiver's type is unknown, `_qualified_callee` returns `""` for it *by documented design*, so the edge can never reach `callees_at`. On **hypergumbo**, **13 of 24 (54.2%)** have no edge at all. The two repos cleanly separate the two halves of INV-mumov: pretix's sites are `obj.method()` attribute chains (the half #231 fixed, so edges exist and the block moved downstream to receiver typing), hypergumbo's are `print(...)` / `isinstance(...)` / `hasattr(...)` — builtin bare names, the half deliberately left unfixed. **#231 worked for its target shape; emission is not "done".**
  - **A pre-registered prediction was refuted and is recorded as such.** The rubric, written before any number was read, called every call-node escape "a missing call EDGE (INV-mumov)". On pretix that is zero of seventeen.
  - **INV-linub's statement under-scopes its own consequence.** Its chain (catalogue → emission → receiver evidence → the F3 gate) is written for `_match_propagation_entry`, the io-boundary lookup. The same missing module slot has a second consumer the item never names: `_qualified_callee` → `_catalogue_key_for_edge` → `callees_at` → the §4 summary lookup inside the §3a walk. One root cause, two symptom families.
  - **Fixing receiver typing would mostly not CLOSE these sites** — of the three pretix sites that did get a key, two hit "no summary" and one hit "propagates". `voucher_list.append(tainted)` is a *genuine* escape (the value enters a container) and is one of the three real leaks the removal-authority trial found. Typing makes these escapes explicable, not absent.
- **The §3a walk can name its own escape sites, and doing so found that INV-mumov is manufacturing INV-busis's blockers.** `_ddg_taint_reaches` gains an optional `escape_sites` out-param recording every `(symbol_id, line)` at which the walk lost track. Purely observational — the verdict is identical whether or not it is passed.
  - **Why this is production code and not an instrument.** INV-busis's shape split decides whether ~79% of its blockers are genuine escapes or misclassified expression reads, and that split needs to know which line each walk died at. The instrument that produced the filed split re-derived the walk's escape logic *outside* the walk, lives in a dead session scratchpad, and no longer matches the function's signature — which the item records as its own durability hazard. A measurement that re-implements the thing it measures drifts from it.
  - **What it found.** On hypergumbo self-analysis: 150 escape sites, 66 resolved to a CFG statement. The largest bucket — **24 of 66 (36.4%)** — has a node type of literally `call`, and **99 of 150 (66%)** have no callee recorded at that line (positive control: `subprocess.run` *is* recorded at other sites, so the empties are real absences). The sites name the cause: `print(`, `isinstance(stderr, bytes)`, `cmd.append("-q")`. Those are calls, and INV-mumov already measured why they carry no edge — Python emits zero call edges for attribute-chain receivers and none for a builtin bare `Name`. No edge → no `callees_at` entry → `_use_site_terminates` cannot decide → `escaped = True`.
  - **Consequence.** A substantial share of INV-busis's "genuine non-call escape sites" are calls made invisible by a *separate, already-filed, false-negative* defect that needs no owner ruling. On this sample the expression-read family is **42.4%, not 78.6%**, and the largest single bucket is not an escape category at all. Neither item's thread mentioned the other; both now do.
  - **Caveats, load-bearing:** Python only (the three Go repos available yielded 15 resolved sites between them — too few to quote as shares), self-analysis rather than a neutral corpus, and the `assignment` bucket does not separate plain assignment from field write so its 19.7% is an upper bound. **Correction, 2026-08-06:** this entry originally added "caddy and pretix, where the filed 81 came from, are not on this machine". That is **false for pretix** and was read off `~/repos` (6 repos) rather than the actual corpus at `~/ALL_REPOS` (271 repos in one subdirectory). pretix — the source of 46 of the filed 81 sites, and a neutral corpus — has been available throughout, as have apollo-server and zoxide. **Further corrected 2026-08-06:** the replacement claim "caddy and express are genuinely absent" was *also* wrong, in the same way and for the same reason — both are present, under `~/ALL_REPOS/curriculum_repos`, along with a crypto-heavy cohort (`age`, `cosign`, `gocryptfs`, `git-crypt`, `encrypted-dns-server`) directly relevant to sanitizer work. Two successive corpus claims were asserted from a partial listing rather than a search; the durable fix is to enumerate `~/ALL_REPOS` before making any availability claim, not to patch the sentence a third time. The self-analysis caveat above was therefore avoidable, not forced. Raw node types are published alongside every bucket so the histogram stands without the shape naming, which is the one part that is editorial.
  - Instrument error recorded because it nearly became a headline: a first pass read positional `a[5]` for `callees_at` and got a clean **100%** have-no-callee. `a[5]` is `summaries`; `callees_at` is `a[4]`. The flat 100% is what gave it away.
- **The coverage gate is wired, on the one arm where it can matter — and its measured effect is ZERO, which is the point (WI-joluk).** `RepoDdg` now carries `forfeit_refutation`, the set of symbol ids whose CFG statement extents do not cover every call node in their body (or whose language declares no `call_node_types`, which forfeits for the same reason). `build_repo_ddg` populates it, `_build_ddg_for_verify_claims` threads it, and `propagate_taint_ddg` consults it.
  - **Only the sanitizer arm changes.** The §3a arm tests `_ddg_taint_reaches(...) is True`, so `False` and `None` already collapse there and the gate could not alter it. On the barrier arm a `False` earns `sanitized`, and a sanitized flow is **dropped from the claim's violation set** — so a `False` from a function the extractor did not fully see suppresses a real violation. That is the arm that got the gate, and the only one.
  - **Direction: strictly fewer suppressions, hence strictly more surviving violations.** That asymmetry is why this could land before removal authority exists at all.
  - **Keyed on the SOURCE function, not consulted globally** — pinned by its own test, because an implementation that forfeited whenever the set was merely non-empty would pass the headline test and silently suppress every sanitizer in the repo the moment one function anywhere was uncovered.
  - **Measured output change: zero, and reported as a closed route rather than an improvement.** alertmanager yields **59 evidence rows, all `ddg_mixed`, 0 sanitized** — the gate can only turn `sanitized` into not-sanitized, so with no sanitized findings it provably changed nothing. This independently corroborates INV-karud clause (b)'s "0 registered sanitizer call sites across 95,950 taint call edges" on fresh substrate: the shipped sanitizer vocabulary is crypto-only (go 1 entry, python 4, rust 3, typescript 3, javascript 0), so a barrier registers only where a repo calls a crypto primitive in the same function as a taint source. **445 of alertmanager's 1,329 walkable functions do forfeit**, so the route is closed *before* anything could walk it wrongly — which is the order WI-joluk demands ("Build the gate BEFORE removal authority, not after — INV-lupav exists because the reverse order was attempted once").
  - Production `build_repo_ddg` reproduces the standalone instrument's forfeit counts exactly (alertmanager 445 of 1329), so the measurement and the shipped path agree rather than being two implementations of one idea.
  - **The forfeit population differs by language, and it was read rather than assumed.** Go forfeits on the `if err := do(); err != nil` initializer its own `cfg_nodes/go.yaml` self-documents. **Python forfeits on a different shape entirely** — the call in a `for x in <call>` iterable and in a `with <call> as f` context-manager header, neither covered by a recorded statement extent. A first non-vacuity test reasoned by analogy from Go and used a call inside an `if` test; that forfeits **nothing** in Python, because the `if` condition contains its calls. The analogy was wrong, which is also why Python measures 12.8% against Go's 33.5% — the languages fail in different places, and the gate is per-function precisely so it does not need to know that in advance.
- **The §3a walk can now be told it did not see the whole function, and forfeit its ability to refute (WI-joluk).** `_ddg_taint_reaches` gains `forfeit_refutation` (default `False`, so no existing caller changes behaviour), and `cfg.uncovered_call_lines` computes the evidence: the call sites in a function's AST body that no recorded CFG statement covers. This is the structural fix behind **INV-lupav**, the tracker's only P0 — PR #199 closed the acute case (a definition the DDG holds *nothing* for now yields `None`), but a *partially* recorded definition still returned `False`, the one verdict that may license removing a reported flow, for a value the walk never followed.
  - **Coverage-gated, not gap-enumerated.** The population is whatever a language's def/use extractor does not model, which is not knowable from inside the walk — it cannot tell a construct nobody taught it about from one that genuinely has no uses. A fix shaped as "handle the known gaps" is a list that decays silently, and it decays in the direction that deletes findings.
  - **Byte extents, not lines, and that is the whole design.** A line test cannot see the motivating case: `CfgBuilder._process_conditional` records only the *condition child* of an `if`, so Go's `if err := do(); err != nil` records `err != nil` and the initializer becomes no statement at all. Both are on the same line, so line-matching reports the call as covered and the gate is vacuous exactly where `cfg_nodes/go.yaml` self-documents the gap (700 of caddy's 6,596 `if` statements carry a call there). The recorded statement's byte range does not contain the initializer's; an extent test catches it. Pinned by a test that would pass under a line-based implementation.
  - **`call_node_types` is declared per language in `cfg_nodes/*.yaml`**, not sniffed from node-type names — "does this node invoke something" is a per-grammar fact (Go `call_expression`, Python `call`, Rust `macro_invocation`), and a substring heuristic is the hand-rolled predicate this subsystem keeps getting wrong. Declared for the four languages with def/use extractors; **java is deliberately left undeclared** (it has no extractor and no `atomic_statement` either).
  - **`None` and the empty set are different facts and do not fold.** Empty means "checked, nothing uncovered" and permits refutation; `None` means "cannot check at all" and forfeits. Collapsing them would make every unconfigured language look fully covered — failing open, in the direction that deletes findings (L54 default-deny: the *permitting* case is the one enumerated).
  - **Blocks `False` only, never `True`.** A `True` is positive evidence of a dependence the walk actually found, and an incomplete picture cannot unmake it. Downgrading it would turn a safety gate into a recall regression; pinned by its own test.
  - **Measured, because the item asked and the answer branches the roadmap.** WI-joluk: *"If it is near 100 percent the gate is correct and §3a refutation is not viable on this substrate, which is a finding rather than a failure; if it is small, refutation is viable on the remainder."* Over five repo-language pairs, counting only functions that produce DDG edges (the population §3a can act on at all): **alertmanager 33.5%, prometheus 35.5%, kserve/go 26.9%, kserve/python 27.8%, hypergumbo 12.8%.** Not near 100% — refutation is viable on the remainder. Positive control passes in both directions (a known-uncovered `if`-initializer reports exactly one site; the same code with the initializer hoisted reports zero). **A first run reported hypergumbo at 21.5% over 158,088 functions; the instrument was sweeping a virtualenv, so the share was partly a statement about third-party code.** Corrected to 24,769 first-party functions at 12.8%.
  - The gate is **not yet wired into production** — nothing passes `forfeit_refutation=True` today. Measuring before wiring is deliberate: the share is what decides whether wiring is worth it, and it would have been unrecoverable after the fact.

### Added
- **The last two Python receiver-typing shapes: an inline expression receiver, and an annotated assignment.** Measured on poetry: **57 → 59 (+2)**, both true positives on direct source reading, and both the same shape — `path.joinpath("direct_url.json").exists()` and `…read_text(encoding="utf-8")` in poetry's `InstalledRepository`. sqlalchemy 47 → 47 (+0), a real zero.
  - **The inline-receiver gap was introduced by PR #247's own scoping, not inherited.** #247 taught derivations to keep their type but entered only from an *assignment*, and the typed-emit branch is gated on `isinstance(func.value, ast.Name)` — so `(d / "f").write_text(x)` and `d.joinpath("f").write_text(x)`, both thoroughly idiomatic, still reported nothing. Same resolver at the new entry point, so the allowlist and the exact-string type comparison apply identically; an expression receiver is not a back door around either, pinned by a test that routes `d.name.replace(...)` and asserts it stays refused.
  - **The `AnnAssign` exclusion was re-priced, not re-asserted — and the test I wrote to prevent that is what forced it.** `d: Path = raw` was excluded from #246 on a measurement showing zero payload. That measurement predates #247: once a derivation kept its type, an annotated assignment began seeding a whole chain, so the payload stopped being zero. `test_annassign_is_not_routed` duly failed, which is exactly what it was written for ("a later PR that routes them has to change a test that says why"); it is retired in place with the reason recorded rather than deleted.
  - **`read_text` is correctly classified while remaining excluded from the allowlist**, and the distinction is the design: the allowlist governs propagating the receiver's type *through* a member (`read_text` returns `str`, so nothing chains past it), not whether the call itself is a boundary. `p.read_text()` on a `Path` is a genuine `fs_read`.
  - **Still excluded, each a recorded decision:** return-annotated factories (`def mk() -> Path`) need external-type resolution of a function's return annotation, a different mechanism from reading an annotation in place; `.parent`/`.parents[n]` are attribute reads measured at 16 sites = 0.7% of chain-on-Name misses; loop targets, interprocedural propagation and `self.attr` receivers are unbuilt. Population note from the same sweep: `self.<field>.<method>()` does **not** emit "no call edge at all" as previously recorded here — 81–83% of those sites do emit, carrying no receiver evidence, which is a typing gap rather than an emission one.
- **A derivation from a typed receiver keeps the type, for an allowlisted member only.** PR #246 types `d` in `def h(d: Path, x)`, and the type was then lost at the first derivation: `p = d / "f.txt"` and `p = d.joinpath("f")` both degraded to the `external` placeholder, so `p.write_text(x)` reported nothing. Measured against a post-#246 baseline so the delta is attributable to this change alone: poetry **52 → 57 (+5)**, sqlalchemy **47 → 47 (+0)** — a real zero, since a database library barely derives paths. (mitmproxy moved 248 → 254 but its baseline predates #246, so that +6 spans both changes and is not counted here.) All five poetry additions are true positives on direct source reading — `Layout._create_default` does `package_path = path / self.package_path; package_path.mkdir(parents=True); package_init = package_path / "__init__.py"; package_init.touch()`. poetry's project scaffolder creates directories and files and none of it was visible. The nested case also confirms the resolver recurses: `tests = path / "tests"` then `tests / "__init__.py"` is a derivation of a derivation.
  - **DEFAULT-DENY, and that is the measurement rather than caution.** Most members of a typed receiver do NOT return that type — `read_text` → `str`, `stat` → `os.stat_result`, `exists` → `bool`, `open` → a file object, `glob`/`iterdir` → iterators, `name`/`stem`/`suffix`/`as_posix` → `str`. Propagating by default scores **25.9% precision** on adversarial fixtures (7 of 27 added boundaries correct) and mints **16 false taint sinks** including 2 `database` and 2 `network`. Across 8 Python repos, **1,955 of 2,296 (85.1%)** hop≥1 propagations are provably wrong or unverifiable, and **20 of the 21** provably-wrong ones are a Path→`str` transition. The allowlist scores 85.7% on the same fixtures and costs **3 boundaries out of 427 (0.7%)**. Enumerating the permitting case is also the standing default-deny rule: a table of blockers fails open the moment the stdlib grows a member.
  - **There is no backstop, which is why the allowlist carries the safety.** A minted hint bypasses `gate_named_entry` and the `ambiguous_names` net by design. Control: the 19 catalogued `pathlib.Path` member names — 10 of them taint sinks, including `replace` (also `str.replace`) and `rename` (also `DataFrame.rename`) — match **zero** times with no module hint, and every one becomes reachable the instant a hint appears.
  - **The type is compared by exact string, never through `_module_matches`.** That predicate is permissive by design and accepts an unqualified reference as a component suffix, so it treats a vendored `mylib.pathlib.Path` as the real one and mints an `fs_write` boundary plus a `host_fs` taint sink for a third-party class that merely shares a name. The table is keyed by qualified type, so the lookup is a dict miss.
  - **The concept was NOT given a new catalogue, because it already has a home.** `FileAnalysis.method_return_types` (INV-dihos / WI-kuroj) is the language-neutral return-type registry Go and Rust already populate from parsed signatures; Python populates it zero times. A first design proposed a ninth YAML catalogue (`receiver_types/<lang>.yaml`) and was **refuted 3/3** on exactly this point. `TYPE_PRESERVING_MEMBERS` is the stdlib complement — those types ship no source for Pass 1 to parse — stating the same fact in the same shape (qualified member → returned type). The `/` operator rides as `__truediv__`, Python's own name for it, so an operator needs no new vocabulary and no new axis.
  - **This is Python-specific, and that was measured rather than assumed.** Only 5 of 14 io_primitives catalogues key any primitive on a path/file-*typed* receiver. Go, JS, Swift, ObjC, C, C++, Elixir, Erlang and Haskell structurally cannot have the gap — their join returns a bare string, or the path is an argument, or the catalogue holds no method-kind entries. Rust has the shape but 83% of its 69-site gap is base receiver typing (the PR #246 equivalent, which Rust has not done) and only 6% is derivation. Java already has base typing and 100% of its residual gap is chain-root receivers; 46 of 89 of its name-level candidates are `String.length`, Mockito and Eclipse `IResource`, so a name-keyed fix there is net-negative. No second language was manufactured to satisfy a rule.
  - **Disclosed:** `.parent`/`.parents[n]` are attribute reads, a different AST shape with a smaller population, and are not routed. Neither are `Path.cwd()`/`Path.home()` (classmethods on the type, not derivations), interprocedural propagation, `for p in d.iterdir()` loop targets, or `self.attr` receivers — each pinned or recorded as an exclusion. Kotlin and Swift populations are **not measured**: both failed their positive control (Kotlin emits no unresolved method-call edges at all; Swift puts the receiver *variable name* in the module slot), so their zeros are instrument nulls, not absence. The corpus contribution of the earlier ~464-call-site estimate for this family did not reproduce and is superseded.
- **An annotated parameter is now receiver evidence, so a typed receiver reaches the I/O catalogue (WI-zilag, INV-linub instance).** `def h(p: pathlib.Path, x): p.write_text(x)` produced **no I/O boundary at all** — the annotation names the receiver's type exactly and the catalogue holds `pathlib.Path.write_text` as `fs_write`, but the analyzer never routed the annotation into the symbol id's module slot, so the edge arrived carrying the `external` placeholder and `gate_named_entry` correctly refused it as an untyped method call. Same for bare-with-`from`-import, aliased module imports, and `socket.socket`. Measured on poetry: **38 → 52 reported boundaries (+14)**; sqlalchemy 43 → 47. The additions are `pathlib.Path.{exists,is_dir,is_file,stat,glob,iterdir,mkdir}` on annotated parameters — e.g. poetry's `def is_dir_writable(path: Path, create: bool = False)` calling `path.exists()` and `path.mkdir(...)`, read against source and confirmed a true positive.
  - **Scoped to PARAMETERS, on measurement, not taste.** Over six Python repos: **95,245** method-call edges carry the `external` placeholder; **4,160** have a receiver with a resolvable annotation; **63** actually reach the catalogue — and **96% of that payload is the annotated-parameter shape alone**. `AnnAssign` (`p: Path = raw`), return-annotated factories, attribute reads and generics contribute **exactly zero**, so routing them would add trust surface for no recall. Both exclusions are pinned by tests that say why, so a later PR that routes them has to argue with a test rather than silently widen the surface.
  - **This is the first change in this subsystem that ADDS classifications, and the binding check is what makes that safe.** A minted hint is trusted: it bypasses both `gate_named_entry` and the `ambiguous_names` net by design — routing the hinted path through that gate was measured to destroy 61.5–87.2% of all reported boundaries for zero gain, so it is not available as a backstop. A wrong hint is therefore a confident false boundary **and** a false taint sink, never silence.
  - **Emitting the raw annotation text — what the existing tree-sitter-side resolver does for dotted annotations — was measured to produce CONFIRMED false boundaries.** `conn: Connection` in sqlalchemy matched `sqlite3.Connection.execute` and minted a database-zone taint sink, with **174** such annotations in the corpus, because `_module_matches` accepts an unqualified reference as a component suffix. Resolved through its import binding the same annotation yields `sqlalchemy.engine.Connection`, which does not match — verified directly against the predicate. Every one of the eight negative tests is one of these measured FP classes: the sqlalchemy collision, `from fastapi import Path`, an unimported dotted root, an in-file `class Path` shadow, a forward-reference string, and `Optional[...]`. All eight already passed before the change and must keep passing.
  - **Resolution reuses `_import_binding_for` (INV-kipor, this release),** so "does an import in this file rebind this name, and to what?" now has one answer serving three callers instead of drifting into a sixth private copy. An alias expands (`import pathlib as pl` → `pl.Path` becomes `pathlib.Path`); an unbound name returns `None` and stays `external`; a parameter already resolved to an in-repo class wins, so a first-party `class Path` is never overridden by a same-named import.
  - **GO is NOT CALLED FOR GO, and that is a measured result rather than a deferral.** Go has no equivalent gap — **96.7%** (3,618/3,741) of package-qualified-receiver catalogue-name call sites already reach a usable module slot, and `func h(c net.Conn, b []byte){c.Write(b)}` already mints `net.Conn.Write`/`net_send` end-to-end. Go's residual gap is initializer type *inference* (`tr := &http.Transport{}`), and the candidate fix for it is **net-harmful**: **118 of 692 moved edges (17.1%) land on the WRONG in-repo symbol** — 66 of those on a struct field — because Go strips the package prefix and probes the repo symbol table before the import-path fallback, all for **+8 boundaries on a 1,309 baseline (+0.61%)**. So a single shared producer mechanism is the wrong factoring; the Go gap is filed with its hijack measurement rather than built.
  - **Disclosed, because it is a cold start:** 17 of the 18 catalogue entries this activates fire **zero** times on the corpus today, so their boundary labels have never been validated against real output. `Path.{exists,is_dir,is_file,stat}` are catalogued `fs_read` though they stat rather than read contents — a catalogue-labelling question, not a producer one, and left alone here. Relative imports, `importlib`, conditional and `TYPE_CHECKING` imports are unprobed. The 63-site population is a property of the six-repo bag, not of the tool: adding one repo moved it +20.6%, and per-repo the change ranges from +9.3% (sqlalchemy) to +36.8% (poetry).

### Added
- **`p = Path(raw)` types the receiver, and an UNBOUND constructor name no longer does.** `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` held exactly two rows — `open` and `socket.socket` — so the most common filesystem idiom in Python produced no boundary at all, while `pathlib.Path` had been sitting in `python.yaml` the whole time with 9 `fs_read` methods and 10 `fs_write`. The catalogue entry existed and nothing could reach it.
  - **Measured A/B on four repos, both directions, paired on the same tree: +131 I/O boundary chains (3,977 → 4,108), ZERO chains lost, taint findings 22 → 22, `sanitized_flows` 0 → 0, no verdict changed.** That last pair is the one that mattered — receiver typing arms the ADR-0017 sanitizer barrier, and since PR #214 an unearned `False` there earns `sanitized` and **drops a flow**, so the risk being measured was suppression, not merely false positives. It did not fire. Per repo: meson +90, poetry +19, mitmproxy +16, kserve +6. By boundary: `fs_read` 93, `fs_write` 38, led by `Path.exists` 40, `is_file` 16, `mkdir` 14.
  - **TWO TABLE KEYS, NOT ONE.** `from pathlib import Path` arrives as a bare `ast.Name` and `import pathlib` as a dotted `ast.Attribute` — different branches of `_external_constructor_module`. Measured on the corpus, bare is 103 of 136 constructor sites and dotted 33, so a one-key patch delivers roughly two thirds of the payload and looks complete.
  - **THE UNBOUND RULE HAD TO CHANGE, AND THAT IS THE SAFETY-RELEVANT HALF.** The bare-name branch trusted an unbound name on the reasoning "for a bare name that means the builtin". True of `open`; **false of `Path`**, which is not a builtin — an unbound `Path` is a local class, a star-import, or a name from a module never read, and 254 corpus sites have a constructor name that is also an in-repo class name. Trusting it would mint an `fs_write` boundary **and a `host_fs` taint sink** for any class merely named `Path`. `BUILTIN_CONSTRUCTOR_NAMES` now enumerates the permitting case; everything else must be **positively bound** to the module it claims, tightening INV-kipor's check from "not contradicted" to "confirmed".
  - **A constructor call is now a valid derivation ROOT** — `Path(raw) / "out.txt"`, `Path(p).expanduser()` — measured at 82 of 294 assign-from-`Path`-constructor sites (28%), so not a rounding error. `TYPE_PRESERVING_MEMBERS` still gates which members propagate, so widening the root does not widen the propagation rule. The resolver is threaded as a parameter because it needs per-file import maps from an enclosing scope; the inline-receiver branch lives in a different function and is deliberately **not** covered here.
  - **INV-kipor's parity test caught this, which is what it was built for.** Its positive control assumed unbound-is-trusted and so could not pass for a non-builtin row. The control now **derives** its import from the claimed module string (`pathlib.Path` → `from pathlib import Path`) rather than hard-coding one, so it grows with the table, and the new rule joined the same enumerated loop: every non-builtin bare key must refuse when unbound, each paired with a bound control proving the zero is not a dead fixture.
  - **A fixture that had gone vacuous was removed.** `TestBindingOwnershipDecidesTrust` monkeypatched `Path` into the table because the row did not exist yet — "pinned before anyone lands it". It landed, the monkeypatch became a no-op setting the same value, and those tests would have kept passing against a patched table even if the real one lost the row. Removed, with a guard asserting the row is genuinely present.
  - **Adjudicated against source, and the split is reported honestly.** 3 of 3 sampled sites read as genuine, including two exercising the new constructor-root path (`Path(p).expanduser()` in mitmproxy, `Path(repo.path) / submodule.path` in poetry). Product/test split is **91 / 40 (69.5% / 30.5%)**, all 40 test-path chains in meson. A first pass reported **100% product**, which was wrong: production's `classify_test_file` is real (it fires on `tests/test_foo.py`) but does not recognise meson's `unittests/` and `test cases/` naming, so the clean extreme was a classifier-convention artifact rather than ground truth.
  - **Deliberately NOT added, each measured rather than cautious:** `io.StringIO` / `io.BytesIO` (160 corpus sites — the catalogue's `io` module declares **zero** method-kind primitives, so typing them reaches nothing); `multiprocessing.Pipe` and `asyncio.open_connection` (both return a **tuple**, and they score zero here only because the corpus idiom is tuple unpacking, which the Name-target guard already refuses — a row for either is a latent wrong-type source awaiting its first non-unpacked use). `from pathlib import Path as P` misses the table by key: a false negative in the safe direction, measured at 0 of 136 corpus sites.
  - **NOT MEASURED:** precision at scale (3 sites read, not a precision figure); any repo outside the four A/B'd; whether the taint neutrality holds on a corpus where the barrier arm is reachable at all.

### Fixed
- **A directory is a virtualenv because of what it CONTAINS, not what it is called — `DEFAULT_EXCLUDES` was deleting 427 first-party source files.** `venv` / `.venv` / `env` sat in the list as bare names, and the matcher applies an exact name to **every component of the relative path at any depth**, so every directory named `env` in every repository was dropped before analysis. Measured across 467 corpus repos: **427 source files, 77 directories, 39 repos (8.4%), of which 425 (99.5%) classify FIRST_PARTY** under hypergumbo's own `supply_chain.classify_file`. The worst two are whole subsystems — RocksDB's entire POSIX I/O layer (`env_posix.cc`, `fs_posix.cc`, `io_posix.cc`, `file_system.cc`) and U-Boot's environment storage subsystem (`env.c`, `ext4.c`, `fat.c`, `mmc.c`, `nand.c`) — and both sit at the repo root. Exclusion is now conditioned on PEP 405's `pyvenv.cfg`, or `bin/activate` / `Scripts/activate` for older tooling.
  - **The deleted code was systematically I/O-dense, which is the worst thing for an exclusion list to be hiding from a boundary analysis.** On poetry the dropped package is its *virtualenv manager*; re-including it takes unique I/O boundary chains **70 → 107 (+37, +52.9%)** and `total_io_edges` **59 → 96**, with **zero chains lost**. Independently reproduced: 107 chains, exactly 37 anchored in an `env/` path (env_read 24, fs_read 7, subprocess 5, fs_write 1).
  - **Root-anchoring was evaluated and rejected on measurement, not taste.** `env/*` already works in this matcher and needs no new machinery, but it recovers 379 of 427 (88.8%) and **structurally cannot** recover rocksdb's and U-Boot's `env/`, which are at depth 0 — precisely where a virtualenv would be. Only content separates them. Conversely `vendor` must stay unanchored and unconditioned: **19,251 of its 124,721 files sit below the root and are genuine dependencies** (linuxkit, kata-containers, gvisor-tap-vsock), so that exclusion is doing correct work.
  - **ONE RULE, EVERY WALKER — and the drift it prevents happened during the change.** This module has two independent tree walkers: `FileIndex.build`'s `os.walk` prune loop (the fast path every real run takes) and `_is_excluded_classified`. The venv rule was added to one, and the result was `find_files` excluding a virtualenv correctly while a full `run_behavior_map` still analysed it — **the same input, two answers**. Caught by the suite, not by review. Both now call `is_pruned_dir`, and `test_all_three_walkers_agree` asserts parity **behaviourally** across all three public entry points rather than by grepping for the call, so a fourth copy that merely looks right cannot satisfy it.
  - **Three existing tests were invalidated and their FIXTURES were fixed, not their assertions.** `test_excludes_venv` / `test_excludes_dot_venv` built a `venv/` holding nothing but a `lib/` tree — which is no longer a virtualenv, so analysing it is correct. Each fixture grew a real marker (one `pyvenv.cfg`, one `bin/activate`, so both arms are exercised end-to-end) and each keeps asserting its original property. Deleting the assertions would have removed the guard against the regression that matters most here.
  - **Scope held deliberately.** No discriminator was evaluated for `build` (1,965 files / 65 repos), `coverage` (246), `target` (1,183), `out`, `dist`, `site`, `.cache` — two of them larger by file count than `env` — and they are **not** fixed here on the strength of the `env` result. The `Scripts/activate` arm has **no real-world positive control**: zero corpus hits and no Windows virtualenv available, so it ships pinned by fixture and is not claimed to be validated. The corpus that produced "0 of 110 venv-named dirs carry a marker" contains **no installed virtualenvs at all** (467 bare clones), so that zero is partly a property of the corpus; the live control is this repository's own `.venv`, asserted still-excluded against the real directory.
  - **DIRECTION: this ADDS analyzable source.** It can add findings and add analysis time; it cannot delete a finding.
- **`verify-claims` no longer certifies a boundary clean when the I/O could have left through a library it has never catalogued (INV-fibis).** Reproduced on two **unmodified upstream repos**, each with a positive control firing in the *same run*, so this was never the analysis being blind and saying so. poetry's `src/poetry`: `✗ [CONTROL-FSREAD] violated — 20 fs_read chain(s)` alongside `✓ never sends data over the network — No net_send chains found`, against 14 files that `import requests` and call `requests.Session()` / `.get()` / `.post()`. `full-stack-fastapi-template/backend`: `✓ never writes to a database` against `crud.py:14-15` `session.add(db_obj); session.commit()`. Both now return **`inconclusive` at exit 2** naming the libraries — poetry: `cachecontrol, cleo…, dulwich, keyring, requests` (51 modules); the template: `alembic, emails, fastapi…` (18).
  - **The gate had the resolution of "did this language produce ANY call edges".** Both prior blind spots (WI-kajil / INV-bitig) ask that, and Python answers it hundreds of thousands of times on any real repo, so a clean verdict stayed confirmable no matter how much I/O left through an uncatalogued module. This is **exactly the residual `cli.py` had already recorded** — "closing it needs a signal with resolution finer than 'any', e.g. the share of method-construct call edges that resolve to something the catalogue can match" — filed there as a Kotlin problem. It is not language-specific, and Python is the likeliest trial substrate.
  - **The permitting case is enumerated, not the blocking one.** A module supports a clean verdict when the catalog can decide it either way: it declares a primitive for the module, or `is_stdlib_module` knows it (an examined negative — `math.sqrt` is not a gap). A denylist of known-risky libraries would fail open on the first library nobody had thought of, which is precisely how `requests` slipped through. `confirmed` stays **reachable**: a repo whose external calls all land in known modules still confirms, which matters because blanket downgrade is the other way to make a verdict worthless and `cli.py` records that happening once already.
