  - **Two refinements were forced by measurement, not foresight, and the first draft was wrong on both.** It reported **258** modules on poetry. **231 came from `imports` edges** — an import performs no I/O, so counting them reports every module a repo merely *mentions*; the catalog classifies call sites (`calls` / `module_attr_ref` / `instantiates`, deliberately **not** `_COVERAGE_CALL_EDGE_TYPES`, which answers a different question and includes `imports`). And **120 of the remaining 171 were poetry's own modules**: an unresolved *first-party* callee is not a catalogue gap, because its source was read and whatever I/O it performs was examined on its own edges. `supply_chain.tier` cannot discriminate these — it reads 3 for every `external_symbol` node, poetry's included — so first-party is derived from the `src` side of the edge set, which always names an in-repo file. Filtered: **51**, with `requests.Session` and `dulwich` intact and zero `poetry.*`.
  - **One predicate, both callers, and no seventh extractor.** `compute_boundary_coverage` is shared by the boundary and taint arms, so the check lands there rather than growing a parallel copy; `catalogs` is a **required** parameter, because a safety gate that silently skips itself when a caller forgets an argument fails open in exactly the way this fix exists to prevent. Module extraction reuses `_module_from_symbol_path` — symbol-id module extraction already has six homes and three different correct mechanisms (WI-ribuz), two of them naive and wrong.
  - **Disclosed residual, pinned as a test rather than left in a comment.** An edge carrying the bare `external` placeholder (an untyped receiver) names no module to adjudicate and is **not** counted — it is the largest edge population in a Python repo and counting it would send nearly every repo to `inconclusive` while identifying no library. So a repo reaching its I/O *only* through untyped receivers still confirms today. That population is the receiver-typing gap (INV-linub L3). **Not measured:** any non-Python language; the false-downgrade direction beyond the fixtures and these two repos.
- **Derived confidence now follows finalize's resolution verdict instead of the producer's advisory flag (INV-fazim).** Two features disagreed about who decides `is_resolved`. confidence:F1 (ADR-0039) derives `Edge.confidence` from `(evidence_type, is_resolved)` inside `Edge.create` — at **construction** time — while ADR-0037 ruling 1 makes that same producer value **advisory**, `Edge.create` defaults it to `True`, and `ir.py`'s own field docstring states "the producer-time value … is ADVISORY; the finalize edge-resolution sub-step's verdict is what serializes." `_finalize_edge_resolution` duly flips the flag to `False` for every external dst and left the stale number behind, so unresolved calls shipped at the **resolved** base: `ast_call` at 0.85 where the verdict says 0.40, `ast_call_direct` at 0.85 where it says 0.50. `finalize.py`'s header even recorded "`Edge.confidence` untouched" as deliberate — a note written before confidence depended on the flag at all.
  - **23,043 edges across four repos and two languages**, measured with production's own `create_boundary_nodes` + resolution sub-step: caddy 6,167, mitmproxy 8,880, poetry 4,074, alertmanager 3,922. **Not a Go producer defect** — any producer that takes `Edge.create`'s default and whose dst resolves external hits it, which is why the correction lands at the one place that knows the verdict rather than at each analyzer's emit site. The filed remedy (pass `is_resolved=False` at the Go emit) would have patched a slice of a language-agnostic defect.
  - **THE FILED MECHANISM WAS A DIFFERENT, ALREADY-CLOSED DEFECT, AND BOTH OBVIOUS MEASUREMENTS OF IT ARE TAUTOLOGIES.** The item described a live taint FP channel — an edge carrying `is_resolved=True` at an external dst reaching the ungated bare-name fallback. Probing *after* finalize finds 0 of 125,007 such edges across 9 repos, because finalize defines that predicate away; probing *before* finalize also finds none, because the flag has not flipped yet. Neither zero is evidence. The defect is visible only by running the resolution verdict and then comparing confidence against it. Closing the item on the post-finalize null would have repeated the INV-nufob false-close exactly; two independent adversarial re-measurements inverted that proposed closure.
  - **Direction: strictly downward, and confined to unresolved edges.** Measured on caddy: 6,140 edges down, **0 up**, and **0 resolved edges changed at all**. `receiver_blind_magnets` bands at 0.80 and was the obvious exposure, but its contract requires `is_resolved` to be *true*, so this change cannot add or remove a magnet finding — structurally, not just observationally. Its 0-in-both-arms reading was an unexercised instrument and is reported as such rather than as evidence of safety. `slice`'s `min_confidence` defaults to 0.0, so no default view loses an edge.
  - **Two things deliberately not touched, each with its own test.** An explicit producer confidence keeps its value (ADR-0039: re-deriving it would replace a considered judgement with a table lookup). And `rank_score` is re-mirrored only while it still equals the old confidence — ruling 3 says it diverges once a producer relocates a ranking adjustment onto it, and clobbering that would silently discard the adjustment. An evidence type with no resolution split (`ast_import`, 0.95 either way) and one with no seed at all are both no-ops, pinned so the re-derivation cannot become an accidental rewrite.
- **A bare constructor name was emitted as receiver evidence without checking what the name is bound to, inventing filesystem reads that do not exist (INV-kipor).** `_external_constructor_module` answers "does this call construct a catalogued I/O object?" from two branches. The `ast.Attribute` branch (`socket.socket(...)`) verified its base against `module_imports` before trusting it; the `ast.Name` branch beside it verified **nothing** — it looked the identifier up in `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` and emitted the catalogued module as the receiver type of whatever the call returned. So on the shipping tree, with no configuration, `from decoy_lib import open` / `v = open(spec)` / `v.read()` reported an **`fs_read` boundary against `file.read`** for an object that is not a file. Wrong in the expensive direction for a security tool. Both callers of "does an import rebind this name?" now share `_import_binding_for`; a binding to anything other than the claimed module withholds the hint, the edge degrades to `external`, and every consumer refuses it as an untyped method call.
  - **The item's own filed remedy was self-defeating and is corrected rather than implemented.** It read *"a module hint MUST NOT bypass the `ambiguous_names` / `gate_named_entry` protection"*. But that gate vetoes every name in `ambiguous_names` **and** every `call_construct == "method"`, and **48 of python.yaml's 222 primitives (21.6%)** have an ambiguous short name — `socket.socket.send/recv/connect`, `file.read/write`, `asyncio.StreamReader.read`, `pathlib.Path.replace/rename`, all of `django.db.models`. Measured A/B across 8 repos: routing the hinted branch through that gate destroys **61.5%** of what `io-boundaries` prints by default (3812 → 1468; **87.2%** of the JSON `total_io_edges` field, 2689 → 345), gains **zero** at every repo and every primitive, and drives `subprocess` to 0 in 5 of 8 repos and every boundary type to 0 in caddy. **92.9%** of the loss is the `call_construct == "method"` clause, not the ambiguity clause the item's title names — and in Go it lands on *function*-kind entries with correct package hints (`io.ReadAll`, `os.ReadFile`, `os/exec.Command`), because `pkg.Func()` is a selector expression. No single clause is safe: the cheapest costs 37.8%.
  - **The two remedies differ on precision, not on strictness, and that is the whole argument.** `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` is `{"open": "file", "socket.socket": "socket.socket"}` and has never contained `Path` — the filing's worked example added it to demonstrate a *future* risk, so the FP it describes does not exist on the shipping tree in that form. Pinned as a test anyway, since it is the case every planned receiver-typing change walks into: across 5 Python repos there are **467 bare `Path(...)` call sites, all 467 import-bound to `pathlib`**. The filed remedy refuses 467/467; this one refuses 0/467 and refuses only the `from fastapi import Path` site. The gate could not tell them apart because it cannot see the binding — the lying edge and the honest one arrive byte-identical, same `call_construct`, same `resolution_quality: type_inferred`. The evidence is destroyed at the producer, which is why the fix is there and no consumer changes.
  - **`io_boundary._module_matches` was specified for the comparison and rejected on measurement.** It answers a deliberately permissive question — "could this *hint* refer to the catalogued module?" — and accepts an unqualified reference as a component suffix. It returns **True** for `mylib.pathlib.Path` against `pathlib.Path` and for `mypkg.file` against `file`, i.e. it trusts precisely the vendored bindings this guard exists to refuse. Binding identity is a different question from hint compatibility, so it gets an exact comparison; the reasoning is recorded on the predicate rather than left for the next reader to rediscover.
  - **Not a new rule — a site that skipped an existing one.** The identical test already lived inline in `_receiver_type_id_trustworthy` (WI-supat D3), which refuses to stamp a receiver-type id a same-name import could rebind. Two bare-name inferences asked the same question separately and one didn't ask at all; they now share one predicate.
  - **Direction: strictly stricter, and it can only withhold.** Census over 5 Python repos / 2,939 files: **330 bare `open(...)` sites, 0 import-shadowed**, so the corpus delta today is **0 boundaries in either direction** — with three positive controls proving the shadow detectors fire (a `Path` control returns 467/467 shadowed; the def/assign detectors return 2,538 and 337 hits for other names). The gain is one real false positive removed, demonstrable in a ten-line file, plus the future population above.
  - **The parity test enumerates the DATA TABLE, not the consumers.** For every bare key it derives a method from `python.yaml`, builds a shadowed fixture and an unshadowed twin, and asserts 0 and ≥1 through real `analyze_python` + `tag_io_boundaries` — so a future key auto-generates its own shadow fixture and fails unless the check holds. Every negative assertion is paired with a positive control, because INV-kipor's pre-existing regression test stayed green through the entire defect by hand-constructing its `external` dst instead of running the analyzer.
  - **Disclosed gaps:** `def`/`assign` shadowing is out of scope (import binding only, matching WI-supat D3's precedent; measured 0 for `open` today). `from io import open` is refused — a conservative false negative, since the claimed module `file` is synthetic and no import can equal it. Aliased imports (`from pathlib import Path as P`) miss the table by key and mint nothing. Relative imports, `importlib`, conditional and `TYPE_CHECKING` imports are unprobed. No other analyzer has an `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` analogue, so a cross-language parity test would assert nothing today and is not built.
- **SECURITY.md asserted the opposite of what hypergumbo reports about hypergumbo, and the "gate" it credited did not exist (WI-vanun).** The file claimed *"regressions land as taint-flow findings and fail the gate"*; no workflow referenced `verify-claims`, so nothing checked, and the text drifted until it published falsehoods. Measured by running the tool on its own source: **2 violated** (`runtime-cli-no-host-fs` 1178 flows, `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` 1101), **16 inconclusive**, **0 confirmed**. All 18 were presented as assurances; they are now presented as *claims under test* with that table at the top and the two false ones marked inline.
  - `runtime-cli-no-subprocess` is disproved **independently of the tool** — a canary caught `git` executed 28 times by `sketch`, 10 by `io-boundaries`, 6 by `survey`. Removing `git status` did not make it true: `rev-parse`, `rev-list` and `config --get remote.origin.url` remain (a 15-key config sweep fired no canary for those three, so they are documented rather than urgent). `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` says "all writes go through the safety_zones wrappers"; `cmd_sketch` holds a raw `shutil.rmtree` that bypasses them.
  - **"The load-bearing claims verify cleanly" was also false** — they are `inconclusive`. The sixteen inconclusives are not near-misses: this repo contains code in sixteen languages with no taint catalogue, so the analysis declines to confirm rather than reporting silence as safety. The section now also states the two limits bounding **every** `confirmed` verdict the tool emits anywhere — `pathlib.Path` method calls are invisible to the I/O catalogue, and one call edge is enough to pass the coverage check while the rest of a language's call structure stays unanalysable. **A `violated` verdict is trustworthy; a `confirmed` one means "found nothing, having looked at what it could see."**
  - **The gate now exists.** `scripts/check-self-claims` diffs live verdicts against a committed snapshot, wired into `full-suite.yml`'s existing self-tree job — which already runs a full self-analysis, so no new job and no per-PR cost. It is a **snapshot, not "all must confirm"**: with 2 violated and 16 inconclusive, an all-confirmed gate would fail forever and be switched off, which is how the text drifted originally. Drift fails in **either** direction, because a verdict silently improving is also how you stop noticing what the tool says, and the failure message requires updating the snapshot **and** the prose. Positive-controlled both ways — exit 0 on match, exit 1 on a perturbed snapshot naming the claim.
  - **A trap caught from the neighbouring job's own comment:** the first draft guarded on `command -v hypergumbo` and exited 0 when absent. That console script is **not on PATH in CI** (it ships in the meta-package, which CI never installs), so the gate would have passed silently forever in the only environment that matters. It now invokes `python -m hypergumbo_core`, and a missing import is exit 2, never exit 0.

### Added
- **A repo with Rust files is now told the rust-analyzer backend exists — and what enabling it costs.** The advisory names the capability and the trust implication in the same breath, and fires whether or not the binary is installed, because someone deciding *whether to install it* deserves the caveat first: *"Indexing runs this project's build scripts (build.rs) and proc macros as you — the same trust you extend by opening it in an editor. Enable it only for repositories you trust."*
  - **`rust-analyzer scip <workspace>` executes that workspace's `build.rs` and expands its proc macros**, as the invoking user, exit 0, silently — canary-verified. That is inherent to Cargo rather than a hypergumbo defect, but hypergumbo is routinely pointed at repositories the user has not read.
  - **A safe mode was attempted, FAILED, and the failure is recorded so it is not re-attempted blind.** `--config-path` with `cargo.buildScripts.enable=false` / `procMacro.enable=false` was accepted, reported no config errors, and ran the build script anyway — under **both** the nested and the `rust-analyzer.`-prefixed key spellings. The first reading that it worked was **Cargo caching the previous run's build-script output**; on three fresh never-built crates the canary fires in every arm (bare invocation, and both config spellings). So there is no known way to index a Cargo project without running its code, and the opt-in gate is load-bearing for **safety** — not only for the ~10x indexing cost its own docstring cites, which is the weaker reason. If revisited: sandbox the invocation, or generate a `rust-project.json` to bypass cargo (losing dependency resolution).
  - **Wired, not merely written:** the check is reached through `check_partial_install_warnings`, the entry point the CLI already calls, and a test asserts that — otherwise it is a WI-ratuv family member, correct and called by nobody. Verified live on a real crate via `hypergumbo survey`.
  - **A test-isolation trap fixed in passing.** The wiring test first used a hand-rolled linker-context stub carrying only what the aggregator read *when no linkers were registered*; it passed alone and failed twice under the full suite, where every package loads — first the JNI linker read `ctx.symbols`, then gRPC's requirement check globbed `ctx.repo_root`. Neighbouring tests pass `repo_root=None` and survive only because they **patch** `check_linker_requirements`. It now uses production's own `LinkerContext` with a real directory.

### Fixed
- **`verify-claims` reported code it never analysed as safe. A claim may now only be `confirmed` if the analysis could actually look.** A PHP file containing `file_put_contents("/tmp/out.txt", $_GET['payload'])` — a textbook leak — returned **`confirmed` at exit 0** for "untrusted input must not reach the filesystem", because PHP has no taint catalogue and *found no flows* was reported as *no flows exist*. A Kotlin socket-read-to-file did the same.
  - **The rule already existed for boundary claims** (WI-kajil / INV-bitig: a blind analysis yields `inconclusive`, never a silent confirm) — taint claims skipped it. INV-javam's signal *was* computed, and printed to stderr as a note asking the reader to apply the downgrade by hand (*"Treat 'confirmed' verdicts on these languages as inconclusive"*), which no CI gate and no hurried human does. The verdict word and the exit code both already existed; only the wiring was missing.
  - **One rule, one place.** `_require_coverage_to_confirm` runs at the exit of `verify_claims` over **every** constraint kind, rather than becoming a third copy of `if not coverage.complete` beside the two already in `verify_claim` — so a constraint kind added later cannot ship unable to tell "looked and found nothing" from "did not look". The blind-language half **reuses `compute_boundary_coverage`** instead of growing a second implementation of "did this language produce call edges", which is exactly why the boundary side never had this bug. A `violated` verdict is never downgraded: finding something is trustworthy regardless of coverage; it is the *clean* result that depends on having looked.
  - **The first version of this gate was wrong and the test suite caught it.** Blocking on `unsupported_taint_languages` directly meant a repo containing one YAML, JSON or markdown file — every real repo, including one that merely keeps its own claims file in-tree — returned `inconclusive` forever, making `confirmed` unreachable. **A verdict that is always inconclusive is exactly as useless as one that is always confirmed.** The gate now keys on languages that produced **call edges**: a language with no call structure has nothing for a taint flow to travel through, so its missing catalogue says nothing about the claim. Caught by `test_python_source_without_any_sink_confirms`; the hand-written control missed it because its fixture was a lone `.py` file.
  - **Verified live:** php leak → `inconclusive`/exit 2 (was `confirmed`); clean Python repo containing YAML → `confirmed` (not blanket-downgraded); encrypt-then-remove control → `confirmed`.
  - **KNOWN GAP, pinned as a strict `xfail` rather than left in a comment.** The check asks "did this language produce ANY call edges", and one edge is enough to look covered. Kotlin emits exactly one `calls` edge while every external method call — ~95% of its 93 catalogued sinks (WI-nasuf) — stays invisible, so it still confirms. Closing it needs a finer signal than "any" (e.g. the share of method-construct call edges the catalogue can match), which is a measurement exercise, not a predicate change.
  - **A test that passed for the wrong reason, fixed rather than left.** `test_verify_claims_notice_for_unsupported_taint_language` has no edges in its fixture, so it reaches `inconclusive` down the *vacuous-analysis* path — deleting the uncatalogued-language rule outright would leave it green. The new `test_uncatalogued_language_WITH_code_blocks_confirmation` puts a code-bearing uncatalogued language beside a covered one so only the rule under test can produce the verdict, and asserts the output names **which** language was blind. Two further fixtures that asserted a vacuous analysis could confirm a taint claim were corrected to describe a repo the analysis actually looked at.
- **SECURITY: a target repository could delete the user's files and execute code as the user. Both verified with canaries, both closed.** These are preconditions for anyone running hypergumbo on a repo they did not author.
  - **`cache-clear --repo <path>` deleted that path.** `cache_dir / repo` discards the left operand when `repo` is absolute and `..` traverses out; the only check downstream was `is_dir()` — existence, not containment. Since every other subcommand's `--repo`-shaped flag takes a path, `cache-clear --repo "$REPO"` in a script deleted the working tree, reporting it as `Deleted repo … (36.0 B)`. **The structural cause was that `safety_zones` wrappers declared a zone in prose and enforced nothing** — `_safety_zone_barrier()` was an empty function. All three destructive wrappers now refuse an out-of-zone path (resolving symlinks first, so `<cache>/evil -> /` cannot slip through), a **parity test** enumerates every `*_rmtree`/`*_unlink` and fails if one does not enforce, and the CLI refuses a path-shaped `--repo` at the boundary by enumerating what is permitted rather than blocklisting separator spellings.
  - **Analysing a repo executed code from that repo.** `hypergumbo io-boundaries <hostile-repo>` ran an attacker-supplied program **6 times**, as the invoking user, exit 0, silently — because runtime subcommands shelled out to `git` with cwd inside the target. Three independent vectors were demonstrated on `git status` alone: `core.fsmonitor`; a bare `.git/hooks/post-index-change`, which fires with **no config keys set at all** (so auditing `.git/config` is not a mitigation); and `filter.<driver>.clean` armed by an in-tree `.gitattributes`, measured to **survive `core.fsmonitor=false` + `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` + `core.attributesFile=/dev/null` + `--literal-pathspecs` + `--no-optional-locks` applied together**. The attacker names the filter driver, so no hardening list closes it — and a deny-list would rot silently as git gains exec-capable keys. **The fix is to not run `git status`.** Its result was never load-bearing: it fed only a cache-key/provenance digest, and the fingerprint now hashes the working tree directly, which is strictly more precise. Original exploit re-run against the fix: **6 executions → 0**.
    - **Priced before taking it:** 0.01s → 0.12s (pretix) / 1.7s (hypergumbo), once per analysis, against runs that take minutes. Two measured consequences: non-analysed files (`.txt`/`.md`) no longer invalidate the cache — correct, and **fewer** cold runs; and gitignored source files now count, which is **2 files on hypergumbo and 0 on pretix/kserve**. Two tests asserting the old over-invalidating behaviour through a `.txt` file were updated to use an analysed file, with the companion property (a non-analysed file must **not** bust the cache) pinned explicitly, so the security pin cannot be satisfied by a fingerprint that ignores the tree.
  - **Two gaps found and deliberately NOT fixed here, recorded rather than carried silently.** (1) `_SOURCE_EXTENSIONS` names no `.yaml`/`.toml`/`.json`/`.xml`/`Dockerfile` though hypergumbo analyses all of them, so editing a `pyproject.toml` serves a stale analysis — *under*-invalidation, pre-existing. Deriving the set from `taxonomy.get_language` was tried and **reverted**: `.json` is both an analysed language and the extension of hypergumbo's own output, so it re-pollutes the cache key every run. It needs artifact-name exclusion, which is a cache-semantics change that should not ride along with a security fix. (2) `rust-analyzer scip` executes a target project's `build.rs` (canary-verified); it is fixable — `--config-path` disabling `cargo.buildScripts` and `procMacro` produced an index with the canary silent — but is a separate change.
- **The sanitizer gate read the wrong slot for receiver evidence, so its permit branch was unreachable and the taint barrier arm was dead at every idiomatic call site.** `_register_sanitizer_callers` refuses an unresolved `call_construct == "method"` edge unless the callee is *qualified* — the INV-finoh gate, correct in purpose, since binding a bare `x.encrypt()` to `Fernet.encrypt` would falsely sanitize a real flow. The permit compared a sanitizer's `qualified_name` against `_extract_callee_name(dst)`, which reads the **name slot**. Production analyzers put an inferred receiver type in the **module slot**. So the comparison was `"cryptography.fernet.Fernet.encrypt" == "encrypt"` — false by construction for any method-shaped sanitizer, and **every sanitizer in the shipped catalogue is method-shaped**. The refusal was therefore unconditional in production. The fix consults the module slot via `_module_from_symbol_path`, which returns `""` for the `external` placeholder, so an untyped receiver still yields no candidate and is still refused: the guarantee is *preserved*, not widened. The whole qualified name must match — a typed receiver of the wrong type is evidence **against** this sanitizer, pinned by its own test (`AESGCM.encrypt` must not satisfy a registered `Fernet.encrypt`).
  - **The near-miss twin is the point.** `py:external:0-0:Fernet.encrypt:unresolved` (name slot) is a shape no analyzer emits for a method call; `py:cryptography.fernet.Fernet:0-0:encrypt:external_symbol` (module slot) is what they all emit. INV-finoh's own regression test — `test_qualified_sanitizer_still_registers`, whose docstring calls its fixture "the REAL qualified `Fernet.encrypt`" — uses the synthetic form, which is why a satisfied item shipped an unreachable branch. Its docstring's claimed "parity with `_lookup_named_entry`'s qualified-first branch" was aspirational: that function consults the module slot, and this one did not.
  - **The barrier arm now has a positive control, which is what S4 could not build.** Two probes differing only in receiver form, run through `verify-claims` with the shipped catalogue and no overrides: the module-qualified probe fires the arm (`barrier_lines=frozenset({7})` → `False`, `sanitized_flows: 1`, method `ddg`), and — before this fix — the **idiomatic** probe (`def handler(f: Fernet)`, `f.decrypt`, `f.encrypt`, `os.remove`) reported **`violated` at `precise` confidence about code that visibly encrypts**. Both dsts are byte-identical (`python:cryptography.fernet.Fernet:0-0:encrypt:external_symbol`); only `meta.call_construct` differed, and that alone flipped `sanitizer_lines` from `[7,7,7,7]` to `{}`. After the fix the idiomatic probe reports `confirmed`. **Caveat carried rather than buried:** the first probe calls instance methods on the class object and would raise at runtime — it exercises the machinery, not a shape anyone writes. The second is the real one.
  - **Direction is SUPPRESSIVE and was measured before shipping, not argued.** More sanitizers register → more barrier walks → more `False` → more `sanitized`, and since PR #214 a `False` drops the flow from the violation set. New in-repo instrument `scripts/measure-sanitizer-module-slot-ab.py` runs both arms over a corpus, disabling the module slot *only inside sanitizer registration* so source/sink matching is untouched and the A/B measures the change under test rather than a superset of it. Predictions were pre-registered before any number was read (`S5-PREREGISTRATION.md`) and all four held. **Corpus delta: zero, both directions** — pretix, hypergumbo, kserve, caddy show identical evidence counts in both arms. The load-bearing row is **pretix**, the corpus's only edge that reaches the gate at all (the bare `encrypt` WI-fasub found): it stays **refused in both arms**, which is live evidence that an untyped receiver still cannot sanitize. Everywhere else the zero is ABSENCE, not refusal — decomposed because those two readings carry identical evidence. Positive control differed as required (+4 call sites, +1 barrier walk), so the corpus zeros are controlled nulls.
  - **This is prevention, not improvement**, on the same terms WI-fasub earned. Value scales with a sanitizer catalogue and with receiver typing, neither of which today's corpus exercises: there are **zero** plaintext sanitizer call sites across it, and the module slot reaches taint only for Python (via `taint_refine`'s annotation pinning, gated `lang == "python"`) and Java (via #227's analyzer emit). Go, Rust and TypeScript sanitizers cannot present a module slot at all today, so this fix buys them nothing until their analyzers emit one.
- **A Python attribute chain rooted at a local emitted NO call edge at all, and depth was the only reason (INV-mumov).** `obj.bar()` has emitted `python:external:0-0:bar:unresolved` since WI-fuvuj; `event.organizer.issued_gift_cards.create(...)` emitted nothing. Measured on pretix: four calls to the same Django manager sink on adjacent lines, two emitting and two silent.
  - **Root cause, localized to a line — which the filing said it was not.** In `py.py`'s catch-all `elif isinstance(func, ast.Attribute)` branch, `_unwind_attribute_chain` *does* return a chain for a local-rooted receiver (the root is an `ast.Name`), but the emit is gated on `root_name in module_imports and len(chain_attrs) >= 2`. A chain rooted at a local fails the first conjunct and falls through with **no `else`**. The fix is that `else`, emitting `external`-module — the receiver's type is genuinely unknown, and synthesising `event.organizer.issued_gift_cards` as a module would assert one that does not exist. Placed so the class-rooted Django marker, which emits a module-qualified `django.db.models` edge earlier, keeps winning; pinned by its own test.
  - **It costs twice.** A function whose sink calls are all chain-shaped has no sink edge, is never considered, and its flow is never reported — a false negative. The same absence also keeps the call out of `callees_at`, so the §3a walk cannot ask whether the callee consumes the value and records an **escape** instead.
  - **Only the attribute-chain half is fixed; the builtin half is deliberately left alone, because the two have OPPOSITE directions.** Attribute-chain can miss a catalogued sink → recall up. Builtin bare `Name` (`print`, `len`, `isinstance`) mostly turns a call into an escape site, and emitting it only changes anything where a *terminating* summary exists — where it does, it produces more `False`, which since #214 earns `sanitized` and drops a flow → suppressive. Fixing both as one change would net two effects in opposite directions and report the sum.
  - **A claim of mine, corrected before it shipped:** this defect does **not** suppress `builtins.open` sink detection. `open()` emits and matches the catalogued `builtins.open` host_fs sink — `py.py` carries an `EXTERNAL_CONSTRUCTOR_TYPES` whitelist for it. Measured: `open` yes; `print`/`len`/`isinstance`/`eval`/`exec`/`input`/`sorted` no. The builtin gap is a small whitelist, not builtins in general.
  - **Direction measured on both signs, on kserve:** evidence rows 22 → 22, sanitized 0 → 0, violated verdicts 4 → 4, escape sites 3 → 3 — **zero change** — against **2,155 new call edges** at the analyzer level. Capability moved, adjudication did not (the WI-nonad shape). No suppression introduced, which is the half that mattered. The byte-identical result was checked against the stale-read tell rather than assumed: the cache key includes an analyzer-identity hash that genuinely differs between the arms (`dfb37ba5a9bfc9f9` vs `9fff4c1df3d299e5`), so both analysed fresh.
  - **Why land it with a measured zero:** the new edges are `logger.info`, `dict.get`, `str.replace`, `argparse.add_argument` — none catalogued sinks. The population that would move is Django-shaped, and pretix is not on this machine. A false-negative route closed before a repo that exercises it arrives, same reasoning as the WI-joluk gate. **The size of the population whose sink calls are ALL chain-shaped remains unmeasured** — this fix makes it measurable for the first time; it does not size it.
- **Java inferred the receiver's type, then discarded it before the taint catalogue could read it (INV-linub).** `File f = new File(p); f.createNewFile();` emitted `java:external:0-0:f.createNewFile:unresolved` — the dst named the *variable*. The type was not missing: `var_types` already infers it from four sources, and the analyzer already threads it out as `meta["receiver_type_hint"]` — but that hint's only consumers are the Tier-2 `inherited_calls` / `receiver_type_dispatch` linkers, which resolve **project-internal** symbols. Nothing carried it to the external surface. The typed-local branch now writes the imported fully-qualified type into the module slot (`java:java.io.File:0-0:createNewFile:unresolved`), and the edge reaches `java.io.File.createNewFile`.
  - **This is fatal rather than lossy, and the measurement is why it was picked first.** `io_boundary.gate_named_entry` opens with an unconditional `if call_construct == "method": return None`, so with no module hint a method call matches **nothing** — not a method-kind entry, not even a function-kind one. A nine-language measurement (one instrument, production's own `_match_propagation_entry`, per-language positive controls) found **not one language legitimately matching a method-kind sink**: java, scala, swift, python-two-step, rust and objc all *emit* and all die at that gate. **67 of java's 69 catalogued sinks are method-kind.** Emitting the edge was never the missing piece.
  - **Gated on the type being in `imports`, so the module slot only carries a path the file declares.** An unqualifiable type is left alone rather than written in bare: a simple name in the module slot asserts a module that does not exist and can collide with a catalogued entry of the same short name.
  - **It opened a phantom-barrier channel on the way in, and the guard is the interesting half.** Naming the type also *shortens* the callee from `w.doFinal` to `doFinal`. `_register_sanitizer_callers` matches sanitizers on the **short name** and never consults the module hint — so the shortening alone made `com.example.Widget.doFinal` bind the catalogued `javax.crypto.Cipher.doFinal`. Measured, not feared: the barrier really did register. A phantom barrier earns `sanitized`, and a sanitized flow is **dropped** from the claim's violation set (#214) — so the recall win would have been paid for with silently deleted findings. `make_unresolved_edge` grows a `call_construct` kwarg (additive, default `None`, no existing caller affected) and the typed-local branch declares `"method"`, which both taint gates already honour. It costs nothing on the sink side: `_lookup_named_entry` returns from its module-filter branch before `gate_named_entry` ever sees it.
  - **Direction, measured on both signs.** Sinks: java's two-step form moves from no-match to match. Sanitizers: **zero barriers register either way** — not for the wrong receiver type (the phantom, which is the point) and not for the *correct* one either, because the sanitizer path has no module filtering to distinguish them. That trade is deliberate and follows `_register_sanitizer_callers`'s own ruling: a missed barrier costs precision, an invented one costs a real finding.
  - **Scope, stated rather than implied.** This moves the **typed-local** form only. `new File(p).createNewFile()` (chained) still emits with `external` and does not match — a receiver-shape gap, not a typing one. A fully-qualified declaration (`java.io.File f = new java.io.File(p)`) also still misses, because the inferred type is then `java.io.File` while `imports` is keyed by simple name. Both are untouched and neither is claimed.
- **A first-party callee could match a SHIPPED stdlib summary and close a taint branch, because the INV-rozaj fix removed an accidental barrier (WI-zumud).** `_qualified_callee` normalises an in-repo callee's module slot with `_module_from_symbol_path`, which strips the source file extension — correct, and what makes a first-party callee catalogueable at all. But the extension had also been a barrier: `net/http.go.Get` could not equal stdlib `net/http.Get`, and stripping it means a Go repo with a root-level `net/http.go` now produces a key equal to a shipped summary. That is the WI-damir shape — a first-party symbol matching a catalogue primitive **by name alone** — reappearing in the §4 lookup rather than in sink matching, and WI-damir already settled the premise: resolution establishes *which in-repo symbol* is called and says nothing about whether that symbol *is* the catalogued primitive.
  - **The gate is deliberately NOT in `_qualified_callee`.** Gating key *construction* would break `test_in_repo_callee_key_has_no_file_extension`, which pins the INV-rozaj fix. The key **shape** was never the defect; handing a first-party key to a catalogue that describes stdlib and third-party surfaces was. The provenance decision therefore gets its own home, `_catalogue_key_for_edge`, and the key builder is untouched.
  - **The verdict is read from `Edge.is_resolved`, never from the dst suffix** (ADR-0037 ruling 4). WI-pubiv's boundary-id remap rewrites `:unresolved` to `:external_symbol` on the final graph, so a string check would read every unresolved edge as *resolved* and bypass the gate entirely — pinned by its own test.
  - **Direction, and it is the reason this was safe to land now.** A summary that says "terminates" lets the §3a walk CLOSE a branch, and a false close **removes** a real finding. Refusing to catalogue produces strictly *fewer* terminations, hence more unknowns and more surviving violations. The `.get("is_resolved", True)` default carries the same direction (L54 default-deny — enumerate the permitting case, which here is "the callee is known to be external"): an edge from an older artifact with no resolution verdict is treated as first-party and is not catalogued.
  - **Measured effect: zero, with a positive control so the zero is readable.** Across **55,779** taint call edges on caddy + pretix, **2,668** matched a shipped qualified key before the gate and **2,668** after — nothing was refused. The control is a synthetic resolved edge whose key is deliberately a real shipped key (`Array.prototype.filter`); it *is* refused, so the zero is absence rather than a broken predicate. The filing had sized this only on a hypergumbo self-survey (0 collisions over 72,822 call edges, 42 of 69 shipped keys shadowable in principle) and explicitly never on the cohort.
  - **Two claims in the item's own shipped red test are corrected in place.** It predicted "if a guard lands, this should FAIL and be rewritten to assert the guard" — it did not fail, and should not have, for the reason above. And its urgency argument rested on "§3a is confirm-only and **nothing acts on `False`**"; the first half still holds (`adjudicated = (walk is True)` collapses `False` and `None`), but the second half has been false since PR #214, where the same-function sanitizer path runs a *second* walk whose `False` earns `sanitized` and drops the flow from a claim's violation set. A `False` does act today, which is why this landed now rather than waiting on WI-kabif's removal authority.
- **A tracked heir suppressed a sibling escape on the same statement, so the §3a walk reported "everything accounted for" while a tainted value it had lost went unasked about (WI-votom).** One statement can do two things: `acc.append(x); y = x` both hands `x` to a receiver the intraprocedural walk cannot follow *and* derives a tracked `y`. `_ddg_taint_reaches` set `followed = True` from the second fact and `continue`d, skipping the `_use_site_terminates` escape check for the first — so the walk returned `False` (everything accounted for) where the honest answer was `None` (unknown). Following an heir accounts for the heir, not for the statement. Reproduced with a control: with the heir present the walk returns `False`; with the heir removed and nothing else changed it already returned `None`, so the tracked heir was the sole cause.
  - **This deletes findings, and that is why it mattered now rather than later.** Since the same-function sanitizer fix, a `False` from this walk earns the `sanitized` label, and a sanitized flow is dropped from a claim's violation set — so an unearned `False` silently removes a real security finding. The route was closed before a cohort member with a same-function sanitizer existed to be hurt by it.
  - **The guard enumerates the permitting cases, not the blocking ones.** Skipping the escape question is licensed only by (1) no call at the line at all — a pure rebinding, where the heir genuinely is the value's only exit — or (2) a catalogued callee that consumes and discards, which `_use_site_terminates` already decides. A callee shape nobody has modelled therefore reads as "ask the question" rather than "assume it is fine".
  - **Direction, stated because in this area it is not obvious:** the change produces strictly *fewer* `False`s, hence strictly *more* surviving violations. It cannot suppress a finding. Measured output change on the 7-repo cohort is zero — 0 walks return `False` and 0 barrier walks run there — so this is a closed route, not a demonstrated recall improvement.
  - **The item's other filed route is not a defect and was deliberately not built.** WI-votom filed a second hole (a seen-set cycle draining the frontier with nothing classified). Traced against the shipped function, that shape has a byte-identical execution profile to `test_barren_seed_poisons_an_otherwise_closed_walk`, whose contract is that `False` there is *correct and earned*; `followed` fires at every step, contradicting the filing. Building it would have reddened three deliberate guard tests.
  - Two docstring claims that had gone stale are corrected in the same change: §4b (`load_function_summaries`) **does** have a production caller and a live index of 38 terminating summaries — only §4a (`infer_summary`) has none — and `False` is no longer inert. §3a remains confirm-only; `inclusion_decided_by` does not move.
- **The mypy strict ratchet failed OPEN under `FORCE_COLOR`, reporting the 672-error surface as 0 and recommending the baseline update that would have disabled the gate permanently.** With `FORCE_COLOR` exported (agent harnesses set it), mypy colorizes stdout even into a pipe; `check-mypy-ratchet`'s trailing-`[code]` regex then matches nothing, so the live run printed `whole-tree strict errors = 0 (baseline 672, delta -672)`, exit 0, plus *"surface shrank — run `--update-baseline` to lock in the progress"*. A shrink-only gate's most seductive failure is a fabricated shrink: the same defect read as growth would merely block; read as progress it invites `--update-baseline` to write `total: 0` and end INV-zogud's enforcement with no visible event. The environment-dependence made it worse — CI runners don't set `FORCE_COLOR`, so `TestGateFiresForReal` (which requires the live ratchet to fail an all-zeros baseline) passed in CI while failing on any harness shell, and R19's "smart-test refuses to run pytest over a breach" never fired locally because the gate saw no breach.
  - **Three defences, stacked.** `_run_mypy` now passes `--no-color-output` and scrubs `FORCE_COLOR` / `MYPY_FORCE_COLOR` / `CLICOLOR_FORCE` from the child env, so the live path cannot produce colour. ANSI escapes are stripped before parsing, so a colorized `--input` capture is read rather than refused. And the structural fix is default-deny reconciliation (L54): a verdict is rendered only when the parsed count equals mypy's own `Found N errors` tally, or a `Success` line accompanies a parsed 0 — anything else (empty stdout from a crashed run, truncated output, a future format change) exits 2, the infrastructure code, in every mode **including `--update-baseline`**, which now refuses to write a baseline from output it cannot reconcile.
  - The reconciliation is what makes the fix durable rather than a patch for one env var: any *future* route to unparseable output — a mypy format change, a new colour knob, a partial write — fails loud as a broken instrument instead of open as a clean surface.
- **A sanitizer called in the same function as the taint source now neutralizes the flow, on the DDG pass (WI-fasub).** `plain = decrypt(t); safe = encrypt(plain); write(safe)` reported an unsanitized flow about code that visibly sanitizes — a false violation on an ordinary shape. The cause is the BFS barrier's seed exemption (`taint_label in node_sanitizers and node != seed_id`): the seed **must** stay reachable, since it is the taint origin, and that same exemption means a barrier called *from* the seed function is never consulted.
  - **The barrier helper cannot fix it, and that is structural rather than an oversight.** `_reachability_past_sanitizers` sees a call *graph*, where "handler calls encrypt" and "handler calls write" are two edges with no order between them — the graph is byte-identical whichever order the source has, so no work on call-graph reachability can distinguish encrypt-then-write from write-then-encrypt. Answering it needs statement ordering inside the seed function, which is `stmt_defuse`, and that reaches `propagate_taint_ddg` alone. **Consequence, published rather than left in a docstring: `propagate_taint_structural` can never honour this shape, for any language** — a permanent scope limit, and it applies to most of the catalogue because every language without a def/use extractor is served by that pass.
  - **Two walks, and both halves are load-bearing.** Marking a flow `sanitized` suppresses it from a claim's violation set, so it needs positive evidence exactly as §3a's removal arm does. `_ddg_taint_reaches` gained a `barrier_lines` parameter and is now run twice: unrestricted (is there a data route source→sink at all?) and with the sanitizer call sites as barriers (does any route *avoid* the barrier?). The label is earned only when the first says `True` and the second says `False` — exhausted with every step accounted for. The barrier walk's `None` never earns it, because "I lost track of it" is not "you protected it" (L58 applied to sanitization rather than to removal). Four soundness guards are mutation-tested and each reddens exactly its own test: accepting `None`, dropping the confirmed-dependence precondition, making the barrier flag an escape, and claiming protection whenever any barrier exists in the function.
  - **The sanitizer line index is collected by the shared registrar**, not re-derived at the point of use, so the INV-finoh resolution-/kind-aware gate governs both. Re-matching sanitizers at a second site is how this module previously acquired a private, ungated copy of that registration which silently deleted real flows. `_edge_call_sites` likewise folds the `meta.call_lines` parse into one home now that two consumers need it.
  - **Known residual, stated rather than left as folklore.** The barrier drops every heir at its line, which is wrong for a statement that *both* sanitizes and rebinds the raw value (`safe, leak = encrypt(plain), plain`). The conservative alternative — treat >1 heir as ambiguous — was rejected on measurement, not taste: Go's `ct, err := aead.Seal(...)` binds two names from **every** sanitizer call in that language, so it would forfeit the fix wherever a multiple-return language is involved, trading a rare false negative for a systematic false positive. The two cases are indistinguishable from the DDG alone (one statement row, `defines=(a, b) uses=(x,)` in both), so closing it needs argument-position information ADR-0017 §7b excludes. A sanitizer and sink on the *same* line (`write(encrypt(plain))`) likewise still reports unsanitized.
  - **Effect on the nine-repo cohort: zero, by construction, and established without running an A/B (L59).** The fix fires only where a sanitizer is *registered* against a function, so that gate was measured first with production's own registrar: **0 sanitizer call sites across 95,950 taint call edges** in all nine repos, with a synthetic `Fernet.encrypt` edge confirming the instrument fires (L9). Decomposed one level further, because a zero has two readings with identical evidence: **exactly 1 pre-gate name hit** exists cohort-wide (pretix, a bare `encrypt`), and it is refused correctly — `call_construct: method` on an unresolved edge is precisely what INV-finoh's gate exists to reject, since binding a bare `x.encrypt()` to `Fernet.encrypt` would falsely sanitize a real flow. So the cohort's zero is **absence, not refusal**, the repos simply do not call a catalogued crypto API. Report it as prevention, not an improvement; its value scales with the sanitizer catalogue rather than with today's output.
- **`verify-claims` publishes what its sanitizer catalogue can express (INV-karud clause b).** Clause (b) holds where it can be measured, and the population it can speak about is much smaller than the source/sink catalogue's: **12 entries across 5 languages, every one cryptographic** (`plaintext -> ciphertext`, `key_material -> derived_key`). That makes a repository-wide "0 sanitized flows" ambiguous in exactly the L58 way — nothing was protected, or nothing *could* be, because the claims' taint labels and the sanitizers' input labels are disjoint sets. A nine-repo cohort produced zero for the second reason. New `sanitizer_scope` block nested in `dataflow_coverage` (envelope schema `1.7 → 1.8`, additive, on **both** the JSON and text surfaces): the catalogue size, the languages carrying entries, the `input -> output` categories it can express, the `sanitizable_labels` a flow must carry to be reportable as sanitized at all, and `same_function_honoured_by`. That last is a declared constant with a test on it (R16) — it reads `["ddg"]`, and the test fails the moment the structural pass gains the ability the constant says it lacks. The categories and labels are **derived from the catalogue**, so extending it moves the disclosure automatically instead of requiring an edit by whoever is least likely to re-read it.

### Changed
- **The mypy strict ratchet now actually blocks — in CI and locally — and a violation forbids running tests, including one you inherited (WI-vilaf).** AGENTS.md and the Wave-5 notebooks described INV-zogud's ratchet as "BLOCKING, instrument-pinned to mypy 2.1.x". On the pipeline that gates merges it was neither, and nothing ran mypy locally at all.
  - **The gating pipeline is Woodpecker, and its ratchet step could not fail.** `auto-pr` polls exactly one CI context — `ci/woodpecker/pr/woodpecker` — and merges on it (observed live on PR #204); the GitHub Actions `--mode=strict` job and its `ci-complete` gate are never consulted by the merge path. That Woodpecker step ran `--mode=warning` **and** carried `failure: ignore`, so it could not gate by either route. **Three edits were required and two are silent alone:** with `failure: ignore` still set, `--mode=strict` exits 1 and the step passes anyway, so flipping the mode by itself reads as a fix and gates nothing. The pin moved `mypy~=2.1` → `mypy~=2.1.0` to match `ci.yml`, whose comment already explained why: a baseline's counts are only comparable against the mypy that produced them, and under the loose spec a fresh install can resolve 2.2/2.3, at which point the ratchet refuses to compare — silently, under the old warning mode.
  - **`smart-test` runs the ratchet first**, before slicing and before pytest. On a regression it prints the breach and exits **without running pytest**. The message closes the specific exit that let this happen: *you may not run tests until it is clean, even if you did not cause the regression.* A shrink-only ratchet whose breaches every agent in turn declines to own is exactly how the surface drifted **672 → 682 across sixty commits** touching 24 source files. Exit 2 (infrastructure — mypy missing, no source roots) warns and continues, per the ratchet's own documented contract that an infra failure is not a type regression.
  - **Verified end to end, not just asserted.** With the baseline doctored to zeros, `smart-test` exits 1, prints the block banner, and produces **zero** test-result lines — pytest never runs. The two silent-failure assertions are mutation-tested: restoring `failure: ignore` fails exactly `test_mypy_step_does_not_ignore_failure`, and loosening the pin fails exactly the pin test. Two earlier drafts of the "no invocation uses warning mode" test were wrong in opposite directions and are recorded in place — one grepped raw text and tripped on the prose *explaining* why warning mode is wrong; the next dropped comments and tripped on `check-schema-coverage --mode=warning`, a different tool that is legitimately advisory, which would have forbidden correct configuration.
  - **Honest limit:** the gate lives in `smart-test`, which is what the `pytest` alias invokes, so it covers the normal path. A direct `python -m pytest` bypasses it; there the enforcement is the banner text, not a mechanism.
- **`verify-claims` publishes its data-flow coverage scope, and a flow's adjudication label stopped depending on which *other* languages the repo contains (INV-karud clause a3).** The clause requires that a reader can tell data-flow-adjudicated flows from call-reachability-only ones *from the emitted record*, and that "the scope of data-flow coverage may not be left to assumption". Neither half held.
  - **The label was not a property of the flow.** `cmd_verify_claims` builds one DDG for the whole repo, then dispatches per language on `if ddg_edges:` — a repo-global truthiness test — so *every* language went through `propagate_taint_ddg` whenever *any* language produced DDG edges. A JavaScript function is never in `ddg_symbols` (JavaScript has no def/use extractor), so the walk could not run and the finding still came out `ddg_mixed`, whose documented meaning is "the walk ran and did not confirm". **Reproduced end to end on two repos whose JavaScript is byte-identical and which differ by one unrelated `util.py`: `structural` without it, `ddg_mixed` with it.** The label is now chosen on whether the DDG actually covered the source function, which is what `TaintFlowFinding.analysis_method`'s own docstring has always said it meant. No existing test moved — every one of the five that assert `ddg_mixed` already places the source function inside `ddg_symbols`, which is why nothing caught this.
  - **New `dataflow_coverage` block** (envelope schema `1.6 → 1.7`, additive; rendered on the **text** surface too, because a disclosure that exists only under `--json` is half shipped — WI-bifob's exclusion bucket reached the dataclass and never the text renderer). One row per analyzed language with a taint catalog, carrying the catalog it would serve and four independent capability bits — `cfg_mapping`, `atomic_statement`, `def_use_extractor`, `ddg_spec` — with `dataflow_capable` their conjunction and `blockers` naming the missing ones. **Four bits rather than one flag because each is individually sufficient to keep the machinery silently inert:** Rust shipped an extractor in March and emitted zero DDG edges for months while failing three at once, and Java ships `cfg_nodes/java.yaml` while declaring no `atomic_statement` and registering no extractor — a single "supported?" boolean would read `yes` for a language that can adjudicate nothing and would never have said which of the four to fix.
  - **Two constants state what no per-run measurement can.** `inclusion_decided_by: "call_graph_reachability"` — §3a is confirm-only, so every reported flow *including every `ddg` one* was included by call-graph BFS; reading `analysis_method == "ddg"` as "inclusion decided by data flow" is the misreading INV-sadah exists for, made twice in this repository's history. Emitting it as data gives that prose claim the executable re-evaluation trigger R16 requires. And `coverage_granularity: "language"` — capability is **not** a per-function completeness claim, because `cfg_nodes/go.yaml` self-documents that `if err := do(); err != nil` initializers are invisible to def/use, so a capable language still holds functions the analysis cannot see into (the finer signal is WI-joluk's).
  - **Measured with production loaders, not re-derived (L53):** 553 sources / **1062 sinks** / 12 sanitizers across 15 languages; **274 sinks (25.8%) sit in a data-flow-capable language** (go, python, rust, typescript). The campaign plan recorded 244/23% — rust's 30 joined when WI-tohuk landed `atomic_statement`, which is precisely the kind of drift a computed table removes. TypeScript is fully capable and has **zero** sinks, so the 274 come from three languages; Java's 69 are the largest block behind a language that looks supported.
  - **The def/use force-import list moved** from `cli._build_ddg_for_verify_claims` to `dataflow_scope.ensure_def_use_extractors_registered`, because both registries are populated as an *import side effect* and the coverage table needs them at the same moment — a scope table computed against an empty registry reports every language incapable without erroring. One home, not two; the existing wiring gate follows it. The non-vacuity floor for exactly that failure is mutation-tested: deleting the registration call turns `go` into `def_use_extractor: false` and the test fires.
- **The mypy strict ratchet was breached by +10 and is restored to 672 (INV-zogud).** Measured while pre-flighting the change above: dev at `ffbf8a5b93` produced **682** whole-tree strict errors against a committed shrink-only baseline of **672** — a baseline that `ci-complete` treats as a merge gate. Verified as real drift rather than an instrument delta by running the ratchet at the baseline commit itself (`d229793409`), which reproduced **672 exactly**, and by confirming the local mypy is the pinned 2.1.0 the baseline records. **Nine of the ten are from this campaign's own taint PRs**, so a shrink-only gate absorbed ten new errors across sixty commits without anything stopping them; how they got past a blocking gate is filed separately rather than guessed at here.
  - **One of the ten was actively dangerous.** `propagate_taint_ddg` binds `line` to an `int` in its statement loop and rebinds it to `edge.get("line")` (`Any | None`) sixty lines later, so mypy inferred `int` and reported the guard `isinstance(line, int) and line not in sites` as **"Left operand of 'and' is always true"** — while the comment three lines above it says exactly why that guard exists ("a non-int member would reach the `sink_line > source_line` comparison below as a TypeError"). Draining that `redundant-expr` the obvious way deletes a live guard in security-relevant code, which is the shape that once took the whole-tree ratchet to a *clean* 832 → 789 on a rewrite deleting 43 live guards. The same function reused `site` for both an `int` call-line and a `(dst, sink)` tuple, which made its deduplication membership test read as a non-overlapping — i.e. dead — container check. Both fixed by giving the second binding its own name (`edge_line`, `sink_site`); no runtime behaviour changes.
  - **Three were a real signature mismatch, not a nit.** `_ddg_taint_reaches` and `_use_site_terminates` declared `dict[..., frozenset[str]]` for parameters every production caller supplies as `defaultdict(set)`. `dict` is **invariant** in its value type, so that is not a stylistic difference — it is three type errors at the one call site that matters. Both now declare `Mapping[..., AbstractSet[str]]`, which is what the functions actually require (iterate and membership-test, never mutate).
  - The remaining two: `_HANDLERS` in `go_def_use` is typed as the `(node, source) -> DefUseResult` callable it holds rather than `Any` — the bare `Any` was silencing the only place the dispatch contract could be checked — and `ddg_build`'s `get_language` call carries a documented cast. `fingerprint.py` makes the identical `get_language` call and carries the identical error *in the accepted baseline*; it is deliberately left alone so this diff stays scoped to its own drift. The baseline file is **not** raised: a shrink-only baseline may only ratchet down, and raising it to accept a breach ratifies the drift.
- **`compact`'s default selection is now the connected core, and its `--max-symbols` budget is containment-monotone (WI-zulij).** Two changes that only make sense together, both measured on the eight 2026-07-17 bakeoff maps.
  - **The default flips back to connectivity-aware.** This reverses the 2026-06-10 D12 reading ("centrality-ranked everywhere") **for `compact` only**, on evidence rather than taste: compact's output is consumed as a *graph* — it emits induced-subgraph edges, and its one in-tree diagnostic, `hypergumbo_diag.analyze_compact`, scores it on **connected components** — but a centrality-ranked prefix of a graph is frequently not connected. Measured: centrality-ranked crops carried **41.9% isolated nodes at a 25-symbol budget** and got *worse* as the budget grew (12.6 → 23.6 → 42.1 components at K=25/100/400), where the connected core stays flat (3.5 → 4.0 → 3.2) with isolated nodes at 8.5% → 2.5% → **0.3%**. `sketch` keeps centrality-ranked — it is a reading surface, not a graph. **The two surfaces therefore disagree on their top symbols by design**, which dissolves WI-zulij's original complaint rather than resolving it. `--connectivity` stays accepted as an explicit no-op (it shipped in v7.0.0); `--no-connectivity` opts into centrality-ranked selection matching the sketch.
  - **Connectivity mode gained the containment property WI-vofud thought it could not have.** `nodes(K1) ⊆ nodes(K2)` went from **55/120 budget pairs to 120/120**, pretix included. WI-vofud's recorded justification — *"bridge expansion is not a ranked prefix, so budget-containment was never that mode's contract"* — is **false**, and it had been quoted as settled for two sessions. The greedy loop never reads the budget (`max_additional` appears only in its `while` guard), so bridge expansion was *already* prefix-nested; with any budget-independent seed set, containment measured 120/120 unchanged code. What broke it was the **en-bloc, budget-sized seed preload**: admitting every seed before the first pick handed the scorer a different union-find state at every budget, producing a different *sequence* of picks rather than a longer one. Porting WI-vofud's monotone seed *quota* moves 65 violations to 66 — the seed **order** was never the problem, the preload was.
  - **The fix is an interleave, not a cap.** Seeds are metered into one budget-independent stream — fixed seed order (entrypoints by confidence, then cross-cutting endpoints by edge count) alternating 1:2 with greedy bridge picks — so the budget becomes pure truncation and a smaller budget's node list is an ordered *prefix* of a larger one's. The old adaptive entrypoint cap (1/3 vs 1/2, switching regimes on `len(eps) > max_symbols`) is deleted: alternation guarantees bridges a slot rather than leaving them the remainder, which serves the keycloak case the cap was written for more directly. **The 1:2 ratio is tuned, not arbitrary** — at 1:1 a seed can be admitted and then have both its neighbours out-scored globally, stranding it (measured 14 singletons per 100 nodes on the keycloak-shaped fixture); 1:2 drops that to 1 and 1:3/1:4 buy nothing further. Any *fixed* ratio preserves containment, so the ratio is a pure quality knob. Determinism (WI-nivuj) is untouched: the scorer, the sorted-frontier iteration and the strict `>` tie-break are reused verbatim.
  - **Cost, stated plainly.** Fragmentation is unchanged-to-better versus the old connectivity policy (components 3.5→3.5, 4.6→4.0, 3.4→3.2), and entrypoint reachability *improves* markedly at small budgets (62.0% → **80.0%** at K=25). The one real cost is a boundary artifact: truncating a stream can land on a seed, so the **final emitted node** may have no edge in the crop — measured as exactly one singleton at index 99 of 100. It cannot be special-cased away, because "don't take a seed in the last slot" is a budget-*dependent* decision and reintroducing budget-dependence is precisely what broke containment. The keycloak test's `singletons == 0` assertion is relaxed to "at most the final emitted node", with that reasoning recorded in place and a positional assertion so a singleton anywhere *else* still fails.
  - **Note on the preceding entry:** the sketch/compact filter unification below narrowed compact's default population, which cost connectivity nothing but did raise centrality-mode fragmentation (isolated 8.2% → 17.6% at K=400) by dropping `file`-kind hub nodes. With this flip that narrowing lands on `--no-connectivity`, whose stated job is now precisely *"matching the sketch"* — so dropping the same nodes sketch drops is correct for that mode rather than a regression in it. Every default consumer is strictly better off (isolated 17.6% → 0.3% at K=400).
- **`sketch` and `compact` now rank the same population — "what counts as a key symbol" has one home instead of living inside one function's body (WI-zulij).** Both surfaces advertise "the important symbols" and both already ranked with `compute_dampened_centrality` (the 2026-07-09 default flip settled the ranking *function*), yet their top-10 lists disagreed. The cause was never the ranking: sketch filtered to key declaration kinds over test-source-free edges, and compact's default `select_by_coverage` filtered **nothing at all**. The policy that made sketch's list good was a `frozenset` declared *inside* `sketch._format_symbols` with its four clauses as an inline comprehension beneath it, reachable by nothing else in the codebase — so there was no shared definition to drift from, only one function's private opinion. `KEY_SYMBOL_KINDS`, `is_key_symbol`, `key_symbols` and `production_edges` now live in `selection/filters.py`, the module whose own header already named sketch, compact and tiered JSON as its consumers.
  - **Measured on the eight 2026-07-17 bakeoff maps, through the production entry points on both sides** (`sketch._format_symbols` capturing its own `selected_symbols_out`, and `format_compact_behavior_map`): top-10 agreement **31.2% → 55.0%**, and at the shipped default budget of 100, **42.8% → 73.0%**. Eight of eight repos improved or held at K=10 and all eight improved at K=100 — SwiftyJSON 16.7% → 100%, express 18% → 74.7%, sherpa-csharp 25% → 76.1%. The 31.2% before-figure independently reproduces the 32% the item recorded.
  - **The edge filter turned out to be the dominant clause, which is the opposite of the obvious guess.** Measured per clause against sketch's ranked top-10: the symbol filter alone reaches 7.1/10, the edge filter alone 8.4/10, both together 10/10 on all eight maps. Compact had been crediting symbols for being called by their own test suites. This is why both clauses are applied rather than only the visible one, and why a denylist-only variant (which would have subsumed WI-fadab) was rejected — it reached 9.4/10 and missed two repos outright.
  - **Scope held deliberately.** `--connectivity` is exempt and keeps the broader population: its contract is bridging disconnected components, and a `file` node is frequently the only thing joining two islands, so narrowing it would remove the bridges the mode exists to find (pinned by an over-reach guard test). Narrowing happens before seed extraction rather than at the ranking call, so it also constrains which symbols can be force-included — which is the point, since a seed that is not a key symbol is precisely the noise leading compact's output today (sherpa-csharp's top five emitted nodes were all `run*.sh` file nodes; zoxide led with a shell completion and `install.sh`). The `variable` kind sits in **both** `KEY_SYMBOL_KINDS` and `EXCLUDED_KINDS` and stays in both: a Terraform variable is a real declaration surface, a CSS custom property is zero-edge noise, and which set a consumer applies is a policy choice about its own output rather than a fact about the kind.
  - **Residual, stated rather than implied:** agreement is 55%, not 100%. With the population shared, the ranked orders match exactly; what remains is that the two use different *selection policies* over that shared ranking — compact reserves half its budget for entrypoint and cross-cutting seeds, sketch uses two-phase coverage-first selection with per-file caps and language-proportional allocation. That is a separate design difference, not a filter divergence, and it is what WI-zulij still tracks.
  - **Testing.** Three red-first property tests pin the shared population (non-key kinds excluded, test-file symbols excluded, test-sourced edges not credited) plus an over-reach guard that fails if connectivity mode is narrowed, and a direct suite for the extracted policy. Two of the three property tests were **vacuous on first draft** and are documented as such in place: one passed because the coverage stop had truncated the selection to a single symbol, and the other because its fixture used `calls` — a member of `CROSS_CUTTING_EDGE_TYPES`, so every endpoint became a seed candidate and the ranking under test was never exercised. Both now carry explicit non-vacuity assertions. The sketch extraction is behavior-preserving, pinned by its existing 526 tests passing unchanged.

- **`auto-pr`'s Scenario B gate could not see an uncomputed mergeability, so it closed green PRs (INV-rahib Surface 1).** `_pre_scenario_b_gate` exists to stop the timeout and hung-run paths from closing-and-repushing a PR that already merged or is about to. Its second sub-case read `mergeable` through the shared `json_field`, which prints the empty string for a JSON `null` **and** for a missing key — so `mergeable: null` was indistinguishable from `mergeable: false`, and the gate silently ordered the close. GitHub computes mergeability *asynchronously* and reports `null` until that background job finishes: `null` is the expected value in the seconds after a push, which is exactly when this gate fires. **The sub-case was therefore inert on the live backend for as long as it existed**, and the PR-churn cycle it was built to prevent is the one WI-buniz was a downstream consequence of.
  - **New `json_bool_state` reads a JSON boolean as a tri-state** — `true｜false｜null｜absent｜other｜unreadable` — because "the forge has not computed this yet", "the forge says no" and "we could not parse the response" are three different facts that a caller is the only layer able to act on. `json_field` stays the string reader. Unknown mergeability now **re-queries `/pulls/<n>` once** (GitHub's first read is frequently what *schedules* the computation, so the second usually returns a real boolean) and, if still unknown, **defers to a green CI** rather than destroying a PR on an unread field. `false`, `absent` and `unreadable` still fall through to the close.
  - **Measured in both directions against the pre-fix library, not just asserted after** — the same payload set through the production function on both arms: `null` **1 → 0**, `true` 0 → 0, `false` **1 → 1**. Exactly one cell moves, and `false` holding is the control that matters: the fix is not a blanket "never close".
  - **Why the existing 19 tests did not catch it (L13).** They fed the gate `{"mergeable": true}` and `{"mergeable": false}` only — never `null`, never absent — while one test's docstring claimed it covered "false (or absent)". Worse, a structural test asserted the **literal source text** `mergeable=$(echo "$API_RESPONSE" | json_field "mergeable")` appears in the gate body: the exact line the fix had to change, so the test broke precisely when the bug was fixed and pointed the fixer at keeping it. It is now loosened to the dependency it was really guarding. The harness gained a *sequence* of API responses, because a stub returning one payload forever cannot distinguish "the re-query happened" from "no re-query happened".
  - **`ci-debug pr-status` now prints `Mergeable:`,** as a tri-state. This field is what the gate branches on, and until now it was unobservable through any approved script — `pr-status` read only state/merged/title/head.sha/head.ref/base.ref — which is why the defect went unverified against the real backend for as long as it did. A GitHub-shaped fixture pins that `null` renders as `null` and never as blank or `false`.
  - **Scoped honestly:** five other call sites read a boolean through `json_field`, all of them `merged`, which both forge APIs document as a non-null boolean — so they are not currently defective. The class is that the helper could not express absent/null/false, not that six sites were broken.
- **INV-rahib's invariant was unsatisfiable as written, and is refined to a declared closed set (owner ruling 2026-08-03).** The statement demanded convergence to "merged or explicit failure" with "no third state". Measured: auto-pr has **20 distinct terminal states**, and three are deliberate non-merge outcomes — `queued_vpr` (four sites, exit 0, and the vPR queue was re-affirmed the day before under WI-hajif Ruling 2), `governance_pending` (a PR left open for a human by design) and `noop_empty_queue`. No conforming implementation could satisfy the literal wording, so it was a defect in the invariant rather than in the code. The refined property: every run records a state drawn from a **declared** vocabulary, bucketed as merged / explicit failure / deliberate-non-merge, with `unknown` — the initializer, written in exactly one place — as the violation signal. Two tests enforce it in both directions: no emitted state may be undeclared, and no declared state may be unreachable (a vocabulary is allowed to be neither incomplete nor aspirational), plus a non-vacuity floor so a regex that stopped matching cannot pass the subset check trivially.
- **A convergence ledger, because the invariant had no instrument.** `.git/AUTOPR_LAST_RESULT.json` is written `open(..., "w")` — one object, overwritten every run — which is correct for what it is (WI-miriz's "did my backgrounded run succeed?" sentinel, with the overwrite pinned by its own test). But convergence is a property of the *sequence* of runs, so the item's 2026-06-17 validation criterion — "observe auto-pr converge during a real concurrent nightly" — had nothing to evaluate and went unexamined across **579 commits**. Rather than overload the sentinel with a second meaning, auto-pr now also appends one compact JSON line per invocation to `.git/AUTOPR_HISTORY.jsonl`. Append-only, best-effort by construction: an observability artifact must never turn a successful run into a failed one, pinned by a test that points it at an unwritable path.

- **`verify-claims` scopes taint flows to PRODUCTION by default, and discloses what it set aside (WI-bifob).** A claim verdict is a **disjunction** over its flows, so one flow from test code held an entire claim at `violated` and no amount of precision work elsewhere could move it. Measured on the 9-repo cohort: **2 of 18 violated claims rested entirely on a single test-sourced flow each** — `pretix/host-secret-no-network` on a `tests/e2e/conftest.py` reading an env var into `urlopen`, and `alertmanager/untrusted-input-no-network` on a `tls_transport_test.go` dialling TLS. Neither is a statement about the shipped product. Flows whose **source** is test, fixture or migration code are now excluded from the verdict; `--include-non-production-sources` restores the old behavior, so this is a default rather than a filter.
  - **The exclusions are disclosed, not dropped.** Each verdict carries an `excluded_flows` bucket keyed by reason (`test_sourced` / `migration_sourced`), reported on the **confirmed** path as well as the violated one — that is where a silent filter misleads most, because the claim reads clean and the reader has no way to learn flows existed and were set aside by a policy they did not choose. Follows the D7 shape (a production default plus labelled disclosure buckets) already used for dead-code seeds. Envelope schema `1.1 → 1.2`, additive.
  - **Measured, against a prediction registered before the run.** Which claims flip and how many: **exactly as predicted** (those two, no wrong-direction flips). Volume: predicted in the wrong unit and worth recording as such — evidence rows fell **354 → 270 (−84)** while the underlying exclusions were **692 flow-claim pairs** (675 test-sourced, 17 migration-sourced). The gap is the evidence cap: `pretix/untrusted-input-no-database` went **748 → 140 flows (−81%)** yet still displays 100 rows, because removing flows from an over-cap claim changes *which* rows are shown rather than how many. A row-level view understates this change by an order of magnitude on capped claims.
  - **`is_migration_file` is a new predicate with a DECLARED scope**, deliberately not folded into `is_test_file`: a migration is production code that ships and runs once at deploy, not test scaffolding. What the two share is only that neither describes the running application. It matches `**/migrations/` (Django), `db/migrate/` (Rails), `alembic/versions/`, `db/migration/` (Flyway) — and `versions/` **only** under `alembic/`, `migrate`/`migration` **only** under `db/`, because claiming words that common outright would silently drop real production flows from a security verdict. Anything else is an acknowledged gap, not an implied guarantee. Measured need: the existing `is_test_file` caught 84 of the 98 non-production rows and missed exactly the 14 Django migrations.
  - **Scope-axis caveat, stated because the percentages invite the wrong reading.** This is not a cohort-wide property: 74 of the 98 non-production rows are caddy (64 from a *single* file, `caddytest/integration/reverseproxy_test.go`), with pretix 15, alertmanager 7, kserve 2. Treat the effect as "one Go project's integration suite plus a Django project's migrations", not as a law about repositories.
  - **The path slot is parsed RIGHT-ANCHORED**, since it is the one colon-tolerant slot in the id grammar (ADR-0036 D1a) — `dart:dart:io:0-0:module:module` has path `dart:io`. `ir._extract_path_slot` returns `parts[1]` and is wrong for exactly this case, while `ir._parse_dangling_id` twelve lines below it is correct and documents the hazard. Rather than add a seventh naive copy, this one is correct and pinned by a test; the six existing homes and the two defective ones are filed (WI-ribuz, WI-guror, WI-nukas, WI-dosir).

- **Taint findings now report WHICH boundary a flow entered through, without relabelling anything (WI-vazal).** `AUTO_SOURCE_LABEL_MAP` collapses three io_primitives boundaries — `net_recv`, `ipc_recv` and `db_read` — into the single label `untrusted_input`, and discarded which one it was at the moment of derivation. So "a request body reached the database" and "a row read from the database reached the database" were the same fact downstream, and on an ORM-backed application the second dominates: **all 140 flows** on pretix's largest violated claim are database-read-to-database-write. `TaintSource` and `TaintFlowFinding` now carry `source_boundary`, each verdict carries a `flow_origins` breakdown, and each evidence row carries the boundary for drill-down.
