Envelope schema `1.2 → 1.3`.
  - **The label is deliberately untouched, and that is the whole design.** The alternative considered was giving database reads their own label — which would have been a **silent semantic change to every claim already written against `untrusted_input`**, since those flows would simply stop matching. Measured: relabelling would have flipped **3 of 16** violated claims to `confirmed` (all pretix — `untrusted-input-no-database`, `-no-host-fs`, `-no-subprocess`) with nobody told. Reporting the split instead changes **zero** verdicts and **zero** evidence rows (270 → 270 on the cohort), which the arm's instrument asserts in both directions before measuring: a run where `db_read` flows quietly stopped matching would produce a *cleaner-looking* number while measuring the option that was declined. A test pins that a `db_read`-sourced flow still matches an `untrusted_input` claim.
  - **No new vocabulary was minted.** The distinction already existed as `io_boundary`'s controlled categories and was being thrown away, so the fix carries the existing axis through rather than promoting a new `db_at_rest`/`external` one (the ADR-0024 / R1 "the fact already has a home" reversal, now at eight instances). `declared` is the bucket for YAML-declared sources (crypto, key_material, project-local catalogs), which have no boundary.
  - **This measurement corrected two of my own earlier claims, and the feature is what caught them.** Both "dropping ORM reads changes zero verdicts" and its later "correction" (that 7 non-ORM flows remained) were produced by a hand-rolled ORM predicate whose read-set omitted `exclude`, `none`, `annotate` and `select_related` — all genuine Django reads. Production classifies 100 of 100 shown rows as `db_read` where the ad-hoc predicate found 93. **Name the instrument, then verify it is the one production runs** (L53): the whole point of carrying `source_boundary` is that consumers stop re-deriving this classification badly.

### Added
- **ADR-0017 §4 function summaries are wired into the §3a walk, with python and go catalogues — and the measurement says they cannot unlock refutation on this cohort.** §3a's own step 3 says *"at call sites, apply function summaries (§4)"*, and that is the branch where a tainted value is used at a line that defines nothing: it may have been **consumed** (`fmt.Printf(cwd)` returns a byte count) or **escaped into a receiver** (`lst.append(x)`), and only the first lets the walk close a branch. `load_function_summaries` / `infer_summary` had zero production callers; the walk now consults the catalogue at exactly that branch.
  - **An empty catalogue is a strict no-op, by design.** `get_default_summary` returns `param_to_return = {0..9: True}` — *everything propagates* — so using it would make an empty catalogue **change** behaviour, with every callee reading as passing taint on. An uncatalogued callee instead reads as unknown, so a catalogue covering nothing reproduces the pre-§4 output exactly and every behaviour change is attributable to an entry somebody wrote. Pinned by its own test.
  - **Qualified names only — the safety property.** `load_function_summaries` also indexes every entry under its bare last component; roughly two fifths of the loaded index are such aliases, including `log`, `map`, `filter` and `parse` (the exact count is deliberately not pinned in prose — see the INV-rozaj entry). A short-name match would let `audit.log(secret)` resolve to `console.log` and read as terminating. A false "terminates" **removes a real security finding**, so the alias index is never consulted. Every clause of the terminating predicate is a conjunction, for the same asymmetry: a wrong "terminates" costs recall silently, a wrong "propagates" only leaves an unknown unknown.
  - **The catalogues are enumeration-derived, not a stdlib survey.** The two shipped catalogues were `rust` and `typescript` — the two languages that had **no working DDG**, one of them with zero sinks — while python and go, the only two that do, had none. Rather than guess at stdlib breadth, the walk was instrumented on the cohort to enumerate the callees it actually consults at escape sites: **264 escape sites, 47 distinct callee names**. `go_stdlib.yaml` and `python_stdlib.yaml` name **all 18 of those a qualified-name catalogue can express**; the remaining 29 are `external.*` (callee module unresolved) or first-party in-repo, neither of which YAML can address.
  - **Measured effect: 25 of 264 escape sites close (9.5%), and ZERO flows change.** Cohort A/B on the same binary with the catalogue live vs forced empty: **16 confirmed / 40 unknown / 0 refuted, identical in both arms.** The reason is structural rather than a coverage shortfall — a walk is unknown if *any* branch escapes, so a flow becomes refutable only when *every* escape on it closes. 5 of 37 unknown walks did close a site; **every one still had another open**. What holds them open: **81 no-call-at-line**, 40 catalogued-but-propagates, 21 unresolved module, 12 uncatalogued. And at the margin — the 14 walks held open by exactly ONE site — the single blocker is **`no call at the line` in 10 of 14**.
  - **So the binding constraint on §3a refutation is not the catalogue.** It is that 40.5% of escape sites are not calls at all: the value went into a field write, a container subscript or a closure capture. That is ADR-0017 §7a field-sensitivity / alias analysis, which **§7b explicitly excludes**. Filing this rather than papering over it: **INV-sadah does not close and WI-kabif's removal authority stays blocked**, now for a reason that has been measured instead of assumed. WI-kabif's filed list of four prerequisites was already six; this makes seven.

### Fixed
- **Taint matching could index a callee against another language's catalogue, and `_extract_callee_language` — written to prevent exactly that — had zero production callers.** `cmd_verify_claims` selects a language's edges with `src.startswith(lang:)` **OR** `dst.startswith(lang:)`. The `OR` is correct and stays: the propagation BFS needs both endpoints to walk a cross-language call. But it means a bridge edge `python:… → go:…` is handed to **both** languages' matchers, so a `go:` callee can be looked up in the Python index. The guard therefore belongs at the **match**, not at the selection, and `_match_propagation_entry` now takes the language whose catalogue built the index and refuses a foreign callee before any lookup. `_extract_callee_language`'s docstring has always claimed it is *"used by sink/source matching to filter cross-language pollution"*; its only reachability was its own unit test, which made it a WI-ratuv family member. A test now asserts the matcher references it, so prose and code cannot drift apart again.
  - **Measured, and it moves nothing today:** the 9-repo cohort holds **208** cross-language taint call edges (95% typescript↔javascript) and **zero** of them match a sink in the wrong language's index. This is a latent guard. The dead docstring's claim of *"thousands of false positives"* from an elixir/Python `get` collision is **not** validated here — the cohort contains no elixir — and is left standing as an untested assertion rather than repeated as a result.
  - The parameter defaults to `""` (no gating), so every existing caller and test is unaffected.
- **`TaintFlowFinding.to_dict()` dropped the three fields that cannot be recovered from the emitted symbol.** `source_module` / `sink_module` (the module a catalog entry declared — WI-joruv) and `source_boundary` (the io_primitives boundary the source came from — WI-vazal) were absent from the serialized form, so a serialized finding was strictly weaker than the object it came from. The verify-claims path survived only because it reaches the dataclass directly; any consumer of the serialized form silently lost the WI-vazal split that shipped specifically to be readable — *"data off the wire reached the database"* versus *"a row read from the database reached the database"*. The guard test asserts the **property** (every declared field appears in `to_dict`) rather than the three names, so a fourth cannot be dropped silently.
- **`ujson` is catalogued as a `json` drop-in; `orjson` deliberately is not (WI-nibav).** Both were candidates from the cohort enumeration, and the item's own rule is *read the package, do not assume the surface*. Read: `ujson` exposes `dump`/`dumps`/`load`/`loads`, so it mirrors the catalogued `json.dump` file-write; `orjson` exposes only `dumps`/`loads`, has **no** `dump`, and therefore has no file-write surface to catalogue — adding it would match nothing. That asymmetry is pinned by a test so a later completeness pass does not add `orjson` by symmetry.
  - **`defusedcsv` is NOT added, and the reason is a defect elsewhere.** Its real API is the `defusedcsv.csv` submodule, and the analyzer emits `module=defusedcsv, name=csv.writer` for `from defusedcsv import csv; csv.writer(...)`. Verified against the live pretix edge: the conventional declaration (`module: defusedcsv.csv`, `functions: [writer]`) does **not** match, and only a dotted *name* does — a form used by exactly 1 of 1979 built-in primitives. The catalogue should not contort to fit a malformed id, so this is filed as an analyzer module/name-split defect instead.
- **`ambiguous_names` was incomplete for Python, so a bare unresolved `copy()` matched `shutil.copy` (INV-mivud).** WI-razol made taint propagation *honour* the list; this is about the list itself. Seven catalogued primitives had short names that also name a builtin or a container/`str` method and were not suppressed: `all`, `copy`, `count`, `filter`, `remove`, `update`, `values`.
  - **Derived, not hand-picked, because hand-picking is what left the gap.** The item was filed for `copy` alone; the campaign plan then generalised to *"all seven `shutil` peers"*, which is wrong in **both** directions — `copytree` / `rmtree` / `make_archive` collide with nothing and must stay matchable, while five Django ORM names that do collide were missed entirely. The guard test asserts the **rule** (every catalogued primitive whose short name is a builtin/container method must be suppressed) rather than seven literals, so a colliding primitive added later fails CI on the day it lands. A second test asserts the converse — distinctive names must *not* be suppressed — because over-suppression costs real recall silently.
  - **Its own filed repro is closed and re-run.** `python:external:0-0:copy:external_symbol` no longer matches `shutil.copy` under any of `call_construct` ∈ {`None`, `function`, `method`}, and a module-qualified `python:shutil:0-0:copy` still matches — suppression must not trade a false positive for a false negative. This also settles the question the item's own discussion left open in June: io-boundary:F3 *did* close the method-form case; the **function-form and meta-absent forms** were the residue.
  - **Measured, and it moves nothing on the cohort — stated because the number is easy to overclaim.** 1509 python unresolved taint call edges carry one of the seven names, so the shape is abundant; but **1505 of 1509 are method-form**, already suppressed by F3's kind-aware gate, and the remaining 4 carry disambiguating module hints. This is prevention, not a measured improvement.
  - **Verification limit, disclosed:** the only source change is a YAML data file, which is invisible to `smart-test`'s import-graph slicer (L43), so the committed manifest selects one test file and CI will exercise that alone. The regression evidence is a local full run of the `hypergumbo-core` package (10,929 passed) plus the twice-daily `full-suite`, not the per-PR gate.
- **The taint catalogue inherited nothing from parent io_primitives, so C++ ran taint analysis on three primitives while C had 67.** `io_boundary._CATALOG_PARENTS` declares four inheriting languages (`cpp ← c`, `kotlin ← java`, `scala ← java`, `elixir ← erlang`) and `io_boundary.load_catalog` merges the parent. The taint catalogue is derived from the same YAML files by `_derive_auto_imports_from_io_primitives`, which called `IoBoundaryCatalog.from_yaml(path)` directly and inherited **nothing** — while its own docstring claimed `ambiguous_by_lang` lets the taint matchers *"disambiguate exactly as io-boundaries does"*. Two consumers of one data file disagreeing about inheritance, with a docstring asserting the parity that did not hold (L50).
  - **Measured, catalogue-level:** taint sources+sinks go `cpp 3 → 70`, `elixir 231 → 469`, `kotlin 26 → 138`, `scala 23 → 137` — **1588 → 2119 overall, a 33% increase in the whole catalogue**. The short-name collision guard was missing for the same four: `ambiguous_names` cpp `0 → 19`, kotlin `34 → 58`, scala `73 → 80`, elixir `50 → 54`. So the inheriting languages had **both** a fraction of their primitive surface **and** a weaker guard against false matches on what remained.
  - **The published coverage percentage goes DOWN, and that is the fix working.** `verify-claims`' `dataflow_coverage` block now reports 740 sources / **1406 sinks** (was 553 / 1062), with the data-flow-capable count unchanged at **274** — none of the four inheriting languages has a CFG mapping — so coverage reads **19.5%** where it read 25.8%. The earlier figure was not wrong arithmetic; it was computed over a catalogue that was missing 344 sinks it should have been matching. A coverage table that moves against you when capability is added is doing the job the table was added for.
  - **`- on` in `ambiguous_names` was a YAML 1.1 boolean.** `javascript.yaml` and `scala.yaml` each declared a bare `on`, which parses as `True`, so the string `"on"` — `EventEmitter.on`, the single most common callback name in Node — was never suppressed in either language while the list *looked* correct. Both are now quoted, with the reason recorded in place. **A text grep cannot see this defect** (the file reads `on` either way), so the guard test asserts against the *loaded* value and scans every catalogue file, not just the two known ones.
  - **Honest scope:** the 9-repo cohort contains no cpp, kotlin, scala or elixir source, so no arm of it can observe any of this (L59 — before running an A/B, check that a path exists by which the difference reaches the number). The evidence here is catalogue-level and structural, asserted directly rather than inferred from a cohort that is blind to it. What this predicts for repos in those languages is untested and is not claimed.
- **The symbol-id path slot had four parsers, two of them naive, and one made the taint matcher reject correctly-identified Rust sinks.** Per ADR-0036 (D1a) the path slot is the one colon-**tolerant** slot in `{lang}:{path}:{span}:{name}:{kind}`, so the parse must be right-anchored. `ir._parse_dangling_id` and `verify_claims._symbol_path_slot` did that; `ir._extract_path_slot` and `taint._extract_callee_module` took `parts[1]`. The two in `ir.py` sit thirteen lines apart, one wrong and one right, and `_symbol_path_slot`'s docstring had already named `_extract_path_slot` as wrong and warned *"this must not become a third naive copy"* — while a **fourth** copy was live in `taint.py`. All four now delegate to a single `ir.symbol_path_slot`.
  - **Rust is where truncation turns fatal rather than lossy.** It is the only language whose taint catalogue uses `::` module paths, and **all nine** of its distinct sink modules are colon-bearing (`std::fs`, `std::process`, `std::io`, `std::net::TcpStream`, …). `parts[1]` truncated every one to `std`; `_module_matches("std::fs", "std")` is `False`; and `_lookup_named_entry` treats a present-but-mismatched module as a **rejection**, not a degrade. The outcome was inverted — the better-informed edge lost:
    ```
    rust:std::fs:0-0:write:external_symbol     name=write hits=3  ->  MATCH=None
    rust:external:0-0:write:external_symbol    name=write hits=3  ->  MATCH=std::fs.write
    ```
  - **`_extract_callee_module`'s docstring was the tell (L50).** It claimed to "mirror `_extract_callee_name`'s parsing" — which *is* the right-anchored scan — while the body did something else. A comment asserting a false fact about the present supplies a rationale, and a rationale forecloses the fix.
  - **Effect today is one flow; the exposure is forward-looking and large.** On a 9-repo cohort (95,950 taint call edges) the fix is **+1 gained, 0 lost, 0 re-targeted**, recovering `rust:std::env:0-0:var_os` → `std::env.var_os`, a real environment-variable source. It is small only because Rust analyzers currently emit the `external` sentinel for 396 of 449 unresolved taint call edges, bypassing the module filter entirely, while the 53 that carry real module paths name non-catalogued callees — so their rejections were *accidentally* correct. When external-id module coverage rises (WI-jokij), those 396 begin carrying `std::fs`, and an unfixed truncation would have taken Rust from 45 matched sinks to **zero**. The delta was predicted by simulation before the edit and reproduced exactly by the shipped code, with the non-destructiveness clause asserted first (L57): the correct result here is that nothing is lost, and an implementation that dropped matches would have looked like a precision win.
- **A shared `def_line` laundered taint between unrelated variables, so `precise` was stamped on flows with no data dependence (INV-sadah).** The §3a walk's index was keyed `(symbol_id, def_line)` with `DdgEdge.variable` **discarded**, merging the use-sets of every variable defined on one line. Reproduced on real source through the real pipeline, not a hand-built dict:
  ```
  6   server = await asyncio.start_server(...)   # the SOURCE
  7   keep = str(server); path = name            # uses server, defines path
  8   os.mkdir(path)                             # the SINK, on `path`
  ```
  `path` derives from the parameter `name`, so no value flows from source to sink. The production DDG emits `server def@6 → use@7`, `keep def@7 → use@9` and `path def@7 → use@8`; the line-keyed index collapses the last two into `def_line 7 → {8, 9}`, the walk steps 6 → 7 → 8, finds the sink line, and reports a dependence that does not exist. A security tool asserting a precision it did not have is exactly what INV-sadah forbids — and this was inside the fix *for* INV-sadah.
  - **Variable-keying alone is not the fix, which is worth stating because it looks like it should be.** `path` genuinely *is* defined at line 7, so separating the index entries still leaves "which variable defined here inherits from `server`?" unanswered, and the edge set cannot answer it. The **statement's** own `defines`/`uses` can: measured on the fixture, line 7 yields two rows — `defines=['keep'] uses=['server']` and `defines=['path'] uses=['name']`. `RepoDdg` now carries `stmt_defuse` (collected where `populate_def_use_for_cfg` already ran and the values were being thrown away), and the walk follows a hop only when a statement at the use line consumes the tainted variable.
  - **Exactly one cell moves, and the other two arms are controls.** Verdict composition went `{ddg: 2, ddg_mixed: 1}` → `{ddg: 1, ddg_mixed: 2}`: `conflated` demoted to `approximate`, `connected` (a real dependence) **kept** `precise`, `disconnected` (no dependence, walk escapes) unchanged. **Recall is untouched — all three flows are still reported**, because §3a confirms and never refutes, so this costs precision-of-label and never a finding.
  - **The test named for the label never asserted the label.** `test_ddg_labels_a_data_connected_flow_as_precise` contained the word "precise" only in its name and docstring; its sole assertion was recall. So the label §3a exists to earn had **no test anywhere in the corpus**, which is why an unearned one shipped in #188/#192 and survived three PRs. It now asserts both arms, and a new `_confidence_by_source` helper makes the label readable at all.
  - **Missing statement data degrades safely, and that is pinned.** A caller that omits `stmt_defuse` — or a language whose def/use pass annotates no statements — cannot justify a hop, so the finding falls back to `approximate` rather than silently confirming. A companion test asserts the flow is still reported and only the label moves. The alternative reading ("no statement data, so assume the hop is fine") is precisely what produced the unearned label.
  - **A fifth property joins the per-language DDG wiring gate.** A language emitting edges but annotating no statements would lose every confirmation *silently* — findings keep arriving, only the label quietly degrades — which is the ADR-0017 inertness shape (a capability with no production effect, passing its own tests). Property (4) asserts the edges exist; property (5) asserts they can be interpreted. All four registered languages pass; Go was checked explicitly before the change landed, since a Go-shaped gap would have been invisible from the Python fixtures.
- **Two tests demanded a mechanism the project retired, contradicted the gate that forbids it, and failed on clean `dev` (WI-kasin).** `test_smart_test_slice_robustness.py::test_baseline_detection_is_failover_aware` and `test_smart_test_coverage_gate.py::test_baseline_detection_still_failover_aware` both asserted that `scripts/smart-test` **contains** `CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE`. WI-hajif step 1 (`e7e43d678d`) retired the self-hosted-Forgejo failover, updated `get_baseline`, and added `tests/test_ci_failover_retired.py` — a recurrence gate that **forbids** exactly that string. The suite therefore held a test asserting X and a test asserting not-X, each correct by its own lights, with the retirement gate green and the other two red. Verified on `dev`: `git show dev:scripts/smart-test | grep -c CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE` → **0**.
  - **Why CI never noticed.** Nothing but `scripts/smart-test` maps to those two files, so the per-PR smart-test selection never picks them up — L48 (*a test that cannot be selected is indistinguishable from a test that passes*), here at the level of a retirement sweep rather than a single test. They surfaced only because `check-package-coverage` was run for an unrelated YAML change.
  - **Rewritten, not deleted.** The property they protected is still real — a baseline anchored on something stale inflates the changed set and forces a whole-repo slice — and only the mechanism moved. Both now assert the post-retirement contract: a `git merge-base HEAD` against `origin/dev`, the sole authoritative remote. Deleting them would have dropped the baseline-anchoring assertion entirely.
  - **The recurrence gate now scans the test trees, which is the third instance of its own header lesson.** Its comment already recorded that the first draft scanned only `scripts/` and `.githooks/` and went green while two other trees still carried failover machinery — *"a gate's '0 violations' is only ever scoped to what it actually scans (L23)"*. It then scanned five **source** trees and no test tree. `packages/hypergumbo-core/tests` and `tests` are added.
  - **Residue is declared as data rather than suppressed.** Widening the scan surfaced six further files, all a *different kind*: they defensively scrub the retired forge's credential env vars, or stub `apply_failover_overrides` to a no-op inside a fixture. Those assert nothing and cannot resurrect anything — untidy, not wrong — so they are listed in a `KNOWN_VESTIGIAL_RESIDUE` set that **may only shrink**: a companion test fails if an entry stops referencing the tokens, so a cleanup cannot leave the list stale, and a genuinely new offender is not covered by it. One stale `selfh` mention in a message string was corrected in place.
  - **The non-vacuity floor was strengthened after its own mutation test found a hole in this change.** The floor asserted the scan reaches `.agent`, `packages` and `scripts` — but `packages` is satisfied by the `src` tree alone, so removing `packages/hypergumbo-core/tests` from the scan left it green. That is precisely how the original gate missed the test trees. It now asserts both test trees specifically; mutation-tested in four directions (drop an offender from the residue list → main gate fails; add a cleaned entry → ratchet fails; name a missing file → ratchet fails; drop either test tree → floor fails).
- **`check-package-coverage` reported test failures as coverage gaps, with wrong advice attached (WI-kasin).** A run whose real problem was two failing tests printed `Coverage gaps for core:` followed by an **empty list**, then "Some packages have coverage gaps when tested in isolation" and two suggested fixes — move the test to the other package, stop using subprocess — while the underlying log said `TOTAL 29770 0 100%` and "Required test coverage of 100% reached". A reader trusting the banner would hunt a test-placement problem that did not exist. Both report sites now route through one `report_failure` helper that names the failing test ids when tests failed and reserves the coverage-gap advice for an actual shortfall; the summary distinguishes the two cases. Verified by replaying the original log, which now names both failing tests.
- **The ADR-0017 §4 catalogue lookup key embedded the source file extension, so no first-party callee could ever match (INV-rozaj).** `_qualified_callee` composed `_extract_callee_module` — the **raw** path slot — with the callee name, and an in-repo id carries a file path where an external one carries a module. `python:src/app/views.py:10-20:handler:function` produced the key `src/app/views.py.handler`, which no declared summary can equal. `_module_from_symbol_path` exists precisely to normalise that (added by WI-damir for the same class of bug) and was sitting sixty lines below in the same file. This is L53 applied to code rather than to a measurement: *when a production classification exists for the thing you are computing, computing it yourself is the bug.* The function's own docstring claimed it was "built from the two existing production extractors rather than a fresh parse" — true of the name half, false of the module half.
  - **The controls are the load-bearing half of the test, because every key the shipped catalogues declare is external.** A normalisation that fixed in-repo ids by breaking those would trade a dead capability for a live regression. `fmt.Printf`, `net/http.Get`, `go.uber.org/zap.String`, `os.path.join` and `logging.info` are all unchanged — `rpartition(".")` leaves a dotted module and a slashed one alone, and the named-extension set (not a length heuristic) keeps `org/zap` and `path` from being mistaken for suffixes. The `external` placeholder now yields `""` rather than the fiction `external.print`, which routes the callee to "uncatalogued, therefore unknown" and holds the branch open.
  - **Effect on the §4 numbers: derivably zero for the shipped catalogues, and stated rather than re-measured.** The shipped catalogues declare only stdlib and third-party names; an in-repo callee's key is a repo-relative path plus a name, so it still matches nothing. Measured on a self-survey (141,494 edges, 72,822 call-like, 6,685 distinct callees): the fix changes the key for **5,981 of 6,685 distinct callees / 64,177 call-edge instances**, and the catalogue hit count is **0 before and 0 after**. Tracing whether a difference can reach the number you are about to read — rather than running a 16-minute cohort A/B to rediscover a null derivable from the code — is L59 applied in the direction it was learned.
  - **A known limitation is introduced and pinned rather than left latent.** The extension was an accidental barrier: `net/http.go.Get` could not equal stdlib `net/http.Get`, and stripping it removes that. **42 of 69 shipped qualified keys are shadowable in principle** by a repo-root file named after a stdlib module; **zero collisions occur** across the 72,822 measured call edges. Since a false "terminates" *closes* a branch, this direction deletes findings rather than adding them. It is latent while §3a is confirm-only and nothing acts on `False`, so the provenance guard is filed as **blocking WI-kabif** rather than bolted on here, and a test asserts the current behaviour so a future guard fails loudly instead of silently changing it.
  - **Five of six shipped Python terminating entries could never fire, and the catalogue now declares the form that actually occurs.** `python_stdlib.yaml` keyed `logging.Logger.{debug,info,warning,error,exception}`, which needs module slot `logging.Logger`. Measured across 64 logger-method call sites on the self-survey, the slots present are `warnings` (51), `external` (12) and one file path — **not one carries `logging.Logger`**, because `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` binds from a *call* expression and receiver resolution does not follow those. Bare-module `logging.*` entries are added alongside; the `Logger`-qualified ones are kept (they are correct, an unmatched key is never consulted, and they fire if receiver resolution improves) with the split documented in the file rather than left as silent dead data. Whether the bare-module form fires on the 9-repo cohort is **unmeasured** — it matches nothing on the self-corpus.
  - **A decaying number in a rationale is replaced by an executable trigger.** The qualified-names-only safety argument was carried by the docstring claim "11 of its 33 keys are such aliases". That count moved **33 → 108 → 113 across two catalogue edits in two days**, which is L50's shape exactly: a rationale that goes wrong with nobody deciding anything. The count is gone from the prose in all three homes; the *property* it rested on — that the alias index contains bare short names capable of colliding (`log`, `map`, `filter`, `parse`, `get`) — is now asserted by a test that fails if a future catalogue ever makes the argument moot.
- **The §3a walk returned its removal-licensing verdict for definitions the DDG never recorded (INV-lupav, P0).** `_ddg_taint_reaches` is three-valued: `True` (a data dependence was found), `False` (the walk ran to completion *and accounted for the value at every step*), `None` (the value escaped tracked ground). Only `False` may ever license removing a reported flow. It was also being returned when the index held **nothing at all** about a definition — `_ddg_taint_reaches("f", [1], [9], {})` → `False`, reproduced live. The walk did `uses = ddg_uses.get(...)` and, on an empty result, `continue`d **without setting `escaped`**: every other way of losing the value flags itself, and this one did not.
  - **Two return values, three causes, and the third one is the dangerous one.** A negative walk means (1) the value genuinely goes nowhere near a sink, (2) it left the tracked chain into a container ADR-0017 §7b excludes, or (3) the construct that defined it was never modelled, so no use was ever recorded. Only (1) is exhaustion. (2) leaves an escape to classify; **(3) leaves silence**, so no amount of work on the escape vocabulary would ever have surfaced it. `cfg_nodes/go.yaml` self-documents a matching extraction gap — `if err := do(); err != nil` initializers are invisible to def/use — and 700 of caddy's 6,596 `if` statements carry a call there.
  - **Behaviour-neutral today, and verified rather than asserted.** The sole production call site is `_ddg_taint_reaches(...) is True`, so `False` and `None` already collapse; the change can only prevent a future false negative, never cause one. The recall corpus — the only test that runs the real pipeline end to end — is unchanged.
  - **The population is narrower than the item filed, and the correction is measured.** The barren branch is reachable **only for seed lines**: every other frontier entry is membership-tested before being appended. `propagate_taint_ddg` additionally gates the walk on `source_tracked = any(...)`, so a source whose *only* call line is untracked never reaches the walk. The production-reachable shape is therefore **a source with ≥2 call sites where at least one is untracked** — which the `if`-initializer gap supplies whenever a source call sits in an initializer. The caddy trace recorded on the item (`cwd, _ := os.Getwd()` → `msg := …` → `if n, err := fmt.Printf(msg)`) does **not** reach this path: run through a reimplementation of the pre-fix walk beside the current one, it returns `None` on **both**, because the intermediate line fails the membership test one step earlier and hits the escape branch. The defect is real; that particular example was misattributed.
  - **This is the conservative floor, not the structural fix.** Refutation should be **coverage-gated** — forfeited for any function whose CFG statement extents fail to cover every call node in its body — which catches the class mechanically instead of enumerating known gaps. That is not implemented here.
  - **A test documenting the defect as intended behaviour is retired.** `test_barren_seed_line_is_skipped` asserted "a source line the DDG recorded nothing for contributes nothing", and its assertion passed for an unrelated reason (the `None` came from a later escape, not the barren seed), so it never exercised the branch it named. Replaced by the bare repro plus an isolation pair — a closed cycle from a tracked seed yields `False`, and adding one barren seed degrades it to `None` — which pins the production-reachable shape rather than the unit curiosity.
- **`TaintFlowFinding.path` was not reproducible across processes (INV-havos).** Two runs of the identical binary on an unchanged repo reported **different paths for the same flow** — same source, same sink, same label, different middle hop, both hops genuine callers. Adjacency is set-valued, so BFS dequeue order followed `str` set iteration and therefore `PYTHONHASHSEED`; the parent map recorded whichever predecessor happened to arrive first. Measured on kserve: **2 of 182 distinct flows** differed run to run.
  - **Cause confirmed before fixing, not assumed.** A direct probe with eight equally-valid middle hops: a fixed seed produced an identical path twice, and five different seeds produced four distinct witnesses. The item was filed with the cause marked *suspected*; this is what promoted it.
  - **Not cosmetic.** A user diffing two runs on an unchanged repo saw evidence churn with no cause, which trains them to distrust the diff. It also made any path-level A/B between builds unreliable — it cost real time during the WI-kabif measurement, where a first reading of "30 flows appeared and disappeared" turned out to be entirely this artifact.
  - **Fixed by a declared tie-break, and the field now says what it is.** BFS visits each node's callees in sorted id order, so the witness is a deterministic function of the input. The item warned against doing only that and continuing to call the result "the path" — so `path`'s docstring now states it is **one witness among possibly several equally-valid routes**, that the others are real and unlisted, and that a consumer needing "every route" must not read it as if it were that.
  - **Scope measured rather than assumed.** Both propagators shared the defect (they share `_build_adjacency`), and after the sanitizer-barrier refactor above the entire blast radius is **two lines** in one helper. Repo-wide sweep for the same shape found one other set-iterating traversal (`compact.py`'s component-root accumulation), which is order-independent — it accumulates into a set and reads only `len()`.
  - **Verified against the item's own filed repro:** kserve, two runs, **0 flows with a differing path (was 2)**. Pinned by an in-process test asserting the specific declared winner (with a non-vacuity floor proving the fixture really offers competing witnesses) plus a subprocess test across five hash seeds — the latter contributes no coverage, and exists because the tie-break assertion is a proxy while the cross-seed run is the property itself.
- **A sanitized flow is now REPORTED as sanitized instead of being pruned into silence (owner ruling).** The BFS barrier dropped the subtree beyond a sanitizer, so a flow a developer had deliberately protected produced *exactly the output of a flow that does not exist*: nothing. A reader could not tell "no path from this source to this sink" from "a path exists and your `encrypt()` call is what makes it safe" — and the second is what they need before deleting that call. `TaintFlowFinding.sanitized` existed for this and was written `False` at both and only construction sites, so `verify_claims`' `and not f.sanitized` was a **tautology** and the `confirmed_safe` branch of the `verdict` property was unreachable in production.
  - **Unsanitized wins.** A sink the taint reaches by any unprotected route is reported unsanitized even when another route encrypts; labelling it safe because *some* path sanitizes is the dangerous direction of wrong. Where two distinct sink *sites* differ (one protected, one not), both are now reported and distinguished by the label — previously only the violation survived, leaving a reader unable to tell "the other route is safe" from "there is no other route".
  - **One barrier implementation, two callers.** The structural and DDG propagators each carried a copy of the walk and had **already drifted** — the structural one tracked `sanitized_nodes`, the DDG one dropped them on the floor. That is the shape that produces "fixed in Python, still broken in Go", so the two now share `_reachability_past_sanitizers`.
  - **The count is disclosed, not just the label.** `ClaimVerdict.sanitized_flows` reports how many flows matched the claim but were not counted because a sanitizer lies on every route, and the **confirmed** verdict says so in prose — that is where it matters most, because the claim reads clean and the reason it reads clean may be one function call away from deletion. Sibling of `excluded_flows`, same D7 shape. Envelope schema `1.5 → 1.6`.
  - **A test that pinned the silent drop was VACUOUS, and had been for months (L17).** `test_sanitized_path_no_violation` asserted `len(findings) == 0` using a bare `py:external:0-0:write_text` sink — but the sink entry is `kind="method"` with module `pathlib.Path`, and a bare unresolved method call carrying no module context is suppressed outright (io-boundary:F3 / INV-tapat). Measured on the pre-change code: **0 findings with the sanitizer and 0 without it**, so the barrier the test names was never exercised. Fixture corrected to the module-qualified form its own sibling already used, and a non-vacuity floor added.
  - **Measured on the 9-repo cohort: zero verdicts move, and zero sanitized flows are produced.** The null was pre-registered and its cause is structural rather than incidental: the taint labels the cohort's claims constrain (`host_secret`, `untrusted_input`) and the labels any shipped sanitizer can transform (`plaintext`, `key_material`) are **disjoint sets**. No sanitizer could fire on this corpus under any implementation. So this ships correct and unit-proven but **inert on the current cohort**, and is reported that way rather than as a measured win — which also makes INV-karud clause (b) ("sanitization is verified") unverifiable on this corpus by construction, evidence Phase 6 needs rather than a gap to paper over.
- **A violated claim now discloses what was set aside and where its flows came from — in text, where WI-bifob's own contract said it already did.** That item states exclusions are reported "on the **confirmed** path as well as the violated one". Only the confirmed path implemented it: on the violated path `excluded_flows` was attached to the dataclass and never rendered, and `cli.py` prints `details` alone — so a text-mode reader of a violated claim never learned flows existed and were dropped by a policy they did not choose. `flow_origins` (WI-vazal) reached no text surface at all, on either path. Both are now spliced into `details`, which is where this module already keeps the confirmed-path clause. **Measured: 5 violated verdicts across the cohort carry exclusions that were previously invisible in text** — caddy's `untrusted-input-no-network` alone was silently setting aside 42 flows.
  - **`flow_origins` in text matters where it was measured.** All 140 flows on pretix's largest violated claim are database-read-to-database-write, which changes what a reader does about the claim; a text consumer could not see it. Verdicts now read `[origins: 1 net_recv]` / `[origins: 2 env_read]`.
- **`is_test_file` stays broad, and the exclusion bucket now says WHICH rule fired (owner ruling).** The predicate matches `bench/`, `benches/`, `fixtures/`, `testdata/`, `mocks/`, `fakes/`, `harnesses/`, `fv/`, `testutils/` and more — correct breadth for its callers (deprioritize as a non-production entrypoint), but reporting all of it as `test_sourced` told the reader something false about, say, a benchmark directory, sending them to look for a test that does not exist. `excluded_flows` keys now carry the reason: `test_sourced`, `mock_sourced`, `fixture_sourced`, `benchmark_sourced`, `test_support_sourced`, plus the unchanged `migration_sourced`.
  - **One classifier, two consumers — not a second predicate beside the boolean.** `paths.classify_test_file` holds the rules and returns the reason; `is_test_file` is now literally `classify_test_file(path) is not None`. A separate categoriser would drift from the boolean on the first edit, and *"when a production classification exists for the thing you are counting, counting it yourself IS the bug"* (L53) is a lesson this codebase has already paid for five times. A property test over a 46-path corpus asserts the two can never disagree, with a non-vacuity floor so an all-True corpus cannot satisfy it trivially.
  - **Measured, and the honest result is that the split reclassifies nothing here.** Cohort-wide the excluded population is **692 flows before and after**, still `{test_sourced: 675, migration_sourced: 17}` — every excluded flow in these nine repos is genuine test code or a migration. That is conclusive for this cohort rather than a sampling limitation, since the bucket counts *are* the classification. So this is insurance against a misleading label, not a correction of one that was firing; it is reported that way rather than as a win.
  - **A production function named `test_*` is a footgun**, found on the first run: pytest collects any `test_`-prefixed symbol imported into a test module as a test case, so the first draft failed with `fixture 'path' not found`. Named `classify_test_file`.
  - Envelope schema `1.4 → 1.5`. Not purely additive, deliberately: a flow formerly counted under `test_sourced` can now appear under a sibling key. *Which* flows are excluded does not change — the population is defined by the same function — only what they are called.
- **A taint verdict now says HOW it was adjudicated, instead of asserting `approximate` regardless (INV-karud clause (c)).** `verify_taint_claim` hardcoded the literal string `approximate` into every violated verdict's `details`, and `_flow_evidence_dict` dropped `confidence` and `analysis_method` entirely — so the ADR-0017 §3a walk could confirm a flow by data dependence and **both** surfaces a user reads would still say the analysis was approximate. The label was produced and then discarded, which is the same provenance discontinuity §3a itself was built to close, one layer further down. Two consequences that are worth stating plainly: no consumer could have noticed the hardcoded literal, because the field it contradicted was not emitted either; and the two preceding entries' improvements were, until this change, **invisible from every CLI surface** — they had to be measured with an instrument wrapping the propagator.
  - **Each evidence row carries `confidence` + `analysis_method`; each verdict carries an `analysis_methods` breakdown.** The breakdown mirrors `flow_origins` (WI-vazal): the structured field is the contract, the prose a courtesy. `analysis_method` is the finer axis and the one worth carrying — `confidence` collapses `ddg_mixed` (the walk ran and did not confirm) and `structural` (no reaching-def data existed, so no walk was possible) into one `approximate` bucket, and *"the analysis looked and found nothing"* is a different fact about a security verdict from *"the analysis could not look."*
  - **The aggregation policy is stated, not left to `max()`.** A verdict is a **disjunction** over its flows, and after §3a those flows can be adjudicated differently. Collapsing upward would claim a precision most flows did not have; collapsing downward would erase the ones that earned it. So the composition is reported — `[host_secret confidence: 9 approximate, 1 precise]` — with the single-value case staying terse, which keeps the rendering byte-identical for a verdict whose flows all agree. That uniformity is precisely why the hardcoded literal survived unnoticed.
  - **Both breakdowns count every counted flow, not the shown rows.** `evidence` is deduplicated and capped at `_MAX_EVIDENCE_ROWS`; `details` counts every violation. A breakdown computed over the shown rows would silently disagree with the total printed beside it.
  - **`to_dict` dropped the new field on the first pass, and a red test caught it.** That is the same defect class in miniature: a dataclass field the serializer omits is discarded as completely as one never computed, and is *harder* to notice because every in-process test still sees it. Pinned by its own test.
  - **Measured on the 9-repo cohort against the pre-change baseline: zero verdicts move.** 54 verdict rows, **0 changed** on (verdict, evidence_count, shown rows); 38 confirmed / 16 violated both arms. That null is the whole point of the check (L57 — when the correct result is "no change", a broken build looks identical to a correct one), so it is paired with a positive control: **16 verdicts now carry a populated `analysis_methods`**, cohort-wide `{ddg_mixed: 300, ddg: 8, structural: 3}`. Live repro on caddy, through the real CLI: `analysis_methods: {'ddg': 1, 'ddg_mixed': 9}` with the matching evidence row labelled `confidence: precise` / `analysis_method: ddg`.
  - **Two stale prose claims about the vocabulary corrected in place.** `TaintFlowFinding.analysis_method`'s own inline comment read `"structural" or "ddg"` while the propagator had been assigning `ddg_mixed` — a reader enumerating the vocabulary from the field declaration got it wrong. The module docstring's *"structural analysis produces `approximate` confidence"* was equally incomplete once §3a shipped. Envelope schema `1.3 → 1.4`, additive.
- **The §3a walk now asks about every call site, not whichever one the edge happened to carry.** `propagate_taint_ddg` read `edge["line"]` alone, which is the *first-encountered* of N sites for that `(caller, callee)` pair — so "does the taint reach the sink?" was silently a question about an arbitrary call. Two guards added with §3a (`source_tracked`, `sink_after_source`) were the visible symptom: both were written to refuse adjudication when the recorded line could not be the relevant site, and one of them carries a comment naming the caddy case exactly. With `meta.call_lines` (above) the walk gets the real site list.
  - **Measured on the 9-repo cohort, both arms on the same binary.** The label this moves reaches **no CLI surface** — `_flow_evidence_dict` drops `confidence`/`analysis_method` and `verify_claims` hardcodes the literal `"approximate"` (INV-karud clause (c), still open) — so a `verify-claims` diff would report zero rows changed for a working implementation and a no-op alike. The label was therefore read where it is produced: an instrument wrapping `propagate_taint_ddg`, with the B arm stripping `meta.call_lines` from the edge dicts to reproduce the pre-change input exactly. **Confirmations 14 → 16** (caddy 5 → 6, kserve 0 → 1); **flow count identical in all nine repos** (1428 → 1428), which is the control that matters — §3a is confirm-only, so any flow movement would have been a regression wearing an improvement's numbers.
  - **Both new confirmations read against source, not tallied (L55), and both are real.** caddy `cmd/main.go:printEnvironment` does `cwd, err := os.Getwd()` at 465 and `fmt.Printf("os.Getwd=%s\n\n", cwd)` at **469**, while the edge carried **454** — the first of twelve `fmt.Printf` calls. kserve `bgtest/main.go:handle` does `r.Source = os.Getenv("target")` at 39 and `fmt.Printf("res = %v\n", r)` at **40**, while the edge carried **38**, a `Printf` *before* the source. These are the two flows the §3a guards were written to refuse to judge.
  - **The meta value is validated, not trusted.** `meta` is an open dict that may come from an older artifact or a hand edit; a non-int member would reach the `sink_line > source_line` comparison as a `TypeError`. Non-list values and non-int members are ignored and the edge's own `line` still counts.
  - **Found while measuring, filed rather than chased (INV-havos):** `TaintFlowFinding.path` is **not reproducible across processes**. Two runs of the identical binary on kserve gave different paths for 2 of 182 distinct flows — same source, same sink, same label, different middle hop, both hops genuine callers. Adjacency is built with sets, so BFS order follows `str` set iteration and therefore `PYTHONHASHSEED`; the parent map records whichever predecessor arrived first. Flow identities, the confidence histogram and the verdicts are all stable — this is a reproducibility defect in user-facing *evidence*. It also cost real time here: the first A/B read as "30 flows appeared and disappeared" purely because the comparison keyed on the path.

### Added
- **Edge deduplication now RETAINS the call sites it collapses, instead of discarding all but one (`Edge.meta.call_lines`).** One edge per `(src, dst, edge_type)` is the right call-graph model and is unchanged — emitting one edge per call site would multiply the graph (a single Go function's `fmt.Printf` calls alone turn 1 edge into 12, inflating every centrality and fan-out metric downstream). What was wrong is that `edge.line` — whichever call site happened to be *encountered first* — was the only site any consumer could recover. ADR-0017 §4 cannot work without the rest: deciding what a dataflow use at line U flows into requires knowing which callee is invoked at line U, and with one line per relationship that question was unanswerable for every call site but one. The survivor now carries the sorted union, its own line included.
  - **Absence is the contract.** A single-site edge carries no `call_lines` key at all, so "absent" reads as "exactly one site, and it is `line`" — a one-element list on every edge would add a list to every edge of every behavior map while saying nothing `line` does not. Capped at 50 (the sibling of `_REFERRING_PATHS_CAP`), because generated code can call one target hundreds of times from one function. Truncation is **conservative for every consumer**: knowing fewer call sites can only narrow what a dataflow walk adjudicates, never broaden it.
  - **Both collapse sites, not just the obvious one.** `apply_external_id_remap` runs *after* `deduplicate_edges` and collapses again — two edges genuinely distinct at dedup time (distinct external dsts) become one once both dsts remap to a single boundary node. It unions `call_lines` the same way it already unions `referring_paths`; without that, whichever edge loses takes its call sites with it.
  - **Measured on hypergumbo's own repo, and verified in both directions rather than counted.** 13,121 of 141,312 edges (**9.29%**) record more than one site — median 2, p95 7, 28 edges at the cap — adding **0.22%** to the artifact (411 KB of 186 MB). Spot-checked against source: every line recorded for `catalog.py:file → _ts` is a genuine `_ts(...)` call site, and the *unrecorded* lines inside the same function's span (`cli.py:2559-2568`) contain no `.get(` — so the field records call sites rather than "every line in the caller".
  - Registered in `axis_meta_keys` (the `Edge.meta` vocabulary registry) and regenerated into `docs/schema.json`, since a meta key that producers write and consumers read without a registry entry is exactly the drift that registry exists to police.
- **Rust and TypeScript def/use extractors are wired into the pipeline at last, behind a gate that closes the trap for every future language (WI-tohuk).** Both shipped in March with tests and 100% coverage and have produced **zero** DDG edges ever since, because *four* independent things must all hold before an extractor has any effect and each one alone is enough to make it silently inert: its CFG mapping must declare `atomic_statement` (without it `CfgBuilder` recurses past statements down to bare identifiers, so an extractor keyed on statement types is never handed one — and the CFG stays structurally correct and fully reachable, which is why the CFG tests cannot see it); a `LanguageDdgSpec` must be registered or `build_repo_ddg` skips the language; the registering module must be **imported on the production path**, since registration is an import side effect; and the chain must actually yield an edge. Rust and TypeScript failed the first three simultaneously.
  - **A gate, not three fixes.** `test_ddg_language_wiring.py` asserts all four properties for *every* language with a registered def/use extractor, present and future, so the next language cannot rediscover the same trap. Red-first it failed 7 of 14. The production import list stays explicit — greppable, and import cost stays visible — but it is a second home for a fact the filesystem already holds, so an exhaustiveness test fails when the two disagree.
  - **Measured, not asserted.** Rust and TypeScript go **0 → 2 DDG edges** on the identical minimal fixture, and stripping `atomic_statement` returns them to 0 — the mutation proof that the check is load-bearing rather than decorative. On real repos: zoxide **273 edges / 72 symbols**, apollo-server **1,176 / 91**.
  - **`atomic_statement` swallowed Rust's control flow, and the fix is in the builder rather than the mapping.** Declaring `expression_statement` atomic — which Rust must, to get def/use on bare calls — broke three existing CFG tests, because tree-sitter parses `if c { } else { }` as `expression_statement > if_expression` and `let x = f()?;` as `let_declaration > try_expression`. The atomic declaration stopped the descent before the branch was ever classified, so the CFG lost its true/false edges while every def/use measurement kept working — the wrapper had been declared *for* def/use. In a statement-oriented grammar (Go, Python) this cannot happen: `if_statement` is its own statement and is never wrapped. `CfgBuilder` now honours `atomic_statement` only when the subtree contains no control-flow-classified descendant, so the decision is made per *occurrence* rather than per node *type* — which is all a YAML mapping can express. Measured impact: Python **identical** (61,113 edges / 6,946 symbols on pretix, unchanged), Go caddy 28,716/1,635 → **28,650/1,678** (closures now decompose), Rust zoxide 297/86 → **273/72**. Only Python feeds taint today, so no verdict can move.
  - **The new gate did not catch that regression, and now does.** Edge counts cannot see a swallowed branch. The gate gained a per-language check that an `if`/`else` still yields a block with both `true` and `false` successors — mutation-proved by removing the builder guard, which reddens Rust alone.
  - **Effect on taint findings: zero, and that is the contract.** 9-repo cohort A/B, identical inputs, re-run after the builder guard below: 54 verdict rows → 54, **270 displayed evidence rows → 270**, 311 total flows → 311, 38 confirmed / 16 violated unchanged, and **0 cells differ** across full evidence tuples (both primitives and both symbol ids). Nothing reads Rust or TypeScript DDG edges yet, so movement would have meant a leak rather than an improvement — Python's propagator receives the *whole-repo* edge list, not a language-filtered one. The arm's instrument asserts in both directions before measuring: that the wiring is live (specs registered, edges produced) and that foreign symbol ids stay disjoint from Python seed ids via the language prefix, which is *why* the null is expected by construction.
  - **The bigger gap points the other way, and is disclosed rather than implied.** The glob is `*.ts`, so JavaScript gets nothing — express, all `.js`, yields **0**. And the catalog inverts the priority: **JavaScript carries 50 sources and 83 sinks; TypeScript carries 6 and zero.** `cfg_nodes/typescript.yaml` claims in its own header to "also cover JavaScript", but it is keyed to `typescript` and the extractor registers only for `typescript`, so a `.js` file reaches neither. So this registration is *not* a taint improvement and is not presented as one: no TypeScript DDG edge can move a verdict while the language has no sinks. Class methods are also excluded, because the analyzer decides their kind slot inline (`method`/`getter`/`setter`) and re-deriving that here would put a second copy of a production classification in a consumer.
  - **`select_ddg_targets`'s docstring was false and is corrected.** It claimed "DDG analysis is expensive, so only 2-10% of functions are analyzed" while the function has **zero production callers** and `build_repo_ddg` walks every function of every file with no sampling, budget or cache — a reader sizing DDG cost from it would be wrong by more than an order of magnitude. Wiring it is deliberately sequenced after §3a, since it *reduces* which functions carry DDG data and switching it on first would make §3a's effect unreadable.
  - **Scope stated as data.** The gate is blind by construction to a language with no extractor at all, so it is a regression guard rather than a coverage claim: `javascript` (83 sinks, nothing wired) and `java` (69 sinks, a CFG mapping but no extractor to wire) are outside its population and are named in place.
- **A source-to-verdict RECALL corpus for taint — the first test in the tree that runs the real pipeline end to end.** Every other taint test hands `verify-claims` a hand-written behavior map via `--input`, so the analyzers never run; `verify_taint_claim` is fed hand-built `TaintFlowFinding` objects, and the one test that puts real source on disk asserts `rc in (0, 1)`. The consequence, stated plainly: **nothing in the suite would have failed if the pipeline stopped reporting an entire class of real flows** — which is how ten ADR-0017 capabilities came to sit inert while each was closed `done` on 100% coverage. `test_taint_recall_corpus.py` generates Python and Go repos into `tmp_path`, runs analysis → propagation → claim verification, and asserts *which* flows come out.
  - **Vacuity is structurally impossible, not merely guarded against.** A corpus of "this must NOT be reported" assertions passes trivially when the pipeline reports nothing (L17), and that is the exact failure mode here — a broken propagator makes every precision assertion greener. So each language arm puts the must-find and must-not-find shapes in **one repo** and asserts the whole finding set at once: a pipeline emitting nothing fails the must-find half in the same test.
  - **Proven by mutation, in both propagators.** Silencing `propagate_taint_structural` turns the **Go** arm red; silencing `propagate_taint_ddg` turns the **Python** arm red. That the two mutations fail different tests is itself the finding: `cli.py` routes Python through the DDG pass and everything else through the structural pass, so a Python-only regression test could never have covered the path Go runs on.
  - **The first positive control FAILED, and that is why the corpus exists in this shape (L9).** A source and a sink under a common caller — `main` calls `handler`, `main` calls `write_it` — reports **nothing**: propagation seeds at the source's *caller* and walks forward only, so two siblings are never connected. `reverse_adj` is built in `propagate_taint_structural` and never read. Recorded as `xfail(strict=True)` rather than as an assertion of the wrong behaviour, so it XPASSes and demands attention the moment source-side propagation lands (L19) — the imperative `pytest.xfail()` form can never XPASS and would silently disable the signal.
  - **Catalog entries are chosen, not assumed.** Every source and sink is module-qualified and verified absent from its language's `ambiguous_names` before use (`asyncio.start_server` → `os.mkdir`; `net/http.ListenAndServe` → `os.WriteFile`). INV-karud's own first control returned a clean zero because it used a bare ambiguous name and a chained call the analyzer does not emit.
  - **Generated into `tmp_path`, deliberately.** A fixture corpus analyzed in place measures the pipeline's own test-path heuristics rather than the producer (L20/L21) — and since `verify-claims` now excludes test-sourced flows by default, a corpus committed under a directory named `fixtures` would be excluded from its own verdicts.
- **Go def/use extractor, and the statement-level Go CFG it needs (ADR-0017 §1c).** `go.yaml` gains `atomic_statement`; without it `CfgBuilder` recurses past `short_var_declaration` / `assignment_statement` down to bare identifiers, so an extractor keyed on statement node types is never handed one — measured, Go's CFG statements were `{identifier, field_identifier, interpreted_string_literal_content}`. `python.yaml` was the **only** mapping that declared this, which is why every non-Python extractor emitted zero DDG edges. The new extractor covers short-var and var/const declarations, assignment (including selector and index targets, and augmented forms that read their target), inc/dec, send, `go` statements and bare calls; unrecognised nodes fall back to "every identifier is a use", which can over-link but can never invent a definition that drops a real flow.
  - **The repo-level DDG builder moved out of `cli.py` and stopped being Python-only.** `_build_python_ddg_for_verify_claims` was a ~200-line repo-walking analysis pipeline inside a 10,313-line module whose own docstring scopes it to *"argument parsing and dispatching to command handlers"*. Its name encoded both faults — a hardcoded language and a single consumer — and both were load-bearing: the sole call site passed the literal `"python"`, which is one of the two independent reasons the Rust and TypeScript extractors had no production caller. Now `hypergumbo_core.ddg_build` owns walk → parse → CFG → reaching-defs → aggregate, parameterized by a registered `LanguageDdgSpec` (core owns the registry, language packages register into it — the idiom `register_def_use_extractor` already uses, needed here because naming a Go method requires a receiver helper that lives in the language package). **Verified pure move:** 22,725 edges, 1,306 symbols, 403 hint-callers, edge digest `97f8fea3700e82bb` and symbol digest `51cb00bdad717e18` identical before and after. That measurement was taken on a Python-only corpus; on a polyglot repo the aggregated edge list can now carry other languages' edges into Python propagation, which is inert today but is *not* covered by the digest.
  - **`DdgEdge` gains `symbol_id`**, stamped at its single construction site. Block ids are function-local, so an aggregated repo-level edge list cannot attribute an edge without it. This is a **prerequisite** for §3a, not a fix.
  - **Effect on taint findings: zero, and measured rather than assumed** — 149 flows across the 9-repo cohort, 0 added, 0 removed. It cannot be otherwise until §3a exists (see Fixed). On caddy the pipeline now builds **28,716 Go DDG edges over 1,635 symbols** with ids matching the Go analyzer's, and every one is discarded: the propagation gate stays `lang == "python"` deliberately, carrying a comment that says so, because widening it today would stamp `analysis_method="ddg"` on structurally-derived findings — a security tool asserting a precision it did not use. Leaving the build in place rather than gating it costs ~1.9s per caddy-sized repo and buys attributability: when §3a lands it changes exactly one variable.
  - **Non-vacuity is asserted at the pipeline level, not the extractor level** — a Go function must yield ≥1 DDG edge, and 0 when `atomic_statement` is removed. The extractor being correct in isolation is precisely what the preceding layers of this bug looked like.
  - **Disclosed gaps, tracked:** `if err := do(); err != nil` and `for i := 0; ...` initializers and `range` clauses are not reached by the CFG mapping's conditional/loop hooks, so those bindings remain invisible to def/use; pointer aliasing is not modelled.

### Removed
- **The self-hosted-Forgejo CI failover is retired, not repointed (WI-hajif).** With the GitHub migration live (`origin` is github.com; Codeberg is now a passive `git push --mirror` target), the failover's job was taken over by the validated 2026-07-23 outage SOP: `woodpecker exec` runs the entire gate offline with no server and no network (drill: 10,331 passed, 100% coverage), and a plain bare git repo we control holds commits until the forge returns. Standing a Forgejo back up as the fallback would also re-introduce the exact vendor dependency the migration exists to remove. Deleted: `scripts/ci-failover` (981 lines), `scripts/lib/failover-git-shim.sh`, `docs/sops/CI_FAILOVER.md`, and `apply_failover_overrides` from the forge library.
  - **It was a layer, not a script, and the entry point was the small part.** The `selfh`/`CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE` awareness also lived in `auto-pr` (push remote, flush remote, ops-diff base, merged-PR recording), `merge-pr` (pull remote, token, PR URLs), `ci-debug`, `smart-test` (merge-base re-anchoring), `install-hooks`, `.githooks/pre-push` (a push guard), `.githooks/test_hooks.sh` (a 77-line test block), `.agent/hooks/_shared/stop_logic.sh`, and the tracker package (`_FailoverState` + `_detect_failover` + three consumers). Each limb reads like ordinary remote-selection logic in isolation, which is why the removal ships with a **recurrence gate** (`tests/test_ci_failover_retired.py`) rather than trusting a one-time sweep.
  - **The gate's own first draft was too narrow, and that is the point of recording it.** Scanning only `scripts/` and `.githooks/`, it went green while `stop_logic.sh` still *called* the deleted `apply_failover_overrides` and the tracker package still parsed the flag file. It now scans `.agent/hooks` and `packages/*/src` as well, matches the shell-variable spelling (`FAILOVER_ACTIVE`) separately from the flag-file spelling (`CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE`), and carries a non-vacuity floor asserting it actually *reaches* those roots — a gate's "0 violations" is only ever scoped to what it scans.
  - **Three green checks each hid a broken edit, in three different ways.** A block transformer that did not understand `elif` collapsed two if/elif/else chains and deleted the `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` credential branch and the GitHub PR-URL branch — **`bash -n` passed**, and every GitHub API call would have lost its token. Removing `_detect_failover` left `failover.active` referenced downstream: the AST parsed, and it would have `NameError`d at runtime. Removing test methods by `def` anchor orphaned their `@patch` decorators, surfacing only as an `IndentationError` two classes later. Syntax, AST, import and behavioural checks each caught a different one; none of them would have caught all three.
  - **The vPR queue is deliberately kept**, diverging from `migration.md`'s recommendation to retire it alongside the rest. The bare-mirror SOP covers a forge *outage* (a procedure a human invokes); the vPR queue covers *transient* unreachability inside a single `auto-pr` run (queue locally, flush later, automatically). Those are different needs, and the queue is live machinery whose three failure modes were repaired the same week (WI-buniz).
  - **Governance surface updated with it:** `AGENTS.md` no longer lists `ci-failover` as an approved network-calling script or a governance file, its pre-work checklist no longer instructs agents to prefer a `selfh` remote, and its secrets line no longer describes the GitHub backend as "dormant" — that has been backwards since the migration. `CODEOWNERS` drops the deleted path, `.env.template` drops the `SELFHOSTED_FORGEJO_*` block, and the pre-work playbook keeps the 2026-04-01 incident that motivated the authoritative-remote check while recording that the class of error is now gone by construction rather than by vigilance.

### Fixed
- **ADR-0017 §3a exists: the DDG forward walk now runs, and the `precise` label is earned rather than asserted (INV-sadah).** Since March, `propagate_taint_ddg` stamped `analysis_method="ddg"` / `confidence="precise"` on findings produced by a plain call-graph BFS — it built a forward index and a tainted-variable set and read *neither*, and the one branch that would have consulted the DDG compared a function-local block id against a symbol id, so it could not fire. The walk is now real: it keys on `DdgEdge.symbol_id` (added as a prerequisite in the previous release and read by nothing since), seeds at the line where the source is called, follows def→use transitively, and asks whether a tainted value is used **at the line where the sink is called**. That is §3a's step 5 read as the item's own filing corrects it — *an argument at the sink call site* — since "a tainted definition reaches the sink's block" is satisfied by any function that calls a source and a sink on unrelated data. `precise`/`ddg` is now set only where the walk confirmed a dependence; everything else is `approximate`/`ddg_mixed`. The literal `lang == "python"` gate is gone.
  - **Measured live rather than assumed: caddy 5 of 169 findings confirmed (3.0%), pretix 8 of 821 (1.0%).** Small, and stated as such — but non-zero is the point, since the failure mode this whole area suffers from is a capability that runs and changes nothing.
  - **It CONFIRMS and never refutes, and that restraint is the finding.** A first arm did remove flows: **−32 (−10.3%)** on the 9-repo cohort, close to the pre-registered ~13%, with two claims flipping to `confirmed`. Reading every removal against source (rather than tallying the delta) found real flows deleted — caddy's `printEnvironment` does `cwd, err := os.Getwd()` and `fmt.Printf("os.Getwd=%s", cwd)` four lines later, and pretix's `vouchers_send` does `voucher_list.append(vouchers.pop(0))` then `bulk_update(voucher_list, ...)`. **Three of nine verified removals were wrong.** Every aggregate said the change was an improvement; only the individual flows said otherwise.
  - **WI-kabif filed four prerequisites "each independently sufficient to keep it inert". There are six.** (5) **§4 function summaries** — §3a's own step 3 requires them, and `infer_summary` / `load_function_summaries` have zero production callers. Without them a use that *terminates* the taint (`fmt.Printf(cwd)`) and one that *propagates* it (`lst.append(x)`) are the same event: a use that defines nothing. So every "no dependence found" is really "lost track of it", and removal on that basis is a false-negative generator — the expensive direction for a security tool. (6) **Per-call-site lines** — call edges are deduplicated by `(caller, callee)`, so `line` is *a* call site, not each one; `printEnvironment` calls `fmt.Printf` twelve times and the graph keeps only the first (454), making the tainted call at 469 invisible. The walk is therefore three-valued internally (dependence / exhausted / escaped) and only the first value earns a label; removal becomes sound once §4 runs.
  - **Cohort A/B, 9 repos: 54 verdict rows, 311 total flows, 270 displayed rows, 38 confirmed / 16 violated — all unchanged, 0 cells differ.** For a confirm-only change that null is the contract, and it is only meaningful because the arm's instrument asserts in three directions *before* measuring: the walk confirms a real dependence as `precise`, does **not** remove an unconfirmed flow, and leaves cross-function flows intact and `approximate`. A reduction on this arm would be false negatives wearing precision's numbers.
  - Two soundness guards travel with the walk, each added after a verified false negative rather than as tuning: a source whose value the DDG never tracked cannot license a refutation, and a sink call site recorded *before* the source proves nothing because later sites may exist that the edge model cannot express.
- **Two taint-propagator defects that silently lost real findings — one of them hiding a violated security claim.** Both sat on the path `verify-claims` runs Python through, and both failed in the direction that *loses* findings, which for a security tool is the expensive error: a suppressed flow is indistinguishable from a clean repo.
  - **The DDG pass kept a private, ungated copy of sanitizer registration (INV-finoh, second site).** `propagate_taint_ddg` matched sanitizers on bare short name with no `is_resolved`, `call_construct` or `ambiguous_names` filter. Because a barrier *prunes* the walk, that copy did not merely over-register — an unrelated unresolved `x.encrypt()` bound `Fernet.encrypt` and **deleted a real flow**. INV-finoh's own `root_cause` and `fix` text named **two** sites, "the helper and its DDG mirror"; the fix landed at the helper, and its closure evidence was gathered on `propagate_taint_structural` — not the function Python routes through (L53: name the instrument, then verify it is the one production runs). Now routed through the shared `_register_sanitizer_callers`, so the two cannot drift again. Repro pinned in tests: identical four-node graph, one unresolved bare method call added on an **intermediate** hop (a collision on the source function does not reproduce it — the seed is exempt from the barrier check by design) takes the DDG pass from 1 finding to 0 while structural holds at 1.
  - **`sink_callers` was a dict keyed on the calling symbol, so a function calling two sinks reported one.** Populated by assignment, each sink overwrote the last; present in **both** passes. Now a list per caller consumed through a shared `_iter_sink_sites`, so neither loop body changed and the two passes share one implementation.
  - **Measured on the 9-repo cohort, instrument asserted in both directions first** (`OK both_fixes_live` on the fix, `BAD:sanitizer_gate_unfixed,sink_overwrite_structural,sink_overwrite_ddg` against the prior tree): flows **149 → 193** (+47, caddy 34 → 68 where `printEnvironment` calls both `Printf` and `Println` and only one was reported; kserve 13 → 18; alertmanager 14 → 15). **One claim verdict changed: `pretix/host-secret-no-network` `confirmed` → `violated`** — a claim reported as *satisfied* while it was being violated. The three apparent removals in pretix are **not** removals: its `untrusted-input-no-database` verdict sits at exactly 100 evidence rows in both arms, an output cap over ~741 flows, so added findings displace which 100 are shown. Neither fix can remove a flow.
- **ADR-0017's precision half is documented but does not run — six artifacts, each closed `done` on "N tests, 100% coverage".** `_sink_module_compatible` (retired last release) was the first found member of a family, not an isolated case. Measured 2026-08-02: **§3a DDG propagation** builds its forward index `ddg_forward` and its tainted-variable set `tainted_at` and reads neither, so DDG data selects only a confidence *label* and has never influenced which flows are reported, for any language, since March 2026; **§4a/§4b function summaries** (`infer_summary`, `load_function_summaries`) have zero production callers; **§7a field-sensitivity** (`is_field_tainted`) has zero production callers; the **Rust and TypeScript def/use extractors** have zero production callers *and* their CFG mappings lack `atomic_statement`, so they would emit zero DDG edges even if invoked; the **§3c mixed-coverage verdict** is never emitted, because `TaintFlowFinding.confidence` reaches no consumer (`verify_claims` hardcodes the string `"approximate"` and the evidence dict omits the field). A second, independent blocker under §3a: `DdgEdge.def_block` is a **function-local** block id (`bb_5` recurs in every function — 306 distinct values across 1,306 functions) compared against a *symbol* id, so that branch can never fire.
  - **The documents said otherwise and are corrected in place.** ADR-0017's phase table opened with *"All originally-planned phases have shipped"* — deleted, and the table gains a **Runs?** column stating per phase whether it affects output. The `Status:` line now discloses the four unimplemented sections, because `docs/adr/README.md` requires that *a grep of the status line must not lie*. §3a's numbered algorithm is marked **NOT IMPLEMENTED — target design** at its head rather than in a trailing note, since a fragment read of the steps would otherwise be indistinguishable from a description of the code; the correction also records that step 5 is underspecified in the load-bearing way (*"if tainted data reaches a sink"* must mean *a tainted variable is an argument at the sink call site*). The spec's claims that DDG-backed tracking is live "currently Python, Rust, TypeScript", that it "eliminates false positives", that the solver "consults a function summary", and that field-sensitivity applies are each marked specified-not-implemented. **Note the recurrence this closes:** last release's `_sink_module_compatible` correction edited the very paragraph containing *"→ DDG-backed propagation with module filtering"* and left that phrase standing — the correction pass reproduced the defect it was correcting.

- **`_sink_module_compatible` is retired — ADR-specified, described as live by four docstrings, and never called.** Added 2026-05-14 with **zero production callers** — its only reachability was six unit tests, which is exactly what held it at 100% coverage and kept it invisible. Meanwhile the contract it was written for was implemented by a *different* function (`_lookup_named_entry` → `io_boundary._module_matches`) with different internals, and the two drifted: the live one was bidirectional *substring* containment until WI-zazul and ungated resolved edges entirely until WI-damir.
  - **Harvested before deleting.** The dead function was **more correct** on one point: it exempted both `external` *and* `<external>` from module filtering, per ADR-0017 §3a, while the live path tested only the bare spelling. So an `<external>` hint fell into the module-*filter* branch and was compared as though it were a real module name — matching nothing and **suppressing** the finding, the opposite of the ADR's "degrade to short-name matching, because rejecting outright would suppress legitimate findings". `<external>` is not hypothetical: the pretix cohort emits `javascript:<external>:0-0:file:external_symbol` for receivers no DDG resolution can recover. Both spellings now live in one `_UNRESOLVED_MODULE_PLACEHOLDERS` frozenset, which is what stops the pair drifting apart again. **Measured on the 9-repo cohort: zero change** (272 flows / 13 non-realizable, identical, 0 added and 0 removed) — the instrument check confirms the exemption is active, so this is a real null rather than a stale read: `<external>` occurs there as flow *seed symbols* rather than as callee module hints reaching the matcher. The change is justified by ADR-0017 §3a conformance and pinned by a unit test; its practical impact on real corpora is **unmeasured, not zero**, and it is the only permissive-direction change in this series so it can only ever add flows.
  - **Four docstrings described a mechanism that does not run**, including `taint_refine.py`'s **module** docstring — that pass's stated reason for existing was *"the rewritten dst then participates in `_sink_module_compatible` directly"*.
