The genuine finding: `_annotate_node_centrality` looked up `centrality.get(n.get("id"), 0.0)`, where `n.get("id")` is `Any | None` and a `None` key can never be present — so an id-less projected node silently scored `0.0`, indistinguishable from a node genuinely at 0.0.
  - Annotations are the measured types, not blanket `Any`: adjacency maps are `Dict[str, set[str]]` of node ids, `UnionFind` elements `list[str]`, frequency counters `Counter[str]` (and `Counter[int]` where the key is a tier). `dict[str, Any]` appears only where the value really is heterogeneous projected JSON — a blanket `[Any, Any]` fill would satisfy the checker while laundering unknown types as declared ones, which is worse than the missing annotation.
- **The whole-tree mypy strict ratchet now BLOCKS merges (WI-rabum), and its blocker turned out to be a defect in the ratchet rather than a reason to wait.** The documented ramp — "grind 1080 → 0, *then* flip CI to blocking" — reads as a hard dependency and is not one: `check-mypy-ratchet` fails only when an error code **grows** against the baseline, never on the absolute count, so 1080 errors and 0 errors gate identically. `--mode=strict` exits 0 on dev today. The cost of the old rung is on the record: WI-basat existed because seven errors accumulated silently over ten days, not because anyone ignored a failure but because **a non-blocking warning is by definition a signal nobody must act on**.
  - **The one risk the item had to weigh is now closed rather than accepted.** WI-rabum's open question was a mypy release recategorising errors and blocking every PR at once, independent of any code change. Investigating it found something worse and older: the baseline recorded `total` and `by_code` but **not the mypy version that produced them**, while CI installed `mypy~=2.1` — which admits 2.2. A shrink-only baseline whose measuring instrument can change under it was never a ratchet; in warning mode a release could have moved every number and nobody could have told tooling drift from code drift. So the baseline now records `mypy_version`, the ratchet **refuses to compare counts across versions** (a distinct `INSTRUMENT MISMATCH` diagnostic instead of reporting six unrelated codes as "grown" — the most confusing possible way to fail), and the pin is tightened to `~=2.1.0` so a bump is a deliberate edit that must carry its own `--update-baseline`. The mismatch blocks **even when counts shrank**: a shrink measured by a different instrument is not evidence of progress, and banking it would ratify tooling drift as a drain.
  - **The risk was not hypothetical — it was already live, and this PR's own CI proved it.** The dev extras in `hypergumbo-core` and `hypergumbo-tracker` declared `mypy~=2.1`, which admits 2.2 and 2.3; a fresh CI install resolved **mypy 2.3.0** while the baseline had been measured locally at 2.1.0. So the instrument already differed between a developer's machine and a clean install, silently, with the ratchet comparing counts across the two and reporting the difference as code drift. The dev extras are now pinned to `~=2.1.0` alongside the CI job, so one series is used everywhere; **adopting a newer mypy is now a deliberate change that must carry its own re-measured baseline**, which is exactly what the mismatch check exists to force.
  - **The flip is four edits, three of them individually silent — so all four are pinned by a static guard.** Removing `continue-on-error` makes the job red and changes nothing on its own, because the merge verdict is `ci-complete` and a job absent from its `needs` cannot fail it; being in `needs` is itself insufficient, since `ci-complete` runs `if: always()` and decides by explicitly testing each result. That is the same shape the old rung had — a job that looked like a gate and gated nothing. `tests/test_check_mypy_ratchet_blocking.py` pins no-`continue-on-error`, membership in `ci-complete.needs`, that the verdict actually reads `needs.mypy.result`, `--mode=strict`, the patch-series pin, and that the committed baseline records its instrument. Two revert paths were mutation-tested. Disclosed gap: `ci.yml` is not a mapped source, so editing *it* selects no tests (the INV-kinin class) — a silent revert is caught by the twice-daily full suite, not per-PR.

### Fixed
- **PHP emitted only classes — `interface`, `trait` and `enum` were all invisible (INV-tihim).** Three of PHP's four type containers had no branch in the symbol walk, so an `interface` declared in the same file surfaced as an **`external_symbol`** (the boundary synthesizer's placeholder for a name with no in-tree definition), and members declared inside an interface or trait were emitted **bare** — `area` rather than `Shape.area` — because the enclosing-owner walk recognised `class_declaration` alone while the sibling `_enclosing_type_name` helper already walked all four. Fixed with **one branch keyed off a container→kind map** rather than three copies of the class branch, so a fifth container cannot be added to `_PHP_TYPE_CONTAINERS` and silently skipped. **All three of PHP's stacked defects fall out of that one change:** the interface becomes a real node, its member is owner-qualified, and the relationship self-corrects from `extends`→`external_symbol` to **`implements`→`interface`** — because the inheritance linker classifies the edge by the *target's* kind, which is why Java gets this right despite lumping extends and implements into the same `base_classes` meta. PHP now emits `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area` and its parity cell is promoted from hole to hard pass.
- **TypeScript records `abstract` on abstract classes (audit-findings 0018).** Five languages (java, csharp, php, scala, kotlin) mark an abstract class `kind="class"` + `modifiers=["abstract"]`, which is where `is_abstract_type` reads it; TypeScript emitted an empty modifier list, so its abstract classes were indistinguishable from concrete ones. Nothing had to be inferred — the grammar hands the analyzer a distinct `abstract_class_declaration` node and the Symbol simply never set the field.
- **C++ emits pure virtual methods, and a class declaring one is marked abstract (audit-findings 0018).** The member walk filtered out every function declaration, so `virtual int area() = 0;` vanished entirely and an abstract base had no method to dispatch *from*; `modifiers` was likewise empty on every C++ symbol. Both closed. **Deliberately scoped to pure virtuals (`= 0`)**: a non-pure declaration (`virtual int perim();`) has its definition in another translation unit and hypergumbo has no decl/def merging for C++, so emitting those would produce two symbols for one method across a header/source split — a pure virtual has no definition anywhere by construction and cannot duplicate. Guarded by a test asserting non-pure declarations are still skipped.
  - **C++'s parity cell stays red, on a different cause, and the reason is worth recording.** Every ingredient is now present — `Shape::area` exists, containment roots it, the class carries `abstract` — and no `dispatches_to` appears, because `type_hierarchy._extends_admits_dispatch` asks whether the **child** is abstract while a C++ abstract-base hierarchy has the abstract **parent** (`extends: Square -> Shape`, with `Square` concrete). The rule is correct for the case it was written for (Go interface embedding and C# interface inheritance, where both endpoints are abstract so the two tests coincide) and wrong for abstract-base inheritance. Widening it changes dead-code reachability across all four `NO_VIRTUAL_EXTENDS_LANGUAGES`, so it is filed rather than bundled here.

### Fixed
- **Rust gets precise interface dispatch, and the member-name separator vocabulary gets one home (INV-tihim).** `linkers/type_hierarchy` — the language-neutral linker that emits interface-method → impl-method `dispatches_to` for every language — split qualified member names on `#` and `.` but **not** `::`, so `_get_method_short_name("MyTrait::method")` returned the whole string and `_get_class_name_from_method` returned `None`. It was structurally incapable of firing for Rust, which is the reason a Rust-specific dispatch linker exists at all. Rust now emits `dispatches_to: Shape::area -> Square::area`, matching every other language, and its parity-column cell is promoted from a strict-xfail hole to a hard pass **in the same PR** — which is what the strict marker forces.
  - **The fact now has one home, and it was recorded in six places.** New `hypergumbo_core.member_names` owns `MEMBER_NAME_SEPARATORS = ("#", "::", ".")` plus `split_member_name` / `member_owner` / `member_short_name`, and six language-agnostic sites were swept onto it: `linkers/containment`, `linkers/method_call_recovery`, `linkers/type_hierarchy`, `receiver_blind_magnets` (whose private copy was ordered `("::", "#", ".")` — wrong, since Ruby's `Foo::Bar#baz` splits at the `#`, so it would have returned `Foo` as the owner), and three sites in `linkers/route_handler` that knew only `("#", ".")` and so could not index a `Type::handler` route. A live-tree linter with a planted-offender test prevents a seventh copy.
  - **Deliberately NOT folded into `qualified_name_axis`.** That module maps a language to its *module-path* separator (`php` → `\`), while this one is the owner↔member separator in `Symbol.name`. They coincide for Rust and diverge for PHP, whose members are emitted dotted — folding them would silently mis-split every PHP member. The linter therefore exempts any literal containing a separator outside this vocabulary (a `\`-bearing set is asking the other module's question), and exempts per-language analyzer packages, where knowing your own language's separators is correct rather than a re-declaration.
  - **`linkers/rust_trait_dispatch` narrowed rather than retired, and no edge is lost.** It fanned `dispatches_to` from a trait to *every* method of implementing structs — deliberate over-approximation, since a Rust trait impl looks unreachable to a naive call graph and the alternative was declaring ~3,000 live functions dead. Now that the shared linker covers the trait-required methods precisely, this one skips them and keeps only the inherent methods the shared one cannot reach. **An empty requirement set means "cover everything", not "cover nothing"** — an external trait, or an analyzer that failed to emit members, falls back to the old behaviour. Measured on three fixtures: trait method → precise `method:Shape::area -> Square::area`; inherent method → `trait:Shape -> Square::helper` retained; marker trait with zero requirements → `trait:Marker -> Square::helper` retained.
  - **The ADR-0023 §3 allow-list entry added under INV-fimon stays, and is now permanently legitimate.** The `(trait, rust) → (method, rust)` partition still carries both `contains` and `dispatches_to`, because inherent-method dispatch keeps the trait anchor. The ADR's own caveat — that the entry's removal was conditional, not guaranteed — is the condition failing, as written.

### Added
- **`constructed_from` swept to six more languages, with a parity column and the first framework YAMLs that key on it (WI-nopod).** The Python pilot proved the fact could be recorded; this makes it usable. **`fastapi.yaml` and `flask.yaml` now tag `app = FastAPI()` / `Flask(__name__)` as `concept: application` and `APIRouter()` / `Blueprint()` as `concept: router`** — the first framework integrations written against a *construction* site. The contrast is the point: `aiohttp.yaml` reaches its `Application` with a plain `base_class` rule because you subclass it; FastAPI's is constructed, and until now no rule could reach it at all.
  - **A parity column first, and it changed the plan.** "Eight analyzers need the key" was the scope until `test_constructed_from_parity` measured it. There are **two** hole shapes, not one: six languages were **KEY MISSING** (the analyzer emits the binding and just doesn't stamp the callee — javascript, typescript, go, swift, rust, clojure, all now closed), and three are **NO SYMBOL** (ruby, php, elixir emit no module-level variable at all, so there is nothing to stamp). The second is a prerequisite gap of a different size, filed separately rather than bundled. Holes are strict xfails carrying which shape they are.
  - **One shared helper, not six copies.** `analyze.base.constructed_from_callee` owns the rule and its three narrowings for every tree-sitter analyzer; Clojure's s-expression form is the one genuine exception. The helper falls back to a leading name-shaped child when a grammar names no callee field — which is how **Swift** works without a special case, since its `call_expression` is simply `(simple_identifier, call_suffix)` with no fields at all. Go needed its own unwrap: the `value` field resolves to an `expression_list` rather than being absent, and assuming absence is why Go silently recorded nothing on the first attempt.
  - **The expected callee is per-language, and a single expectation was silently wrong.** Rust's construction idiom is an associated function (`C::new`), Go's is a constructor function (`NewC`), JS uses `new C()`. The column carries the expectation per language after a shared one quietly mis-passed Rust.
  - **Caught by measuring end-to-end, not by review:** the first version of the YAML patterns was appended to the end of `fastapi.yaml`, which put them inside the `linkers:` list rather than `patterns:`. yamllint passed, the file parsed, and `load_framework_patterns` returned **zero** of them. A regression test now asserts the loader actually sees them, because nothing else would.
- **`Symbol.meta["constructed_from"]` — the framework-object binding, which nothing recorded (WI-nopod).** A whole class of frameworks is configured by *constructing* an object rather than decorating or subclassing one (`app = FastAPI()`, `Base = declarative_base()`, `runner = CliRunner()`), and a YAML author had no key to write a pattern against. Python now stamps the callee on the bound variable, qualification intact (`fastapi.FastAPI`, not `FastAPI`) so a namespaced callee stays distinguishable from a same-named local. Registered on the `symbol_meta` axis; **`docs/schema.json` regenerated**.
  - **The item's diagnosis was wrong in a way that changed the fix's module.** It reported that the matcher "lacks call/instantiates/constructor_call entry families." The matcher has had a call surface since 2026-01-30 — `UsagePatternSpec` matches `UsageContext` on `(kind, name, position)` — four months before the filing. But ADR-3ccc's model is *"a first-party symbol appears **inside** a framework call"*, and every `position` it defines is an input slot (`args[1]`, `args[0].handler`, a map key, `default`, `block`); WI-nopod's cases invert that, since the symbol to tag is the call's **result**. The item's own alternative — "the substrate already has ~7,939 `instantiates` edges" — fails for a related reason: measured, those are anchored at the **file** (*"this file instantiated FastAPI somewhere"*) and the `app` variable carried no link to `FastAPI` at all. Both proposed fixes matched nothing because the binding was never recorded.
  - **Chosen home is `Symbol.meta`, mirroring `meta.base_classes`** — both record what type a symbol relates to, as metadata a linker can later promote to edges. The decisive property: **the matcher needs no change**, since the existing `meta_match` field takes a `{key: regex}` map over `Symbol.meta`. That is asserted by test, not inferred. The alternatives each cost an ADR ruling — a result-position `UsageContext` widens `symbol_ref` from "the symbol being used" to "the symbol produced by this call" and adds a fifth pattern category to an ADR whose status is already *Implemented*; a new edge type is ADR-0023's territory.
  - **Deliberately narrow in three ways, each guarded by a test.** Only a call result is stamped, so the key stays useful as a filter (`TIMEOUT = 30` carries nothing). Factory and constructor calls are treated identically, because static analysis cannot distinguish them and asserting "this is a class" would be a guess. A **computed callee** (`factories[key]()`, `registry[0].Build()`) records nothing rather than a partial name — a YAML matching on a fiction would fail silently, so absence is the honest signal. Python only: nine analyzers emit `UsageContext`, each with its own hardcoded call-name allowlist, and sweeping all nine before one is proven is the failure mode this codebase has the most scar tissue about.
  - **Found while testing: `meta_match` applies `regex.match`, not `search`.** The natural-looking `(^|\.)FastAPI$` silently fails on `fastapi.FastAPI`; `(.*\.)?FastAPI$` is the form that works. Pinned in a test with an explicit tripwire, so the next author keying on a qualified callee does not rediscover it the hard way.
- **G2 parity column for interface dispatch — and it found five holes nobody was looking for (INV-tihim).** `test_interface_dispatch_parity.py` injects the same fixture into 16 languages (one abstract type declaring one required method, one concrete implementor) and asserts on the **emitted artifact** after the full pipeline: does a `dispatches_to` edge run from the abstract type's method to the implementor's method? Uniform fixtures mean an empty cell is an analyzer or linker gap, never construct-absence. Measured: **8 hard passes** (java, csharp, go, typescript, scala, swift, groovy, python) and **8 strict-xfail holes**, each carrying its measured cause and an owning tracker item. One pipeline run over a polyglot corpus rather than 16 runs — 0.7s instead of ~30s, with per-language xfail granularity preserved.
  - **The item that produced this column named one of the eight causes.** INV-tihim was filed as "one fact, two homes" — the `::` separator divergence — which is real and is *Rust's* cause alone. Probing four languages found three unrelated causes; probing sixteen found **eight**, of which three are producer gaps (php, cpp, elixir), two are consumer-vocabulary gaps (solidity, ruby), one is an anchoring choice (rust), one is a missing edge (dart), and one is an unrelated analyzer crash (kotlin — this fixture has two bodied type declarations, which is exactly the shape that makes that analyzer emit zero type symbols). Generalizing from the cause in hand is the failure this column exists to make impossible.
  - **Two of the holes are the same disease as audit-findings 0018, one layer over — on `Edge.edge_type`.** `edge_types.py` exports a registry-backed `INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES = {extends, implements, inherits}`; only `slice.py` and `ranking.py` consume it. `cli.py` hand-rolls a complete copy, `_transitive_bases.py` omits `inherits`, `inherited_calls.py` has `includes` but omits `inherits`, and `type_hierarchy.py` branches inline on two string literals and never consults the registry at all — **five vocabularies, all disagreeing**, against an ADR-0023 §3 instruction to call `edge_types_on_axis(...)` instead. Solidity emits `inherits` and Ruby emits `includes`; both are invisible, so neither gets dispatch despite emitting every symbol correctly. Filed as INV-nosoz rather than folded, since ruby's value is absent from the grouping while solidity's is present-but-ignored — fixing either alone leaves the other broken.
  - **Built to resist the ways this kind of gate goes quiet.** End-to-end rather than analyzer-only, because WI-duguk's column read green for Swift while the live slice was unmoved. Corpus generated into `tmp_path_factory`, because the pipeline suppresses linkers under `tests/fixtures/` paths and because a gate that preserves defects cannot host its corpus inside the tree the project ratchets against itself. Holes are `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` markers, never imperative `pytest.xfail()` — mutation-tested: marking a passing language as a hole XPASSes and fails the run, forcing the entry's removal. Plus a liveness floor, a non-vacuity floor (≥6 hard passes, so the column cannot go green by becoming all-xfail), a scope-disjointness check, and a declared not-applicable map with reasons.
- **`Symbol.kind` gains a type-family taxonomy, and Swift protocols get interface dispatch for the first time (audit-findings 0018 / INV-tihim).** The audit established the vocabulary was sound and the defect was a missing *predicate*: 138 values on one flat axis, so `symbol_kinds_on_axis()` could not express "the abstract types" and every consumer wrote a literal instead. `SymbolKindSpec` now carries `type_family` (`abstract_type` for `interface`/`trait`/`protocol`; `concrete_type` for `class`/`struct`/`enum`/`record`/`object`/`union`/`contract`), with `type_like_kind_names()`, `abstract_type_kind_names()` and `is_abstract_type(kind, modifiers)` as the `Symbol.kind` analogue of `edge_types_on_axis()`. **Deliberately not a new axis under ADR-0024** — no IR field changes and no value is reclassified; it is a taxonomy over the already-declared `language_construct` axis, so nothing touches `stable_id` or the v9/v10 scheme sequencing.
  - **Measured behaviour change.** `linkers/type_hierarchy` gated virtual dispatch on `child_kind == "interface"` — one of the three inherently-abstract kinds — so a Swift `protocol` produced `implements` and **no `dispatches_to` at all**, while the byte-equivalent Java `interface` produced both. That gate is now `is_abstract_type(...)`, which also reaches `abstract class` through the modifier, something the literal could never express. End-to-end on a one-interface/one-implementor fixture, Swift goes from **no dispatch edge** to `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area`, matching Java exactly. Four sites swept onto the resolver: `type_hierarchy.py`, `inheritance.py` (which held a *correct* abstract set at line 91 and an incorrect one at 426), `_transitive_bases.py` (a mutable-default literal replaced by a `None` sentinel resolved from the registry), and `cli.py`'s class-meta index.
  - **A linter so the copy-a-sibling's-set mechanism cannot recur**, with two principled exemptions rather than an allow-list. `find_partial_abstract_family_literals` flags any language-agnostic literal that enumerates *part* of the abstract family, but exempts (a) expressions guarded by a sibling `language` comparison (`s.language == "graphql" and s.kind in (...)` is a per-language predicate that merely lives in core) and (b) modules whose declared `@register_linker(depends_on=...)` languages cannot produce the missing kind. That second rule is what separates a defect from a correct set: `jackson_dispatch` and `kafka_streams_dispatch` declare `depends_on=[["java"]]` and omit `trait`/`protocol` **correctly** — no Java symbol is ever either — while `type_hierarchy` declares scala/rust/swift among twelve languages and omitting `protocol` is the measured bug. Of eleven literals the naive rule flagged, exactly **five** survive as real defects. The kind→language map backing it carries a written re-evaluation trigger (R10): a new analyzer emitting one of these kinds for an unlisted language silences the linter for it.
- **Audit-findings 0018 — `Symbol.kind` type family: the leak hypothesis is REJECTED and the real defect is a missing consumer predicate (INV-tihim).** Seven language-agnostic consumers hand-roll mutually inconsistent sets of "type-like" kinds, and `linkers/type_hierarchy.py:113` gates virtual dispatch on `child_kind == "interface"` alone, so Rust `trait` and Swift `protocol` fall outside a rule the neighbouring linker gets right. The suspicion was that `class` is apex/peer overloaded — naming both a concrete class and an abstract type — which would have meant folding values, an ADR-0027 amendment, and `stable_id` churn behind the v9/v10 scheme gate. **Measured across 11 languages, it is not:** every analyzer emits `class` for a class *declaration*, and abstract-ness is carried on a different field — `Symbol.modifiers == ['abstract']` in java/csharp/php/scala/kotlin, derivable from `meta.base_classes` in python. All six values in scope are **CANONICAL**; no fold, no ADR change, no scheme gating.
  - **The defect is enumeration-completeness, which the four leakage tests are explicitly not designed to catch.** `Symbol.kind` carries 138 values on exactly one axis (`language_construct`), so `symbol_kinds_on_axis()` cannot express "the abstract types" — unlike `Edge.edge_type`, whose ADR tells consumers outright to call `edge_types_on_axis(...)` instead of maintaining their own list. With no expressible grouping, every consumer writes a literal. An AST walk over every `packages/*/src` set literal found **47 sites carrying ≥2 type-like kinds, 26 under a strict all-members-registered filter, spanning 24 distinct vocabularies** — against a manual sweep that had found 7.
  - **`protocol` is missing from five language-agnostic sites** (`type_hierarchy.py:493`, `inheritance.py:426`, `_transitive_bases.py:51`, `di_resolution.py:669`, `cli.py:6629`) while three others are complete — so `inheritance.py` holds a correct set at line 91 and an incorrect one at line 426. Per-language analyzer sets are legitimately incomplete (Java has no traits) and are excluded. **Proven consequence, same construct one word apart:** a Java `interface` yields both `implements` and `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area`; a Swift `protocol` yields `implements` and **no dispatch at all**. The propagation mechanism is recorded in a comment — `type_hierarchy.py:493` says the set was copied "to match the inheritance linker's broader definition," and the copy inherited the omission. WI-duguk added `protocol` to two of the seven sites the day before and swept none of the rest.
  - **A second, orthogonal gap:** `modifiers` is the right home for abstract-ness but is under-populated — typescript emits `abstract class` with `modifiers=[]`, and cpp emits a pure-virtual class with `modifiers=[]` while dropping abstract method declarations entirely. Remedy is a registry-backed type-family predicate (the `Symbol.kind` analogue of `edge_types_on_axis()`), a sweep of the five sites, and a linter so copying a sibling's literal cannot recur — **not** a new axis under ADR-0024, since no field and no value classification changes.

### Fixed
- **The G1 validator ratchet was RED on `dev` and no per-PR run reported it (INV-fimon).** `test_validation_report_empty.py::test_substrate_within_ratchet_baseline` failed on all four substrates, carrying **2** un-allow-listed ADR-0023 §3 `runtime_coherence` offenders against a shrink-only baseline of **1**, alongside a stale Go route-extraction assertion. Neither is a new regression and both were invisible for the same reason: smart-test selects `test_validation_report_empty.py` only when a file it *imports* changes, and the manifest committed on `dev` did not contain it, so every merged PR passed CI over a red gate until an unrelated annotation batch touched `cli.py` and pulled it into scope. This is INV-kinin's first measured consequence with a blocked PR attached — a gate whose selection is content-dependent reports green for the wrong reason.
  - **Provenance measured, not inferred.** The second offender, `(trait, rust) → (method, rust)` carrying both `contains` and `dispatches_to`, was introduced by **WI-duguk (`9baaa9afbd`, "emit enum variants and trait members")**: checking out that commit's parent and re-running the corpus yields exactly **1** offender, the declared `(file, rust) → (external_symbol, rust)` one. The item's own framing ("not clear what was producing it"; "pre-existing") was right about the counts and wrong about the attribution window — the edge predates the *session* that found it, not the *change* that caused it.
  - **Ruled legitimate and allow-listed, deliberately narrowly (ADR-0023 §3 amendment).** The two edge types do not even share a destination — `contains` names the trait's own requirement (`MyTrait::method`), `dispatches_to` names the implementation (`MyStruct::method`) — so this is two distinct relationships over one endpoint shape, the same reasoning that admitted the file-anchor entries, not an endpoint property leaking into `edge_type`. The entry rules on the **partition only** and explicitly does not ratify the anchoring that made it necessary; the follow-up fix would *shrink* the allow-list rather than contradict it. The baseline stays at 1 and was **not** raised — a shrink-only baseline lifted to accept a breach ratifies the drift.
  - **Root cause is one level deeper, and is the familiar shape: one fact, two homes (INV-tihim).** `linkers/type_hierarchy` is the framework-agnostic linker that emits interface-method → impl-method `dispatches_to` for every language, and it **cannot fire for Rust at all**: its `_get_method_short_name` / `_get_class_name_from_method` split on `#` and `.` but not `::`, so a `MyTrait::method` returns `owner=None`, while `linkers/containment`'s `_SEPARATORS` knows all three. `linkers/rust_trait_dispatch` exists to cover that gap, and because trait members did not exist when it was written it anchors dispatch at the **trait** rather than at the trait's requirement. Its docstring said so — *"hypergumbo's Rust analyzer does not emit the trait's required-method list, so distinguishing trait-required methods from inherent methods would require SCIP-level information"* — and WI-duguk made that false. The docstring now records the supersession instead of the stale rationale.
  - **A second stale rationale removed.** The in-test note explaining why the surviving offender was pinned in the baseline rather than allow-listed said doing so would be a "governance-touching ADR-0023 allow-list amendment." It is not: neither `docs/adr/**` nor `docs/edge-type-runtime-allowlist.yaml` is on the AGENTS.md governance list. That sentence is what made the breach look unfixable in-band.
  - **The Go assertion tests a convention that no longer exists.** `BRANCHES_test_go.py::TestGoRouteExtraction::test_route_handler_as_selector` read `"GetUser" in n["name"]`; WI-zugob moved route-marker names to `"{METHOD} {path}"` (so multi-method registrations at one span keep distinct ids) and handler identity to `meta`. It now asserts on `meta["handler_name"]` — measured, not assumed: the item proposed `meta["handler_ref"]`, which is the key *other* producers use and does not exist on the Go path. Every other route-name assertion in the tree already reads the post-WI-zugob convention.
- **Scala, C++ and Kotlin emit enum members — the three languages the G2 parity matrix structurally cannot see (WI-dorop).** Each emitted the enum container and none of its members, so the containment linker had nothing to root and a reverse slice from the enum returned the container alone: the WI-duguk defect, in the analyzers that sit outside the gated set. Measured end-to-end, all three go **`contains_out` 0 → 3** and **`slice --entry Color --reverse` 1 node → 4**. Member naming follows each analyzer's own convention, because the containment linker splits on the first separator it finds: scala `Color.Red`, cpp `Color::Red`, kotlin `Color.RED`.
  - **Kotlin's container is a `class`, not an `enum`** — `enum class` is emitted as `kind="class"` with an `enum` modifier, and there is no `kind="enum"` anywhere in that analyzer. The member loop therefore gates **structurally on the presence of an `enum_class_body` node**, not on the kind or the modifier string. `class` is in both `CONTAINER_KINDS` and the slicer's set so the members root correctly without any kind change — but anything keyed on the enum kind (including the parity matrix's own `_ENUM_CONTAINER_KINDS`) will never see a Kotlin enum, which is why this was verified by containment count rather than an enum-kind query.
  - **Two per-language traps, each of which would have shipped a partial fix.** Scala has *two* member node types (`simple_enum_case` and `full_enum_case`), and `case Green, Blue` is a single `enum_case_definitions` holding two siblings — so the walk iterates cases, not groups; a per-group loop silently drops every case after a comma. C++'s emission sits deliberately *inside* the `if name_node:` guard: an anonymous `typedef enum { P, Q } Anon;` emits no container, so emitting its members would produce symbols whose owner does not exist — orphans by construction, worse than the recall miss.
  - One existing test changed: `test_enum_body_val_emits_field` asserted `_fields(syms) == {"Color.rgb"}` over a fixture that always contained `case Red`. The case now emits, so the set gains `Color.Red` — the assertion was pinning the defect.
- **The G2 parity matrix's scope is now declared and machine-enforced, and the false claim that gated it is corrected (WI-duguk follow-up).** The matrix carried the comment *"All eight are availability='core' (no grammar wheel needed)"*, and it was read as the reason the gate stops where it does — extending it looked like it would drag in optional dependencies. Measured against the live catalog, **only `python` is `availability="core"`**; java, javascript, go, rust, csharp and swift are all `"extra"`, the *same tier* as the scala/kotlin/cpp analyzers the matrix does **not** gate, whose grammars are hard dependencies of `hypergumbo-lang-mainstream` and install wherever it does. Two unrelated senses of "core" had collided — the catalog's "needs no extra dependency" versus the file's prose "the important ones" — and the collision manufactured a dependency barrier that does not exist. The real boundary is **7 of 38 mainstream analyzers, chosen historically and never argued for in an ADR**.
  - **Scope is now three declared maps plus a test that they exhaust the tier.** `UNGATED_CODE_LANGUAGES` names the 14 mainstream code languages this gate says nothing about (php, ruby, scala, kotlin, cpp, groovy, objc, c, bash, lua, perl, powershell, sql, jupyter); `NON_CONSTRUCT_FORMATS` names the 17 config/data/markup/build formats with no class-or-callable surface for the uniform construct set to be uniform *about* — because a row made almost entirely of `COLUMN_APPLICABILITY` exemptions is where a real gap hides behind a plausible excuse. `test_matrix_scope_is_declared_and_exhaustive` fails when a mainstream analyzer appears in none of the three, so analyzer #39 forces a one-line classification at the cheapest possible moment, and a stale entry naming a deleted analyzer fails too. Both directions were mutation-tested rather than assumed: removing `ruby` from the maps and adding a nonexistent `kobol` each produce the intended failure with an actionable message.
  - **Why this matters beyond bookkeeping.** An empty `KNOWN_HOLES` reads as "the fleet is at parity"; it means "every *gated* cell is a hard lock" — the standing lesson that a gate's "0 violations" is bounded by what its fixtures execute. The spec already models the honest alternative, reporting identity coverage as "~41 of ~70" and delegating to ADR-0014's census rather than claiming completeness; the module docstring now states the same way, and a second test fails if that disclosure is ever deleted.
- **Java and C# emit enum constants — the container-member parity matrix is fully drained, 8 holes to 0 (WI-duguk).** Both analyzers emitted the `enum` container and none of its constants; Java additionally emitted an enum's *methods* (`Color.label`), which is what made the gap easy to miss — an enum with one method looks populated to any "does this container have a member" probe while every constant is invisible. Constants become `kind="field"` named `Color.RED`, matching each analyzer's existing class-field naming. Java reads only the direct `enum_constant` children of the `enum_body`, leaving the sibling `enum_body_declarations` (the enum's methods and fields) to their own branches; a constant with arguments (`RED("r")`) carries an `argument_list` after its identifier and is otherwise the same node. Measured: `slice --entry Color --language java --reverse` **2 nodes → 5**, `--language csharp` **1 → 4**.
  - **Two findings recorded in the gate itself, because they generalize past this item.** (1) A container that already emits *some* member kind reads as healthy while another kind is wholly invisible — java and swift enums emitted their methods the entire time — so the parity fixtures deliberately declare enums with members only, which is what makes those cells measure what they claim. (2) The columns measure **one analyzer in isolation** via span nesting, so a green cell is *not* evidence that reverse-slice expansion works end to end: the container's kind must also be in `linkers.containment.CONTAINER_KINDS` and in the slicer's own set. `protocol` was in neither, so swift read green here while the live slice was unmoved. The comment above `KNOWN_HOLES` now says both, and says to re-run the end-to-end repro rather than certify from the matrix.
- **`protocol` was missing from the containment linker's `CONTAINER_KINDS` and from the slicer's container set, so Swift/Obj-C protocol containment was structurally impossible (WI-duguk).** Found by refusing to certify from the unit test: after the Swift analyzer began emitting protocol requirements, the isolated-analyzer tests were green and the parity column read green, but the end-to-end reverse slice from a protocol was **unchanged at 2 nodes**. In the full pipeline the requirements existed and were correctly named (`Drawable.draw`, `Drawable.area`) while the protocol scored `contains_out=0` — with a sibling `enum` and `struct` in the same file each rooting 3 members. No analyzer-side change could ever have fixed it. `protocol` is a registered `Symbol.kind` emitted by both `swift.py` and `objc.py`, and it belongs in both sets for exactly the reason `interface` does. With it added, `slice --entry Drawable --language swift --reverse` goes **2 nodes → 4**. This is the concrete instance of a standing hazard: **a component correct at unit level can be inert in the pipeline**, and an emission-parity column that measures one analyzer in isolation is blind to it by construction.
  - **Three stale comments named `service` as a container kind; none of them was true.** The linker's module docstring listed "services", its `CONTAINER_KINDS` comment said "service for proto (contains RPCs)", and `slice.py` cited containment's set as one that "adds `service`, `message`" — using that as the stated justification for the slicer's set being narrower. The ADR-0027 Cluster-D fold made `interface` + `meta["framework_role"]="service"` the canonical years earlier, and `proto.py` emits exactly that today, so no producer emits `kind="service"` at all. Corrected in all three places rather than left to mislead the next reader into the same audit.
- **TypeScript emits enum members and interface members, draining both of its container-member parity holes (WI-duguk).** The JS/TS analyzer emitted the `enum` and `interface` containers while emitting *class* members (`Sq.side`, `Sq.draw`) correctly, so the gap was per-container rather than per-analyzer. Members now follow the analyzer's own class-member shape — `Owner.member` with `.`, the JS/TS separator the containment linker splits on: an `enum_assignment` (`Red = 'red'`) or a bare `property_identifier` (`Green`) becomes `kind="field"` named `Color.Red`; an interface `method_signature` becomes `kind="method"`; a `property_signature` becomes `kind="field"`, mirroring the class `public_field_definition` branch. Measured: `slice --entry Color --language typescript --reverse` goes **1 node → 4**, `--entry Drawable` **2 → 4**.
  - **Two interface members are deliberately not emitted.** `construct_signature` (`new (x: number): D`) and `index_signature` (`[key: string]: unknown`) carry no `property_identifier`, so there is no name to anchor them to. Skipping them is a fails-safe recall miss; inventing a name would be a wrong-name phantom, which is worse. Pinned by a test asserting the interface's *only* owned member is the named one.
  - **Members are emitted from inside the container's own branch**, not as top-level node-type cases, so the owner name is already in hand and no span- or ancestor-walk is needed to find it. That is what keeps an interface and an implementing class in the same file — same member names, overlapping concerns — from claiming each other's members, which a span-based owner lookup would get wrong; a regression test pins `Drawable.draw` and `Sq.draw` both existing and distinct.
- **Swift emits enum cases and protocol requirements, draining both of its container-member parity holes (WI-duguk).** The analyzer already emitted an enum's methods and computed properties (`Color.label`, `Color.name`) but never its **cases**, and emitted nothing at all inside a `protocol`. The enum half is a worked example of why "the container has a member" is not a safe check: an enum carrying one method already looked healthy on a span-nesting probe while every one of its cases was invisible. Cases become `kind="field"`; a `protocol_function_declaration` becomes `kind="method"`; a `protocol_property_declaration` becomes `kind="property"` rather than `field`, because `{ get }` is a computed-access contract and never storage — the same field-vs-property distinction the analyzer already draws for stored and computed members of a struct.
  - **`case green, blue` is a single `enum_entry` carrying two `simple_identifier` children**, so emission iterates identifiers rather than entries. A per-entry loop would have silently dropped every case after the first comma — the kind of partial-recall bug that leaves the container looking populated. An associated-value case (`case rgb(Int, Int)`) keeps its types in a sibling `enum_type_parameters` node, so the direct-child scan reads the bare case name and nothing else.
- **Rust emits enum variants and trait members, draining both of its container-member parity holes (WI-duguk).** Three distinct gaps behind one symptom, each measured rather than inferred: an `enum_variant` produced no symbol at all; a `function_signature_item` (a trait method with no default body) produced no symbol at all; and a `function_item` *inside* a `trait_item` — a method **with** a default body — did produce one, but as `kind="function"` with a **bare** name (`area`, not `Drawable::area`), indistinguishable from a module-level free function and unrooted by the containment linker, whose parent extraction needs a separator in the name. The third is the one a naive fix would have missed: it was already emitting a symbol *inside the trait span*, so a test asserting only "the container has a member" would have passed against the bug. Variants are `kind="field"` named `Color::Red` (the shape `rust.py`'s own struct-field loop already uses, and the choice the D and Nim analyzers already made); trait members are `kind="method"` named `Drawable::draw`, matching the impl path's `Service::run`. Measured end-to-end: `slice --entry Color --language rust --reverse` goes **1 node → 4**, and `--entry Drawable` **2 → 4** (the old 2 were reached via `implements` edges, not class expansion — so a node-count check on a trait would have read as a false pass).
  - **The trait owner is a separate lookup from the impl target, deliberately.** `_get_impl_target` has six callers, most in call/receiver resolution where it answers "what type is `self` here?" — and a trait is not a type a receiver can be, so widening it would have silently changed how those sites resolve. The new `_get_trait_owner` is used only by the emission paths and stops at the first `impl_item`, because in `impl Drawable for Service` the methods belong to **Service**, not to Drawable. A regression test pins that distinction (`Service::draw` present, `Drawable::run` absent) alongside a guard that a free function acquires no owner.
  - **An existing gate caught a real cross-module break, which is why the fix is two files.** `rust_scip.compute_rust_stable_id_from_source` recomputes rust.py's identities from source so the SCIP-backed rust-analyzer pass and the tree-sitter pass can dedup when both index a workspace (WI-zakub parity). It walked `function_item` only, so the moment rust.py emitted a trait-signature method the parity test failed with `helper=None` — rust-analyzer's SCIP emit *does* index trait method declarations, so these are exactly the symbols both passes see. Both the node-type filter and the owner resolution are now mirrored there. `_extract_rust_signature` likewise accepts `function_signature_item`: that node carries the same `parameters`/`return_type` fields and differs only in having no body, which the extractor never read — and for a trait contract the signature is the member's entire content, so declining to extract it would make the emitted symbol strictly less useful than its impl-side counterpart.
  - **Ratchet promotion, forced not remembered.** The G2 holes are `xfail(strict=True)`, so both rust cells XPASSed and *failed the run* until they were removed from `KNOWN_HOLES` in this same commit. Remaining: 4 of 5 analyzers still emit no enum variants (typescript, java, csharp, swift) and 2 of 6 still miss abstract-type members (typescript, swift).
- **smart-test always runs a test file you edited — root-level `tests/` included.** Two defects compounded. The changed-test pattern matched `^packages/.*/tests/(test_|BRANCHES_test_)` only, so root tests never qualified; and the check lived inside the TDD-mode fallback, which fires **only when nothing else was selected**. A PR touching only root tests therefore wrote `Selected tests: 0` and `ci.yml:811` skipped pytest — and a PR editing a root test *alongside* any mapped source had the source's tests selected while the edited test was silently dropped, which no amount of widening the pattern alone would have fixed. The changed-test set is now computed once and **unioned unconditionally**, next to the WI-jozan top-level union. Found by hitting it twice in a row: the WI-kahoj harness fix touches only `tests/`, and its five new tests were selected by nothing; after the pattern fix, they were *still* dropped because `scripts/smart-test` had itself become a mapped source. That is the third and fourth distinct route to one failure this cycle (after WI-libok's misnamed test file and WI-bisar's 1:1 map), which is what makes it a class rather than a series of accidents: **a test that cannot be selected is indistinguishable from a test that passes.**
  - **`scripts/smart-test` had no tests at all** — the tool that decides what CI runs was itself ungated, which is why a selection gap could persist unnoticed. `tests/test_smart_test.py` starts that coverage by **extracting the live regex out of the script** and running it through `grep -E` exactly as the script does, rather than restating it where a copy could drift. Six cases pin both directions: root tests and `packages/*/tests/` (both `test_` and `BRANCHES_test_` conventions) select; sources, `CHANGELOG.md`, `conftest.py` and the `tests/_forge_github_harness.py` helper do not; and the pattern stays anchored at the repo root so `vendor/foo/tests/test_bar.py` cannot match. The filename is deliberate — the WI-jozan mapper resolves `scripts/smart-test` to `tests/test_smart_test*`, so future changes to the script now select these tests instead of nothing.
- **The GitHub-arm test harness replaces `PATH` instead of prepending to it, so an un-faked tool is unresolvable rather than silently real (WI-kahoj).** `tests/_forge_github_harness.py` stubs the network with fake `curl` and (optionally) `gh` binaries placed on `PATH` — but it *prepended* the stub dir to the inherited `PATH`, which intercepts only the tools it remembered to fake and passes everything else through to the real binary. `bindir_with_fakes(gh=False)` places no fake `gh`, so `/usr/bin/gh` stayed reachable, `scripts/contribute:240`'s `command -v gh` probe succeeded, and the class literally named `TestGitHubFallbackWhenGhAbsent` ran with `gh` present — the real binary calling `https://api.github.com/graphql` and failing on HTTP 401. It passed on CI only because those runners have no `gh` installed: a green that measured the runner image rather than the code. The harness now builds a **shadow directory** (one symlink per executable on the inherited `PATH`, minus `gh`, built once per process in ~400 ms) and runs scripts with the fake bindir followed by that shadow, so the only `gh` a harnessed script can reach is one a test explicitly asked for.
  - **No prepend-side trick was available, which is why the fix is structural.** A shim satisfies `command -v`; a directory or dangling symlink named `gh` is skipped and the lookup continues to the real one; and `gh` lives in `/usr/bin` next to `git`, `sed` and `awk`, so filtering its directory out of `PATH` would take the script's genuine dependencies with it. Replacing `PATH` wholesale is the only formulation under which "no fake `gh` was requested" and "`gh` cannot be found" are the same statement. The alternative — an `AUTO_PR_SIMULATE_*`-style env seam in `scripts/contribute` — was rejected because it puts a test-only branch in production code *and* `scripts/contribute` is a governance file; the harness-only fix needs neither.
  - **A live token could have reached that un-faked `gh`.** The scrub list popped `FORGEJO_TOKEN`, `SELFHOSTED_FORGEJO_TOKEN`, `HYPERGUMBO_FORGE_BACKEND` and `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` — **none of which `gh` reads** — while omitting `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, which it does. All three were unset on the machine where this was found, so the observed failure was an unauthenticated 401; on a developer box with `gh auth login` or an exported `GH_TOKEN` the same test would have driven an authenticated client. All three are now scrubbed.
  - **Scope, corrected downward from the filing.** The item claimed "19 call sites share the premise" and implied a real pull request could be created. Measured: there is exactly **one** `command -v gh` probe in the entire `scripts/` tree, exactly **one** failing test, and the fixture's upstream is the placeholder `https://github.com/up/r.git`, so an authenticated run would have produced an API error rather than a real PR. The 19 figure counted uses of the helper, not exposure to the defect.
  - **Testing.** `tests/test_forge_github_harness.py` pins the guarantee rather than any single caller, because the failure mode is silent — an un-faked tool simply works, so nothing looks wrong. Five tests: `gh` is unreachable when not requested; the **positive control** that a requested fake `gh` still resolves (isolation that broke `gh=True` would trade one silent wrong branch for another); the ten tools `scripts/contribute` actually invokes all still resolve; `git` genuinely *runs* rather than merely resolving; and the three `gh`-native token variables are absent from the constructed environment. The previously-failing `TestGitHubFallbackWhenGhAbsent` now passes for the right reason, and the full root suite is green at 1421 passed.
  - **The WI-bisar ratchet caught this change's own new test**, one commit after it shipped: `test_forge_github_harness.py` covers a *test helper*, a third category the mapper does not claim (it maps `scripts/` and `.agent/hooks/_shared/`). Extending it to `tests/_<name>.py` would be a rule for a category of exactly one — that harness is the only underscore-prefixed module under `tests/` — so it is recorded in `KNOWN_UNREACHABLE` with that reasoning and a revisit trigger, which is the workflow the ratchet's own failure message prescribes.
- **The top-level test mapper reaches 55 of 73 root tests instead of 27 (WI-bisar).** `.agent/hooks/_shared/top_level_test_map.py` exists because a PR touching only `scripts/*` or `.agent/hooks/_shared/*` produces no reverse-slice (that walks `packages/*/src/**`), so `ci.yml` skips pytest entirely. Its rule was 1:1 and exact — `scripts/<name>` → `tests/test_<name, hyphens→underscores>.py` — which reached **one** test per script and left every sibling selected by nothing. The rule is now a **separator-insensitive prefix match**: a source reaches every `tests/test_<stem>*.py` whose stem, reduced to lowercase alphanumerics, starts with the source's basename reduced the same way. `scripts/auto-pr` goes from 1 test to **11**, `scripts/bakeoff-deep` from 1 to 6, `scripts/agent-supervisor` from 1 to 2.
  - **Both halves of the rule are load-bearing, and the obvious one is the wrong one.** The fix as originally filed was plain prefix-matching, asserted to "pick up all ten immediately". Measured, it picks up **zero**: `scripts/auto-pr` collapses to `auto_pr` while its test files are named `test_autopr_*`, and `test_autopr_x` does not start with `test_auto_pr`. Prefix-matching alone raises reachability 27 → 43 while leaving the motivating case at 0. Dropping separators before comparing is the half that actually fixes it, and it was not in the filing.
  - **`_shared/` now accepts `.sh` as well as `.py`.** That directory holds **13 shell helpers against 8 Python ones**, so requiring `.py` there left most of a directory the module claims to cover unreachable — while `scripts/` already accepted `.py`, `.sh` and extensionless names. The asymmetry was an omission, not a decision; `tests/test_touch_heartbeat.py` was dark despite `_shared/touch_heartbeat.sh` sitting right next to it. `scripts/lib/` stays unmapped, because that one *is* a recorded decision in the module's own docstring (generic sub-script basenames over-match), and overriding it silently would be the wrong direction.
  - **Widening selection surfaces latent tests, which is the point and has a cost.** `scripts/contribute` previously mapped to nothing (no `tests/test_contribute.py` exists) and now reaches `test_contribute_github.py` — a test that fails on any machine with `gh` installed, because its harness prepends the fake bindir to `PATH` rather than replacing it, so `TestGitHubFallbackWhenGhAbsent` never actually makes `gh` absent and the real binary calls the live API. Verified pre-existing on clean `dev` in an isolated worktree; CI runners have no `gh`, so it passes there and this change does not break CI. Filed as its own item rather than fixed here.
  - **Severity, stated accurately: this is a feedback-latency gap, not a coverage hole.** `full-suite.yml`'s `test-agent-infra` job runs `pytest tests/` — the whole root directory — twice daily, so these tests were never unrun. What they were is unrun *before merge*: a regression in the script that merges every PR in this repo could land on `dev` and stay green for up to ~12 hours. That cuts against "keep `dev` green and deployable at all times" without being the "no test signal at all" the gap first looked like.
  - **Testing, and why this survived so long.** The mapper's existing tests build isolated tmp trees "so they do not depend on the repo's current test layout" — deliberate and defensible, and precisely why nothing ever compared the mapping against the repo's actual filenames. The unit was correct while the wiring was inert. Added: five tmp-tree tests for the new rule (multi-file match, separator-insensitivity, prefix-anchoring so an unrelated name stays out, `_shared/*.sh`, and a stem that normalizes to empty — without which `"anything".startswith("")` would select the entire root suite), plus a **two-sided reachability ratchet measured against the real tree**: a newly-added unreachable root test fails, and an exempt test that becomes reachable also fails so the list cannot rot. The 18 remaining exemptions carry a verified reason each, in three groups — `scripts/lib/` (deliberately unmapped), hook paths outside `_shared/`, and tests named for the *behaviour* they pin rather than the *file* they cover. That last group is the floor of any name-based rule: `test_bakeoff_resolve_workdir_prefix` covers two scripts, `test_workflow_cli_invocation` covers `check-schema-coverage`, and `test_dead_code_prospector`'s name is a prefix *of* its source rather than the reverse. Reaching them needs a declarative "covers:" marker, not a cleverer heuristic.
- **`auto-pr` no longer amends a commit the branch does not own (WI-libok).** `git checkout -b feature dev` leaves `HEAD` pointing at **dev's tip**, and until the branch makes a commit of its own an `--amend` does not amend "my work" — it rewrites the *inherited base commit*, folding whatever is staged into it while keeping that commit's message, author and author-date. auto-pr amends `HEAD` at three sites (manifest refresh, and two tracker-ops exclusion paths) and none of them checked ownership, so work merged impersonating whatever the base tip happened to be. Live instance 2026-07-29: dev's tip was a tracker sync commit, so seven files of analyzer work landed on dev under the subject `tracker: 6 files (104 ops)` — `git log` for that change now finds a `tracker:` commit. All three sites now call `_assert_branch_owns_head`, which refuses when `git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD <base>` holds. That predicate is chosen over counting `base..HEAD` because it asks the question that actually matters ("is HEAD already contained in the base?") and stays correct after auto-pr rebases onto a moved base; it accepts several candidate refs and **fails open** when none resolve, since a guard that cannot locate a base ref must not block an otherwise-valid PR.
  - **The filing blamed the wrong mechanism, and all three of its proposed fixes were wrong.** It was filed as "tracker auto-sync ran `git commit --amend` on a feature-branch commit", which sent the fix at the tracker. The tracker contains **zero** `--amend` calls: `do_sync` has built its commit with plumbing (`read-tree`/`write-tree`/`commit-tree` against a temporary index, no checkout) since 2026-03-06, five months before the incident, precisely so feature-branch state cannot leak into a sync. So (a) "auto-sync must never `--amend`" was a **no-op**. (b) "auto-sync should defer while `.git/PR_PENDING` exists" is **orthogonal** — the sync's fast-forward landed sixteen seconds *before* the branch was created, so nothing was concurrent. (c) "capture the commit body before auto-pr begins" is **already the behavior** (`DESC` is read at `auto-pr:1428`, the amend is at `:1430`); the git-note net recorded a bare sign-off trailer not because it read too late but because there was never a good message to read — `HEAD` was the inherited commit from the moment the branch was created.
  - **The decisive evidence was an absence.** The damaged branch's reflog reads `checkout -> commit (amend) -> checkout`; every healthy branch that day reads `checkout -> commit -> commit (amend) -> checkout`. The missing plain `commit:` entry *is* the signature — 23 minutes of work and no commit of its own. Corroborated by the objects: the merged commit is a **child** of the tracker commit rather than a replacement of it (an amend would have inherited the tracker commit's parent), and both carry the identical author date that `--amend` preserves while issuing a fresh committer date. Nothing about the defect was tracker-specific; the same hole would impersonate a feature, docs or release commit just as readily, so the item was re-titled off its tracker framing.
  - **Testing — and the filename is load-bearing.** The suite is `tests/test_auto_pr.py` rather than a descriptive name, because `.agent/hooks/_shared/top_level_test_map.py` (WI-jozan) maps a changed `scripts/<name>` to exactly `tests/test_<name with hyphens→underscores>.py`. A change touching only `scripts/auto-pr` produces no reverse-slice (that walks `packages/*/src/**`), and smart-test's TDD fallback scans only `packages/*/tests/`, so a root-level test under any other name is selected by nothing and `ci.yml:811` skips pytest outright — the ratchet would have been **vacuous in CI while passing locally**. Naming it to the mapper's contract makes it run. Surfaced in passing: `tests/test_auto_pr.py` did not exist before, so the ten pre-existing `tests/test_autopr_*.py` files (141 tests) are still selected by nothing when only `scripts/auto-pr` changes — the WI-jozan mechanism's stated purpose is unmet for the script it most needs to cover, since the map is 1:1 rather than prefix-matched. Filed separately; widening it is a governance change.
  - **Testing.** `tests/test_auto_pr.py` **extracts the guard's real definition out of `scripts/auto-pr`** and sources it into a one-shot harness, rather than restating the body inline as the sibling `test_autopr_result_sentinel.py` does with a comment conceding the copy must be hand-updated — extraction means the pinned behavior is by construction the shipped behavior. Both arms are measured (no commits of its own → refuses; own commit, including after a rebase onto an advanced base → silent), and `test_amend_without_a_guard_really_does_impersonate` is the **positive control**: it reproduces the damage from scratch with no tracker involved, so the passing guard tests are known to be measuring something git actually does. It was the only test green before the fix. A fourth test is a **ratchet** — it locates every `git commit --amend` in the script and fails if one is not preceded by the guard, so a newly-added unguarded amend cannot land silently. Not established, and recorded as such: whether the 2026-07-29 amend was issued by auto-pr's manifest step or typed by hand. The reflog cannot distinguish them, and the guard closes the hole either way — which is the argument for fixing it structurally rather than adding a rule about it.
- **The mypy strict baseline is honest again — drained to the truth rather than raised to it (WI-basat).** `.ci/mypy-strict-baseline.json` recorded **1083** while a clean `dev` measured **1087**, so every PR opened with a `REGRESSION` banner for errors it did not introduce. Establishing "is this mine?" cost a stash, a whole-tree re-measure and a per-code diff — on the WI-zugob migration, of a reported `arg-type: 104 -> 106` and `type-arg: 445 -> 452`, only two were the PR's. **Re-baselining upward was declined:** `--update-baseline` is documented for use after a PR *reduces* the surface or after an owner-approved config change, and the ratchet is shrink-only by construction, so raising the ceiling would both ratify the drift and reproduce the condition that caused it — the same reasoning that refused to baseline `cross_field|info` at 9 in the entry below. Instead the drift was measured exactly (a worktree at `dc17b03597`, the commit that last wrote the baseline, re-measured at **1083** — confirming the instrument is reproducible and the baseline was honest when written) and drained: **7 errors had landed since, 3 had been fixed by other work**, netting the +4.
  - **The 7 are two families, not the "mixed bag spread across unrelated modules" the item feared.** Five are bare generic annotations introduced by recent work — `make_route_symbol`'s `extra_meta: Optional[dict]` and `meta: dict` (WI-zugob), the `distinct_violations: list` / `_seen_flows: set` pair (WI-kikis), and `_prune_grammars_to_used`'s `list[dict]` — each given its real element type rather than a blanket `Any` where one was recoverable (`list["TaintFlowFinding"]`, `set[tuple[Any, ...]]`). The other two are the same `make_typed_stable_id` argument in two languages, and **one of them was a genuine dropped guard**: go.py's interface-method site (WI-vibad) carries a comment saying it mints the typed stable_id "exactly as the concrete-method site does", and then omits the `if norm_sig else None` that *both* concrete sites carry. Left as written, a signature-less method would have formatted `None` into the sha256 input and minted a real-but-wrong id where its siblings honestly emit `None` — precisely the name-only key the typed id exists to avoid colliding on. It now guards as they do. In solidity.py the annotation was the defect rather than the code: `_extract_solidity_signature` is declared `Optional[str]` but has a single unconditional `return sig` and no failure path at all, so the return type is tightened to `str` and the call site's `str | None` disappears at its source.
  - **A stale baseline conceals progress, not just manufactures regressions — and that direction is the more dangerous one.** The remaining delta after the drain is `index: 21 → 18`: three errors fixed by unrelated work since 07-20 that the baseline never recorded. Because the ratchet compares per code, that slack was live headroom — **three new `index` errors could have landed with no warning at all**, where the drift in the other direction at least printed one. The baseline now reads **1080**, three below the number it had recorded since the keystone landed.
  - **Recorded, not acted on: `--mode=strict` does not require a drained surface.** The ramp is documented as "grind to zero → WI-rabum flips CI blocking", which reads as a hard dependency. It is not one — strict mode fails only when a code *grows* against the baseline, and it exits 0 on this tree at 1080 today. A blocking ratchet is what would have prevented this drift, since a non-blocking warning is exactly the signal nobody had to act on; that remains WI-rabum's call to make, with the evidence now on the item.
- **The full-suite self-tree validation ratchet is green again, and the fix went to the label rather than the number (INV-zatug).** The `self-tree-validation` job had been RED on `dev`, blocking the full suite on a matrix of exactly `{'cross_field|info': 9}` — nine ADR-0039 confidence-band violations, every one an *over-claim* above the `ast_call_direct` ceiling of 0.85, with four sitting at exactly **1.0**, the value `confidence.py` reserves on the stated principle that no detection method is certain. The nine came from three producers, and **the obvious fix was wrong for one of them.** The four 1.0 edges are `defines_target` edges minted by `toml_config.py` for `[project.scripts]` entries; they never passed an `evidence_type` at all, so they inherited `Edge.create`'s **`ast_call_direct` default** — "inferred from a direct (non-method) call site" — which a `my-cli = "pkg.cli:main"` line plainly is not. The range check then judged a manifest declaration against a *call-site* band. Lowering their confidence to satisfy that band would have encoded the mislabel as truth: an entry that literally names its target is not a low-confidence inference. Both `toml_config.py` sites (the `[project.scripts]` entry point and the Cargo `[[bin]]`/`[[test]]`/`[[example]]`/`[[bench]]` target path, the latter unexercised by the self-corpus and found by auditing the producer rather than the violation list) now declare the registered **`build_target_main`** pathway — "inferred from a build target's main entry point", which is exactly what both are — and drop the literal so the confidence derives at the `ir.py` chokepoint, landing at the seeded 0.95 and stamping `confidence_source=evidence_derived`. The other two producers were genuine over-claims whose *declarations were already correct*, so there the value moved to the pathway instead: `websocket.py`'s endpoint `references` edge hand-wrote 0.90 while declaring `ast_call_direct` (its declaration is the deliberate ADR-0028 Phase-3 fold, so it stands), and `java.py`'s resolved direct call scaled a hardcoded 0.95 base by the resolver multiplier, publishing 0.95 on a fully-resolved lookup — the multiplier is meaningful and is kept, only its base moves to the registry seed. **Self-tree measured 9 → 0**, so the gate passes with **no baseline change**: `cross_field|info` remains unbaselined at ceiling 0, which is the honest outcome, since baselining it at 9 would have raised a ceiling the gate's own guidance says may only ratchet down.
  - **Scope, stated plainly: this fixes the three producers that fire, not the class.** A static producer audit over every `Edge.create` site passing both a literal `evidence_type` and a literal confidence found **32 out-of-band sites** tree-wide; the self-corpus exercises three of them. The remaining **29** (plus a separate cohort passing computed `<literal> * lookup_result.confidence` expressions on banded pathways) are the unfinished ADR-0039 ruling-1 migration off per-emitter constants, and most are `DEFAULTED` — they never declared a pathway, so each needs a per-site judgment about what it actually is, and some will need new registrations under the ADR-0024 workflow. That is a campaign, not this PR, and it is filed rather than left implicit. The existing per-site items (WI-radim fixed three sites, WI-rafut is one more) are members of that class.
  - **Testing.** Three property tests asserting band membership via `confidence_within_band` against the registry rather than golden numbers, so they follow the seed if it changes; the websocket one sweeps *every* edge the linker emits rather than the one known site, with a non-vacuity guard. The pre-existing `test_edge_confidence` pinned `references == 0.90` — the defect as expected behaviour, the same shape as the WI-kalub and WI-gapir guards — and is corrected to the derived value.
- **`ci-debug logs` can now actually fetch Woodpecker logs, and says what is missing when it cannot (WI-solob).** A failing pipeline was undiagnosable from the agent side: `_github_fetch_job_log` was a stub that resolved the run URL from the commit status and printed "not retrievable". This session hit exactly that cost — a PR failed CI twice, and two blind re-runs were spent before the owner read the log and identified the cause in one line (a stale `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, not the change under review). **Measured finding: allowlisting the CI host is necessary but NOT sufficient** — an unauthenticated GET of the run URL is **302-redirected to the Cloudflare Access login** and never reaches Woodpecker (some clients surface that as a 403). Two independent credentials are therefore required, and the function now uses both when present: `CF_ACCESS_CLIENT_ID` / `CF_ACCESS_CLIENT_SECRET` (a Cloudflare Access *service token*, sent as `CF-Access-Client-*` headers, to pass the edge) and `WOODPECKER_TOKEN` (a Woodpecker API token, sent as Bearer, to authenticate to the app). Both are read from `.env` alongside the existing `FORGEJO_TOKEN` / `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` and never echoed. The server is taken from `WOODPECKER_SERVER` in `.env` and the pipeline coordinates from the `target_url` the commit status already carries, so the host is never written into the repo. **Fetching a log takes TWO calls, not one** — and the reason is a trap: the trailing number in the UI url (`/repos/<repo>/pipeline/<number>/<n>`) is the *workflow index*, not the step, while the logs endpoint keys on the step's **global id**. A path built from the UI url returns 404 however correct the credentials are. So it resolves the step from `GET /api/repos/{repo}/pipelines/{number}` first, then fetches `GET /api/repos/{repo}/logs/{number}/{step_id}`, **auto-selecting the failed step** when no job name is given so `ci-debug logs` lands on whatever actually broke. Two decoding details came from a real response rather than a guess and silently corrupt output if assumed away: entries can carry `data: null`, and each entry is one line with a `line` ordinal, so they must be sorted and joined with newlines rather than concatenated. **When either credential is absent the behaviour degrades to exactly the previous message plus a line naming what to set**, so the script is strictly more capable and never less. `ALLOWED_WEBSITES.md` records the permission as `$WOODPECKER_SERVER (see .env)` rather than a literal hostname, so the allowlist documents the policy without publishing the host.
- **The two equivalent soft file-drop channels now signal identically, and the spec contradiction behind the filing is resolved (WI-zafid).** Two files can vanish from a map for equivalent reasons — one unparseable, one over `--max-file-bytes` — and only one of them said so. A parse-error drop increments a run's `files_skipped`, which `finalize` keyed on to set `limits.partial_results_reason`; an oversize drop lands only in `limits.truncated_files` (`add_truncated_file` never touches `files_skipped`) and set nothing. Same user-visible outcome, one signalled and one silent. `finalize` now triggers on **either** channel, with a non-vacuity control asserting the reason stays empty when nothing was dropped, so "widen the trigger" cannot degrade into "always set it". **The item's other half — that `analysis_incomplete` never trips — is correct behaviour and is deliberately unchanged.** The spec defines that flag as analysis having "terminated **early**", and every drop reaching this path is fail-open: the run completes over everything else, and even the analyzer-crash rule explicitly says "and continue". What the filing actually found on that side is a **spec self-contradiction**: `partial_results_reason` was documented as "present only when `analysis_incomplete: true`", while the 🟩 analyzer-crash behaviour prescribes setting that reason *with the flag unset* — so the documented constraint forbade a state the spec elsewhere mandates, and no implementation could satisfy both. The **crash rule is the correct one** (it follows from the flag's own "terminated early" definition), so the constraint was the defect and is removed; the field now documents that partial results are routinely produced without early termination and that a consumer must read it rather than infer completeness from the flag. Worth recording because the first fix attempted here was the opposite one — binding the flag to the reason, which would have satisfied the constraint while contradicting the crash rule; reading the third passage is what caught it.
- **Python cyclomatic complexity no longer counts `with` as a decision point (WI-gapir).** `_compute_cyclomatic_complexity` counted `with` / `async with`, and its own docstring listed "with statements" among the decision points — so the deviation was stated rather than hidden, which is what made it decidable. A context manager introduces no alternative path through the function; it is setup/teardown, not a branch. Both the schema description and the spec commit to "**McCabe** cyclomatic complexity (decision points + 1)", and the reference implementations of that definition (flake8's `mccabe`, radon) do not count it. **This is consumer-visible**: it lowers `Symbol.cyclomatic_complexity` on any Python function using a context manager — 7.9% of them on the self-corpus — and therefore shifts which clusters `repeat-finder --min-complexity` treats as trivial. That is the *correct* consequence: a straight-line function whose only "branch" was a `with` genuinely is a trivial cluster. **Python-only by construction** — `BRANCH_NODE_TYPES["python"]` is empty, so Python uses this AST path exclusively and no tree-sitter language shares the change; every other `cyclomatic_complexity` assertion in the tree belongs to a language with its own computation. Blast radius in tests was measured before changing anything and came to exactly one assertion: `test_cyclomatic_complexity_with_context_managers`, which pinned "with statements add 1 each" and so encoded the defect as expected behaviour — the same shape as the WI-kalub guard that had to be rewritten rather than resurrected when its clause was deleted. It is **inverted**, and paired with a **positive control** asserting that real branches *inside* a `with` block still count, so a too-broad change that stopped descending into the block would fail rather than pass.
- **The entrypoint-confidence contract is rewritten from measured ground truth (WI-logad, WI-hofav, WI-sohov).** Three items, one section, and every claim in it was wrong in a different way. **The tier table omitted the base that most entrypoints carry.** It listed 0.99/0.95/0.85/0.80/0.70/0.50 and named neither 0.75 nor 0.90 — but `library_export` emits **0.75** and is the single most common entrypoint kind on hypergumbo's own corpus (45 of 118, 38%), so the documented table was missing the plurality case; 0.90 (`FORM`, `SERIALIZER`) was missing too. Both rows are added, each annotated with the `evidence_type` that carries it (WI-logad). The 0.70 and 0.50 rows are **kept** despite being unexercised on this corpus — they are live producer constants (`naming_heuristic` at `entrypoints.py:1062`, `connectivity_heuristic` at `:1368`), and deleting a tier because one corpus does not exercise it is the mistake the INV-lifik guard warned against. **The claimed 1:1 mapping between `evidence_type` and confidence does not hold** — measured, it fails for three of the four emitted pathways: `framework_pattern` carries {0.95, 0.90}, `structural` {0.85, 0.80}, `language_convention` {0.80, 0.75}; only `manifest_declared` {0.99} is single-valued. The base is chosen per *detector*, and several detectors share one pathway at different bases, so the spec now states that `evidence_type` is a **coarser grouping** and that consumers must read `confidence` directly rather than infer it either way (WI-hofav). **The discrete-vs-continuous contradiction WI-sohov filed was two descriptions of what are now two different fields.** The section still said penalties apply to `confidence` and that ADR-0039's move to `rank_score` was "slated" — it shipped (WI-lutad / WI-dojor). `confidence` is now pure detection reliability holding only the base; `rank_score` takes the ranking adjustments (test ×0.1, vendor tier ≥ 3 ×0.3, utility ×0.5, plus an additive `min(0.25, log(1 + out_edges) / 10)` boost, from which deliberately demoted entries are excluded so an additive boost cannot undo a multiplicative demotion). Both are emitted on all 118 entrypoints. **Sorting was documented on the wrong key**: the spec said "ranked by confidence", `entrypoints.py:1600` sorts by `rank_score`, and the emitted array is verifiably ordered by `rank_score` and *not* by `confidence`. The spec and the two producer docstrings that repeated the stale claim are corrected. Docs only; no emitted value changed.
- **The three in-map tool-version surfaces are reconciled, and `confidence_model` now matches the grammar the spec mandates (WI-tinul, WI-huhin).** **(1)** `__version__` surfaces under three names, and the spec named two. `reproducibility_context.hypergumbo_version` and `analysis_runs[].version` emit it bare (`7.0.0`); `limits.analyzer_version` emits it prefixed (`hypergumbo-7.0.0`). The Version-fields taxonomy documented only the first and third, and — worse — the `AnalysisRun`/`AnalysisPass` field examples showed `"hypergumbo-0.1.0"`, a prefixed form the producer has never emitted, so a consumer joining `analysis_runs[].version` against `limits.analyzer_version` for an equality check failed on a prefix the spec told them to expect on both. The examples now show the bare emitted form, and the taxonomy names all three with the discriminator stated: **only `analyzer_version` carries the prefix**, deliberately, because it is the one surface naming the *producing tool* rather than reporting the version of a record the tool already authored. Consumers joining them must strip it. Docs-only; no emitted value changed (WI-tinul). **(2) `confidence_model` emitted `hypergumbo-evidence-v2` against a documented grammar of `hypergumbo-evidence-vMAJOR.MINOR`** (Appendix C, "Semantic versioning"), which assigns MINOR a specific job: "Refinements (new evidence types, score adjustments)". A bare MAJOR makes that channel unexpressible — so ADR-0039's refinement, which added evidence types and is exactly what MINOR exists to signal, had no way to announce itself, and the next refinement would have had to choose between a misleading MAJOR bump and silence. **This is a consumer-visible value change**: the field now emits `hypergumbo-evidence-v2.0`, the first *conforming* rendering of the **same** model — MAJOR is unchanged and no scoring behaviour differs, which the spec now says explicitly so `v2.0` is not misread as a refinement of `v2`.
