The value was fixed rather than the grammar because the grammar is a considered versioning taxonomy defining MAJOR/MINOR across three version families, while the bare constant is one nobody revisited; reconcile toward the artifact carrying the decision. Blast radius was one producer constant and two assertions. A format ratchet (`test_confidence_model_matches_documented_grammar`) pins the shape rather than the literal, so a legitimate future bump passes and only a malformed value fails (WI-huhin).
- **Four spec passages corrected against what the code actually emits (WI-vuton, WI-liruk, WI-mikin, WI-latip / WI-tubim).** Each was a place a consumer following the spec would have been wrong. **`nodes[].origin` was documented twice, incompatibly** — §6 defines `origin: list[str]` (INV-jidat, schema-breaking change from scalar at SCHEMA_VERSION 0.10.0) while the output-fields appendix still said "String, pass ID that created this node". The appendix now describes the list and points at §6 as canonical, so the field has one definition rather than two (WI-vuton). **The `profile` example documented a `repo_kind` field no code produces** — a fresh self-survey emits no such key, and it appears nowhere outside that example, so it is removed rather than implemented (WI-liruk); this is the same phantom-declaration class as the `total_files`/`total_lines` removal in the entry below, arriving from the prose side instead of the schema side. **`metrics.debug` was described as carrying "two" counts while emitting three** — `profile_files_sum` was undocumented; it is now documented *with the property that makes it non-interchangeable*, namely that it sums `profile.languages[*].files` and so counts a file once per claiming language, over-counting relative to the path-deduplicated `unique_paths_in_analysis` sitting beside it (WI-mikin). **`not_captured` names two disjoint fields under two parents** — `limits.not_captured` (analysis-coverage categories static analysis never captures anywhere) and `reproducibility_context.not_captured` (determinism factors, the disclaimed L3–L5 levels). The spec disambiguated only the `limits` side; the reciprocal note is added, so a reader arriving at either lands on the distinction. **Documented rather than renamed**, with the reasoning recorded: both are already declared in `docs/schema.json` with distinct descriptions, each name is correct within its own block, and a rename would be a breaking change purchasing no disambiguation the parent path does not already give — the verdict reached for `edge_key` vs `stable_id` (WI-niboh) and for the `sha256:` surface shared by `stable_id`/`shape_id` (WI-tisar). WI-latip and WI-tubim are the same defect filed twice.
- **`docs/schema.json` documents its own registered meta vocabulary, and `profile` matches what the producer emits (WI-zisig, WI-zamum, WI-sirir, WI-sizis, WI-mitob).** Five filed items, one root cause each side of the same contract: the schema was hand-authored where a registry already existed. **(1) The `meta` blocks described 2 of 79 registered keys.** `axis_meta_keys.META_KEYS` registers 32 edge-axis and 47 symbol-axis keys; `Edge.meta` hand-listed **2** and `Symbol.meta` — a bare `Dict[str, Any]`, so the generator emitted a contentless `{"type": "object"}` — listed **0**. So keys that are registered, emitted and spec-documented were absent from the schema: `resolution_quality` (1076 self-corpus edges, WI-zisig) and `channel` (145 edges, WI-zamum). Both `meta` blocks now derive their properties from the registry, so a newly registered key documents itself instead of accruing the next filed item. Edge.meta 2 → **34**, Symbol.meta 0 → **47**. **Entries carry a description and deliberately no `type`**: `MetaKeySpec` records none, and the values genuinely are not uniform (`refresh`/`disambiguation_fallback` are booleans, `referring_paths` a list, most strings), so emitting `"type": "string"` across the board would convert a documentation gap into a *wrong constraint*. Because `additionalProperties` stays open on both blocks, this changes no validation outcome and needs **no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump**. The two curated entries keep their richer hand-written text (`evidence_type` carries the ADR-0028 axiom and the `x-axis-of-values` map, which the registry does not). **(2) `profile` disagreed with its producer in both directions.** It declared `total_files`/`total_lines`, which nothing emits (WI-sizis), and omitted `dev_frameworks`/`framework_mode`/`framework_evidence`, which it does (WI-sirir). The phantom pair is **removed rather than populated** — precedent sits in the same file, where WI-bozid removed the phantom `lines`/`percentage` keys from `languages[*]` for exactly this reason, and populating them would mint a second home for a derivable fact (per-language counts already live in `profile.languages[*].{files, loc}`, repo-wide counts in `metrics`), the duplicate home the WI-palon ruling declined. **(3) The spec documented a `usage_contexts[].id` prefix that has never been emitted** — `"uc:..."` against the producer's `usage:sha256:<16hex>` (`ir.py:1080`), so a consumer following the spec's filter got 0 matches (WI-mitob). The **spec** is corrected, not the emitted value: no code or test anywhere expects `uc:`, the spec's example was the only artifact carrying it, and churning a serialized identity value to adopt the *less* readable of two prefixes would invert WI-talos Ruling 4, which rejected terse contractions precisely because readable prefixes are the point. Guarded by two ratchets: every registered key must appear in the schema for its axis (so registering without regenerating fails here rather than later), and the registry-derived entries must stay description-only with `additionalProperties` open (so documenting can never silently become a closed whitelist).
- **The pyffi linker stops minting FFI edges out of test code (WI-razib / INV-kodan).** It had no test-file awareness at all, and on the self-corpus that made its entire observable output its own fixtures: every one of the 10 `C_stdlib` FFI `calls` edges it emitted (`fopen`/`fwrite`/`popen`/…) originated from `test_pyffi_linker.py`, because no production code here uses ctypes or cffi. Those are phantom edges in the graph, and — per INV-kodan, which files the same defect as a member of the INV-nanon "passes that burn resources for nothing" umbrella — 4.6s of CPU per analysis spent validating the pass against itself. **The fix is the convention ~22 sibling linkers already follow**, the shared `paths.is_test_file` chokepoint. Worth recording is *why* pyffi could not simply adopt `discovery.find_non_test_files` the way the 2026-06-27 linker-evidence-gating sweep did for its siblings: pyffi never walks the repo — it consumes in-memory symbol lists — so the "non-adopter" framing does not fit it and the gate belongs on the symbol path instead. **Both emit phases are covered**, not just the one the filing measured: Phase 1 (the ctypes/cffi file scan) filters the scanned path set, and Phase 2 (PyO3) — which never sees a path, walking existing `edges` and keying on `edge.dst` — matches `edge.src` against the ids of Python symbols in test files. Gating only Phase 1 would have closed the measured half and left the same defect open on the other. Each phase has a test, paired with a **positive control** asserting production ctypes calls still link, so a too-broad gate that silently deleted the linker's real output could not pass as a fix.
- **`smart-test` no longer reports a green verdict when test modules fail to COLLECT (INV-vilag).** It printed `11756 passed … 3 errors`, then `✅ All changed source files are at 100% coverage`, and exited **0** — while three modules never imported, so the coverage claim was computed over tests that did not run. One root cause, in `run_pytest`: the captured invocation ended `… > "$PYTEST_LOG" 2>&1 || true`, and after `cmd || true` `PIPESTATUS` reports the `true`, so `exit_code=${PIPESTATUS[0]}` was **always 0** and pytest's real status (2) was discarded. The verdict then had to be reconstructed from the log, which cannot distinguish a collection-error run: pytest prints `ERROR …` / `N errors`, never `FAILED `, so neither failure clause fires while the affirmative-green clause (`N passed` && ! `N failed`) matches. `run_pytest` returned 0, the caller's `TEST_EXIT -eq 0` gate therefore ran the scoped coverage check, and `exit $TEST_EXIT` carried the false green all the way out. **The fix is the capture, not another grep clause:** pytest's own exit code is preserved via `|| exit_code=$?`, and the affirmative-green clause is **deleted** rather than repaired — it was a no-op while `exit_code` started at 0 and would actively mask a real failure now that it does not. It is precisely the "latent landmine" the WI-kalub comment beside it predicted. The two corrective clauses remain, but they may now only *escalate* to failure. **Testing:** the existing guards in `test_smart_test_coverage_gate.py` are source-level by convention (smart-test is bash and contributes no coverage); the two added here are **behavioral** — they sed-extract the real `summarize_pytest_output` + `run_pytest` out of the shipped script and drive them against a stub `pytest`, so they exercise the code rather than pinning its text. A **positive control** asserts a genuinely green run still returns 0 and still prints the ✅, without which a harness that always failed would make the guard pass while proving nothing. WI-kalub's Step-1b guard is rewritten rather than resurrected: its word-bounded `[0-9]+ failed` match protected the clause that is now gone, so the property is satisfied structurally and the test pins the stronger successor (the exit code is sourced from pytest; `PIPESTATUS` may not reappear in code).
- **The textconv driver never writes to stderr, so one un-compilable op log can't break history for the whole repo.** Fixing the driver's *invocation* (previous entry) took a full-history walk from 1 commit to 1086 of 6343 — then it aborted again, on `Op log has no create op`. That shape is **normal history, not corruption**: promoting or demoting a tracker item moves its op log between tiers, so the segment before the move holds ops whose `create` lives in the other tier's file. `_render_textconv` treated it as fatal, wrote to stderr, and git aborts *any* diff whose textconv driver writes to stderr — so a single such blob anywhere in history broke `git log -p` repo-wide. Un-compilable content now degrades to a `# <id>: op log not compilable at this revision — <reason>` marker on **stdout** with exit 0, the same philosophy `scripts/tracker-textconv` already used for its "package not installed" fallback. Only a genuinely missing *file* stays an error, which git cannot produce (it always hands the driver an existing blob). `test_textconv_corrupt` deliberately inverts from asserting exit 1 to asserting exit 0 plus empty stderr. The new e2e case commits an op log with no `create` op — the input shape the previous gate's fixtures all lacked, which is why its positive control (a deliberately broken *driver*) passed while the *real* driver's failure path went unexercised. Result: a full-history scan now completes **6241 commits / 66.18 MB** where it previously stopped at **1 commit / 3.66 KB** while reporting success. Known cost, not addressed here: textconv spawns an interpreter per blob, so a whole-history `git log -p` takes ~15 minutes.
- **The tracker's git textconv driver actually works: `git log -p` / `git diff` / `git blame` no longer exit 128 on `.ops` paths (ADR-0013).** Two `.gitattributes` families declared *different* diff drivers for the same files — `diff=tracker` at the repo root (installed by `scripts/install-hooks`, pointing at the working `scripts/tracker-textconv`) and `diff=tracker-ops` in the nested `.agent/*/.ops/.gitattributes` (installed by `tracker setup`, pointing at `python -m hypergumbo_tracker.cli textconv`). Git resolves attributes **most-specific-path-first**, so the nested name always won and the root declaration was live-looking dead configuration. The winning driver did not exist: ADR-0013 documents a `textconv` **subcommand** in two places (the CLI table and §"Local Diff Declutter"), but only the `hypergumbo-tracker-textconv` console script had been built — so `setup.py`'s invocation was a faithful reading of the spec against an unimplemented surface. Because git **aborts any diff whose textconv driver writes to stderr**, argparse's "invalid choice" usage dump turned every patch-producing walk into exit 128 — including plain `git log -p` on the repo, which only needs to reach one `.ops` file. That silently truncated history-walking tools: a full-history `gitleaks` scan stopped after **1 commit / 3.66 KB** and still printed "no leaks found". **Fixes, per the chokepoint-not-per-site rule:** `textconv` is now a genuine subcommand sharing one `_render_textconv` body with the console script, so the two documented surfaces cannot drift (and it dispatches early in `main()`, before any `TrackerSet` construction — git calls it once per blob per diff); root, nested, and the git-config key all use the single name `diff=tracker`; `migration.py` writes the driver line for fresh repos; and `tracker setup` now **executes** the configured driver against a real op log, asserting exit 0 *and* empty stderr, instead of only checking that the config key was set. The nested files are kept, not deleted — they carry `merge=union` for standalone `pip install hypergumbo-tracker` repos with no hypergumbo root `.gitattributes`; only the conflicting `diff=` line was accretion. **Also removed:** `setup.py`'s `git config --global` fallback, which leaked a per-repo diff driver into the user's machine-wide config where it applied to unrelated repos and outlived the checkout (it had fired in practice); it now warns with the exact local command instead. ADR-0013 §"Local Diff Declutter" gains the one-driver-name invariant and the specificity hazard that caused this.
- **Test coverage that mocked away the only thing that could fail.** `_check_textconv` had extensive tests, and every one mocked the subprocess while asserting `"python -m hypergumbo_tracker.cli textconv"` was the *correct* configured value — so the suite encoded the defect as expected behaviour at 100% coverage. Three tests were rewritten, including one that asserted the `--global` fallback. Two new files split the concerns deliberately: `test_textconv_git_integration.py` drives real `git` (proving attribute resolution reaches the configured driver and that `log -p`/`diff`/`blame` succeed, with a **positive control** that fails on a stderr-writing driver so a green run can't mean the gate never fired), and `test_textconv_wiring.py` covers the same branches in-process, because subprocess tests contribute no coverage and mocking is what hid the bug the first time.
- **`make_symbol_id` sanitizes the id name-slot `':' → '.'` for every producer (WI-sikar; ADR-0036 Ruling 1) — repairs selector- and coordinate-style ids that were previously unparseable.** The canonical parse is anchored from the right (`span, name, kind = parts[-3:]`), so a colon in the name slot shifts every anchor left by one and the id stops parsing; colons in the *path* slot are harmless and stay verbatim, which is why Rust's `std::cmp` module ids are canonical. Sanitization had been deferred since 2026-06-13 on the premise that an always-on rewrite "would silently churn colon-bearing source identifiers like Objective-C selectors," a premise a guard test enforced. **Measurement reversed it.** Probing the objc analyzer on a single keyword-selector method: `Symbol.name` is `FileManager.removeItemAtPath:error:` and the emitted id was `objc:T.m:2-4:FileManager.removeItemAtPath:error::method` — **seven** segments, unparseable into its ADR-0036 slots. It is now `…:FileManager.removeItemAtPath.error.:method`, five segments. Selector-style languages were the largest victims of the deferral, not its casualties, and every colon-bearing selector id in the fleet was already broken. Full fidelity stays on `Symbol.name`; the substitution is idempotent, so the producers that already call `sanitize_id_name_segment` explicitly (the WI-vuzaf synthetic linker stand-ins, the INV-dulah Maven sites) are unaffected — those calls stay because they pin the right *value* into the slot, while the chokepoint only guarantees the slot is colon-free. **`js_ts.py` stopped re-implementing the grammar**: its private `_make_symbol_id` was an f-string copy — the mirror the item predicted — and delegates now. Consumer-visible for any symbol whose name contains a colon; on the self-corpus that is zero ids (all 7 colon-bearing names already routed through the explicit helper), so the change is a repair for corpora with Objective-C, Swift selectors, or Maven coordinates rather than a churn of this one. The item's second deliverable — a validator sub-check asserting the name-slot *equals* `sanitize(Symbol.name)` — was **not built, on a measured false premise**: that equality fails 1430 of 40855 canonical ids (3.5%), of which 1398 are the documented `make_file_id` sentinel `{lang}:{path}:1-1:file:file` and the rest are four further deliberate conventions (Rust's `::`→`.` *collapse*, the js/ts `@NN` occurrence suffix, the synthetic websocket route normalization, the Maven coordinate fold). The weaker colon-free requirement the grammar actually states is already enforced by `_check_id_format`, upstream of where the sub-check would sit, so it would have been unreachable code.
- **Manifest-analyzer `node.id` slots conform to the ADR-0036 grammar; self-tree `id_format` violations reach zero (INV-dulah).** Six sites across the three manifest analyzers escaped the grammar in two distinct ways, and both are the same class the route sweep drained: N producers, no shared minting contract. **(1) Four kind-slot ROLE fossils.** `toml_config` (pyproject `[project.scripts]`) and `json_config` (package.json `scripts`, package.json `main`, composer.json project) wrote the symbol's *role* or *ecosystem* — `script`, `main_entry`, `composer_package` — into the id kind-slot while `Symbol.kind` was `file` or `package`, so the id round-tripped to the wrong record and named an unregistered kind. Each role already had a home (`meta["entry_role"]`, `meta["package_ecosystem"]`) that consumers read, so the fold is lossless and needs no new vocabulary. Only the toml site was firing on the self-corpus; the other three were found by auditing all three producers rather than by reading the violation list, which is the difference between what a corpus happens to exercise and what exists. **(2) Two Maven coordinate name-slots.** `xml_config`'s dependency and project symbols put a `groupId:artifactId` coordinate into the name slot verbatim. Because the canonical parse is anchored from the *right* (`span, name, kind = parts[-3:]`), that colon shifts every anchor left by one and the id stops parsing — colons in the *path* slot are harmless, which is why Rust's `std::cmp` ids are canonical. Both now route the name through `sanitize_id_name_segment`, the same scoped pattern the six synthetic-linker families use; full fidelity stays on `Symbol.name` and in `meta.groupId`/`meta.artifactId`. Evidence: fresh self-analysis over 40841 symbols goes **9 `id_format` violations → 0** (8 warnings + the 1 error the ratchet baseline had carried as a known open Ruling-1 follow-up since 2026-07-02); both baseline cells ratchet to 0. Gated by a new 17-case property test over all three analyzers with a non-vacuity guard, so a fourth manifest analyzer inherits the contract.
- **`json_config` no longer re-implements the id grammar.** Its private `_make_symbol_id` was an f-string copy of the canonical format — the last of the three manifest analyzers holding its own, which is precisely how it opted out of the shared minter's guarantees. It now delegates to `make_symbol_id`.
- **The id-format validator names a name-slot colon instead of blaming the span.** When an id carried more than five segments, `_classify_id_format_problem` reported `malformed_span_segment` — true of the *parse* but not of the *defect*: the span was well-formed and a name fragment had been shifted into its position. The misdiagnosis pointed at the wrong producer during this investigation, so ids with more than five segments and an unparseable span now report `colon_in_name_slot` with the remedy named. Genuinely malformed five-segment spans still report as before.
- **The remaining route producers mint through the shared chokepoint; the identity ratchet reaches zero (WI-zugob, producer batch 3).** With py.py, the CBV expander, go's mount site, js/ts and play-routes migrated, all four route-marker identity properties hold for **every** producer in the fleet: the gate goes 76 passed / 7 xfailed → **89 passed, 0 xfailed**. Three defects close with it. **(1) A six-segment `node.id` (WI-javag).** The Django class-based-view expander built names by concatenation — `f"{sym.name}.{method_name}"` — inheriting py.py's colon-bearing `django:{view}`, so the colon went unsanitized into `make_symbol_id` and produced `python:urls.py:6-6:django:AdminView.get:function`: an id that could not be parsed back into its ADR-0036 slots at all. Fixed by deriving the name from method+path, which removes the class of bug rather than one instance of it. **(2) go's fifth minting site.** The chi/gorilla `r.Mount()` path still hand-rolled its record, violating both the id kind-slot (unregistered `route_mount`) and the name-slot; it was invisible because the go route-parity fixture is net/http only and never exercised a mount. **(3) A new `flask-restful` fixture case** puts py.py's third route site under the gate — the existing `python` fixture is Flask `@app.route`, which surfaces `concept=route` on the handler and mints no standalone marker.
- **Dead route-vs-function disambiguation removed from the JS/TS resolver.** Two blocks in `js_ts.py` looked a name up in `global_symbols`, noticed the result was a route marker rather than a function, and preferred the same-named function instead. That collision was only possible because route markers were *named after their handlers* — so the naming fix made both branches structurally unreachable (`"GET /users"` is not a valid identifier and can never be an identifier-lookup hit). Deleting them is the point of fixing identity at the producer: the ambiguity the naming created had forced consumers two thousand lines away to carry a workaround for it. Surfaced by the scoped coverage gate, which flagged the blocks as newly uncovered.
- **gRPC and `@hg:route` annotation route markers mint through the shared chokepoint (WI-zugob, producer batch 2) — `node.name` unchanged, `node.id` now canonical.** Both are ADR-0031 Class B *synthetic* markers (fabricated from a protocol definition rather than declared in source), so `make_route_symbol` gained a `protocol_origin` argument: when set, `Symbol.language` becomes `None` and the protocol moves to the typed `protocol_origin` field, while the id lang-slot still names a language so the id stays a parseable five-segment ADR-0036 record. `discovery_language` is an *explicit* caller-owned argument rather than derived from the language slot, because the two are genuinely different judgements — the grpc linker deliberately leaves it `None` (a gRPC route is fabricated from a service definition, not discovered inside a host file) while the annotation linker sets it to the language of the file carrying the directive; deriving it would have silently reversed grpc's recorded decision from a factory two packages away. **gRPC:** the id kind-slot was the literal `route` fossil and the lang-slot was the *protocol* `grpc` rather than a language — now `proto:user.proto:5-5:RPC /demo.UserService/ListUsers:function`. The linker's local `_make_route_stable_id`, a copy of the core function that existed "to avoid a cross-package import", is deleted — that reason disappeared once the linker imports the factory from the same module, and the core function keeps its own test. **Annotation:** the id was a raw f-string of only **four** segments with no language slot at all, plus an unregistered `annotated_route` kind-slot — an id that could not be parsed back into its ADR-0036 slots; now `javascript:svc.js:1-1:GET /users:function`, and the marker gains a `stable_id` it previously left `None` (a null→value gap-fill, additive, no scheme bump per the scheme-bump principle). The `@hg:route <method> <path>` argument splits on the first whitespace run, so both documented forms (`POST join`, `POST /api/users`) keep byte-identical names; a single-token argument is read as a method with the root path via the factory's existing `""` → `"/"` normalization rather than a second naming rule. Drains four more ratchet entries: the gate goes 72 passed / 11 xfailed → **76 passed / 7 xfailed**.
- **Ruby, PHP, Elixir and Swift route markers mint through the shared chokepoint (WI-zugob, producer batch 1) — `node.name` unchanged.** Four more producers stop hand-rolling their route-marker `Symbol` and call `analyze.base.make_route_symbol`: ruby (Rails `resources` / `resource` / plain-verb routes, 3 sites), php (Laravel resource + single-verb routes, 2), elixir (Phoenix `resources` + single/LiveView routes, 2), swift (Vapor, 1). **These four are deliberately name-preserving:** each already set `Symbol.name` to `"{METHOD} {path}"`, which is exactly what the factory mints, so the only output change is the `node.id` **kind-slot** — the ADR-0027 Phase-3 fossil where the id carried the literal `route` while `Symbol.kind` was already `function` (and `route` is not a registered symbol kind, so `id_format` flagged every one of them). Measured before and after on the route-parity fixtures: names byte-identical (`GET /users`, `POST /users`), ids `…:GET /users:route` → `…:GET /users:function`. This drains four entries from the `KIND_SLOT_HOLES` ratchet.
- **Elixir route markers carry producer provenance again (WI-zugob).** Every Phoenix route marker was emitted with an **empty `Symbol.origin`** — no producing pass recorded at all. The cause was not in the route code: `TreeSitterAnalyzer.pass_id` defaults to `""` and the effective id is resolved downstream as `self.pass_id or make_pass_id(self.lang)`, so elixir threading `self.pass_id` into its route extractor stamped the empty string while every *other* elixir symbol got the correct id from the fallback. The route path was the one place that bypassed the resolution. Now passes the module-level `PASS_ID`, with the reason recorded at the call site. This was invisible to the previous gate, which iterated `origin` to check each element was a registered pass-id and so passed vacuously on an empty list — the "missing provenance" half of WI-zugob's own title, hidden by exactly the mechanism it names.
- **Go route markers mint through the shared chokepoint; `Symbol.name` becomes `"{METHOD} {path}"` (WI-zugob, first producer migration).** Four of the five inline minting sites in `go.py` (net/http `HandleFunc`, the gorilla-mux builder chain, chi/gorilla `Handle`, and the framework route path) now call `analyze.base.make_route_symbol` instead of hand-rolling the record. **Consumer-visible:** a go route marker's `Symbol.name` changes from the handler name (`submitHandler`) to the method+path pair (`POST /submit`), which is what java and javascript route markers have emitted since WI-tufil — go was the odd one out. The handler identity is not lost: it stays in `meta["handler_name"]`, which is the key `linkers/route_handler` already resolved against (it never read `Symbol.name`), so route→handler edge recovery is unchanged. The change is forced rather than cosmetic — a multi-method registration emits several markers at one span, so only the method+path pair keeps their ids distinct. This closes the `go` entry in the `NAME_SLOT_HOLES` ratchet: the strict xfail XPASSed on migration and failed the suite until promoted, which is the ratchet working as intended. Behavioral evidence on real Caddy: 3 route markers, 0 violations across all four properties (id name-slot round-trip, id kind-slot, non-empty `origin_run_id`, registered `origin`). The other producers (py.py Django/Starlette/Flask-RESTful, ruby, php, elixir, swift, play_routes, js_ts, grpc, annotation_convention) are unmigrated and stay tracked on WI-zugob. **Correction to this entry as first written:** go's *fifth* minting site — the chi/gorilla `r.Mount("/api/v1", handler)` path, which emits `framework_role="route_mount"` — was missed by this migration and still hand-rolls its record. It was invisible because the go route-parity fixture is net/http only and never exercised a mount; the `go-mount` fixture case added below now holds it under the ratchet.
- **Vendored library exports no longer seed slices, and the three demotion skip-sets become one (WI-batit).** A tier≥3 *vendored* `library_export` could still be the top `slice --entry auto` / `--reverse` seed, so a slice of a Django app started from a bundled `pdf.worker.js` rather than a view. Two independent causes, both structural rather than a tuning threshold. (1) The `library_export` demotion is gated on the entry's language having semantic entrypoints of its own — a carve-out built to protect **first-party** API surface (a Lean library's exports must not be suppressed by six incidental Python helper scripts). Vendored third-party code is never the analyzed repo's API surface, in any language, so the carve-out no longer extends to tier≥3 entries; scoping by supply-chain tier keeps the judgment on the existing axis instead of introducing a second hand-curated vendor-path list. (2) The demotion alone was **not enough**, and this is what the filed "0.75 × 0.3 = 0.225 > the 0.10 floor" framing missed: the connectivity boost is **additive**, so a ×0.1 demotion to 0.0225 plus an in-degree boost of up to +0.35 climbs straight back over the ranking floor. The codebase had already learned this twice — `infra_export_ids` (77 gemini-cli telemetry exports surviving at ~0.14 purely on the boost) and `build_wrapper_ids` (mvnw/gradlew) — so the three now share one `boost_suppressed_ids` set and one skip check rather than a third parallel set. Behavioral evidence on zellij (real repo, identical nodes/edges, only the ranking function differs): the two vendored `termwiz` exports go from surviving at rank 0.60 / 0.57 to filtered out entirely, with the rest of the top-ranked seeds unchanged. The reverse slice resolves `--entry auto` through the same `detect_entrypoints` call, so it is covered by the same fix; the item's proposed blanket "constrain reverse-slice seeds to tier 1" is deliberately **not** implemented, because tier 2 is `internal_dep` — genuinely first-party monorepo code and a legitimate seed — and `--max-tier` already exists as the explicit user knob.
- **Statement-bound names now shadow a colliding module import alias (WI-luhah residual).** The module-attr / variable-ref retarget resolves `cfg.CONFIG` to a real first-party symbol when `cfg` is an in-tree module alias, guarded by a shadow set so a local that rebinds the alias stays phantom. That set was built from `ast.Name`/`ast.Store` assignment targets only — but four forms bind a name *without* one: a module-level `def cfg` / `class cfg` (the statement binds in the enclosing scope), `except E as cfg` (`ExceptHandler.name` is a bare `str`, not a `Name` node), a walrus `(cfg := …)` inside a comprehension (PEP 572 binds in the *containing* scope), and the same `def`/`class` collision one scope out, where a nested read closes over an enclosing statement name. All four left a **confidently-wrong RESOLVED** edge pointing at the module's real symbol — the INV-fahub failure class — and all four were documented in-code as an accepted residual. A new `_collect_statement_binding_names` collects them; the module-scope walk unions it, and `_enclosing_shadow` unions a per-function variant. It is kept deliberately separate from the LEGB `_collect_scope_local_names`, which must keep excluding `def`/`class` names because the scope lookup resolves those to their `NestedDef` bindings directly — the "dedicated per-function shadow collector distinct from the LEGB local_names set" the residual note called for. Every added name can only turn a retarget resolved→phantom, never phantom→resolved, so the change is INV-fahub-safe by construction; a non-vacuous control test asserts an unshadowed alias still retargets, so the shadow cannot silently over-approximate.
- **`Route::match` routes are no longer silently dropped, and HTTP-verb coverage moves to one chokepoint (WI-zunal).** Three verb-vocabulary defects, each of which silently *lost* a link or a route. (1) **`Route::match` vanished entirely.** Laravel declares its verbs as args[0] (`Route::match(['get','post'], '/path', …)`), but the PHP analyzer read the route path from args[0] unconditionally — found an array, not a string, and skipped the call. Not the filed "lossy `MATCH` aggregate persists": the whole route was absent from the map. Every positional argument now shifts by one (path args[1], controller args[2]), and the real verb list is emitted comma-joined as `GET,POST`; a dynamically-built verb list still falls back to the `MATCH` token rather than inventing verbs. (2) **`ALL` was an unrecognised wildcard.** The node-http framework YAML emits `literal:ALL` for `http.createServer` catch-alls, but the OpenAPI linker's inline check knew only `ANY` and `*` — so an `ALL` route never linked where an equivalent `ANY` route would. (3) **Comma-joined multi-method routes could never link.** `framework_patterns` joins a list-valued YAML `method` key with `","` and only the route-materializer splits it back, so the linker compared `"GET,POST"` against a single verb — never equal. All three now route through a new `routes.method_matches()` / `WILDCARD_HTTP_METHODS` in the canonical route accessor, which understands the absent, wildcard (`ANY`/`ALL`/`*`), and comma-joined forms; the OpenAPI linker delegates instead of re-implementing a partial version. The `*` spelling has zero producers and is **kept deliberately** as the conventional wildcard, now documented rather than sitting as an unexplained literal in one consumer. Two filed defects were **not** reproducible and are recorded as such: Laravel `RESOURCE`/`API_RESOURCE` already expands to 7/5 real-verb route symbols (the aggregate only labels the call-site `UsageContext`, which is honest), and Phoenix `resources` already expands to its 7 routes.
- **Spec §12's ceiling-breach cohort is documented as closed (WI-limom, WI-radim follow-through).** §12 still named `build_dependency`, `subdir_include`, and `tree_sitter` as "not yet derived" pathways whose producers emit a reserved 1.0 — stale since WI-radim seeded the two meson pathways at 0.95 (literals dropped) and relabelled the jsonnet import edge off the generic `tree_sitter` mechanism-type onto the already-seeded `import`. The prose now records the cohort as closed and names the one genuine residual (the jsonnet *calls* edge, whose confidence arrives via a computed variable — WI-rafut), and describes unseeded pathways by their actual contract (`derive_confidence` returns `None`, producer keeps its literal, `confidence_source` stays `emitter_constant`) rather than as a fixed three-item list that decays.
- **L3 producer-coherence gate resolves dict-indirection / tuple-unpack emits — and immediately caught an unregistered `Symbol.kind` (WI-zigih).** The producer-side axis walker resolved a `kind=` / `edge_type=` / `evidence_type=` argument only when it reached a literal, a ternary, an f-string, or a Name bound to one; a Subscript RHS poisoned resolution and a tuple target was never inspected at all. So `edge_type = MAP[k]` and `suffix, edge_type = MAP[k]` shipped values the gate could not see — the blind spot behind WI-rorul's unregistered `otp_call`/`otp_cast`. The walker now resolves a subscript over a module-level or function-local dict display (LEGB order) to the union of its literal string values, indexing per-position for tuple-unpack targets, at the assignment form *and* the bare `edge_type=MAP[k]` kwarg site. The posture stays all-or-nothing: one non-literal map value, a starred unpack target, a short value tuple, or a non-Name subscript base is a silent skip, never a partial (and therefore misleading) candidate set. **The hardening was not preventive-only as filed.** On its first run it flagged `Symbol.kind="scalar"`, emitted by the GraphQL analyzer's `type_kinds` dict — the sole unregistered member of a six-way sibling set (`type`/`input`/`interface`/`enum`/`union` were all registered). The cause is self-referential: the corpus enumeration audit-findings 0007 was built from could not see dict-indirected emits, so the value was structurally invisible for as long as the walker was blind. `scalar` is now registered as a `language_construct` (registered, not folded — `scalar Date` is its own GraphQL construct and `type` is already taken by `object_type_definition` in that same dict, so conflating them would fuse two constructs per ADR-0027). Spec §6's registry count corrected 143 → 152 (it had drifted independently); `docs/schema.json` + `docs/concept-axes.md` regenerated; the schema-coverage baseline picks up `scalar` and sheds 29 dead entries left by the pt.31/pt.32 endpoint_shape prune (shrink-only, no toleration loosened).
- **Scala 3 `given` owner symbols → `kind="instance"` (WI-pujiz, REUSE-INSTANCE).** Scala 3 `given` instances emitted body fields (`intOrd.cached`) with no owner symbol, so they orphaned. Following a three-lens ADR / spec / spirit audit (recorded in the lab notebook), the *named* `given` owner is now emitted as `kind="instance"` — the cross-language typeclass-instance canonical already emitted by the Haskell / Lean / PureScript analyzers — rather than minting a new `given` kind (which would fragment that canonical, an ADR-0027 Cluster-27C apex/peer break) or conflating it with `object`. `instance` is added to `CONTAINER_KINDS` (which also roots Haskell / Lean / PureScript instance bodies — a coverage gain, bounded by the same-language + same-file/unique linker gate), and the `instance` kind description now names its spellings. Anonymous givens emit no owner. Completes the WI-pujiz residual (enum owners landed in the previous PR).
- **Annotated `__init__` own-fields captured (WI-sajub, G5).** The Python `__init__` field scan matched only `ast.Assign`, so an annotated `self.x: T = v` (or bare `self.x: T`) was skipped — captured neither into the class's `meta["fields"]` type map nor into `own_field_names`, leaving an annotated own field eligible for a confidently-wrong Site-3 hint to a same-named parent field of a different type. The scan now handles `ast.AnnAssign` as well. (The same-name-class short-name clobber remains the deferred id-keyed follow-up noted in-code.)
- **DataModel rank/confidence separation (WI-gozik, ADR-0039).** `detect_datamodels` mutated each model's `confidence` with a centrality boost (up to +0.15, and the `min(1.0, …)` sum could reach the reserved 1.0 ceiling), conflating a ranking signal with detection reliability. `DataModel` now carries a `rank_score` field: `confidence` stays the pure evidence-derived seed, `rank_score` = confidence + centrality boost, and both the detection ordering and the sketch data-model rendering sort by `rank_score` (the confidence *stats* still sum `confidence`). Mirrors the Edge/entrypoint `confidence`/`rank_score` split.
- **D-enum and Scala-enum owner symbols (WI-pujiz, G7 / WI-sakug Part B).** The D and Scala 3 analyzers emitted enum members / enum-body fields (`Color.red`, `Color.rgb`) but never emitted the enum itself as a symbol, so the containment linker (with `enum` in CONTAINER_KINDS) had no owner to root them under and they stayed orphaned. Both analyzers now emit the `enum_declaration` / `enum_definition` as a `kind="enum"` owner. (The Scala `given` owner stays deferred — `given` has no registered symbol kind, so rooting its body fields needs an ADR-0027 kind or an explicit ruling rather than conflation with `object`; tracked as a WI-pujiz residual.)
- **Read-write `@property` getter resolution (WI-sizut, G5).** A read-write property (a `@property` getter plus a same-named `@x.setter`/`@x.deleter`) dropped the read edge: getter and setter share the qualified name `C.x`, and the name-keyed symbol registries (`symbol_by_name`, the `(path, qualified)` index) are last-write-wins, retaining the setter — whose recorded decorator is the dotted `x.setter`, failing the property-read producer's bare-`property` gate. The Python analyzer now builds a dedicated property-getter index from the pre-collapse per-file symbols (in both the full-pipeline and single-file paths) and consults it first, so `c.x` on a read-write property emits its receiver-type-hinted read edge (resolvable via the inherited-calls linker) exactly as read-only properties already did.
- **Confidence ceiling breach on build-file edges (WI-radim, ADR-0039).** Three producer sites emitted a reserved `confidence=1.0` (the confidence band reserves 1.0 above the 0.95 ceiling for evidence-derived reliability), bypassing derivation: meson `build_dependency` (`dependency('x')`) and `subdir_include` (`subdir('x')`) edges, plus the jsonnet `import` edge. The two meson pathways are now seeded at 0.95 in `_CONFIDENCE_SEEDS` with the literals dropped so `Edge.create` derives them (`confidence_source=evidence_derived`); the jsonnet import edge is relabeled from the generic mechanism-type `evidence_type="tree_sitter"` to the semantically-correct `"import"` (already seeded 0.95) and its literal dropped.
- **C# overloaded-constructor arity resolution (WI-mivav).** `new X(...)` `instantiates` edges collapsed onto the last-registered constructor when `X` had overloaded ctors (the global/local symbol registries are keyed by name, last-write-wins). The C# analyzer now builds a multi-value constructor registry and, at each `new X(...)` site, binds the edge to the overload whose parameter count matches the argument count — falling back to the collapsed pick on a tie/miss (a heuristic, not full C# overload resolution; optional and `params` arguments are not modeled).
- **Synthetic-node id name-slot round-trip (WI-vuzaf Pattern A, ADR-0036 Ruling 1).** Six linker families that mint synthetic call-site / function stand-ins (subprocess, database-query, HTTP-client, GraphQL-client, GraphQL-resolver, message-queue publish/subscribe) stuffed a *generic category constant* (`subprocess_call` / `db_query` / `http_client` / `graphql_client` / `resolver` / `mq_publisher`) into the `{name}` slot of the canonical `{lang}:{path}:{span}:{name}:{kind}` id while `Symbol.name` carried the specific value — so a consumer reconstructing a node's name from its documented stable id got a different string (collapsing e.g. all subprocess call-sites under one label). Each factory now routes `Symbol.name` through the new `sanitize_id_name_segment` helper (colons `':'`→`'.'`, ADR-0036 Ruling 1; the round-trip is documented-lossy, full fidelity in `Symbol.name`) into the id name slot. The helper is scoped to these synthetic stand-ins rather than folded into `make_symbol_id` globally, because an always-on chokepoint would also rewrite colon-bearing source identifiers (e.g. Objective-C selectors `removeItemAtPath:error:`) — that broader landing is WI-sikar. Deferred to a follow-up (recorded on WI-vuzaf): the websocket linker (route-vs-URL naming nuance in its sanitizing wrapper) and the gRPC linker (separate non-canonical `grpc:` id format).

## [7.0.0] - 2026-07-27

### Summary

This cycle hardens the analysis substrate and the CLI contract and completes the edge-vocabulary canonicalization. Two threads dominate. **Symbol-kind emission parity**: field- and variable-kind `Symbol`s now emit across ~20 languages behind a shrink-only parity gate. **Python call-graph resolution** is overhauled — C3-linearization MRO for inherited methods, cross-file `self.method()` / dependency-injection resolution, module-constant / re-export / workspace-sibling resolution to real first-party nodes, function aliases, and closure reachability — collapsing a large class of dead-code false positives; the cross-language receiver-misbind funnels are closed fleet-wide.

Alongside: the CLI standardizes on `--format {text,json}` across all read views behind one schema-envelope gateway with strict input validation (`rc=2` / `rc=1` errors); confidence becomes evidence-derived (ADR-0039) with ranking prominence separated onto a new `rank_score`; supply-chain tiers are redefined as pure distance with `directness` / `ecosystem` stamps (ADR-0041); and the edge/vocabulary axes are canonicalized and de-overloaded (ADR-0023/0027/0028/0031/0038). The ADR-0023 `endpoint_shape`→`relationship` fold **completes** — the long-tail values and the sibling `pending_classification` family both fold to canonical relationships + `meta`, draining `Edge.edge_type` to a single `relationship` axis. `hypergumbo survey` becomes the primary analysis verb (ADR-0042). SCHEMA_VERSION advances 0.14.2 → 0.19.0.

### Added

- **Supply-chain summary gains a `directness` sub-bucket + `limits.tier_filtered_files`. SCHEMA_VERSION 0.18.0 → 0.19.0.** `supply_chain_summary.external_dep` now carries a `directness` breakdown (`{direct, transitive, undeclared, unknown}`) mirroring the existing `ecosystem` sub-bucket — giving the ADR-0041 §2 `directness` meta stamp a report-only reader instead of leaving it write-only (tier-3 nodes outside the manifest-backed languages fall into `unknown`, exactly as `ecosystem` handles missing provenance). And a supply-chain **tier drop** — e.g. a DERIVED tier-4 file excluded by the default `effective_tier=3` filter — now records the dropped file paths in `limits.tier_filtered_files` (present-when-populated); previously the file's symbols and edges vanished with no diagnostic (`analysis_incomplete` stayed False, `failed_files` empty), so `max_tier_applied` said *why* but nothing said *what*.
- **Field- and variable-kind `Symbol`s emit across ~20 languages.** Struct/class/record members emit `kind="field"` anchored to their type and module/top-level bindings emit `kind="variable"`, with declared types in `signature` and access modifiers driving `is_exported`; function-body locals are excluded and fields stay out of call-graph resolution. C#/Java/TS field declarations attach DI/ORM/reactive decorators (`@Autowired`, `[Column]`, Lit `@property`) as `decorated_by` edges.
- **Emission-parity gate with an identity-field column.** A per-`(language, construct)` matrix locks `emits_variable`/`emits_field` coverage as a shrink-only ratchet (skipping constructs a language lacks), and gains a `shape_id` column — its first identity-field cell — closing the blind spot that let a `csharp shape_id=None` regression slip past every standing test.
- **All eight read views standardize on `--format {text,json}`.** `io-boundaries` and `verify-claims` adopt the canonical flag (`--json` kept as an alias); `cache-status`, `catalog`, and `routes` gain `--format json` emitting `{schema_version, view, …}` envelopes. A new `load_substrate()` chokepoint validates every `--input` consumer (malformed JSON / missing `nodes` / wrong `view` → `SubstrateError` `rc=2`; version mismatch warns), `explain` gains `--language` / `--file` / `--first` / `--limit` disambiguation, and `sketch --no-comparison-sketches` opts out of comparison sketches for batch runs.
- **`hypergumbo repeat-finder` — structural-clone / refactoring-lead detection.** A new read-view groups symbols by `(language, shape_id)` to surface copy-paste / extract-helper candidates, activating `shape_id`'s one non-redundant capability over `fingerprint` (spec §367). Trivial clusters (complexity below `--min-complexity`) are dropped and production clones headline, with test-only clones a labeled disclosure bucket; text and a `{schema_version, view: "repeat_finder", …}` JSON envelope.
- **`hypergumbo survey` is the primary analysis verb; `survey.json` the default artifact (ADR-0042).** The canonical filename plus four historical aliases resolve through one `find_survey_in_dir()` discovery primitive and the `load_substrate` read chokepoint (which warns on a legacy basename). `hypergumbo run`, the legacy filenames, and the `behavior_map_io`→`survey_io` module / `run_behavior_map`→`run_survey` function renames all survive as deprecation shims for one minor version; docs rename the artifact concept "behavior map" → "survey" while the on-disk `view` discriminator stays `"behavior_map"` for schema compatibility.
- **Receiver-type-dispatch linker — shared substrate for extension / UFCS calls.** A new Infrastructure linker resolves an unresolved `x.foo()` carrying a `receiver_type_hint` to an extension method or a UFCS free function whose receiver / first-parameter type matches — one receiver-type-keyed search unifying the two non-hierarchy call forms, with a new `ast_call_ufcs` evidence_type. Kotlin extension-call and D UFCS resolution move onto it (the analyzers emit the hint rather than resolving in-place), Scala trait-linearization + Swift superclass / protocol-extension MRO walkers join the `inherited_calls` linker, subprocess→CLI joins expand to Python Fire and argparse subcommands, and Python nested-function resolution walks the full LEGB scope chain. A new Framework linker recovers Caddy's reflective plugin dispatch, emitting `dispatches_to` edges from each module's `CaddyModule()` marker to its handler methods.
- **Route detection & entrypoints.** Route frameworks (Flask, Express, FastAPI, …) import-promote on bare imports without a manifest via a curated allowlist; a new `routes` module centralizes detection behind `route_of()` / `is_route()`; `EntrypointKind` becomes a catalog-derived axis and entrypoint records gain a `meta` provenance dict. SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.2 → 0.14.3.
- **Confidence-separation field substrate (ADR-0039).** `Edge.confidence_source` (`evidence_derived` / `emitter_constant` / `composite`) makes the migration off hardcoded constants machine-readable, and `Edge.rank_score` / `Entrypoint.rank_score` become the ranking-prominence home (initialized from `confidence`, so published values are unchanged until the producer relocation lands); a guard flags any emitter shipping one constant across many edges. SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.4 → 0.14.5.
- **`profile.framework_evidence` traces each declared framework to its importing nodes.** `profile.frameworks` named frameworks with no graph anchor; the framework-to-importer join is now kept as `{framework: [node_id, …]}` (prod, non-test, language-gated), absent rather than empty when a manifest framework never appears in an import edge.
- **Symbol identity & axes.** Route / event / boundary / config symbol ids carry real kind-slots (ADR-0036) with framework role moved to `meta`; a computed `Symbol.visibility` replaces the asymmetric `modifiers` encoding (SCHEMA_VERSION → 0.14.4); the producer-side axis-coherence linter descends nested emit helpers as a shrink-only ratchet; Python variable / field symbols and previously-null callable / config-analyzer declarations gain a structural `shape_id` / typed `stable_id` (additive — no scheme bump, since null→value gap-fills do not alter an existing value); bash external programs surface as a `command_launch` io-boundary cohort; general `.yaml`/`.yml` files get file-anchor nodes; and `slice --entry X --io-boundary <cat>` filters a slice to a boundary.

#### Codeberg → GitHub + Woodpecker CI migration

The GitHub + self-hosted-Woodpecker backend landed as dormant dual-mode code alongside the live Codeberg/AGit path, then **cut over** this cycle — `origin` is now GitHub, PRs and CI run on GitHub + self-hosted Woodpecker, and Codeberg is retained as a passive mirror. The backend, as built:

- **The forge tooling gains a backend-aware GitHub path.** `scripts/lib/forgejo-api.sh` sets `FORGE_BACKEND` from the origin host and dispatches the GitHub-specific divergences (auth headers, `api.github.com` base, `merged_at`, rebase-merge, a Woodpecker-URL log degrade behind Cloudflare Access) to a new `scripts/lib/github-api.sh`, with a shared `resolve_forge_token` for the failover-aware credential and a `poll_ci` that tolerates GitHub's single combined commit-status. The maintainer scripts (`auto-pr`, `contribute`, `ci-debug`) gain matching GitHub write arms — plain-branch push + PR create, `gh pr create` for contributors, Woodpecker-status rendering — all inert on the Forgejo backend, which stays byte-for-byte behavior-preserving.
- **The GitHub maintainer PAT (`HG_GITHUB_TOKEN`) is documented and governance-sanctioned.** `.env.template` documents the dedicated local PAT `resolve_forge_token` reads on the github backend (falling back to `FORGEJO_TOKEN` while Codeberg is origin, so it stays blank-safe today), and AGENTS.md's §Secrets exception list is amended to permit it.
- **CODEOWNERS migrated to GitHub glob syntax + full governance coverage.** The review gate is rewritten from Forgejo/Gitea regex to GitHub globs and expanded from 5 entries to the complete AGENTS.md §Governance-Files surface, kept in sync with that list by a rot-guard test.
- **Woodpecker CI lands as a `.woodpecker/` folder** — a container-based (`python:3.11-bookworm`) port of the GitHub Actions CI, active on GitHub + self-hosted Woodpecker and inert on Codeberg/Forgejo (which reads `.github/workflows/**`). Three workflows: the per-PR gate `woodpecker.yml` (preserving the committed `.ci/affected-tests.txt` smart-test contract and the required `ci/woodpecker/pr/woodpecker` status context), `full-suite.yml` (whole-codebase `--cov-fail-under=100` + the self-tree validation ratchet, on a cron), and `nightly.yml` (the py3.10–3.13 matrix + `integration-test --quick`).
- **The release pipeline runs on GitHub Actions.** `release.yml` builds on `ubuntu-latest` and publishes to PyPI (twine) plus a GitHub Release (`gh release create`, using the Actions-injected `GITHUB_TOKEN`); multi-Python + integration verification moved to the Woodpecker `nightly` cron, and the obsolete Codeberg release-mirror workflow is removed.

### Changed

#### Edge-type vocabulary — `endpoint_shape` + `pending_classification` folds complete (SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.6 → 0.18.0)

- **The long-tail `endpoint_shape` values and the sibling `pending_classification` family fold to canonical relationships + `meta`, draining `Edge.edge_type` to a single `relationship` axis (ADR-0023 §6).** Each value folds to the relationship it is, its construct / protocol flavor preserved in `meta` (protocol-call families → `calls`, `links_to` / `renders` → `references`, template & mixin → `includes` / `extends`, and so on), then the dead registry entries are pruned — 50 → 25 edge types. Consumers already read the canonical types, so behavior is unchanged apart from the label + `meta`; because `implements_rpc` was call-like (traced for taint / I/O / ranking / slice) a shared `is_grpc_rpc_implementation` predicate transfers that coupling to its folded form. A behavior map produced before the migration no longer validates against the enum.
- **`access_mode` applicability census complete.** With both non-relationship axes drained, every one of the 25 edge types is classified applicable-XOR-N/A, so a consumer reading `access_mode=None` can always distinguish "the question does not apply" from "missing data", locked by a total-census property test.

#### Confidence derivation (ADR-0039)

- **Analyzer edge `confidence` is derived from the inference pathway, not hardcoded.** `Edge.create` derives confidence from `evidence_type` when omitted and analyzer sites drop their hardcoded values (`confidence_model` v1 → v2); linker edges keep explicit confidences (a separate match-quality model).
- **Containment and type-hierarchy confidence is separated from ranking, ending the reliability inversion.** The containment `naming_convention` heuristic hardcoded `confidence=1.0` above the structurally-certain `span_overlap`; it now seeds in-band at 0.85 and derives, and the type-hierarchy fan-out dampener + test penalty relocate to `rank_score` (which the ranking filter re-keys onto), so high-fan-out dispatch edges stay demoted from centrality without undershooting the documented floor.
- **Entrypoint `confidence` becomes pure detection reliability; ranking adjustments move to `Entrypoint.rank_score`.** The test / utility / vendor penalties, library-export demotion, and in/out-degree boosts that used to mutate `confidence` in place now accumulate on `rank_score`, with the entrypoint sort, `MIN_ENTRYPOINT_CONFIDENCE` filter, sketch sorts, and slice seed-selection all re-keyed. Spec §12 is reconciled to the implemented model.

#### CLI, metrics & meta-layer honesty

- **Read-view JSON single-sources one envelope and one version**, and `verify-claims --json` now emits a versioned object instead of a bare array (breaking for array-parsing consumers). `explain` errors on an ambiguous symbol name instead of iterating every match, and `io_primitives` catalog completeness is disclosed via a stderr warning when a query targets an `in_progress` language.
- **Reporting lists are present only when non-empty.** The `analysis_runs[]` / `limits` failure lists, `nodes[].quality`, and `resolution_quality` on the unresolved give-up branch are omitted rather than serialized empty or misleading, and `analysis_runs[]` is written in a deterministic `started_at` order.
- **Metrics accounting corrected.** `metrics.languages.<lang>.files` is populated node-derived, `total_files` excludes the `<external>` sentinel, `io-boundaries` `total_io_edges` counts only the verified surface (view schema → 2.0), and the AnalysisRun `repo_fingerprint` field carries the `sha256:` scheme prefix (`repo_fingerprint_scheme` v1 → v2). `compact` defaults to centrality-ranked selection (design ruling D12) and `is_exported` reads from `visibility`.
- **`reproducibility_context.captured.grammars` reflects grammars actually used, not all installed.** It was seeded at map-init with every installed `tree-sitter-*` dist (51 on a typical dev box), ~38 of them for languages that produced zero nodes; a finalize sub-step now prunes it to the grammars whose analyzer pass emitted ≥1 node, and expands the single masked `tree-sitter-language-pack` entry into distinct `tree-sitter-language-pack:<lang>` entries for the pack languages actually used. An ast-only repo (e.g. python) carries no `grammars` key. The `analyzer_identity` cache key stays install-scoped (unchanged — it must invalidate on any grammar upgrade, even unexercised ones).
- **Inline `kind=file` nodes span the whole file, not just line 1.** The `create_file_symbols` path (used by ~13 tree-sitter analyzers — haskell, elm, dart, ocaml, …) minted the file node with a degenerate `Span(1,0,1,0)`, so its structural fingerprint hashed only the first physical line — often a shebang/comment that filters to empty, yielding a needless `null`. It now spans the parsed extent (`end_point`), matching the bash / js-ts inline minters and the synthesis paths; file-node identity is unaffected (`id`/`stable_id` are span-independent). The bash/ts/go/rust/swift file-node nulls the item originally named are spec-permitted honest-nulls from whole-file parse errors (unchanged), and spec §372's stale "bash has no grammar in the pack" note is corrected — bash now resolves via the language pack.
- **rust-analyzer SCIP symbols keep `stable_id` parity with `rust.py` regardless of the working directory.** The SCIP source reader read `doc.relative_path` (a repo-relative path like `src/lib.rs`) against the process CWD, so any survey run from outside the repo root (absolute-path invocations, monorepo sub-roots, CI runners) failed the read, silently skipped the stable_id parity reassignment, and left the SCIP symbol with a raw-moniker id diverging from the tree-sitter anchor — double-counting Rust functions across the two backends. The reader is now anchored at `repo_root` (upholds the byte-parity contract, ADR-0035 v7).
- **`kind="template"` view-template nodes get a `stable_id`.** The view-template linker minted template stand-ins with a `language` but no `stable_id`, and — being neither a producer-stamped kind nor one of the fourteen backstop kinds — they fell through `populate_kind_stable_ids` to `None` (the last real residual of the synthetic-identity umbrella, which has no single stamping chokepoint by design). `template` now backstops through the shared kind-scoped formula, with `kind` folded into the hash so a template stays distinct from a `file` node at the same path.
- **Eight Python-implemented analyzers stamp `toolchain.name = "python"`, not the analyzed language.** `matlab`, `robot`, `racket`, `purescript`, `puppet`, `rst`, `meson`, and `circom` hand-set `AnalysisRun.toolchain = {"name": "<language>", "version": "unknown"}`, mislabeling the *analyzed* language as the *analyzing* toolchain (these are regex/AST Python analyzers with no tree-sitter grammar). They now use `_get_python_toolchain()` (real interpreter version). Cosmetic — `toolchain.name` feeds only the external `run_signature` (no internal consumer; recomputed at finalize), so no schema bump and no functional change.

#### Edge semantics & supply-chain tiers

- **Edge / vocabulary axes canonicalized and de-overloaded.** `access_mode` gains a declared per-edge-type applicability matrix with FFI direction-smuggling moved to a `data_direction` meta key (ADR-0038); `high_risk` narrows to a display-only subprocess marker (ADR-0016); route transport moves to a `route_protocol` axis (ADR-0031); overloaded `edge.meta` keys split to single referents and ten linker vocabularies register in `MetaKeySpec` (ADR-0023/0024); evidence types fold to the canonical registry, `evidence_lang` is central-stamped, and the dead `evidence_spans` field is removed (ADR-0028/0040); linker `pass_id`s conform to the `-linker` suffix; `framework_dispatch` is documented as one coherent dispatch-convention axis; the `receiver` key is documented as a per-language fold-residue; `inherits` is treated structurally by slice and ranking (via an `INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES` registry) so Solidity hierarchies no longer leak into forward slices; and `Symbol.origin` synthesis values become legitimate synthetic pass-ids (ADR-0044).
- **Supply-chain tier names distance only (ADR-0041).** Declared third-party deps drop from tier 2 to tier 3 with the declaration re-emitted as a `directness` stamp (direct / transitive / undeclared) and boundary nodes carrying an `ecosystem` (stdlib / third_party) stamp; in-repo role files (examples / docs / fuzz / bench) and generated routes become tier 1, leaving `internal_package_roots` the sole tier-2 producer. Test-code classification is unified across the framework-dispatch and content-scanning linkers (no more phantom production edges from test-fixture strings), monorepo workspace siblings no longer mislabel as third-party in either the imported-node or the dependency-declaration facet, and `supply_chain_summary` drops a phantom `by_tier` schema wrapper and counts only file nodes in `.files`.
- **ADR-0041 tier-axis follow-through: vestigial config removed, stale `(tier 2)` annotations corrected.** The `SupplyChainConfig.analysis_tiers` field — round-tripped through `to_dict`/`from_dict` but never read (its only consumer was unbuilt ADR-0004 pseudocode; symbol extraction gates on Tiers 1-2 + the ANALYZABLE role via file classification) — was deleted. Four stale `(tier 2)` annotations that survived the ADR-0041 redefinition are corrected to match the code: the `DependencyManifest` boundary-node docstring, the `DOCUMENTATION_PATTERNS`/`FUZZ_BENCH_PATTERNS` headers, the `by_supply_chain_tier` `edges`-view comment, and the taxonomy + spec symbol-extraction rows.

#### Dev workflow

- **`auto-pr` / `merge-pr` recover from Codeberg DB desync** via a branch-push fallback and a merge resync-retry, verifying success by PR record or git ground truth.
- **`ci-failover disengage-cleanup` no longer aborts with SIGPIPE (141)** before removing the failover flag/shim: two `echo … | head -1` summary pipelines under `set -o pipefail` could 141-kill `echo` when `head` closed the pipe early, leaving failover half-torn-down; both now use a here-string.

### Deprecated

- **`Edge.quality` (`{score, reason}`) is deprecated (ADR-0039 ruling 4).** It carries zero independent signal — `score` mirrors `confidence` on every verification-corpus edge and `reason` encodes the emitter mechanism, not a confidence tier. The `deprecated` annotation lands now (SCHEMA_VERSION 0.14.5 → 0.14.6); the field is still emitted this release and removed the next, per the one-version window. Read `confidence` + `confidence_source` + `is_resolved` instead. (`Symbol.quality` is a separate field, not in scope.)

### Fixed

- **`supply_chain_tier` stops carrying the "don't reclassify" mechanism (ADR-0041 §1).** Six protocol linkers (Tauri IPC, wasm-bindgen, message-dispatch, `@hg:` annotations, Yjs CRDT, Solidity ABI) stamped `supply_chain_tier=2` on their synthetic stand-in nodes purely to trip the `_classify_symbols` skip-guard (which bypassed any symbol with `tier != 1`) — encoding a *mechanism* ("this is a synthetic placeholder, don't reclassify it by host-file path") in a field ADR-0041 §1 reserves for supply-chain **distance** only, and mislabeling those nodes `internal_dep`. The skip now keys on each node's `protocol_origin` field (the canonical ADR-0031 synthetic-stand-in marker, already so-treated at the read boundary), so all 14 tier-2 stamps are dropped and the nodes carry their honest first-party (tier-1) distance while still being skipped from host-path reclassification. `supply_chain_summary` per-tier `symbols` counts shift accordingly (the synthetic nodes are non-`file`, so per-tier `files` counts are unaffected).

#### Migration — first-real-Woodpecker-run red tests (Codeberg → GitHub)

- **RCT public-API pin + `connectivity` docs reconciled with the D12 default flip.** Three docstrings and the RCT pin still asserted the pre-D12 `connectivity=True` default; the first real GitHub/Woodpecker full-suite run surfaced the drift.
- **The per-PR Woodpecker gate's `pytest` step installs `jq`.** Agent-infra bash-hook tests (`session_start_logic.sh`) shell out to it; without `jq` the clean container silently skipped five assertions.
- **`generate-architecture --check` is rebase-stable.** GitHub's rebase-merge rewrites merged SHAs, orphaning ARCHITECTURE.md's recorded SHA and red-lighting the next PR's freshness gate; it now keys on `commit_count` distance, which survives rebase.
- **Tracker auto-sync's Woodpecker CI slimmed to `tracker-validate` only.** A `non_tracker_paths` filter drops `hook-tests`/`prepare-git`/`dco` for tracker-ops-only PRs — four container steps that had outrun `do_sync`'s CI-poll timeout and stranded auto-sync.

#### Cross-language call-graph precision — unresolvable-receiver misbinds

- **Unresolvable-receiver calls no longer misbind to an arbitrary same-named internal def, fleet-wide.** A call whose target class couldn't be resolved fell through to a bare short-name lookup and bound to the first same-named method (the Scala `copy` / `setTo`, Swift `create` / `delete` / `run`, and analogous funnels), fabricating thousands of dead-code false-positive magnet edges. A shared `defer_bare_method_call` gate — wired into fourteen analyzers (scala, swift, go, rust, php, js/ts, csharp, groovy, dart, lua, zig, cpp, objc) — withholds a weak short-name bind to a cross-class `method` as an honest unresolved external, while the `inherited_calls` Site-1 MRO walker (registered for the eight inheriting languages) recovers a genuinely-inherited call. Free functions, same-class implicit-`self`, and strong / import-scoped matches still resolve; the discipline is to withhold, never pick-first.
- **Untyped-receiver method-call magnets are demoted at finalize.** An untyped-receiver call (`d.Val()`) whose receiver type can't be inferred still collapsed unrelated call sites onto one `Owner.method`; a language-agnostic detector now demotes the two cleanly-harmful sub-classes — a production→test-helper misbind and a stdlib-interface-method shadow (`Close` / `Parse` / `Len` / …) — to unresolved externals at a finalize sub-step, gated by a durable `spec_validator` check (`no_harmful_receiver_blind_magnets`) that shares the exact predicate so gate and demotion can never drift. Correct-but-unprovable binds (the Rust trait-dispatch funnel) are deliberately kept as ADR-0012 scope, and Rust `Type::method()` scoped calls are marked `receiver=qualified` so a resolved associated-function call is not counted as receiver-blind.

#### Python call-graph resolution

- **Inherited-method calls resolve across the class hierarchy.** Dispatch walks a true C3-linearization MRO (fixing uneven-depth diamonds), resolves cross-file `self.method()` / `self.field.method()` dependency-injection calls via enclosing-class and field-type hints, biases to unresolved when a builtin base shadows an in-tree method (ADR-0029), and disambiguates same-short-name classes and fields.
- **Imports, re-exports, and submodule reads resolve to real first-party nodes.** Module-constant attribute reads, non-package facade re-exports, imported module-level constants, workspace-sibling imports, and symbols defined in `__init__.py` now resolve to the real in-tree node instead of a phantom `external_symbol` twin; in-tree submodule reads emit a `references` edge to the file node and co-referent module aliases retarget instead of self-shadowing. Cross-*package* submodule reads remain a tracked residual.
- **Scope-shadow, alias, and closure gaps closed.** The `module_attr_ref` / `references` retargets no longer mint a confidently-wrong edge when a name is rebound via closure capture or a module-scope reassignment (a missed retarget is a safe phantom, never a wrong edge); module-level function aliases resolve to the real body, closure-factory decorators become reachable via `dispatches_to`, `@property` reads resolve to the in-repo getter, and cross-language dispatch no longer binds to a same-name class in another language.
- **External / stdlib base classes emit unresolved-external `extends` edges instead of being dropped, for every OO language.** A base that resolves to no in-tree symbol is now represented at the framework-agnostic inheritance-linker chokepoint as an `is_resolved=False` extends edge to a boundary node (generalizing the Python + JS/TS fallbacks to Kotlin, Ruby, Java, C#, Scala, PHP, Swift, and recovering dotted bases like `argparse.ArgumentParser`), and external PascalCase constructors (`Path()`, `MagicMock()`) now type as `instantiates` rather than a plain `calls`, so `instantiates` records external constructions instead of zero.

#### Symbol-kind emission & identity

- **Per-language emission fixes.** Go module-variables gate on package scope, Swift fields require a type-body parent, Julia `const` / Elixir `quote`-block `def`s / Nim exported procs extract correctly, JS/TS generators / const-bound expressions / callbacks / IIFEs and CommonJS `require()` reach parity, and Java emits an `imports` edge per declaration (the last holdout). C# / Swift member attribution is corrected — a user-typed property named after its return type, a cross-file `new X()` landing on the class rather than the constructor, and `extension T` members losing their `T.` prefix — and Swift struct-body subscripts become containable.
- **Rust dispatch resolves to the concrete impl.** Enum match-arm bindings adopt the variant's field type so `q.run()` reaches `Query::run`, and a chained return-position call resolves against the receiver's inferred return type so `Cmd::parse().run()` reaches `Cmd::run` — together putting zoxide's whole subcommand tree back on the forward slice.
- **Identity canonicalization.** Doc / markup / template analyzers mint `node.id` / `stable_id` from one shared factory (canonical `sha256:<16hex>`), container / declaration kinds gain a `stable_id` via the orchestrator backstop, Python `shape_id` folds the symbol kind so different kinds no longer collide (`shape_id_scheme` v2 → v3), and C# / Solidity / WGSL body-bearing symbols finally carry a `shape_id`.

#### verify-claims, writer-contract & entrypoint filtering

- **`verify-claims` violated-flow evidence is now drillable.** A violated taint-flow claim previously collapsed thousands of flows to five indistinguishable `<source> -> <sink>` rows; the verdict now deduplicates on full per-flow identity, renders each row with symbol IDs plus a `via N hop(s)` indicator, discloses the true total-vs-distinct count, and attaches a bounded structured `evidence` array to the `--format json` envelope (view version 1.0 → 1.1).
- **Kind-conditioned writer-contract population contract.** A field populated on one Symbol kind (`qualified_name` on `function`) previously masked a 100%-NULL partition on another (`method`); the validator now partitions symbols by `(language, kind)` and flags a registered cell that is 100% NULL across its non-empty partition. The inaugural entries (Python `qualified_name` on function / method / class) emit zero and stand as a regression guard that trips the self-tree validation gate if a producer drops the field.
- **Manifest CLI entrypoints survive the default-view noise filter.** The Phase-D noise filter treated every `entry_role=script` symbol as noise, silently erasing pyproject `[project.scripts]` console-scripts before detection (the ADR-0043 §5 "C3" defect); the predicate is now subset-refined by `meta["entry_point"]` so a script that declares a code target is exempt while a bare npm run-script is still filtered.

#### Projections, classification & CLI

- **Compact / tiered views no longer collapse to ~1 symbol at small budgets.** A slim `compact_node` projection replaces the full ~24-field survey node and the token-budget tiers re-project `features[]` onto the retained set instead of copying it wholesale, restoring compact / tiered containment monotonicity; the projections also recompute their own metrics, carry `centrality`, and disclose omitted-array counts reproducibly across `PYTHONHASHSEED`.
- **Dense non-web source files are no longer silently dropped as minified.** The average-line-length minification heuristic fires only for web-asset extensions, so a dense Python data module or a header-less generated protobuf keeps every symbol and edge; the `@generated` / sourcemap / webpack heuristics stay universal.
- **CLI commands validate input and fail loudly.** Non-directory paths, invalid numeric flags, whitespace-only patterns, and unknown `--kind` / `--language` / `--require-section` values exit `rc=2` with did-you-mean hints; `symbols --kind file` / `variable` return matches; an unexpected error exits `rc=1` cleanly (traceback under `--debug`); `dead-code-maybe` defaults to `--seeds production`; and cold-cache auto-analysis returns the map it just wrote instead of failing `Input file not found: None`.
- **`depends_on_manifest` edges attribute to the importer's own manifest in monorepos.** When a dependency name is declared in several package manifests the lookup was a global last-writer-wins map; it now attributes each import to the nearest enclosing manifest, with a deterministic `disambiguation_fallback` at confidence 0.5 when no candidate encloses the importer.

#### Frameworks, I/O boundaries & the rest

- **Route & framework detection.** More frameworks import-promote from real namespaces (PHP / Scala / Haskell plus six route YAMLs) and five whose detection key diverged from the YAML basename now load; Spring `org.springframework.web`-only apps no longer demote to dev-frameworks, Django signals gate on Django detection, Lit common-word decorators gate to JS/TS, and Starlette `WebSocketRoute` classifies as a websocket handler; Vapor grouped-builder routes (236 endpoints) and Nim/D external-import ids are recovered; build-wrapper scripts (`mvnw` / `gradlew`) no longer seed forward slices; Python route `usage_contexts` carry the `http_method` string; WebSocket framework attribution is dependency-gated; and content-free route-marker twins are excluded from `features[]`.
- **I/O boundary classification is receiver-verified.** Classification verifies the receiver before matching a method-kind primitive (no more untyped `.replace()` phantom-matching `Path.replace`) and constructor-typed locals classify correctly; Python stdlib database primitives (sqlite3 / dbm / shelve) populate `db_read` / `db_write`, and Django ORM I/O is made visible via type-verified `<Model>.objects` / `models.Model`-subclass receivers, with third-party ORMs deliberately excluded as a receiver-inference problem the untyped-method gate correctly refuses.
- **Analyzer catalog completeness.** Every registered analyzer now resolves to an `analysis_runs[]` entry or a `limits.skipped_passes[]` record — an analyzer whose language is absent from the taxonomy no longer silently vanishes on an empty `run=None` result — and the opt-in `rust_analyzer` SCIP backend self-declares its skip reason and maps edge endpoints to real symbol ids (survey output 0 → 739 edges for backend users).
- **Complexity, taint, sketch & limits.** C / C++ / bash / Solidity / WGSL / CMake / Jupyter callables gain `cyclomatic_complexity` / `lines_of_code` (67 languages; ADR-0033 Phase 4) and Ruby CC no longer double-counts; taint reads the resolution verdict from `Edge.is_resolved` so an unresolved call registers no phantom sanitizer; warm `sketch` reads comparison sketches from sidecars (~27s vs 155s), omits binary content, strips `>` blockquotes from `readme_description`, and drops the consumer-less `vocabulary` field; `config_info` aggregates monorepo `packages/` manifests and every distinct license; and the `limits` honesty signals follow their conditional-presence contracts (`partial_results_reason` / `test_files_excluded` / `skipped_languages` / `not_captured`, with `analysis_depth` removed).
- **Introspection surfaces.** `explain` and `sketch` now display captured `Symbol.docstring` (module and function intent), `Symbol.lines_of_code` is renamed `line_span` (physical span, not SLOC) with `profile.languages[*]` corrected to a `{files, loc}` shape, and Python `Symbol.signature` renders real default values and a wider display window, decoupled from `stable_id` so identities do not churn.
- **Containment & file anchors.** Every content-bearing path gets a `kind="file"` anchor and class-body fields, top-level functions, and module variables root at their enclosing container via `contains` edges (~8.8k new edges, restoring subgraph closure); same-file same-name parents are span-disambiguated.
- **CI & smart-test.** `smart-test` scopes per-PR selection and coverage to `merge-base(HEAD, dev)` instead of a stale last-green marker (ending the false-red coverage gate and the eroded speedup), full-suite gains whole-codebase coverage teeth, `check-package-coverage` checks all six packages, and stop-the-line / full-suite / nightly status calls are failover-aware; `FORCE_COLOR` no longer false-reds a green run; and smart-test honors coverage-only test dependencies and filters its reverse-slice input to source files.

### Documentation

- **Documented intentional schema divergences.** The `skipped_passes` two-channel semantics, `derived_skipped {files, paths}`, `profile.loc` vs `node.line_span`, the `access_mode` / `data_direction` applicability matrix, and the shared `sha256:` `stable_id` / `shape_id` surface + `edge_key`↔`stable_id` correspondence + `by_supply_chain_tier` tier scope are all correct-by-design and now say so in the spec / schema.
